# Sabrage — simplification scan

*2026-08-31 · fifteen Codex `gpt-5.6-sol` reviewers at `xhigh` over the whole tree, two Codex
critics, every proposal adversarially verified by an opus agent that re-measured it against the
files. **Recommendations only — nothing under `sabrage/` was changed.***

---

## The short version

Sabrage is **67,088 lines** for a personal launcher. The scan produced 93 proposals; 46 survived
verification as behaviour-preserving, 28 are scope decisions for the owner, 19 were rejected.

**The behaviour-preserving program is worth −7,250 lines (10.8%), landing Sabrage near 59,800.**
Its 46 proposals *claim* −11,269; 4,019 of those lines are counted twice, and the de-double-count
pass proved it group by group. What the program buys is legibility, not capability:

| | lines | what it is |
|---|---:|---|
| Comment prose → constraint + test pointer | −3,757 | 52% of the program, and the only wave with a mechanically provable safety property: the diff contains nothing but comment tokens |
| One shared test-support seam | −1,410 | five hand-forwarded `impl Executor` doubles (510 verified lines) become one `FaultExecutor`; 30 `fn scratch` helpers and 144 teardowns become `tempfile` RAII |
| Fixtures and tables, re-measured after the above | −1,260 | table-driving what is genuinely table-drivable — and only that |
| Wire-boundary pruning | −437 | unread response fields, a serialized recovery graph nothing renders, unreachable UI states |
| Dead code | −386 | tautological tests, an unused DTO, a dead API tail |

Three findings matter more than the number:

1. **The size is mostly earned, not accidental.** 34% of current lines were written by the five
   review-fix commits; the structure is flat (2 traits, 108 structs); and a shingle scan finds only
   **16 code-to-code duplicate blocks ≥12 lines in the entire tree**. There is no copy-paste pile
   to delete. 19 proposals were rejected precisely because the "bloat" was a confirmed fix.
2. **The test mass is not the lever it looks like.** 28,075 test lines against 16,825 code lines
   invites the assumption that half could go. The reviewer that spent its whole budget on this
   concluded that at *equal assertion coverage* only ~2,058 lines can go — and after
   de-double-counting, ~2,650. The rest is 907 tests pinning a pipeline that must stay
   byte-identical to a shell script.
3. **The comment mass is the lever, and it is the one the owner already suspected.** 12,723
   comment lines, 84 blocks ≥20 lines, four module headers alone totalling 470 lines that
   re-narrate shell scripts sitting in the same repo. −3,757 of those lines have a home in a test
   name or a PARITY.md row.

Beyond that, **−27,445 lines of scope cuts** are on the menu — the largest being "stop being two
implementations of one pipeline" (−4,700) and "one game instead of a library" (−3,150). Those are
product decisions, priced and recommended below, not applied.

---

## Where the 67,088 lines are

| | files | total | code | tests | comments | blank |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Rust | 62 | 58,358 | 16,825 | 28,075 | 11,296 | 2,162 |
| Svelte | 16 | 6,184 | ~5,400 | 0 | ~500 | ~300 |
| TypeScript | 14 | 2,213 | ~1,700 | 0 | ~400 | ~100 |
| CSS | 1 | 321 | — | — | — | — |
| **total** | **93** | **67,088** | **23,595** | **28,075** | **12,723** | **2,695** |

Measured facts that shaped every judgement below:

- **1.67 test lines per line of code** — 1,182 fns inside `#[cfg(test)]` modules, 907 with a test attribute.
- **0.67 comment lines per line of code** — 242 contiguous blocks ≥12 lines, 84 of them ≥20 lines (3,145 lines).
- **22,774 of 66,755 current lines (34%) were last written by the five review-fix commits.**
  One third of this codebase is the output of the two adversarial review rounds. Per-file line
  ranges are in the scan's `fix-blame.tsv`; it is what every verifier checked proposals against.
- Flat, not layered: **2 traits** (`Executor` ×7 impls, `BottleName` ×1), 108 structs, 45 enums,
  613 pub items — 101 with zero non-test references outside their own file.
- **16 code-to-code duplicate blocks ≥12 lines** in the whole tree. The largest duplicates
  anywhere in the repo are test harnesses.

---

## The program

The T1+T2 program is worth −7,250 lines, not the −11,269 its 46 accepted proposals claim: 4,019 of those lines are counted twice, and the largest single duplicate is that six separate proposals (H3-1, H5-4, H1-4, V1-5, V2-6, C1-2) are competing implementations of the same five hand-forwarded `impl Executor` test doubles — 510 physically verified lines that can only be deleted once. −7,250 is 10.8% of 67,088 and lands Sabrage near 59,800. What it buys is almost entirely legibility, not capability: ~3,760 lines of comment and doc prose whose constraints move onto the tests that already enforce them (a diff mechanically provable as comment-only), ~2,650 test lines of forwarding decorators, hand-rolled scratch scaffolding and literal-variant duplicates with every CONFIRMED finding keeping a named executable pin, and ~840 lines of dead API, unread wire fields and a hand-rolled signal subsystem. It removes no feature, changes no artifact byte, and touches nothing in `contract/`, `scripts/`, `demo.sh`, `docs/` or `ext/`. It also does not make Sabrage a small program — 59,800 lines for a personal launcher is still large, and the reason is structural, not stylistic: it is a second implementation of a pipeline, 34% of it was written by five review-fix commits, and the honest way to make it small is the scope menu, where a single decision (H1-2) is worth more than the entire refactoring program.

### Wave 1 — Delete what is already dead

**-386 lines.**

Three accepted comment replacements in wave 2 name tests that these deletions remove — H4-2 cites the defs_binds*/defs_cover* tests, H4-4 cites stop_plan_decides_from_the_status_alone, H4-3 cites payload_carries_kind_message_remedy_and_the_reported_flag. Writing those comments first converts them into misinformation, which is the exact failure the comment program exists to avoid. H2-3 must also precede wave 5's V2-7, or the Branch tag it deletes gets reintroduced into the backend tables. Small, fast, and it makes every later wave's pointers truthful. The StopPlan decision (C1-3 vs V3-4's other half) is raised here and settled in wave 4.

Items:

- H3-4 — delete the eight module-local defs() ordering tests plus four derive/framework smoke tests (owner of the whole tautology set: V4-4's entire −75, V7-3's two commands.rs deletions, V2-7's dry_run_executor_is_dry_run)
- H2-3 — delete the dead and test-only API tail; rewrite_graphics_backend goes from (String, Branch) to String, three return sites and eight call sites in the same commit
- V3-4 (non-StopPlan half only) — inline wine_log_candidate_stamped, drop RuntimeStatus.application_name and RestoreMode; move the attempt-0 / -{n+1} byte rule onto wine_log_candidate's doc
- H6-5 — delete the unused ErrorPayload DTO and its self-test; record that this closes the unlanded half of accepted item S-A2-already-reported
- H6-6 — fold NotImplemented into Skipped; fix BOTH preflight arms (preflight.rs:477 and :807), not the one site the proposal names
- H6-7 — prune five unread response fields; keep binaryContractMatches, the compiled-vs-checkout comparison at commands.rs:2031-2037 and Settings.svelte:866-871 byte-for-byte (that pair is the A1-1 fix)
- V4-4 — subsumed by H3-4, 0 incremental lines

*Acceptance.* `cd sabrage && cargo test --workspace` green; `cargo test -p sabrage-core -- --list` diffed against the pre-wave listing shows exactly the intended test names gone, none added or renamed; `registry_binds_in_contract_order_and_covers_every_slug` (checks/mod.rs:616) and the strict-registry parity test still fail when a slug is dropped, duplicated or reordered; `grep -rn 'ErrorPayload\|NotImplemented\|wine_log_candidate_stamped\|RestoreMode' sabrage/` returns zero hits outside intended survivors; `cd sabrage/ui && npm run check`; `scripts/dev/parity.sh` (tier-1) green.

*Effort and cautions.* Half a day. Only H2-3 is not a straight deletion — its signature change drags eleven sites into one commit and must compile as a unit. Keep every `assert_eq!(out, …)` and matches_doctor_anchor assertion verbatim; delete an `assert_eq!(branch, …)` only where that test's byte-exact `out` assertion already pins the same branch.

### Wave 2 — The comment program — 3,139 lines of prose become ~364 lines of constraint plus test pointer

**-3,757 lines.**

It is 52% of the entire program, it is the only wave with a mechanically provable safety property — the diff contains nothing but comment tokens — and it is where the owner sees movement. Nothing structural blocks it once wave 1 has made the test pointers true: Rust test modules sit below the production comment blocks, so the wave 4/5 test refactors shift these anchors only downward, and the two genuine textual collisions (H4-4's signal essay, V5-3's two paragraphs inside V5-6's harness range) are carved out above. Build a content-addressed block ledger before touching anything: 3,024 raw claim-lines resolve to 2,640 distinct ones, and every current comment line must belong to exactly one proposal so no block is rewritten twice.

Items:

- H4-1 (−442) — the four 470-line module anchor headers (stop.rs 144, reconcile.rs 122, build.rs 118, stages/mod.rs 86) become constraint indexes; FIRST, because it shifts every H4-3 anchor in three of those files
- H4-2 (−965) — 25 module essays and shell mirrors; keep checks/config.rs's outstanding 'needs a PARITY.md row' TODO and checks/mod.rs's reason-clause for the verbatim doctor.sh prose rule
- H4-5 (−203) — crate maps and infrastructure headers; keep lib.rs's 'check message/remedy strings track doctor.sh verbatim because docs/troubleshooting.md quotes them' sentence, which no test enforces
- H4-3 (−698) — 31 review-fix proof essays; anchors re-derived by symbol, never by the proposals' line numbers; fix refuses_when_ → refuses_while_a_matching_game_process_is_running and keep goldberg's 'deliberately not scoped to bs_dir (wine puts a Z:\ path on argv)' clause
- H4-4 (−322) — CLI/Tauri/frontend lifecycle narration, EXCLUDING main.rs:1167-1245 (H5-2 deletes that essay with its subsystem in wave 4)
- H4-6 (−70) and V6-6 (−67) — parity commentary; keep lib.rs:1873-1880's substring-only-renderer inventory verbatim (r2 A1-3 is CONFIRMED and unfixed and that text is its evidence) and correct the stale tier-1 claim
- V2-2 (−217), V5-3 (−307), V4-2 (−94), V6-5 (−163), V6-2 (−91), V6-3 (−105), H2-4 (−13) — the vertical comment cuts at their de-double-counted values; V5-3 skips runtime_toml.rs:2754-2859 and :2938-3119 (V5-6 rewrites that region in wave 5)

*Acceptance.* The diff is comment-only: `git diff -U0 | grep '^[-+]' | grep -vE '^[-+][-+]' | grep -vE '^[-+][[:space:]]*(///|//!|//|\*|/\*)'` must print nothing. `cargo doc --workspace --no-deps` builds with no broken intra-doc links. Every test symbol named by a replacement resolves — extract the identifiers from the replacement strings and require `grep -rq "fn <name>" sabrage/` for each; no pointer may return zero matches. `cargo test --workspace` and `scripts/dev/parity.sh --bottle <name>` produce identical output to wave 1. Re-measure the cut set: 3,139 lines should read as roughly 364.

*Effort and cautions.* 2-3 focused days, and the work is reading, not typing: 84 replacement blocks, each of which must name a test that exists and states an invariant that test actually enforces. Two known defects to fix before applying: H4-1's replacement cites a PARITY.md row that does not exist ('Persisted audio-device restore') — re-point it by grep — and V6-3 is credited −105 here while its own verifier says the honest incremental is nearer −50 once events.rs:428-505's step-id ledger is left alone, so budget this wave landing 40-60 lines short. Landmines with no other home in the tree that must survive verbatim: privilege.rs:398-409 and :907-909, adb.rs:114's A1-3 pub-promotion sentence, backend.rs:59's non-canonical-line caveat, stop.rs:1648's determinism note, install.sh's literal-byte-equality/sudo-thrash rule, contract-gen's minimal-encoding sentence, meta.rs's `[ -n "$_have" ]` guard clause, and paths.rs's per-field lib.sh variable names.

### Wave 3 — Prune the wire boundary, then decide whether to generate it

**-437 lines.**

Prune before you generate. H1-3's ~430-line hand-mirrored declaration inventory currently contains ipc.ts:622-722 (the reconcile graph H6-3 deletes), the DoctorEvent/summary declarations H6-4 flattens, and the fields H6-7 pruned in wave 1 — generating first creates exports that are immediately deleted and can resurrect removed fields during conflict resolution. It sits before wave 4 because H6-3 changes the reconcile command's response shape and V8-5 narrows the same session store that the StopPlan reducer re-enters.

Items:

- H6-3 (−86) — return reconciliation rows instead of serializing the recovery graph; the command keeps taking acquire_operation_lock, awaiting reconcile(&ctx) and propagating its error — only the response shape changes
- H6-4 (−50) — delete commands.rs:230-259's duplicate fail/warn counters by consuming the DoctorReport core_run_doctor already returns; DoctorSummary stays on the wire, counts are never re-derived in untested TypeScript
- V8-5 (−106) — delete the unreachable UI states and narrow ipc.ts to consumed projections; keep FIX_META.consequence, DEFERRED_FIX_IDS and RepoInfo's two contract sha256 fields
- H1-3 (−195) — generate the wire types from the serde derives, IPC behavior stays handwritten; LAST, and gated (see effort)

*Acceptance.* `cd sabrage/ui && npm run check`; `cargo test --workspace` with `the_reconcile_types_serialize_camel_case` (reconcile.rs:2651) unchanged and all A9-era recovery coverage intact; for the same streamed doctor rows, the old returned fail/warn/total values equal the new derived ones including the zero-fail and warning-only cases, and a rejected stream presents no completed summary. For H1-3 specifically: generate from a clean checkout, generate twice to prove idempotence, and diff the emitter's output against today's ipc.ts declaration-for-declaration — especially the flatten index signatures and the `kind`-tagged StageEvent union — before deleting one TS line. Then launch once and exercise reconcile, a doctor run, a settings save and a runtime-config write.

*Effort and cautions.* 1-2 days, most of it H1-3. Honest caveat the owner should see: H1-3's own gate says 're-measure the net after the derives land — if it is under ~200 lines the drift guarantee, not the line count, has to justify it,' and the de-double-counted net is −195. It is already below its own threshold. Land H6-3/H6-4/V8-5 first (−242, unambiguous), then take H1-3 on the drift argument alone — it is the only item in the program that adds a build-time dependency, reverses the design statement written at ipc.ts:1, and makes `npm run check` depend on a Rust build. Port the A13b-8/A13b-4 flatten comments and the `end`/StreamKind notes into Rust doc comments before deleting the TypeScript ones.

### Wave 4 — One shared test-support seam and the two structural moves

**-1,410 lines.**

Wave 5 is measured from the tree this wave produces, so it must land first and be re-counted. H3-1 is the single biggest safe lever in the program and the reason five other proposals (H5-4, H1-4, V1-5, V2-6, C1-2) are dropped rather than sequenced — only one implementation of that decorator can exist. One dependency layer per commit, with a green run between each: never H3-1 + V3-2 + wave 5's fixtures in one diff. That combination is the state where a newly shared fault seam silently stops exercising the timing a CONFIRMED finding pinned, and wave 2 has already removed the forensic prose that would have revealed it.

Items:

- H3-1 (−750) — one `#[cfg(test)] mod test_support` at the crate root (it does not exist today) replacing five hand-forwarded Executor doubles (guards.rs:909-998=90, guards.rs:1111-1210=100, reconcile.rs:1379-1487=109, setup.rs:670-778=109, run/mod.rs:1328-1429=102 = 510 verified lines), 30 duplicate `fn scratch` helpers and 144 explicit cleanup lines
- H5-1 (−285) — tempfile RAII over what H3-1 leaves; tempfile must be added as a dev-dep to all three manifests (it is in none today) or the helper made a feature-gated pub module, since sabrage-parity and contract-gen cannot see sabrage-core's cfg(test)
- V3-2 (−200) — extract SessionMonitor's policy into a pure reducer over watcher.rs:372-656
- C1-3 (0 lines) — the StopPlan decision, made explicitly rather than by default
- H5-2 (−175) — replace the raw signal subsystem with signal-hook; its own isolated commit

*Acceptance.* After H3-1, `cargo test -- --list` must be BYTE-IDENTICAL before and after — same names, same count — and for each of the five affected modules the `ctx.executor.planned()` vector and the emitted StageEvent rows must be diffed, not merely re-run green. The shared decorator overrides exactly 13 trait methods and deliberately NOT create_new/hard_link/remove_dir_all_rollback (four of the five doubles inherit those defaults today); `with_step` returns a new decorator wrapping `inner.with_step(step)` carrying both the fault and the shared recorder Arc; FailSwitchTo and DenyWriteTo still delegate to the inner DryRunExecutor FIRST so every attempt stays in the plan in order; LosesTheCreateRace is ported byte-for-byte (its real create_dir_all + write before Ok(false) is the deterministic form of critical r2 A5-3) and `a_config_created_by_another_writer_is_reported_not_replaced` still ends with the competing writer's exact bytes. After H5-1, every surviving `remove_dir_all` in a test module must model disappearance/rotation/rollback rather than teardown (logs.rs rotation, watcher.rs old-incarnation backlog, store/library.rs:1162/1187/1343, install.rs's dxmt.stock-backup sweeps), and the ScratchDir binding must precede any ForeignProcess or Tailer so it drops last. After V3-2, the phase-precedence table must produce identical SessionStatus values, with one file/global/process-backed integration test retained per confirmed finding (A8-5, A9-5, A9-6, A10-8, A13a-2). H5-2 lands only after the three second-signal tests are rebuilt as re-exec child-process tests (env-gated `#[test]` re-execing current_exe with --exact — the demo.sh vocabulary is frozen and main.rs:1430-1492 asserts the usage/exit-2 surface byte-for-byte), with the conditional default registered BEFORE flag::register for each of SIGINT and SIGTERM and one Arc<AtomicBool> shared across both. `cargo test --workspace` and `scripts/dev/parity.sh --bottle <name>` green between every commit.

*Effort and cautions.* 3-4 days and the highest-risk wave in the program. H3-1 alone is a day and a half if the planned()/event diffing is done properly rather than trusting pass/fail. H5-2 nets well under its −175 once the child-process tests are written back — its −175 is prod-code-only. C1-3 is a decision, not a diff: either finish StopPlan as the single session authority (accepted item S-A9-ensure-idle, half-landed at 6d98864, and the reducer must carry stop_targets_live_session's bottle scoping plus A11-1's TimedOut wording and A11-4's quit timeout), or take V3-4's other branch, delete session/mod.rs:565-602 and its test at :1010-1069, and record in docs/reviews/2026-08-30-simplify.md that the consolidation was dropped. Deleting removes 98 more lines and makes the program total −7,348; −7,250 assumes finishing it. Both are defensible; what is not defensible is letting it go by default.

### Wave 5 — Fixtures and tables, re-measured from the new tree

**-1,260 lines.**

Every figure here was estimated in isolation against today's tree, and wave 4 removes the lines several of them were counting — that is exactly what produced the 2,170- and 220-line overlaps this program corrects. Re-measure before starting: V3-3 falls from −160 to −55 once H5-1 owns directory lifetime, V5-6 from −110 to −78, V5-5 from −90 to −70, V4-3 from −220 to −179, H3-3 from −180 to −145, and H3-2 from −300 to −12 once V7-2 owns the CLI corpus, V2-7 owns backend/build and V4-3 owns the check matrices. If the post-wave-4 recount lands materially under −1,260, that is the right answer, not a failure — this is the least confident block in the whole program (C2 rates it 0.70-0.75).

Items:

- V7-2 (−340) — table-drive the CLI parser and renderer corpus; EXCLUDE the three signal tests at main.rs:2508-2637 (H5-2 rebuilt them in wave 4)
- V4-3 (−179) — table-drive the repeated filesystem outcome matrices in the check modules
- H3-3 (−145) — scenario builders in run/mod.rs, reconcile.rs and stop.rs only; three of its seven target files already have the builders it proposes
- V2-7 (−137) — literal matrices in fixes/backend.rs, build.rs, adb.rs and install.rs
- V1-6 (−118) — fold duplicate phase assertions into the teardown-arm tests, re-hosted on the observing sink
- V5-6 (−78) — runtime-config patch/write harnesses; land before V5-3's two carved-out comment paragraphs
- V5-5 (−70) — store fixtures; keep the_success_message_never_claims_the_original_was_restored as its own named test (A13b-7)
- V4-5 (−75) — generate the trivial evaluator wrappers and hoist first_connected_serial/is_executable/basename; do NOT merge headset's unbounded adb_devices_output into run_only's bounded one (that bound is the A7-4 fix)
- V3-3 (−55) — one RAII session fixture; Drop must be best-effort, lock_session_globals() and run-id-guarded clear_live_session untouched
- V7-3 (−51) — the two genuinely enumerable Tauri policy tables (classify_repo_root_source, quit_intercept_decision)
- H3-2 (−12) — the watcher encoder parser, its only family not owned by V7-2/V2-7/V4-3

*Acceptance.* An assertion ledger per module before any deletion: every pre-existing assertion maps one-to-one to a named table row or a fixture method, counted per field (status, message, remedy, detail). `cargo test -p sabrage-core`, `cargo test -p sabrage-cli`, `cargo test -p sabrage-app`; test-function counts unchanged where the item is an extraction rather than a merge. For V7-2, capture the renderer corpus's stdout/stderr before and after and byte-compare, then run tier-2 (`scripts/dev/parity.sh --bottle <name>`) because it rebuilds and executes that binary. A deliberate one-case mutation must report that row's stable label, not just the containing table fn. These must survive as individually named fns with their own bespoke setup: shadowed_protocol_alvr_then_oxrsys_resolves_to_the_last_assignment, malformed_json_warns, unreadable_session_json_warns (A3b-1/A3b-3), the_devices_probe_gives_up_on_a_wedged_adb_instead_of_blocking_forever (A7-4), the_success_message_never_claims_the_original_was_restored (A13b-7), and the six commands.rs tests that doc comments point at. Every A14-1/A14-3/A14-4/A14-5 finding id and each assert's message string survives as a case label.

*Effort and cautions.* 3-4 days across per-module commits; no single item is large, but each needs its own before/after assertion ledger and none of them can be batched. The one piece of work most likely to be done twice is the CLI signal tests — V7-2 must explicitly skip them because H5-2 already rebuilt them in wave 4. Leave logs.rs's Tailer tests, runtime_toml's edit_protocol family and main.rs's merge_* family alone: H3-2's verifier confirmed they are not table-drivable, which is most of why it fell from −300 to −12.

### After the program lands

Sabrage goes from 67,088 lines (23,595 code / 28,075 test / 12,723 doc+comment / 2,695 blank) to roughly 59,840: about 22,750 code, 25,400 test, 8,970 doc+comment, blanks unchanged. By crate: sabrage-core 49,345 → ~43,700; ui 8,730 → ~8,500; src-tauri 3,253 → ~3,050; sabrage-cli 2,639 → ~1,950; sabrage-parity 2,335 → ~2,200; contract-gen 786 → ~770. The comment-to-code ratio in the worst files collapses — commands.rs from 0.57 to roughly 0.25, ipc.ts from 0.76 to under 0.2, stages/mod.rs from 0.49 to ~0.2 — and the four 470-line module anchor headers become about 28 lines of constraint index. The five hand-forwarded Executor doubles become one `#[cfg(test)] mod test_support` FaultExecutor; ~30 duplicate `fn scratch` helpers and ~144 explicit `remove_dir_all` teardowns become `tempfile` RAII (added as a dev-dep to three manifests that do not have it today); SessionMonitor's interleaved observation and policy become a pure reducer plus an exhaustive observation table.

What remains is genuinely irreducible under the current product contract. (1) The native pipeline itself — ~13,500 lines of stage bodies, check evaluators and artifact writers — exists because CLAUDE.md declares two independent implementations of one pipeline and forbids either from invoking the other; it cannot shrink without becoming a shell wrapper. (2) sabrage-parity's ~2,200 lines are dev-only test product: a two-sided witness that reads the working checkout to catch compiled-vs-checkout drift, not a fourth application. (3) runtime_toml.rs stays around 3,200 lines because it deliberately models two different semantics — the consumer's table-blind, last-valid, line-oriented reader and a byte-preserving toml_edit editor that only answers whether a rewrite is safe; the test mass shrinks by ~385, the dual semantics do not. (4) Executor as the mutation/dry-run boundary, reconciliation's live/dead/recycled/unverifiable identities, and Tailer's bounded rotation/truncation/backlog behavior are load-bearing production boundaries. (5) About 360 lines of surviving one-line landmine comments — host-manifest file-form-vs-comparison-form bytes, logical repo-root spelling, privilege staging, global-test serialization, BSD-sed behavior, the step-id → run.sh `# launch-action:` index — state constraints no test can infer. (6) ~25,400 test lines across ~1,150 test functions, which is where C1-2 wanted to build one labelled scenario corpus; that is rejected here as a sixth implementation of H3-1's work (its own verifier scoped it to the same ~500 lines), so after wave 5 the next real lever is not another refactor — it is the scope menu.

### Dropped from the program

Items removed by de-double-counting or conflict resolution — they are real, but another item
already owns the lines:

- C1-2 — dropped entirely: it is a sixth implementation of the same five `impl Executor` doubles H3-1 owns, and its own verifier scoped it to '~500 lines' of those decorators, so its claimed −1,300 is both duplicate and internally inflated. The broader 'one labelled scenario corpus' idea it proposes is rejected on top of that: forcing concurrency, PID identity, rotation and real-subprocess tests into a generic runner to save lines is exactly what its own what_breaks warns against.
- H5-4 — dropped as a line item: same five executor doubles as H3-1, which is strictly larger (510 executor lines plus 30 duplicate scratch helpers plus 144 cleanup lines plus the event sinks) and supplies the crate-wide support module the others would compose against. Its precautions are kept: LosesTheCreateRace ported byte-for-byte, with_step rebuilt around the fault plus the shared recorder Arc.
- H1-4 — dropped as a line item, precaution folded into H3-1: four of the five doubles inherit the Executor trait's default create_new/hard_link/remove_dir_all_rollback rather than forwarding them, so the shared decorator must override exactly 13 methods and deliberately not those three. That observation is the most valuable thing in H1-4 and it survives.
- V1-5 — dropped: it replaces three of the five doubles (run/mod.rs:1328-1429, guards.rs:909-998, guards.rs:1105-1210), a strict subset of H3-1. Its correct point that the shared type cannot live in `run::tests` (those items are private and guards::tests cannot name them) is carried into H3-1's crate-root `#[cfg(test)] mod test_support`.
- V2-6 — dropped: it reaches the same setup.rs double through install.rs's 230-line pass-through executor instead of a shared decorator, which is more plumbing for less coverage once H3-1 exists.
- V4-4 — dropped: all eight module-local defs() tests measure exactly 75 lines, precisely V4-4's entire measured value, and every one of them is inside H3-4's deletion set. It contributes literally zero incremental lines.
- V7-3 (deletion half) — dropped: commands.rs:2799-2810 and :2844-2857 are inside H3-4. V7-3 keeps only classify_repo_root_source and quit_intercept_decision (−51).
- V2-7 (dry_run_executor_is_dry_run) — dropped: stop.rs:1537-1544 is inside H3-4. V2-7 keeps only its literal matrices (−137).
- V7-2 (signal family) — dropped explicitly rather than left to be discovered: H5-2 replaces the raw signal subsystem and destroys the watch_flag_with_second_action seam the three tests at main.rs:2543-2638 depend on, rebuilding them as child-process tests. Table-driving them afterward is duplicated work. V7-2 keeps the renderer (−180..−230) and parser (−120) corpora, which is where its −340 came from anyway.
- H3-2 (four of five families) — reduced from −300 to −12: checks/config.rs:419-547 is V4-3's range byte-identical, fixes/backend.rs:363-472 and build.rs:826-869 are V2-7's, and CLI main.rs:1430-1564/:1746-1807 sit inside V7-2's. Only the watcher encoder parser at watcher.rs:818-873 is uniquely its own.
- C1-3 — kept in the program at zero lines, not as a saving: its own verifier concluded the wider session consolidation is 'net plausibly zero or positive' once the reducer and its exhaustive observation table are written. V3-2 owns the reducer (−200); C1-3 survives only as the decision to finish StopPlan rather than delete it.
- V3-4 (StopPlan half) — dropped, 98 lines of its −157 given back: session/mod.rs:565-602 (38 lines) plus its test at :1010-1069 (60) stay. `stop_plan` genuinely has zero production callers, but it traces to 6d98864 and docs/reviews/2026-08-30-simplify.md:17's ACCEPTED S-A9-ensure-idle item, so deleting it reverts an accepted fix. V3-4 keeps wine_log_candidate_stamped, RuntimeStatus.application_name and RestoreMode (−59). If the owner picks the delete branch instead, drop C1-3 and the program totals −7,348.
- V6-6 (three of eight prologues) — reduced from −150 to −67: sabrage-parity/src/lib.rs holds 236 doc+comment lines total, yet H4-6 (86 physical) and V6-6 (158) together claimed 244 — a file cannot give up more comment lines than it has. H4-6's three blocks are a strict subset; V6-6 keeps its five distinct prologues.
- H2-4 (core lib.rs and store/mod.rs) — reduced from −100 to −13: both are inside H4-5's verified ranges (64 and 28 `//!` lines). H2-4 keeps only config/mod.rs, and its three landmine amendments (install.sh's literal-byte-equality/sudo-thrash rule, the doctor.sh-verbatim prose rule, store/mod.rs's Goldberg divergence rationale for the still-unfixed A13a-1) are carried into H4-5's replacements instead.
- H4-4 (main.rs:1167-1245) — dropped, 74 lines given back: H5-2 deletes that 79-line signal essay together with the subsystem it describes at main.rs:1247-1387. H4-4 keeps its other thirteen blocks (−322).
- V5-3, V2-2, V4-2, V6-5, V6-2, V6-3 (H4-owned blocks) — reduced by 93, 163, 50, 21, 37 and 20 respectively: each re-claims comment blocks H4-2 or H4-3 already owns, verified to the line (runtime_toml.rs:1-64/:733/:1202; stop.rs:397-417/:488-533/:607-632; install.rs:422-444/:536-557; fixes/mod.rs:366-395; meta.rs:25-62; contract.rs:269-289; privilege.rs:382+28; paths.rs:135-159/:189-208; events.rs:260-283). H4-2/H4-3 win because their replacements carry the most specific invariant-plus-test pointers.
- H5-1, H3-3, V3-3, V5-6, V5-5, V4-3 (scratch and teardown lines) — reduced by 185, 35, 105, 32, 20 and 41: all six count the same repo-wide `fn scratch` / `remove_dir_all` scaffolding H3-1 and then H5-1 remove first. These are the least confident corrections in the set (C2 rates them 0.70-0.75) and wave 5 exists to re-measure them from the post-wave-4 tree rather than carry any of these figures forward.
- V8-5 and H1-3 (wire overlap) — reduced by 90 and 65: V8-5 counts ipc.ts:620-686, the reconcile wire tree H6-3 deletes end-to-end, plus ipc.ts:471-507's isActivePhase/PHASE_TONE that H2-3 also lists; H1-3's ~430-line hand-mirrored inventory then includes declarations H6-3/H6-4/H6-7 have already removed. Generating before pruning would create exports that are immediately deleted.
- The 18 REJECTed proposals are not in the program at all — thirteen comment cuts (H1-5, H7-9, V1-7, V2-1, V3-1, V4-1, V5-4, V6-1, V6-4, V6-7, V7-1, V8-4, H6-2) that would have erased landmines with no other home in the tree, H5-3 (a worse-shaped duplicate of H1-3/H6-1), and five scope cuts (H7-6, H7-7, V1-1, V1-4, C1-1). C1-1 in particular — replacing the Tauri/Svelte boundary with a native Rust UI — deletes ~11,900 lines but adds an unwritten UI of unknown size and dissolves the app.exit interception that PARITY.md's Cmd-Q row and A11-4's fix both stand on.

## Scope decisions — priced, ordered, and recommended

The owner invited these explicitly. Each removes a capability; each is costed in lines and
carries a recommendation, but none is a mechanical cleanup and none was applied.

| lines | recommendation | cut | buys | costs |
|---:|---|---|---|---|
| -3,150 | **take it** | **One configured Beat Saber target instead of a game library** <br/>`H7-2 / V8-1 / V5-1 (à-la-carte: H2-2, V7-4, H2-5)` | The best lines-per-value cut in the menu, because it removes a capability the pipeline underneath cannot use: CLAUDE.md pins Beat Saber to exactly 1.29.4 (newer builds hard-crash on the Meta account gate), and demo.sh's entire game surface is one --bs-dir. Out go library.rs's Ready/NeedsSetup/Goldberg health snapshot, per-game launch overrides, last-session history, the Library and EditGame screens and their IPC. The coupling really is severable — nothing in sabrage-core's stages touches `library::`, and game_id is already an Option. | Saved multi-game entries and per-game flags stop existing. Whoever implements it must re-home library::effective_options' settings-over-override precedence onto Settings::effective_stage_options and bump LIBRARY_VERSION, or existing launchOverrides/lastSession are silently eaten on the next save. It also makes the Goldberg .orig-steam revert unreachable from every front-end — it has no CLI counterpart — which is free if you also take H7-3 and a hidden capability loss if you don't. H7-2 and H7-3 overlap by roughly 150 lines in the health snapshot: take both and budget ~−4,050, not −4,230. |
| -1,080 | **take it** | **Delete the Goldberg revert action and its provenance journal** <br/>`H7-3 / V5-2` | Closes four still-unfixed high findings (A7-3, A13a-1, A13a-2, round-2 A13b-3) by removing the mutation door rather than bolting a fifth guard onto it. It is Sabrage-only: demo.sh's run swaps steam_api64.dll every launch and has never had a revert, so no parity row is weakened by its absence — only by its current false claim. | One-click restore of .orig-steam goes; recovery becomes a manual file copy. PARITY.md's row declaring the revert as landed must be corrected, and A7-3's provenance-marker machinery in run/actions.rs becomes dead and must go with it. Keep actions.rs:473-505 (PARITY.md:115's non-regular .orig-steam refusal) and the already_goldberg skip at :534-546 untouched. |
| -437 | **take it** | **Retire the executable session.json deletion backend, keep the tombstone** <br/>`V2-3` | It deletes the implementation of a remedy this project has documented as harmful: CLAUDE.md states that deleting alvr/session.json 'is a proven-broken remedy that streams a black 800x900 screen — edit the pinned IP in place'. The deferred tombstone stays, so the fix id keeps its contract declaration and the GUI keeps refusing it. Removing code that does a known-bad thing is the cleanest 437 lines available. | PARITY.md:120 currently describes the action as 'withheld' rather than absent and must be corrected. Two fixes/mod.rs lock tests (apply_waits_for_the_operation_lock_then_proceeds, apply_holding_lock_is_not_gated_by_a_live_session) rely on it being a cheap Ok no-op and need re-pointing at another action. The GUI refusal string telling users to 'run `sabrage fix …` explicitly' names a subcommand that does not exist and should go with it. |
| -60 | **take it** | **Reduce the contract-generator binary to the one workflow scripts use** <br/>`V4-6` | --check and --repo-root have no caller in scripts/, in CI, or in the git hook, and tier-1's check_reports_in_sync_against_the_live_checkout (parity lib.rs:139) already runs the identical library check(). Sixty lines for zero coverage loss — the only entry here with no argument against it. | A documented dev affordance disappears; anyone wanting the check from a shell runs `cargo test -p sabrage-contract-gen` instead. |
| -2,050 | lean take | **Turn Doctor's one-click fixes into remedy text** <br/>`H7-5 (deep) / V8-2 (shallow, −659)` | Closes five still-unfixed findings including a critical, and puts the native doctor exactly where demo.sh's doctor already is: every FAIL prints a remedy and the human runs it. Launch autofixes are explicitly retained, so the two conditions that actually recur (reverted graphics backend, swept arm64 helper) still self-heal. V8-2's shallow version removes only the GUI surface for −659 and keeps the fix implementations if you want a reversible step. | One-click set-backend, restage-helper, remove-adb-forwards and edit-protocol vanish from every front-end — the proposal's claim that you can 'invoke the existing CLI fix command' is false, `sabrage` has no `fix` subcommand. DEFERRED_CONTRACT_FIX_IDS must grow from 2 to 8, meaning contract/pipeline.toml keeps declaring six fix ids that nothing implements: real contract rot traded for lines. Must not touch stages/mod.rs:770-795 (A11-3's recheck) or runtime_toml.rs's shared rolling-backup helper. |
| -1,070 | lean take | **Remove the Logs screen, keep the Tailer** <br/>`H7-8 / V8-3 (sub-cut: V3-5, −139)` | The multi-source log browser has no consumer outside Logs.svelte, and Tailer plus every parity-pinned log-naming test is untouched — so the encoder chip and the Stalled warning survive, which is the whole reason to prefer this over C1-4. V3-5 is the smaller version that drops only the OXRSys-runtime tab, the ALVR tab and the past-run picker while keeping current-run tailing in-app. | In-app tailing and filtering of the wine, oxrsys and ALVR logs and past-run browsing are gone, along with Session's 'Open in Logs' and 'Re-attach (follow log)' affordances. The files stay on disk and demo.sh never had a browser, so the answer becomes 'here is the path, use Console.app' — acceptable for a single-user tool, less so mid-session with a headset on. |
| -402 | lean take | **Per-game overrides, Last Session and the App ID copy, à la carte** <br/>`H2-2 + V7-4 + H2-5` | The three severable slivers of the library if you keep the registry: launch_options_for and the per-entry override tier (−196), persisted last-session metadata (−158), and three write-only fields — created/updated timestamps and the per-entry App ID, which steam_appid.txt never reads because it comes from contract().game.appid (−48). | Per-game flags stop existing, and launch_options_for was added by the r1 fix wave as the single home for that merge. The Library's Last Session column becomes a permanent '—' and A13b-5's rationale is stranded. The App ID display goes and those keys are permanently dropped from existing library.json on the next save. All three sit inside H7-2 — if you are cutting the library at all, take that instead and get 3,150. |
| -221 | lean take | **Delete the About screen** <br/>`H2-1 / H7-4` | 221 lines of genuinely inert code: zero findings, zero review-fix lines, no test depends on it, and the measurement is exact. | It is the app's only third-party attribution surface — LGPL-2.1, LGPL-3.0, MPL-2.0 and MIT notices for Wine, DXMT, oxrsys, wineopenxr, ALVR and Goldberg — and Tauri's native About shows bundle metadata only. Take it only after those six notices land somewhere that ships (a bundled THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES file, or README.md), never as a straight delete. |
| -195 | lean take | **Generate the TypeScript wire types instead of hand-mirroring them** <br/>`H6-1 (== H1-3 in wave 3)` | 50 of ipc.ts's 51 exported types have a 1:1 Rust struct or enum, and ~430 declaration lines are maintained by hand across a boundary that has already produced findings. The purchase here is the drift guarantee, not the line count. | It reverses the design statement written at ipc.ts:1 ('No codegen — update both by hand'), adds a dependency to a crate whose Cargo.toml documents deliberate minimalism, and makes `npm run check` depend on a Rust build. This is the same decision as wave 3's H1-3, whose own gate says that under ~200 net lines the drift guarantee must carry it alone — and the de-double-counted net is −195, already under that line. Decide it as an architecture question, not a savings one. |
| -720 | lean keep | **Retire native --wired forward creation, keep stale-forward cleanup** <br/>`V1-2` | 720 lines, and demo.sh --wired still creates the forwards. The half worth keeping — the preflight that removes exactly tcp:9943 and tcp:9944 so a stale forward cannot silently break WiFi discovery — is retained. | It is not a clean cut at any price. It needs a contract per-side launch gate to declare the divergence, and contract/ is out of scope for this program; it deletes the round-2 A7-4 blocking-probe fix; and it orphans the wired_forwards readers in session/reconcile.rs and stop.rs, which then need their own sweep. Three follow-on edits for a feature that costs nothing to keep. |
| -1,330 | **keep it** | **Delete log browsing and derived stream telemetry as one bundle** <br/>`C1-4` | −1,330 incremental on top of H7-8, and it is the honest unit: Tailer exists for the browser and for the encoder chip, and outside the Logs IPC its only production consumers are watcher.rs:28, :233 and :319. Held together, Logs + TailRegistry + Tailer + encoder-log parsing + the Stalled phase are one optional product feature, not five. | It takes the encoder chip, which is the GUI's only signal of a failure mode CLAUDE.md documents as silent: a build-x64 re-configure used to sweep the staged arm64 helper and drop a session to '(H.264, in-process)' with no other symptom. That chip is the clearest thing Sabrage does that demo.sh cannot, and it is still listed as unverified in the project's own notes — killing it before it has been confirmed working is the wrong order. Also loses the Stalled warning and detached-session log reattachment. |
| -380 | **keep it** | **Make run preflight check-only; require Setup or Build for repairs** <br/>`V1-3` | 380 lines of launch-time repair logic, with Setup and Build doing the repairs instead. | It drops self-heal for precisely the two conditions CLAUDE.md documents as recurring in practice — a CrossOver update reverting the bottle's graphics backend, and a build-x64 re-configure sweeping the staged arm64 helper. demo.sh run repairs both, so Sabrage would fail to launch exactly where the shell succeeds. That is a parity regression on the two failures the project has actually hit, sold for 380 lines. |
| -300 | **keep it** | **Remove the public dry-run surface, keep DryRunExecutor** <br/>`C1-5` | The correct unit for this cut, and the one that survives the assertion that killed H7-6: only the CLI flag, the GUI Plan buttons, the IPC dryRun fields and the plan renderer go, while DryRunExecutor, StageCtx::for_fixture, executor plans and every privilege-argv assertion stay as internal infrastructure. | This is a launcher that runs sudo — 'show me the writes, spawns and admin argv before you do them' is the single feature most worth having in it. It retires two declared PARITY.md CLI/GUI rows the proposal never mentions and leaves the A6-2/A5-1 dry-run-honesty rows reachable only from tests. 300 lines is not a price worth paying for that. |
| -2,000 | **keep it** | **Cut dry-run as a cross-cutting launcher feature** <br/>`H3-5` | The maximal version of the dry-run cut, and on paper the second-largest single number outside the pipeline decisions. | It cannot reach its number. DryRunExecutor is also the hermeticity mechanism for 24 core test sites and sabrage-parity's steam_appid planning golden, so an equivalent public non-mutating executor must be built back — and it deletes the fixes for four CONFIRMED findings (A2-3, A5-1, A5-4, A6-2) plus round-2's still-unfixed A5-1. If dry-run goes at all it goes in C1-5's shape, not this one. |
| -138 | **keep it** | **Drop derived Ninja progress events, keep raw build output** <br/>`V2-5` | 138 lines and the only producer of StageEvent::Progress in the app. | The worst lines-per-value in the menu. GateModal's determinate progress bar goes dead for the multi-minute oxrsys build — the one place a GUI is unambiguously better than a terminal — and failing ninja builds also lose their ChildFailed tail, because run_child_ok reports an empty one. You would be paying a diagnostic regression and the app's best moment for 138 lines. |
| -1,758 | **keep it** | **Keep a doctor-only CLI shim; delete the rest of the native CLI** <br/>`H1-1 / H7-1` | 1,758 lines. Tier-2 parity only ever drives `sabrage doctor --bottle X --tap F`, so the live differ survives intact, and demo.sh still covers all six stages from a terminal. | It deletes the CLI half of six PARITY.md ledger rows — those rows are literally `sabrage <stage>` against `./demo.sh <stage>`, and half of each becomes unassertable — and it hollows out A4-3's GUI-vs-CLI cross-process lock, which needs two front-ends to mean anything. The native pipeline stops being runnable without a webview. This is where the two-implementations property starts dissolving, and it should be decided as such, not as a cleanup. |
| -4,700 | **keep it** | **Make setup, build and install shell-only maintenance workflows** <br/>`H1-2` | The biggest number in the exercise: 4,604 lines of native stage bodies plus glue, more than half the entire T1+T2 refactoring program in one decision, and it is technically expressible. | It ends the two-implementations property for three of five stages. The native side of four PARITY.md 'must NOT change' byte invariants and 20 contract-declared Doctor fix buttons goes with it, and sabrage-parity's artifact-byte goldens lose the side they were comparing against — `contract/`, the golden bytes and PARITY.md are then mostly serving one implementation. What remains is a GUI that can run and stop but tells you to open a terminal to set up, build or install: a different product from the one CLAUDE.md describes. If the owner genuinely wants this, it is a decision about what Sabrage is, and the −7,250 program is beside the point. |

---

## Why the headline is 7,250 and not 11,269

HEADLINE: the T1+T2 program is honestly worth about -7,250 lines, not the -11,269 its measured claims sum to. 44 merged ACCEPT/ACCEPT_WITH_CARE proposals sum to -9,819; the two critic proposals that just earned ACCEPT_WITH_CARE add -1,300 (C1-2) and -150 (C1-3). 4,019 lines of that are counted twice.

C2'S ARITHMETIC: I reproduced it exactly. Its eleven corrections sum to 2,471 against a raw -9,819, giving its stated -7,348, and every group's "combined -X" figure resolves against the MEASURED (not claimed) values — C2 used the right column throughout. Two small slips, both under 10 lines and both in the conservative direction: C2-9's stated components (90+58) sum to 148, not the 155 it charges; C2-1's components measure 109 by my reading (75+26+8), not 112. I kept C2's larger figures in both cases. Its per-proposal discounts in the comment group are well-founded and if anything slightly conservative (I get ~86 for V5-3 where it charges 93, ~25 for V6-5 where it charges 21).

WHAT C2 COULD NOT SEE: the critic verdicts landed after it ran, and they are the largest single double-count in the program. C1-2 at -1,300 is a sixth implementation of the exact five `impl Executor` doubles that H3-1/H5-4/H1-4/V1-5/V2-6 already claim, and its own verifier scoped it to "~500 lines" of those same six decorators — so its full -1,300 is duplicate, and internally inflated besides. C1-3 at -150 subsumes V3-2 and is mutually exclusive with V3-4. Together these add 1,548 of raw and 1,548+... specifically 1,300+248 = 1,548 of correction, which is why my total is 98 lines BELOW C2's despite starting 1,450 lines above it.

THE 98-LINE BRANCH: -7,250 assumes C1-3's branch (finish StopPlan, keep the accepted S-A9-ensure-idle item). If the owner picks V3-4's branch instead — delete StopPlan and record the consolidation as dropped in docs/reviews/2026-08-30-simplify.md — drop C1-3 and add 98 lines back, giving -7,348. Both are defensible; -7,250 is the conservative number to quote.

PHYSICAL VERIFICATION: I re-counted every disputed range in the tree rather than trusting the proposals. Every H4 header count matched exactly (stop.rs 144, reconcile.rs 122, build.rs 118, stages/mod.rs 86, core lib.rs 64, runtime_toml.rs 64, privilege.rs 81, install.rs 71, fixes/mod.rs 47, store/mod.rs 28, config/mod.rs 15, contract.rs 29, events.rs 27, meta.rs 17, paths.rs 18). Every cited comment block matched to the line. The five executor doubles measure 90+100+109+109+102 = 510, exactly H3-1's "about 510". The eight defs() tests measure exactly 75, exactly V4-4's whole measured value.

THE CEILING TEST FOUND ONE HARD IMPOSSIBILITY: sabrage-parity/src/lib.rs holds 236 doc+comment lines (per-file.tsv), but H4-6 (86 physical) and V6-6 (158 physical) together claim 244. A file cannot give up more comment lines than it has. The union of their range sets is 158 distinct lines — H4-6's three blocks are a strict subset of V6-6's eight. Three more files are at the edge and confirm the same pattern without formally breaking: runtime_toml.rs raw claims 519 of 555 non-test comment lines (93%; distinct = 411), events.rs raw claims 192 of 197 (98%; distinct = 168), stop.rs raw claims 387 of 398 (97%; distinct = 294). After de-duplication all four fall inside their ceilings.

RESIDUAL OVER-COUNT I DID NOT PUT IN THE TOTAL (about 55-100 lines, verification haircuts rather than double-counts, so the total stays a clean de-double-count): (1) V6-3 is measured -125 but its own verifier concludes "cutting the 81 variant lines and leaving the 60 step-id lines alone still lands roughly -70 to -80" — the step-id ledger at events.rs:428-505 is the map from each step id to the numbered shell block it mirrors, which is how CLAUDE.md's both-sides-same-commit rule is navigated. Honor that precaution and subtract the 24 lines H4-3 owns, and V6-3's honest incremental is nearer -50 than the -105 this accounting credits. (2) H5-2's -175 is prod-code-only; its verifier notes the three second-signal tests lose their seam and "must become child-process tests, which will add roughly as" much back. (3) H4-4's ipc.ts:372 block sits inside the surface H1-3 generates away — a handful of lines, unpriced.

TEST-SIDE CROSS-CHECK (independent of the group arithmetic): the raw test-reducing claims sum to about -5,695 against 28,075 test lines. H3, the reviewer who actually did the file-by-file test census, independently put the credible target at -2,058. After the corrections above the test side lands near -2,700 — within reach of H3's figure once V3-2/V5-5/V5-6/V7-2/V7-3 files outside H3's scope are added. The raw number was 2.8x H3's own considered estimate; the honest one is 1.3x. That is the strongest single sanity check that 4,019 is the right order of correction.

BOOKKEEPING CAVEAT: group memberships overlap by design — H3-1 appears in two groups, H5-1 in two, V2-7 and V4-3 in two each. The honest total is raw minus the sum of PER-PROPOSAL discounts, each of which is charged exactly once (C1-2 1300; H5-4 340; H1-4 290; V1-5 165; V2-6 75; V5-3 93; V2-2 163; V4-2 50; V6-5 21; V6-2 37; V6-3 20; V6-6 83; H2-4 87; H4-4 74; V4-4 75, V7-3 26, V2-7 8 [charged as 112]; H5-1 185; H3-3 35; V3-3 105; V5-6 32; V5-5 20; V4-3 41; H3-2 288; wire group 155; C1-3 150; V3-4 98). Do not add the groups' joint figures together — they share members and would double-count the de-double-counting.

CONFIDENCE: the executor group (2,170), the H4 comment group (384), the parity/crate-header groups (170), the signal group (74) and the test-tautology group (112) are all verified line-for-line in the tree and are solid — call that 2,910 of the 4,019 high confidence. The scratch/RAII/table corrections (220 + 198 + 288 = 706) are implementation-sensitive: a broader H3-1 leaves less for H5-1 and H3-3, and the right move is to land H3-1 first and remeasure rather than carry any of those figures forward. The wire group (155) and the session-authority group (248) depend on ordering decisions that have not been made yet.

### Overlap groups

Each group is a set of proposals that remove overlapping regions. `joint` is what actually comes
out if all of them land; `owner` is the id that should own the region.

| proposals | sum of claims | joint | owner |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| `C1-2`, `H3-1`, `H5-4`, `H1-4`, `V1-5`, `V2-6` | -2,920 | **-750** | `H3-1` |
| `H4-2`, `H4-3`, `V5-3`, `V2-2`, `V4-2`, `V6-5`, `V6-2`, `V6-3` | -3,024 | **-2,640** | `H4-3` |
| `H4-6`, `V6-6` | -220 | **-137** | `H4-6` |
| `H4-5`, `H2-4` | -303 | **-216** | `H4-5` |
| `H4-4`, `H5-2` | -571 | **-497** | `H5-2` |
| `H3-4`, `V4-4`, `V7-3`, `V2-7` | -430 | **-318** | `H3-4` |
| `H3-1`, `H5-1`, `H3-3` | -1,400 | **-1,180** | `H3-1` |
| `H5-1`, `V3-3`, `V5-6`, `V5-5`, `V4-3` | -1,050 | **-852** | `H5-1` |
| `H3-2`, `V7-2`, `V2-7`, `V4-3` | -1,005 | **-717** | `V7-2` |
| `V8-5`, `H6-3`, `H2-3`, `H6-7`, `H1-3`, `H6-4` | -706 | **-551** | `H6-3` |
| `C1-3`, `V3-2`, `V3-4` | -507 | **-259** | `V3-2` |

**`C1-2`, `H3-1`, `H5-4`, `H1-4`, `V1-5`, `V2-6` → `H3-1`.** Six implementations of one refactor. I read all five `impl Executor` test doubles: guards.rs:909-998 (90 lines), guards.rs:1111-1210 (100), reconcile.rs:1379-1487 (109), setup.rs:670-778 (109), run/mod.rs:1328-1429 (102) = 510 lines of near-pure trait forwarding. H3-1, H5-4, H1-4 and V1-5 all cite these same ranges; V2-6 replaces setup.rs:666-778 via install.rs:676-905 (a 230-line pass-through executor) instead. ### Conflicts and ordering hazards

**[mutually-exclusive]** `C1-3`, `V3-4`

Only one can land. `stop_plan` has zero production callers (grep-verified: session/mod.rs:587 definition, referenced only by its own test at :1011-1066), so V3-4's "dead code" premise is factual — but C1-3's verifier traced it to 6d98864 and docs/reviews/2026-08-30-simplify.md:17 S-A9-ensure-idle, an ACCEPTED item that only half-landed, so deleting it reverts an accepted fix. The honest total assumes C1-3's branch: V3-2 owns the reducer (-200), C1-3 adds 0 lines (its own verifier: net "plausibly zero or positive"), V3-4 keeps only its non-StopPlan half (~-59, since StopPlan def session/mod.rs:565-602 = 38 lines + test :1010-1069 = 60 lines = 98 of its -157). If the owner instead picks V3-4's delete-and-record branch, drop C1-3 and add 98 lines back.

**[mutually-exclusive]** `C1-2`, `H3-1`, `H5-4`, `H1-4`, `V1-5`, `V2-6`

Six competing rewrites of the same five `impl Executor` doubles — physically verified at guards.rs:909-998 (90), guards.rs:1111-1210 (100), reconcile.rs:1379-1487 (109), setup.rs:670-778 (109), run/mod.rs:1328-1429 (102) = 510 lines. Only one implementation can exist. Land H3-1's cfg(test) support module and drop the other five as line items; fold H1-4's precaution into it (four of the doubles currently inherit the Executor trait's *default* create_new/hard_link/remove_dir_all_rollback — the shared decorator must override exactly 13 methods and deliberately not those three).

**[mutually-exclusive]** `H5-2`, `V7-2`

H5-2 replaces the raw signal subsystem, which destroys the `watch_flag_with_second_action` seam that all three second-signal tests at main.rs:2543-2638 depend on — they must become child-process tests. V7-2 proposes table-driving those same three harnesses (main.rs:2508-2541, :2552-2576, :2591-2637). No line correction is needed because V7-2's verifier already priced that sub-family at "-40 at most, and I would skip it" and the measured -340 is renderer (-180..-230) plus parser (-120). Land H5-2 and drop V7-2's signal family explicitly rather than letting someone do the work twice.

**[ordering]** `H4-1`, `H4-3`, `H4-2`, `H4-5`

H4-1 removes 442 lines from the stop.rs/reconcile.rs/build.rs/stages/mod.rs module headers (physically verified at 144/122/118/86 = 470 `//!` lines), shifting every H4-3 anchor in three of those files by hundreds of lines. H4-3's own precaution says land after H4-1/H4-2/H4-5 or re-derive every anchor by content. Re-derive by symbol/content, never by the line numbers recorded in the proposals.

**[ordering]** `H3-1`, `H3-3`, `H5-1`, `V3-2`, `V3-3`, `V4-3`, `V5-5`, `V5-6`, `V1-6`

Shared-support refactors must land and be re-measured before the fixture/table proposals are sized. Declared dependencies: H3-3 depends on H3-1; V3-3 depends on V3-2. Required layering: H3-1 (shared executor + scratch + sinks) -> H5-1 (tempfile RAII over what remains) -> H3-3/V3-3/V4-3/V5-5/V5-6/V1-6 measured from the new tree, not from their isolated estimates. Carrying the isolated estimates forward is exactly what produced the 2,170- and 220-line overlaps.

**[ordering]** `H6-3`, `H6-4`, `H6-7`, `H2-3`, `V8-5`, `H1-3`

Prune the wire surface before generating it. H1-3's current-tree inventory of ~430 hand-mirrored TS declarations includes ipc.ts:622-722 (the reconcile graph H6-3 and V8-5 both delete), the DoctorEvent/summary declarations H6-4 flattens, and the fields H6-7 prunes. Generating first creates exports that are immediately deleted and can resurrect removed fields during conflict resolution. Land H6-3/H6-4/H6-7/H2-3, run a full consumer search, then regenerate H1-3's input inventory.

**[overlapping-edit]** `H3-4`, `V4-4`, `V7-3`, `V2-7`

Byte-identical `#[test]` ranges, verified by reading them: the eight defs() ordering tests (network.rs:292-296=5, run_only.rs:567-577=11, toolchain.rs:167-181=15, audio.rs:113-117=5, headset.rs:188-192=5, bottle.rs:358-371=14, system.rs:307-314=8, config.rs:753-764=12) total exactly 75 lines — precisely V4-4's entire measured -75, and all eight are inside H3-4. V7-3's two "tautological deletions" are commands.rs:2799-2810 (12) and :2844-2857 (14), also inside H3-4. V2-7's stop.rs:1537-1544 (8, `dry_run_executor_is_dry_run`) is inside H3-4. Delete each function once, under H3-4; V7-3 and V2-7 keep only their table work.

**[overlapping-edit]** `H4-2`, `H4-3`, `V5-3`, `V2-2`, `V6-2`, `V6-3`, `V6-5`, `V4-2`, `H4-6`, `V6-6`, `H4-5`, `H2-4`

Same comment blocks claimed by two proposals each. Applied in sequence the second either re-rewrites an already compressed block or, worse, restores the deleted prose during conflict resolution. Build a content-addressed block ledger before editing: every current comment line must belong to exactly one proposal, and the final diff must not rewrite any block twice. Verified identical anchors include stop.rs:397-417/488-533/607-632 (H4-3 = V2-2), install.rs:422-444/536-557 (H4-3 = V2-2), fixes/mod.rs:366-395 (H4-3 = V2-2), paths.rs:135-159/189-208 (H4-3 in V6-2), events.rs:260-283 (H4-3 in V6-3), privilege.rs:382+28 (H4-3 in V6-5's 370-409), meta.rs:25-62 and contract.rs:269-289 (H4-3 = V4-2), runtime_toml.rs:1-64 (H4-2 in V5-3) and :733/:1202 (H4-3 in V5-3), parity lib.rs:253-278/1132-1152/1842-1880 (H4-6 in V6-6), core lib.rs:1-64 + store/mod.rs:1-28 (H4-5 = H2-4).

**[overlapping-edit]** `V5-3`, `V5-6`

V5-3's review-history paragraphs at runtime_toml.rs:2754-2859 (27 comment lines) and :2938-3119 (25) sit inside V5-6's disk-write harness extraction range :2729-3219. Different line kinds — comments vs setup code — so no line double-count was added to the total, but it is a direct textual collision that V5-6's own verifier flagged. Land V5-6 first, then re-derive V5-3's anchors in that region.

**[risk-stacking]** `H4-2`, `H4-3`, `H4-4`, `H3-4`, `V3-4`, `H6-5`, `V4-4`

Accepted comment replacements name tests that other accepted proposals delete: H4-2's check headers cite the defs_binds*/defs_cover* tests H3-4 and V4-4 delete; H4-4's session-store replacement cites `stop_plan_decides_from_the_status_alone`, which V3-4 deletes; H4-3's error.rs replacement cites `payload_carries_kind_message_remedy_and_the_reported_flag`, which H6-5 deletes. Zero lines, but landing as written converts the compressed comments into misinformation — the exact failure the comment program is supposed to avoid. After all test/API deletions, resolve every test symbol named by a replacement comment; no pointer may return zero matches.

**[risk-stacking]** `H3-1`, `V3-2`, `V3-3`, `H3-3`, `V1-6`, `H3-4`, `H4-2`, `H4-3`, `V5-3`

Never land these in one commit. A newly shared Executor/fixture seam, plus moved watcher policy, plus deleted redundant canaries, plus deleted rationale, is the state where a normalized fault seam silently stops exercising the timing a CONFIRMED finding pinned — and the forensic trail that would reveal it is gone in the same diff. fix-blame assigns 34-47% review-fix ownership to runtime_toml, reconcile, watcher, run/* and the CLI. One dependency layer per commit, `cargo test -- --list` diffed byte-identical after H3-1, and a green run between each.

## Recommendations in full

### Accepted — safe and behaviour-preserving

*3 items, -601 lines as measured in isolation (before de-double-counting — see above). Verified, re-measured, and clear of every invariant and review finding.*

| id | tier | lines | title | the one thing to know |
|---|---|---:|---|---|
| `H4-4` | T1 | -396 | Remove duplicated CLI, Tauri, and frontend lifecycle narration | Cleanest of the five: measured 444 lines exactly as claimed, only 122 are fix-authored, every cited test resolves, and the cut also removes real agent-scaffolding residue ('only the Frame agent may edit a Cargo.toml', 'this task's hard rules') from shipped source. |
| `H3-4` | T1 | -130 | Delete twelve tests that only reassert aggregate coverage or Rust derives | Correct and safe: build_registry keys evaluators into a BTreeMap and orders the registry from the contract, so the eight per-module defs() order assertions have no behavioural consequence, and cardinality, binding and order are already pinned in checks/mod.rs and sabrage-parity. |
| `V4-4` | T1 | -75 | Delete module-local `defs()` ordering tests | Safe and correctly measured: all eight only assert the order of a private Vec that build_registry re-sorts by the contract anyway, the product invariant is asserted at checks/mod.rs:616, and none of the 75 lines came from a review-fix commit -- optionally keep run_only.rs's variant, the only one that cross-checks against the live contract's run-only group. |

#### `H4-4` — Remove duplicated CLI, Tauri, and frontend lifecycle narration

T1 · comment-cut · **-396 lines** · from H4 — the comment mass

Exact logical replacements:

Completed cancellation exits 130; see PARITY.md SIGTERM row and tests::{watcher_keeps_polling_past_the_first_fire_so_a_second_signal_is_reachable, two_signals_inside_one_poll_interval_are_not_collapsed_into_one, a_second_signal_of_a_different_kind_is_still_fatal_and_reraises_that_kind}.`

*Files.* `sabrage/crates/sabrage-cli/src/main.rs`, `sabrage/src-tauri/src/commands.rs`, `sabrage/src-tauri/src/lib.rs`, `sabrage/ui/src/stores/session.svelte.ts`, `sabrage/ui/src/ipc.ts`

*Evidence.* The 14 blocks are at `sabrage-cli/src/main.rs:1`, `sabrage-cli/src/main.rs:980`, `sabrage-cli/src/main.rs:1167`, `src-tauri/src/commands.rs:1`, `src-tauri/src/commands.rs:283`, `src-tauri/src/commands.rs:526`, `src-tauri/src/commands.rs:770`, `src-tauri/src/commands.rs:1179`, `src-tauri/src/commands.rs:1597`, `src-tauri/src/commands.rs:1651`, `src-tauri/src/commands.rs:1766`, `src-tauri/src/lib.rs:147`, `ui/src/ipc.ts:372`, and `ui/src/stores/session.svelte.ts:123`. Assertions exist at `sabrage-cli/src/main.rs:1916`, `sabrage-cli/src/main.rs:2500`, `sabrage-cli/src/main.rs:2545`, `sabrage-cli/src/main.rs:2580`, `src-tauri/src/commands.rs:2478`, `src-tauri/src/commands.rs:2507`, `src-tauri/src/commands.rs:2554`, `src-tauri/src/commands.rs:2673`, `src-tauri/src/commands.rs:2882`, and `src-tauri/src/commands.rs:2902`.

*What breaks.* No runtime or wire behavior. The frontend and bridge layers lose duplicated explanations of core behavior; readers must follow the named core test or command. Comments that referred to past agent ownership and superseded implementations disappear.

*Blast radius.* Three Rust files and two frontend files, comments only. IPC types, serde tags, command signatures, lifecycle code, and tests remain unchanged.

*How you would know it worked.* Diff only comment tokens; TypeScript/Rust type checks and existing tests remain green; recount 444 lines to about 48. Specifically ensure no exported TSDoc tooling depends on these descriptions.

*Verifier.* I expanded all 14 anchors and summed 444 lines (59+23+79+58+22+21+20+20+20+23+33+20+20+26), matching the claim, against ~48 replacement lines, so -396 is exact. All 17 cited test symbols resolve, including the two I spot-checked by grep: session/mod.rs:1011 stop_plan_decides_from_the_status_alone and sabrage-cli/src/main.rs:1947 text_event_prints_verbatim_including_empty_and_leading_spaces. fix-blame overlap is the lowest of the five at 122/444, concentrated in commands.rs:526-546 (A11-3 queued-run notice), 770-789 (A11-1 stop-and-quit honesty), 1651-1673 (A11-2 settings cache) and 1766-1798 (A11-2 config patch), and the replacements keep each of those rules with its enforcing test named. The two frontend blocks are the ones I scrutinised hardest and both hold up: ipc.ts:372-391's replacement keeps 'reject incomplete teardown', which is the whole point of that fix, and session.svelte.ts:123-148's replacement keeps the actual landmine -- that stopSession would block on run_stage's own OPERATION_LOCK during preflight/launching, so cancelStage(runId) is used instead -- and cites the core test that encodes it. These are TSDoc comments on non-exported/store-local functions with no doc-generation step in the repo, so nothing downstream reads them. The one rationale I expected to be lost, 'why a raw signal(2) trap instead of tokio::signal', turns out to survive: crates/sabrage-cli/Cargo.toml carries its own note that tokio is pinned to rt-multi-thread/macros only, and the deleted comment itself points there. The 130-vs-143 exit-code divergence stays declared at PARITY.md:75. Comment-only in both Rust and TypeScript, so no type-check or serde/wire impact; no golden bytes, no contract/, scripts/, docs/ or ext/. Note the cut is incomplete rather than wrong: commands.rs:839, 1301, 2780 and main.rs:2380 keep the same agent-era residue and are left for a later pass.

#### `H3-4` — Delete twelve tests that only reassert aggregate coverage or Rust derives

T1 · test-cut · **-130 lines** (reviewer claimed -118) · from H3 — the test mass

Delete eight per-module `defs()` slug-vector tests because the assembled registry already asserts exact contract cardinality and order, and parity independently asserts the same effective registry. Also delete four framework/derive smoke tests: LaunchOpts default/field storage, AppState field storage, pairwise inequality of a derived enum, and `DryRunExecutor::is_dry_run`. This removes 118 lines. The assertions actually lost are those eight local slug vectors, `LaunchOpts::default().game_id == None`, two directly reread AppState fields, pairwise distinct RepoRootSource variants, and one `is_dry_run()` getter assertion; no pipeline behaviour or effective registry assertion is lost.

*Files.* `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/checks/network.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/checks/run_only.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/checks/toolchain.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/checks/audio.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/checks/headset.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/checks/bottle.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/checks/system.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/checks/config.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/stop.rs`, `sabrage/src-tauri/src/commands.rs`

*Evidence.* The local defs tests are network.rs:292-296, run_only.rs:567-577, toolchain.rs:167-181, audio.rs:113-117, headset.rs:188-192, bottle.rs:358-371, system.rs:307-314, and config.rs:753-764. The effective registry already asserts length and exact contract order at checks/mod.rs:615-621, and parity repeats strict order, binding, and evaluator presence at sabrage-parity/src/lib.rs:1343-1358. The derive/framework tests are commands.rs:2789-2810, commands.rs:2844-2857, and stop.rs:1537-1544; the tested types derive Default or PartialEq at commands.rs:337 and commands.rs:1884-1887.

*What breaks.* Failures in module-local slug assembly would be reported by the aggregate registry test rather than the originating module test. Rust derive and plain struct-field behaviour would no longer have dedicated smoke tests; that is the intentionally lost coverage.

*Blast radius.* Twelve unit tests disappear. Contract order and slug cardinality remain covered in core and parity; no golden bytes, evaluator outputs, review-finding regression, or production code changes.

*How you would know it worked.* Run the aggregate registry and strict parity registry tests plus the affected crates. Confirm the registry test still fails if a local defs list is omitted, duplicated, or reordered. Confirm no finding index points at the four derive/framework smoke tests before deletion.

*Verifier.* All twelve targets exist at the cited lines and I counted about 130 lines including blank separators — the claim is slightly conservative, not inflated. The load-bearing check is whether the aggregate subsumes the locals: `build_registry` (checks/mod.rs:490-544) folds `all_defs()` into a `BTreeMap<&str, Evaluator>`, errors on duplicates, errors on slugs the contract does not declare, and then builds `checks` by iterating `contract().checks` — so the registry's order comes from the contract, never from a module's defs() vector. `registry_binds_in_contract_order_and_covers_every_slug` (mod.rs:615-621) asserts length and exact slug order, `unknown_and_duplicate_registrations_are_errors_even_leniently` asserts both strict and lenient builds succeed with no unbound slug, and sabrage-parity's `strict_registry_builds_and_covers_the_contract_in_order` (lib.rs:1343-1358) independently asserts order plus `eval.is_some()` for every slug. The only assertion genuinely lost is 'this particular module binds exactly these slugs' — a module-to-group mapping that checks/mod.rs's own header documents as a convention the registry 'does not care' about, since binding is by slug. run_only.rs's variant additionally pins the run-only group membership; that too is convention, and its cardinality survives in the aggregate. On the four framework tests: commands.rs:2789-2810 and 2844-2857 are NOT in the fix-blame ranges for that file (which jump 2453-2671 then 2879-2915), stop.rs:1537-1544 is not in stop.rs's ranges either, and grepping findings-index.tsv and both review documents for these test names returns nothing — no CONFIRMED finding is pinned to them. The proposal correctly leaves `classify_repo_root_source_follows_resolve_repo_roots_own_precedence` (which does carry a finding rationale) in place. Nothing else is touched: no golden bytes, no contract slug cardinality, no PARITY.md row, no fixture, no production code. Worth noting only that the AppState test uses a field-complete struct literal, so it doubles as a compile-time canary when a field is added; that canary is the one incidental thing that goes with it.

#### `V4-4` — Delete module-local `defs()` ordering tests

T1 · test-cut · **-75 lines** (reviewer claimed -74) · from V4 — checks + contract + generator

Remove eight tests that pin the incidental order of each module's temporary `Vec`. Product coverage remains in the strict registry/parity tests, which assert complete binding and contract order. The only assertion intentionally lost is that each private module vector happens to be locally ordered; `all_defs` explicitly declares that order irrelevant because `build_registry` reorders by the contract.

*Files.* `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/checks/config.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/checks/bottle.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/checks/run_only.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/checks/system.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/checks/network.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/checks/headset.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/checks/toolchain.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/checks/audio.rs`

*Evidence.* The redundant tests are at `config.rs:753`, `bottle.rs:358`, `run_only.rs:567`, `system.rs:307`, `network.rs:292`, `headset.rs:188`, `toolchain.rs:167`, and `audio.rs:113`, totaling 74 lines. Their ordering assertion is non-behavioral by declaration: `checks/mod.rs:451` says definition order is irrelevant, and `checks/mod.rs:507` joins and reorders through `contract().checks`. Complete contract coverage/order is already asserted at `checks/mod.rs:615`.

*What breaks.* A future edit could reorder a module's `defs()` vector without a local test failure. Runtime and parity output do not change because the registry always restores contract order. Unknown, duplicate, or missing slugs remain rejected.

*Blast radius.* Eight unit-test blocks only. Contract-order, strict binding, slug cardinality, and run-only parity remain covered by the registry and parity suites.

*How you would know it worked.* Run `registry_binds_in_contract_order_and_covers_every_slug` and the parity strict-registry test; deliberately reordering one local `defs()` vector should leave output unchanged, while deleting or duplicating a slug must still fail.

*Verifier.* I opened all eight test bodies (config.rs:753-764, bottle.rs:358-371, run_only.rs:567-577, system.rs:307-314, network.rs:292-296, headset.rs:188-192, toolchain.rs:167-181, audio.rs:113-117): 75 lines of body plus 8 blank separators. Seven of them assert a hard-coded slug list against defs(); one (run_only's) compares defs() against the contract's NO_DOCTOR_ROW_GROUP members. I verified the claimed redundancy by reading the code rather than the proposal: all_defs at checks/mod.rs:451-453 declares 'Order here is irrelevant -- build_registry re-orders by the contract', build_registry at :507-524 joins contract().checks with a BTreeMap keyed by slug and emits BoundChecks in contract order, and it rejects duplicates (:501), unknown slugs (:514) and, strictly, missing bindings (:533). The product assertions live in checks/mod.rs tests: registry_binds_in_contract_order_and_covers_every_slug (:616) asserts reg.len() == contract().checks.len() and the slug sequence equals contract().check_slugs(); unknown_and_duplicate_registrations_are_errors_even_leniently (:624); doctor_walks_only_doctor_visible_checks (:637) asserts no run-only slug leaks into doctor output; native_preflight_is_the_gating_subset (:672). So the only thing lost is 'module M's private vector happens to be locally ordered' plus, for run_only, 'module run_only owns exactly the run-only group' -- and module ownership is explicitly a convention, not an invariant (checks/mod.rs:18-20: 'The registry does not care (binding is by slug)'). Review history is clean: fix-blame gives bottle/system/headset/toolchain/audio zero review-fix lines, config.rs's ranges stop at 694-696, run_only.rs's at 557, network.rs's at 291 -- every target sits outside them; git log -S confirms run_only's test came from f4d4315 (Phase 3), not a review-fix commit. No contract, tap, golden byte or shell file is involved.

#### `C1-2` — Replace per-module unit-test programs with one labelled scenario corpus

T2 · table-drive · **-1,300 lines** (reviewer claimed -7,000) · from C1 — completeness critic

The accepted test proposals consolidate helpers locally, but leave the fundamental shape intact: 1,182 separate test functions, usually constructing a filesystem, executor, event sink, and expected rows afresh. Build one scenario runner around TestRoot, observed machine inputs, fault injection, emitted events, planned mutations, and final files; express ordinary cases as labelled data, reserving bespoke tests for concurrency and process timing. Every confirmed-finding ID and golden assertion remains a named case. This subsumes H3-1, H5-4, H1-4, V1-5, V2-6, H3-2, H3-3, V4-3, V3-3, V1-6, V5-5, V5-6, V7-2, V2-7, and the test portion of H5-1—the reviewers each compressed one fixture family instead of changing the test architecture.

*Files.* `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/test_support.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/config/runtime_toml.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/session`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/checks`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/fixes`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-cli/src/main.rs`

*Evidence.* metrics/per-file.tsv:96 reports 28,075 test lines. metrics/tests.tsv:2-20 shows the largest 19 files alone carry 20,208 test lines; runtime_toml has 81 tests/1,881 lines, reconcile 76/1,851, run 57/1,624, and watcher 33/1,478. dupes-fine.md:6 reports 63 structural duplicate pairs, and :10-19 shows its ten largest pairs are test-only executor forwarding. The repeated decorators themselves occupy guards.rs:909-1210, reconcile.rs:1379-1487, setup.rs:670-778, and run/mod.rs:1328-1429.

*What breaks.* Individual cargo-test function names and one-test-per-case reporting change; an error in the shared harness can invalidate many cases simultaneously; table execution may stop at the first failure unless cases are isolated. Concurrency, PID identity, rotation, and real subprocess tests cannot be forced into the generic runner merely to save lines.

*Blast radius.* Most Rust test modules. Review-finding regressions, parity tests, the frozen runtime-TOML fixture, and golden bytes are directly involved and must remain represented. This overlaps accepted test cuts and the tests deleted by C1-4/C1-5.

*How you would know it worked.* Create a machine-readable inventory mapping every existing test or intentionally folded assertion to a scenario ID, with explicit finding IDs and golden names. Require no unmapped confirmed finding, compare serialized events/files/argv byte-for-byte, keep bespoke timing tests separate, and mutation-test the shared harness so injected faults demonstrably fail their cases.

*What breaks.* No application behavior. Rustdoc stops being a self-contained design document, and historical explanations move back to git/review history. The retained replacement lines preserve the constraints a future editor needs before touching code.
