*Verifier.* I counted all 25 module-doc blocks with the same awk scan: 81+71+71+67+64+64+58+51+47+47+46+41+39+38+36+34+34+30+29+24+24+22+21+21+20 = 1,080, matching the proposal's own remeasure target, and the 25 replacements are ~115 wrapped lines, so -965 is exact. All 53 cited test symbols resolve to real fns. The reviewer's headline claim checks out and is a genuine find: checks/config.rs:36-47 states 'this crate does not enable the preserve_order feature', while crates/sabrage-core/Cargo.toml:8-10 enables it with its own comment and PARITY.md:18 relies on it -- the comment is factually wrong today and the replacement corrects it. I verified the golden-adjacent cases individually. util/winpath.rs is the strongest case for the proposal: win_path is a listed golden invariant, and the replacement keeps both load-bearing semantics ('drive_c matches only with its trailing slash', 'every other path becomes the full Z: path') and names sabrage-parity's win_path_table, which exists at crates/sabrage-parity/src/lib.rs:1206 -- textbook Rule C compliance. stages/setup.rs's O_EXCL/create_new-vs-exists()-probe race landmine is preserved by 'create-new/write-once' plus the named a_config_created_by_another_writer_is_reported_not_replaced; its dropped 'Byte contracts:' sentence is safe because contract_marker_bytes' own doc at util/mod.rs:317-326 states the pin-plus-one-newline rule in full and toml_template_matches_the_on_disk_contract_file is a parity golden. checks/network.rs's 'needs a doctor.sh change or a PARITY.md row' TODO is already satisfied by PARITY.md:117, and the replacement cites it correctly. 215 of the 1,080 lines are review-fix-authored, but none of the fixes themselves is touched. Two residual losses do bite. First, checks/config.rs:18-36 records that the A3b-3 Unreadable/Malformed Warn divergence still owes either a doctor.sh change or a PARITY.md row; I grepped PARITY.md for 'malformed' and 'unreadable' and there is no such row, so the replacement's pointer to 'PARITY.md JSON parsing' (line 18, which is about serde vs python3 parsing generally) does not cover it -- deleting the block silently erases tracked parity debt. Second, checks/mod.rs:8-11 is the only statement of why the check prose is frozen ('must match scripts/demo/doctor.sh verbatim, because docs/troubleshooting.md quotes those lines'), and the parity harness deliberately never compares prose, so no test enforces it; the replacement's 'shell-matching prose' is strictly weaker. Its group-to-module table also prevents a new evaluator landing in the wrong module for the three folded/renamed groups, and registry_binds_in_contract_order_and_covers_every_slug would not catch that.

#### `H3-1` — Replace repeated test infrastructure with one cfg(test) support module

T2 · extract · **-750 lines** (reviewer claimed -840) · from H3 — the test mass

Add a roughly 150-line `test_support` module containing: an RAII `TestRoot`, an `EventRecorder` with rows/sections/check helpers, and one delegating `FaultExecutor` whose configured fault replaces the five hand-written Executor wrappers. Delete roughly 990 lines of wrapper forwarding, scratch-name generation, explicit cleanup, sink construction, and extraction helpers, for 840 net lines removed at equal assertion coverage. Net per-file targets after charging the shared module back are guards -125, reconcile -100, run/mod -85, setup -75, runtime_toml -40, watcher -25, actions -35, preflight -30, and -325 spread across the remaining core unit-test modules.

*Files.* `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/lib.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/test_support.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/run/guards.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/session/reconcile.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/run/mod.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/setup.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/config/runtime_toml.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/session/watcher.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/run/actions.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/run/preflight.rs`

*Evidence.* The supplied duplicate report identifies its ten largest structural duplicates as test-only Executor forwarding blocks (`dupes-fine.md:10-19`). The actual decorators occupy guards.rs:909-998 and guards.rs:1111-1210, reconcile.rs:1379-1487, setup.rs:670-778, and run/mod.rs:1328-1429—about 510 lines before counting their construction sites. Scratch-root helpers and cleanup recur in runtime_toml.rs:1734-1744, reconcile.rs:1232-1242, run/mod.rs:1079-1088, watcher.rs:905-912, actions.rs:879-888, and guards.rs:762-771. Event sinks and local extractors recur at actions.rs:893-927, run/mod.rs:1094-1131, reconcile.rs:1250-1264, and guards.rs:776-802.

*What breaks.* No assertion or named regression test should disappear. The risk is test-infrastructure coupling: `with_step` must preserve shared recorder/fault state, path-specific failures must still trigger on the same operation, and RAII cleanup must not remove a root before spawned test work completes.

*Blast radius.* Broad but test-only across sabrage-core. No production path, contract, parity golden, fixture bytes, shell fingerprint, or Tauri/UI contract changes. The parity crate's separate SyncFsExecutor remains untouched.

*Precaution.* Make the shared decorator override exactly the 13 required trait methods and deliberately NOT `create_new`/`hard_link`/`remove_dir_all_rollback`, matching what today's doubles inherit (executor.rs documents those defaults as the decorators' contract); keep watcher.rs's pid-stable scratch semantics; and diff `cargo test -- --list` before/after to prove the test-name set is byte-identical.

*How you would know it worked.* Compare `cargo test -- --list` before and after; the test-name set must be identical. Run the full Rust suite and specifically the guards, reconcile, setup, run, runtime_toml, watcher, and actions modules. Confirm every configured fault is exercised once and temporary roots are absent after both passing and panicking test cases.

*Verifier.* I opened every cited region and they exist as described. The five test-only Executor doubles are real and almost entirely forwarding: guards.rs CancelChildren (910-996), guards.rs FailSwitchTo (1111-1210), run/mod.rs DenyWriteTo (1328-1429), reconcile.rs FailSwitchTo (1379-1487), setup.rs LosesTheCreateRace (670-778) — about 508 lines, of which each overrides only one method (run_child, write_atomic, or create_new). `grep -rn 'impl Executor for'` confirms there are exactly seven impls in the tree and five of them are these doubles. The duplicate scaffolding is larger than the proposal's evidence lists: 31 `fn scratch(` definitions (30 in sabrage-core, 1 in sabrage-parity which cannot share a cfg(test) module), 8 `dry_ctx`/`test_ctx`/`fixture_paths` constructors, 8 `rows`/`texts`/`lines` extractors, and 144 explicit `remove_dir_all(&root)` cleanup lines. Charging back a ~150-line shared module plus per-site fault configuration, I measure roughly -750, close enough to their -840 that I am not calling it inflated. Review-history check: guards.rs 909-1055 and setup.rs 666-823 are entirely review-fix lines (fix-blame.tsv), i.e. the doubles were written by the A8-3 and A5-3 fixes, and run/mod.rs DenyWriteTo and reconcile.rs FailSwitchTo predate the fixes. Nothing here is reverted as long as every test and assertion stays — the doubles are scaffolding for the fix regressions, not the fix. No finding in findings-index.tsv anchors at these decorator ranges. Invariants: this is entirely `#[cfg(test)]` inside sabrage-core; no golden bytes, no contract slug count, no PARITY.md row, no frozen phase4 fixture, nothing under contract/, scripts/, demo.sh, docs/, or ext/. The one substantive hazard I found that the proposal does not mention: `Executor::create_new`, `hard_link` and `remove_dir_all_rollback` have default bodies, and executor.rs's own doc comments say those defaults exist specifically 'so a decorating Executor (the test doubles) inherits sane behaviour'. Today's doubles rely on that — DenyWriteTo, for example, gets the racy check-then-write `create_new` and the copy-based `hard_link`, not the real ones. A shared harness that mechanically forwards all 16 methods to `inner` would change what several of these tests exercise without any compile error. Second hazard: watcher.rs's scratch is deliberately pid-only (no uuid) and `remove_dir_all`s first, which matters because those tests serialize on the process-global LIVE_SESSION; a uniquifying shared TestRoot changes that. Third: RAII cleanup deletes the scratch tree on panic, where today a failing test leaves it for inspection, and `remove_dir_all(&root).unwrap()` currently doubles as an assertion the tree still exists.

Corresponding tests are directly present at `session/watcher.rs:1526`, `stages/stop.rs:907`, `stages/stop.rs:1425`, `store/goldberg.rs:397`, `checks/meta.rs:306`, `process.rs:1287`, `session/mod.rs:827`, `stages/run/preflight.rs:1842`, `stages/mod.rs:1209`, `fixes/mod.rs:843`, `privilege.rs:1246`, `session/reconcile.rs:2959`, `paths.rs:657`, `runtime_toml.rs:2730`, `runtime_toml.rs:3394`, `stages/install.rs:1948`, `error.rs:224`, `stages/run/actions.rs:1317`, `stages/run/mod.rs:2407`, `contract.rs:385`, `util/mod.rs:419`, and `executor.rs:1911`.

*What breaks.* No behavior or assertion coverage. Public/function rustdoc becomes a compact contract index instead of a proof narrative. Review-finding history is no longer visible inline, but remains in git and `sabrage/docs/reviews/`.

*Blast radius.* Twenty-two Rust files, comments only. Many regions were added by review-fix commits, but the tests and fixed branches themselves remain untouched.

*Precaution.* Correct refuses_when_ -> refuses_while_a_matching_game_process_is_running, name all three goldberg refusal tests, and keep goldberg's 'deliberately not scoped to bs_dir (wine puts a Z:\ path on argv)' clause verbatim; land this after H4-1/H4-2/H4-5 or re-derive every anchor by content, since those cuts shift these line numbers by hundreds.

*How you would know it worked.* Require a comment-only diff, resolve every named test symbol, run workspace tests and parity, then check these 31 blocks total approximately 129 lines instead of 827.

*Verifier.* I walked every one of the 31 anchors, expanded each to its contiguous comment block and summed: 827 lines, matching the proposal, against ~129 lines of replacement, so -698 is exact. 65 of the 66 cited test symbols resolve; one does not -- store/goldberg.rs's test is refuses_while_a_matching_game_process_is_running (line 555), not refuses_when_..., so the replacement would ship a dead pointer. This is the riskiest of the five because, unlike H4-1/H4-2/H4-5, these are item-level docs rather than module headers, i.e. the last place the rationale lives. Cross-referencing fix-blame.tsv, 472/827 lines came from the five review-fix commits, and the blocks map onto CONFIRMED findings: store/goldberg.rs:65-106 (42/42 fix lines) is A13a-2, session/mod.rs:307-338 (32/32) is A4-1's seven-signal door, stages/run/preflight.rs:125-156 (32/32) is the Config-readers divergence, process.rs:791-823 is round-2 A2-1/A2-3, stages/stop.rs:397-417 and 488-533 are round-2 A5-5 and r1 A5-5, contract.rs:269-289 and checks/meta.rs:25-62 are A1-1. Deleting a comment cannot revert a fix -- the code and every assertion stay untouched, and no runnable doctest is involved (all fences are ```zsh/```text) -- so this is not a kill. And I confirmed the replacements are unusually careful: session/mod.rs keeps the 'do not use generic wineserver liveness' landmine; paths.rs:189 and util/mod.rs:202 keep the host-manifest byte contract (comparison form, no trailing newline, file writes must use host_manifest_file_bytes, JSON escaping) and point at PARITY.md:118 and the privileged-write golden; executor.rs:357 keeps 'wine/dashboard must use spawn_detached, never kill_on_drop(true)', which PARITY.md:87 also carries. Two specific losses are not covered anywhere else. store/goldberg.rs's clause 'It is deliberately not scoped to bs_dir: wine puts a Z:\ Windows path on the command line, which no unix prefix test can match' is the only warning stopping a future editor from narrowing that argv scan and silently reopening A13a-2's pre-telemetry window, and the replacement names only 1 of the 3 is-Goldberg refusal tests (refuses_when_the_backup_is_an_unpinned_goldberg_build and refuses_when_a_launch_recorded_the_backup_as_goldberg also exist and encode the pin+payload+provenance triple the prose explains). Separately, util/mod.rs:216-217's severity signal ('the single most drift-sensitive artifact in the pipeline: one extra byte and the two front-ends thrash each other with sudo prompts') is the deterrent, not the constraint; a weaker version survives at PARITY.md:118.

#### `H5-1` — Use `tempfile` RAII for test scratch directories

T1 · replace-with-dep · **-470 lines** (reviewer claimed -390) · from H5 — dependency substitution

Replace per-module `scratch(tag) -> PathBuf` helpers and end-of-test `remove_dir_all` calls with one shared `ScratchDir` backed by `tempfile::TempDir`. Keep removals that are test stimuli—rotation, disappearance, rollback—and remove only lifecycle scaffolding, with zero assertions lost. Dependency cost is dev-only; `tempfile` is already locked, although macOS test builds may newly compile its transitive stack. Replacement comment: `// ScratchDir owns a TempDir; tests that simulate disappearance remove the child under test, never this owner.`

*Files.* `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/Cargo.toml`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/test_support.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/session/reconcile.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/session/watcher.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/config/runtime_toml.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/executor.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/logs.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/store/goldberg.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/store/settings.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-parity/Cargo.toml`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-parity/src/lib.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-contract-gen/Cargo.toml`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-contract-gen/src/lib.rs`

*Evidence.* Manual ownership is repeated at sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/session/reconcile.rs:1233-1242 with explicit cleanup at :1597-1605; sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/config/runtime_toml.rs:1735-1751 with cleanup at :2196-2225; and sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/logs.rs:677-680 with cleanup at :1215-1262. `tempfile` and its dependencies are already recorded at sabrage/Cargo.lock:3780-3790, currently reached through `uds_windows` at :4173-4181.

*What breaks.* Blind conversion would invalidate tests that deliberately delete a directory mid-test, and dropping the `TempDir` owner before cloned `PathBuf`s or background tasks finish would delete fixtures early. Those cases must retain an owner for the test lifetime and preserve semantic removals.

*Blast radius.* Test scaffolding across roughly thirty Rust test modules plus dev-dependency manifests. Parity, byte-golden assertions, finding-pinned tests, and the frozen runtime-TOML fixture remain byte-for-byte and assertion-for-assertion unchanged; only fixture ownership and terminal cleanup change.

*Precaution.* (1) Keep every removal that is a stimulus rather than teardown: logs.rs rotation/truncate cases, watcher.rs old-incarnation backlog cases, store/library.rs:1162/1187/1343 (bs_dir + bottle.prefix pairs), stages/install.rs's dxmt.stock-backup sweeps, and executor.rs's remove_dir_all/remove_dir_all_rollback trait methods and their decorator forwardings (~30 production lines that are NOT cleanup). (2) Rust drops in reverse declaration order, so bind the ScratchDir first in every test that also holds a ForeignProcess child or a spawned Tailer task; a cloned PathBuf handed to a background task must not outlive the owner. (3) TempDir deletes during unwind, so a failing test no longer leaves its fixture on disk for post-mortem — if that matters, defuse via TempDir::keep() on the failure path. (4) sabrage-parity and sabrage-contract-gen cannot see a #[cfg(test)] module in sabrage-core; either add tempfile to each of the three manifests and use TempDir directly there, or make the helper a feature-gated pub module. (5) Do not touch sabrage-parity's leading remove_dir_all in scratch() blindly — its path has no uuid, only pid+tag, so it is a same-pid leftover guard that TempDir subsumes but a half-conversion would not.

*How you would know it worked.* Run the existing workspace test suite; confirm test names and assertion counts are unchanged. Review every remaining `remove_dir_all` in test modules and require it to model disappearance/rollback rather than cleanup. Confirm generated parity bytes and the frozen fixture remain unchanged.

*Verifier.* The region exists exactly as described. I confirmed 33 scratch-style helpers (grep 'fn scratch' across sabrage/crates) at reconcile.rs:1233, runtime_toml.rs:1735, logs.rs:677, watcher.rs:906, parity/lib.rs:1449 and 28 more, each ~9-10 lines of temp_dir().join(format!(...)) + remove_dir_all + create_dir_all. I confirmed 450 total remove_dir_all occurrences; the shape histogram (uniq -c over the matched lines) shows ~325 are the pure end-of-test one-liners (&root/.ok 80, &dir/.ok 73, &root/.unwrap 50, &dir/.unwrap 41, &tmp/.ok 38, &fixture/.ok 14, ...), ~33 live inside the helpers themselves, and only ~30 are production trait/impl lines (Executor::remove_dir_all, remove_dir_all_rollback, decorator forwardings). So the removable mass is ~640 lines and the replacement (one ScratchDir ~30 lines, x3 crates or one feature-gated module, plus ~33 use lines and a mod declaration in lib.rs) is ~110-130 — net around -470 to -530. Their -390 is conservative, not inflated. Review-history gate: nothing in either Codex round concerns test temp-dir lifecycle. I grepped both round-1 and round-2 review documents for remove_dir_all/temp_dir/tempfile/scratch — every hit is a *regression test recipe* ('a scratch root that copies the real contract/', 'paths.sabrage_appsup injected at a scratch dir'), i.e. the findings care about HOME/appsup injection, never about who deletes the fixture. findings-index.tsv has no finding whose title or anchor is test cleanup. Spot-checking fix-blame: reconcile.rs's scratch helper (1233-1242) is outside every fix range, and although cleanup lines sit inside fix-authored blocks (e.g. reconcile.rs 1747-2201, watcher.rs 980-1520), the fix in each case is the test body and its assertions, not the trailing remove_dir_all — line 1601 is fix-authored (the reconcile_with call) while 1605 (the cleanup) is not. Invariants: no golden bytes, no contract slug, no shell fingerprint, no PARITY.md row is touched; the frozen phase4 fixture is only read by runtime_toml.rs, never written. Dependency reality check: tempfile 3.27.0 IS at Cargo.lock:3780 but only via a Windows-only path, so macOS test builds will newly compile tempfile plus fastrand/getrandom — dev-only cost, acceptable. Rule T is satisfied: zero assertions are lost, only lifecycle scaffolding moves. The only genuine hazards are drop ordering, stimulus-vs-teardown misclassification, and the two out-of-crate consumers; hence ACCEPT_WITH_CARE rather than ACCEPT. Note that H5-4 also wants to create sabrage-core/src/test_support.rs; the two compose but should land in a coordinated order so lib.rs's #[cfg(test)] mod test_support; is added once.

#### `H4-1` — Replace the four 470-line anchor headers with constraint indexes

T1 · comment-cut · **-442 lines** · from H4 — the comment mass

Exact replacements, wrapped to the existing width:

`stages/stop.rs:1` (144 → 7): `//! Stop pipeline; authoritative order/text: scripts/demo/stop.sh (STOP_WINESERVER, STOP_PORTS, STOP_REAP, STOP_AUDIO).\n//! Native differences: PARITY.md rows Stop, Persisted audio-device restore, and Stop reports probe failures.\n//! Mutations use Executor; probes use bounded capture_with; cancellation propagates.\n//! See tests::{a_pre_cancelled_run_yields_cancelled_and_never_reports_stage_finished_ok,\n//! a_wedged_lsof_warns_instead_of_reporting_free_ports,\n//! reap_never_signals_a_pid_whose_identity_no_longer_matches}.`

`session/reconcile.rs:1` (122 → 8): `//! Reconcile persisted sessions: live → adopt; dead → full restore; recycled pid → pid-free restore.\n//! Unverifiable, newer-schema, and live-foreign records remain untouched.\n//! Every mutation uses Executor; stop turns every error except Cancelled into rows and retains retryable state.\n//! See PARITY.md Persisted audio-device restore and tests::{\n//! a_dead_session_restores_every_guard_then_clears_the_record,\n//! an_identity_mismatch_restores_the_pid_free_guards_and_signals_nothing,\n//! a_live_pid_with_no_observed_start_time_is_never_dismantled,\n//! a_cancelled_reconcile_still_reaches_the_caller}.`

`stages/build.rs:1` (118 → 6): `//! Build pipeline; authoritative order, argv, and text: scripts/demo/build.sh.\n//! GUI children use default_child_path; dry-run never claims outputs; the staged helper must be executable arm64.\n//! See tests::{configure_and_build_specs_render_the_exact_argv,\n//! a_dry_run_stages_nothing_and_says_would_build,\n//! a_byte_identical_but_non_executable_staged_helper_is_repaired}.`

`stages/mod.rs:1` (86 → 7): `//! Stage dispatcher and two-level operation lock: process mutex plus advisory file lock.\n//! Mutating doors recheck live-session and contract-skew refusals after acquiring the lock; run alone releases it after spawn.\n//! demo.sh deliberately does not participate; see PARITY.md Cross-process operation lock.\n//! See tests::{a_queued_stage_announces_itself_and_cancels_out_of_the_wait,\n//! a_queued_stage_is_refused_when_a_session_goes_live_during_the_wait,\n//! every_mutating_stage_refuses_a_checkout_the_binary_was_not_built_from}.`

*Files.* `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/stop.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/session/reconcile.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/build.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/mod.rs`

*Evidence.* The headers occupy `stages/stop.rs:1-144`, `session/reconcile.rs:1-122`, `stages/build.rs:1-118`, and `stages/mod.rs:1-86`. Their assertions are executable at `stages/stop.rs:907`, `stages/stop.rs:1425`, `stages/stop.rs:1599`, `session/reconcile.rs:1629`, `session/reconcile.rs:1713`, `session/reconcile.rs:2008`, `session/reconcile.rs:2837`, `stages/build.rs:936`, `stages/build.rs:1138`, `stages/build.rs:1248`, `stages/mod.rs:1209`, `stages/mod.rs:1473`, and `stages/mod.rs:1584`.

*What breaks.* No runtime behavior or assertion changes. Generated rustdoc loses four self-contained walkthroughs; maintainers must follow the named script, PARITY row, or test. Stale pointers would reduce discoverability.

*Blast radius.* Four Rust files, comments only. Contract data, shell scripts, review-pinned tests, parity behavior, and golden bytes remain untouched.

*Precaution.* Rewrite each PARITY.md citation to quote the row's real leading text (or its line number) and re-verify by grep before committing; apply this before H4-3 or re-anchor H4-3's line numbers afterwards, since removing 442 lines from these four files shifts every H4-3 anchor in stop.rs, reconcile.rs and stages/mod.rs.

*How you would know it worked.* Confirm the diff changes only `//!` lines; recount these four blocks as approximately 28 lines total; then run the existing workspace tests and both parity tiers before merging.

*Verifier.* I counted the four module-doc blocks myself with an awk scan for leading '//!': stages/stop.rs = 144, session/reconcile.rs = 122, stages/build.rs = 118, stages/mod.rs = 86, total 470 exactly as claimed; the four replacements are 6+8+6+7 wrapped lines (~28), so -442 is accurate to the line, not inflated. All 13 test names the replacements cite resolve to real fns in the tree (checked by extracting every tests::{...} symbol and matching against every 'fn <name>' in sabrage/). fix-blame.tsv shows 30/144, 29/122, 26/118 and 35/86 header lines came from review-fix commits, so I traced each one: stop.rs:77-91 is the A2-3/round-2 A5-5 capture_with-timeout rationale, stop.rs:108-122 is the A5-7 foreign-helper report, build.rs:95-118 is A5-3's staged-helper mode repair, mod.rs:23-57 is A4-3's cross-process file lock. None of those are reverted -- comments are not the fix, and the code and tests stay untouched. More importantly I confirmed the specific landmines have a second home: the argv-vs-exe-path trade-off (the 'exec-path matching was tried first and rejected' paragraph) is PARITY.md rows 54-57 verbatim; the tokio::sync::Mutex non-reentrancy/deadlock warning is repeated on run_stage_holding_lock's own doc at stages/mod.rs:903-905 and on fixes/mod.rs:457-463, neither of which this proposal touches; the default_child_path PATH rationale (~/.cargo/bin, Finder-launched .app) is stated in full at process.rs:160-166; the copy_if_changed mode-repair rationale is PARITY.md row 47 plus the named test a_byte_identical_but_non_executable_staged_helper_is_repaired. No golden bytes, no contract/, no scripts/, no docs/, no ext/. No runnable doctests are lost (every code fence in these files is ```zsh/```text/```ignore). No crate denies missing_docs or broken_intra_doc_links, so removing intra-doc links is compile-safe. The one real defect is pointer hygiene: 'PARITY.md rows Stop, Persisted audio-device restore, and Stop reports probe failures' -- 'Stop reports probe failures' is a real bolded row (PARITY.md:116) but 'Persisted audio-device restore' is invented; the actual row is the unnamed 'A Dead or IdentityMismatch recorded session has its leftover guards restored' at PARITY.md:103. A pointer that does not resolve is worse than the prose it replaced, which is exactly the failure mode the proposal itself flags in what_breaks.

#### `V5-3` — Compress runtime-config narration into invariant-and-test pointers

T1 · comment-cut · **-400 lines** (reviewer claimed -380) · from V5 — config editor + app store

Replace `runtime_toml.rs:1-64` with: `Runtime values follow Config.cpp, not TOML: tables are ignored, only double quotes protect #, and the last accepted assignment wins. toml_edit is only the format-preserving editor; refuse when its editable-key occurrences disagree with raw_assignments. Sabrage owns EDITABLE_KEYS only. See PARITY.md Config and tests the_line_reader_agrees_with_parse_config_toml, the_multiline_shadow_is_refused_by_apply_patch_write_and_edit_protocol, and a_real_edit_preserves_crlf_a_bom_and_a_missing_final_newline.` Replace the parser blocks with: `C++ prefix-number and float-precision semantics; pinned by the_line_reader_agrees_with_parse_config_toml and the_scale_bounds_are_checked_at_the_runtimes_float_precision.` Replace occurrence/edit prose with: `Edit the last bare, runtime-visible occurrence; quoted/dotted keys and raw/toml_edit mismatches are covered by the quoted-key, dotted-key, multiline, and BOM tests.` Replace write/backup prose with: `Create from the shared template, refuse live or concurrent changes, back up before atomic replacement, and prune only after commit; see the write safety tests and PARITY.md Config.` Replace test histories with the exact section labels `Parser parity regressions`, `Format-preservation goldens`, `Config transaction regressions (A10-1/A10-2)`, `Live-session guard regressions`, and `Doctor fix regressions`.

*Files.* `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/config/runtime_toml.rs`

*Evidence.* The 64-line module header is at `runtime_toml.rs:1-64`; the same mismatch rule is narrated again at `runtime_toml.rs:342-378`, runtime parsing at `runtime_toml.rs:571-785`, occurrence semantics at `runtime_toml.rs:813-852`, byte preservation at `runtime_toml.rs:971-1128`, and transaction history at `runtime_toml.rs:1202-1604`. Review-history paragraphs are repeated beside executable regression tests at `runtime_toml.rs:2754-2859`, `runtime_toml.rs:2938-3119`, and `runtime_toml.rs:3223-3426`.

*What breaks.* No executable or assertion behavior changes. The risk is reduced archaeological context and broken rustdoc links if replacement test names drift; retaining the named invariant/test pointers is therefore part of the proposal.

*Blast radius.* Comment-only changes in `runtime_toml.rs`. Golden bytes, parity behavior, the frozen deployed fixture, and all test bodies remain unchanged.

*Precaution.* Restrict the write/backup compression so the O_EXCL-not-rename rule, the `changed_keys.is_empty()`-not-`text == base` rule, and the reserve-then-unlink / prune-after-commit / prune-is-best-effort ordering all survive with their enforcing test names; keep the same-line-`#`-relocation rule from header consequence 2; and keep a one-line finding pointer above each confirmed-finding regression test instead of a bare section label. Diff must be comment-only.

*How you would know it worked.* Require a diff containing only comment tokens, resolve every named test/PARITY pointer, and run rustdoc link checking. Existing core tests should remain byte-for-byte unchanged.

*Verifier.* I counted comment lines (lines whose trimmed form starts with //) inside every cited region: 1-64 = 64, 342-378 = 28, 571-785 = 88, 813-852 = 21, 971-1128 = 52, 1202-1604 = 157, 2754-2859 = 27, 2938-3119 = 25, 3223-3426 = 41, total 503 of the file's 789 comment lines. With ~95 lines of replacement text that is ~-408, so the -380 claim is if anything conservative. All four named tests exist: the_line_reader_agrees_with_parse_config_toml:2353, the_multiline_shadow_is_refused_by_apply_patch_write_and_edit_protocol:2428, the_scale_bounds_are_checked_at_the_runtimes_float_precision:2534, a_real_edit_preserves_crlf_a_bom_and_a_missing_final_newline:2703. This is Rule C territory and it is the deepest review-fix file in the repo: fix-blame attributes 1681 of 3584 lines to review-fix commits, and 18 confirmed findings cite it (A7-2, A10-1 critical, A10-2..A10-9, A4-9, A2-4). Region 1202-1604 in particular is almost entirely review-fix lines (1216-1225, 1244-1271, 1275-1292, 1317-1380, 1384-1518). Reading it, the comments there are exactly the landmine kind Rule C protects: 'A10-1: the absent-file branch used to be is_file() then write_atomic ... it now goes through Executor::create_new (O_EXCL)'; 'the test is "no key changed", NOT patched.text == base ... comparing rendered text used to rewrite (and back up) any file whose bytes toml_edit would normalise'; 'A10-2: everything from here to the rename is undone on failure ... the reservation itself is unlinked if the commit never happens'; and the prune loop's 'deliberately not ?' postmortem. The proposal's replacement for that block ('Create from the shared template, refuse live or concurrent changes, back up before atomic replacement, and prune only after commit; see the write safety tests') keeps the ordering skeleton but drops all four of those, and unlike its other blocks it names no test. It also drops one landmine from the header: consequence 2, that a same-line `# comment` is relocated to its own line because pre-2026-08 runtime builds mis-parse trailing comments and Sabrage never leaves one on a line it rewrote. I did not reject, because Rule C's escape hatch is met in shape — replacement text plus named tests — and the constraints in question are all genuinely test-enforced, so the fix is a strictly better replacement string, not abandoning the idea. Replacing per-test finding histories with bare section labels is the other soft spot: keep a one-line `Regression: <finding id> — <what regressed>` above each test that pins a confirmed finding. With those carve-outs the realistic delta is ~-300 rather than -400. No code, golden bytes, fixture or test body changes, so parity and the frozen phase4 fixture are untouched; the diff must contain only comment tokens.

#### `V7-2` — Table-drive the CLI parser, renderer, and signal tests

T1 · table-drive · **-340 lines** (reviewer claimed -450) · from V7 — CLI + Tauri command layer

Replace literal-per-function fixtures with four case tables: parser cases `(mode, argv, expected parsed/error)`, option-merge cases `(environment base, parsed flags, expected StageOptions/CheckOptions)`, renderer cases `(StageEvent, Colors, quiet, expected Vec<RenderedLine>)`, and signal-counter cases `(delivery sequence, first calls, second calls, reraised kind)`. Add small event constructors for common `run_id`, `step`, and stream fields. This removes lines at equal assertion coverage: preserve every exact error, row byte, color escape, CR/EOF routing, ignored-event, closing-banner, dry-run, fail-fast-chain, and double-signal assertion currently present.

*Files.* `sabrage/crates/sabrage-cli/src/main.rs`

*Evidence.* Parser cases repeat the same argv construction and equality scaffolding at main.rs:1403-1493 and main.rs:1613-1807. Renderer tests repeatedly construct full StageEvent values at main.rs:1811-2373; for example stdout/stderr CR cases duplicate all fields at main.rs:2119-2153. The signal tests repeat the same AtomicUsize/closure/sleep harness at main.rs:2508-2541, main.rs:2552-2576, and main.rs:2591-2637; the generated duplicate scan also identifies the harnesses beginning at main.rs:2509 and main.rs:2556.

*What breaks.* No assertion is lost. Individual function names become table-case labels, so a failure may report a case name/index inside one test rather than a standalone test binary name; poorly labeled cases would make diagnosis worse.

*Blast radius.* Only the `#[cfg(test)]` module in sabrage-cli. CLI parity and golden-like console bytes are directly involved, but their expected strings remain literal and unchanged.

*Precaution.* Carry every existing assert message into its table row's label, keep the A14-1/A14-3/A14-4/A14-5 section comments verbatim above their tables, and exclude the three signal tests (2478-2638) from the consolidation — their per-test `static`, their leaked polling threads and the pre-start delivery ordering do not survive a naive table.

*How you would know it worked.* Run `cargo test -p sabrage-cli`; require every former assertion to map to a named table row. Run tier-2 doctor parity because it rebuilds and executes this binary, and compare captured stdout/stderr for the renderer corpus byte-for-byte.

*Verifier.* I measured the three regions directly. The renderer block (1811-2373) is 563 lines with 21 full StageEvent literals, 21 identical `run_id: Default::default()` fields and 27 `stage_event_lines(...)` assertions — genuinely near-identical construction that a `(event, colors, quiet, expected)` table plus small constructors compresses well, worth roughly -180 to -230. The parser/rendering/merge block (1403-1807) is 405 lines over 29 tests; the error cases (`--bottle`/`--bs-dir`/`--tap` needs-a-value, unknown-argument, first-bad-token) table cleanly and the doctor row-shape tests do too, but the merge tests do not fully: merge_stage_options and merge_doctor_options operate on different type pairs (StageArgs/StageOptions vs DoctorArgs/CheckOptions), so the A14-1 empty-value cases need two tables, not one, and the env-base tests build heterogeneous partial structs. Call that -120. The signal block (2478-2638) is 161 lines but the least table-friendly: each test declares its own private `static TEST_COUNT: AtomicUsize` (a loop cannot create statics — you would need Box::leak per case), each `watch_flag*` call spawns a polling thread that is never joined so a shared counter would cross-talk between cases, and `two_signals_inside_one_poll_interval_are_not_collapsed_into_one` deliberately delivers both signals BEFORE starting the watcher while the other two deliver after — a plain 'delivery sequence' column loses that distinction, which is the entire point of the test. Call that -40 at most, and I would skip it. Total measured ≈ -340 against a claimed -450, about 32% over, which is inside tolerance. On Rule T no assertion need be lost: the exact error strings, row bytes, ANSI escapes, CR/EOF routing, ignored-event `vec![]` results, closing banners, dry-run rows and double-signal outcomes are all data that survive as table columns. The real risk is the diagnostic text: these tests carry per-case assert messages ('leading spaces belong to the shell's own line, not our indent', 'the second signal of a same-interval burst must still be fatal') and section headers naming A14-1/A14-3/A14-4/A14-5, and they are the record of what each case defends. Nothing outside main.rs references these test names, and no golden byte, contract slug, PARITY invariant or the shell fingerprint is involved. I did not compile — the constructor generics for a heterogeneous StageEvent table are the one place this could turn out awkward in practice.

#### `H5-4` — Collapse five forwarding `Executor` test doubles into one fault injector

T2 · collapse · **-340 lines** (reviewer claimed -320) · from H5 — dependency substitution

Create one test-only `FaultingExecutor` that owns an inner dry-run executor and an enum describing the injected fault: lose `create_new`, deny one `write_atomic`, cancel children, or return non-zero for a selected child argument. One `Executor` implementation performs all forwarding; small constructors retain each test’s vocabulary and recorder. This is cheaper than adding `mockall`, `delegate`, or another proc macro solely for five partial decorators. Replacement comment: `// FaultingExecutor delegates to the dry-run executor except for one named injected fault; the finding-pinned test using each constructor defines its contract.`

*Files.* `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/test_support.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/setup.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/session/reconcile.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/run/guards.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/run/mod.rs`

*Evidence.* Five partial decorators repeat nearly the entire trait: `LosesTheCreateRace` at sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/setup.rs:666-778, `FailSwitchTo` at sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/session/reconcile.rs:1379-1487, two guard decorators at sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/run/guards.rs:909-998 and :1105-1210, and `DenyWriteTo` at sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/run/mod.rs:1328-1429. The generated duplicate scan independently identifies 41-61-line forwarding matches at /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-yifeiding-orca-workspaces-wine-vr-gui/b37293e0-6dee-404f-b334-5695f0315524/scratchpad/simplify/metrics/dupes-fine.md:10-19.

*What breaks.* `with_step` must rebuild the wrapper around `inner.with_step(step)` while preserving the fault and any call recorder. Losing either makes step-attribution or failure-path tests falsely pass. Each special method must still delegate first where the current double records a planned child before altering its status.

*Blast radius.* Test-only code in four production modules plus a shared test-support module. Finding-pinned tests such as setup’s A5-3 race and run teardown failures keep every test function and assertion; only mock implementations collapse. No production binary or parity bytes change.

*Precaution.* with_step must return a new FaultingExecutor wrapping inner.with_step(step) while carrying both the fault and the shared recorder Arc — dropping either makes step-attribution and failure-path tests falsely pass. Each fault must fire only on its selected operation and must delegate to the inner DryRunExecutor FIRST where the current double does (guards.rs FailSwitchTo and run/mod.rs DenyWriteTo call self.inner.run_child(spec).await? before overriding the status, which is what keeps every attempt in ctx.executor.planned() in order). LosesTheCreateRace is the odd one out: its create_new does REAL filesystem work (std::fs::create_dir_all(parent) + std::fs::write(path, other_writers_bytes)) and returns Ok(false) without touching inner — that is the deterministic form of the O_EXCL race in critical confirmed r2 A5-3 (setup.rs:260-276) and its whole body sits inside the review-fix range setup.rs:666-823, so port it byte-for-byte rather than reimplementing it. Put the helper behind #[cfg(test)] mod test_support; at the crate root (it does not exist today — lib.rs declares only 16 pub mods) so no production module can reach it, and coordinate with H5-1, which wants to create the same file.

*How you would know it worked.* Run the existing affected test modules with identical test names and assertions. Confirm the inner dry-run plan remains identical, each injected fault fires only on its selected operation, `with_step` preserves the injection, and no production module can import the helper outside `cfg(test)`.

*Verifier.* All five decorators exist where claimed and I measured their exact spans: setup.rs LosesTheCreateRace 665-779 (115 lines), reconcile.rs FailSwitchTo 1379-1488 (110), guards.rs CancelChildren 909-999 (91), guards.rs FailSwitchTo 1105-1211 (107), run/mod.rs DenyWriteTo 1328-1430 (103) — 526 lines total, of which each body forwards ~16 Executor methods and overrides exactly one. The duplication is not speculative: guards.rs:1105's own doc comment says 'Same shape as super's DenyWriteTo', and guards.rs FailSwitchTo and reconcile.rs FailSwitchTo are literally the same struct with the same constructor. A single FaultingExecutor (enum Fault with 4-5 variants, one forwarding impl, five named constructors, docs) is ~185 lines, plus a mod declaration and five use lines — net about -340, i.e. their -320 is honest. Review-history gate: only ONE of the five sits in a review-fix range (setup.rs 666-823, per fix-blame.tsv), and that is LosesTheCreateRace, the double for r2 A5-3 'Setup's write-once config creation can overwrite a concurrent writer' (critical, CONFIRMED). CancelChildren pins A8-3 ('a cancelled switch leaves the guard armed for the teardown'), and reconcile.rs/guards.rs FailSwitchTo pin the audio-fallback behaviour PARITY.md:103 declares. Collapsing the doubles does not revert any of those fixes provided the faults are ported verbatim, and every test function and assertion stays — Rule T is satisfied because this is a mock collapse, not a case deletion. Invariants: this is test-only code, no production binary, no golden bytes, no contract slug, no shell fingerprint, nothing under contract//scripts//demo.sh//docs//ext/. The verification I could not do is the compile: whether one enum-driven forwarder can express all five faults (one of which, LosesTheCreateRace, changes create_new's return type semantics and does real I/O while the other four are dry-run decorators) is only provable by building, and I am not permitted to run cargo. Hence ACCEPT_WITH_CARE with the fault-fidelity precautions above rather than a plain ACCEPT.
