I confirmed the review docs never reference test function names, so losing per-fn names costs `cargo test <name>` filtering and one-test-per-case reporting, nothing traceability-critical, provided the finding id stays in the row label.

#### `H1-4` — Replace five forwarding Executor test doubles with one fault decorator

T2 · test-cut · **-290 lines** (reviewer claimed -280) · from H1 — cross-crate duplication and the two front-ends

Add one `#[cfg(test)] pub(crate)` decorator beside Executor with optional hooks for `create_new`, `write_atomic`, and `run_child`. It should delegate `with_step`, `planned`, dry-run state, and every unaffected operation once. Express each current failure scenario as a hook/configuration rather than another 80-line trait implementation. Lines are removed at equal assertion coverage: every existing test function, literal, fault point, event assertion, and plan assertion stays; assertion lost: none.

*Files.* `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/executor.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/session/reconcile.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/run/guards.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/setup.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/run/mod.rs`

*Evidence.* Five wrappers repeat the full Executor forwarding surface: `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/session/reconcile.rs:1388-1487`; `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/run/guards.rs:909-1000` and `:1105-1210`; `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/setup.rs:666-778`; and `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/run/mod.rs:1328-1429`. For example, DenyWriteTo's only distinctive behavior is `write_atomic` at `run/mod.rs:1366-1379`; the remaining methods at `:1346-1429` forward. LosesTheCreateRace differs only at `setup.rs:704-713`.

*What breaks.* A faulty shared decorator could stop preserving `with_step`, recorded plans, or the exact injected failure timing. That would weaken review-regression tests even if they still compile. No test or assertion should be deleted; only scaffolding changes.

*Blast radius.* Test-only support plus five existing test modules. Several tests pin confirmed review findings, so equal behavioral coverage—not merely equal test count—is mandatory. No production bytes, parity goldens, fixtures, or public API are involved.

*Precaution.* Give the decorator explicit per-method pass-through choices rather than blanket forwarding: leave create_new, hard_link and remove_dir_all_rollback on the trait defaults for the four doubles that use them today (only LosesTheCreateRace overrides create_new), keep CancelChildren's `calls` recorder as a hook, and diff each affected test's planned() vector and event rows before/after rather than only its pass/fail.

*How you would know it worked.* Downstream: run all tests in the five affected modules, compare test names and assertion counts before/after, and confirm each hook still reaches the same branch and preserves the underlying DryRunExecutor plan. No tests were run during this read-only review.

*Verifier.* I read all five: session/reconcile.rs FailSwitchTo (~1386-1485), stages/run/guards.rs CancelChildren (~908-1000) and a second FailSwitchTo (~1105-1210, whose own doc says 'Same shape as super's DenyWriteTo'), stages/setup.rs LosesTheCreateRace (~666-778), stages/run/mod.rs DenyWriteTo (~1328-1429). That is ~514 lines, of which the distinctive behaviour is one method each (run_child status rewrite, run_child→Cancelled + a call recorder, write_atomic PermissionDenied on one path, create_new returning Ok(false) after writing the other process's bytes). Replacing them with one decorator carrying create_new/write_atomic/run_child hooks plus a call recorder is ~190 lines with ~35 lines of construction sites, so -290 net against a -280 claim — the most accurate estimate in this batch. The trap the proposal does not name: Executor::create_new (executor.rs:265), hard_link (:316) and remove_dir_all_rollback (:297) have default trait implementations, and the default create_new is a real check-then-write against the filesystem. Four of the five doubles never override or forward those, so today they run the default; a decorator that dutifully forwards everything to `inner` would route them into DryRunExecutor::create_new (:1108), which records a PlannedAction instead — changing planned() contents for any path that reaches it, and changing behaviour rather than preserving it. Every assertion is retained under the proposal, which is what Rule T requires, and these tests pin CONFIRMED findings (A5-3's concurrent-writer race in setup, the audio-device fallback in reconcile, #202's save-failure-is-still-a-clean-quit in run/mod), so the coverage must be behaviourally equal, not merely equal in test count. Whether the decorator's lifetimes/BoxFuture signatures compile as cleanly as five bespoke impls is the one thing I could not check without building.

#### `H1-3` — Generate wire types; keep IPC behavior handwritten

T2 · replace-with-dep · **-260 lines** (reviewer claimed -450) · from H1 — cross-crate duplication and the two front-ends

Make Rust serde definitions the sole source of wire shapes using a build-only Rust-to-TypeScript schema emitter such as the `ts-rs`/Specta class of tools. Generate only enums, tagged unions, and DTO interfaces into an ignored build artifact; keep `invoke`/`Channel`/listener wrappers, UI predicates, constants, and FIX_META handwritten. This is the smallest honest mirror: it removes drift-prone declarations without pretending Tauri transport or presentation policy can be generated. The deliberately absent dependencies are HTTP/archive runtime trees needed to mimic shell tools; a build-only type emitter does not replace curl/tar or alter pipeline behavior (`sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/Cargo.toml:16-24`).

*Files.* `sabrage/ui/src/ipc.ts`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/Cargo.toml`, `sabrage/src-tauri/Cargo.toml`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/events.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/session/state.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/config/runtime_toml.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/store/settings.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/store/library.rs`, `sabrage/src-tauri/src/commands.rs`

*Evidence.* `sabrage/ui/src/ipc.ts:1-3` explicitly requires manual two-sided updates. Mirrored declarations span doctor DTOs at `:9-49`, stage/event/fix types at `:84-214`, session types at `:441-573`, reconcile/log types at `:622-722`, runtime-config types at `:844-902`, and settings/library DTOs at `:956-1119`. Rust's authoritative StageEvent union is `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/events.rs:175-332`; Tauri-specific DTOs begin at `sabrage/src-tauri/src/commands.rs:109-172`. FIX_META is behavior rather than a shape and occupies `sabrage/ui/src/ipc.ts:216-340`, so it is deliberately excluded.

*What breaks.* No runtime behavior is intended to change, but clean UI builds now depend on schema generation. Incorrect handling of serde rename/tag/default/Option rules could change the wire contract, so generated output must be compared against current JSON shapes. Generated source still exists in build output; the reduction is in human-maintained repository lines. FIX_META, deferred-fix policy, default value objects, and all transport wrappers remain.

*Blast radius.* Rust DTO annotations/export task, Cargo manifests/lockfile, UI build scripts, and ipc.ts imports. Serialization parity and golden wire names are involved; pipeline bytes, contract files, shell scripts, and runtime dependencies are not.

*Precaution.* Before deleting a single TS line: port the A13b-8/A13b-4 flatten comments and the `end`/`StreamKind` notes into Rust doc comments, generate once and diff the emitter's output against today's ipc.ts declaration-for-declaration (especially the flatten index signatures and the `kind`-tagged StageEvent union), then re-measure the net after the derives land — if it is under ~200 lines the drift guarantee, not the line count, has to justify it.

*How you would know it worked.* Downstream: generate from a clean checkout, regenerate twice to prove idempotence, run Rust serialization tests and `npm run check`, and snapshot every tagged union/command DTO against its current TypeScript form. Fail the build if ipc.ts redeclares a generated wire type. No dependency was installed or code generated during this scan.

*Verifier.* The premise is accurate: ipc.ts:1-3 literally says 'No codegen — when either side changes a shape, update both by hand', and the mirrored declarations are where the proposal says they are. I measured 414 lines of `export type`/`export interface` bodies excluding their leading doc comments (52 exported types against 40 exported functions/consts in a 1219-line file); with doc comments the cited regions total ~630, so -450 for 'human-maintained TS declaration lines removed' is defensible. What the estimate omits is the other side of the trade, which is also human-maintained: `#[derive(TS)]`/`#[ts(export_to=…)]`/`#[ts(rename=…)]` attributes on ~35 types across sabrage-core and src-tauri, a second export config because the shapes live in two crates, an export binary or test, npm script + .gitignore + build-ordering wiring, and manifest entries in both Cargo.tomls — call it 150-190 lines back, hence my -260. Two specific hazards. (1) sabrage-core/Cargo.toml:16-19 carries an explicit 'Deliberately NOT added' dependency policy for this crate; ts-rs/specta is build-only and arguably fine, but it is a policy the manifest states out loud. (2) Several of the declarations being replaced carry landmine comments, not decoration: `Settings`/`LaunchDefaults`'s `[key: string]: unknown` index signature is the wire half of `#[serde(flatten)] extra` and its comment ('Keep spreading the loaded object … or the extras are dropped before they reach the backend') is the fix for CONFIRMED A13b-8 and the still-open A13b-4; `StageEvent.output.end` documents `#[serde(default)]` back-compat; `StreamKind` documents a deliberate rename. A generator emits Rust `///` docs, so these can survive — but only if they are moved into Rust first, which is work the estimate does not include. Finally, viability genuinely turns on a codegen result (serde internally-tagged `kind`, camelCase, `Option` vs `#[serde(default)]`, flatten) that I am not permitted to run here.

#### `V4-3` — Table-drive repeated filesystem outcome matrices

T2 · table-drive · **-220 lines** (reviewer claimed -340) · from V4 — checks + contract + generator

Introduce small local fixture builders and case tables for repeated input → status/message/remedy matrices. The largest wins are six protocol fixtures and the session-shape matrix in `config.rs`, the wine/wired branches in `run_only.rs`, and manifest/config presence matrices in host, bottle, bridge, overlay, game, pinned, and sources. Preserve the named A3b-1, A3b-3, and A7-4 regression functions, but let them call shared setup/assert helpers. This removes lines at equal product assertion coverage: statuses, exact messages, remedies, detail fields, parser edge cases, and cleanup behavior all remain asserted.

*Files.* `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/checks/config.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/checks/run_only.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/checks/host.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/checks/bottle.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/checks/bridge.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/checks/overlay.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/checks/game.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/checks/pinned.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/checks/sources.rs`

*Evidence.* `config.rs:419` through `config.rs:547` repeats directory creation, write, context construction, evaluation, and cleanup for protocol literals; the shingle report independently identifies the repeated blocks beginning at `config.rs:458` and `config.rs:515`. `run_only.rs:302` through `run_only.rs:494` repeats the same fixture construction across wine and wired-adb branches. Equivalent repeated state matrices occupy `host.rs:144`, `bottle.rs:215`, `bridge.rs:177`, `overlay.rs:109`, `game.rs:116`, `pinned.rs:115`, and `sources.rs:102`.

*What breaks.* No assertions should be lost. The main risk is a table helper accidentally normalizing a path, permission, or missing-file distinction; the finding-pinned tests must retain their names and explicit special setup, especially the unreadable-file and wedged-child cases.

*Blast radius.* Tests only in nine owned modules. Golden generator tests, registry/parity tests, live-machine checks, and frozen fixtures are not involved.

*Precaution.* Land it module by module and diff assertion counts per field (status, message, remedy, detail) before and after; leave shadowed_protocol_alvr_then_oxrsys_resolves_to_the_last_assignment, malformed_json_warns, unreadable_session_json_warns and the_devices_probe_gives_up_on_a_wedged_adb_instead_of_blocking_forever as standalone fns with their own setup.

*How you would know it worked.* Count the pre/post assertions by field and branch, then run the relevant core test modules. Specifically verify that the A3b-1 shadowing, A3b-3 malformed/unreadable, and A7-4 timeout tests still run as individually named tests.

*Verifier.* I read the cited regions rather than trusting the shingle report. config.rs:419-547 is six protocol tests that each repeat scratch/create_dir_all/write/ctx_with_toml/evaluate/remove_dir_all, and config.rs:563-751 is seven more session-pin tests on the same shape; run_only.rs:302-494 is eight tests that each rebuild fixture_ctx plus a bespoke fake adb; host.rs:144-257 is six manifest-state tests on one fixture shape. The repetition is genuine and Rule T is satisfied in principle -- collapsing near-identical fns into a case table loses no assertion as long as the table carries status, exact message, remedy and detail, which the proposal explicitly requires. My delta is the problem: the nine modules hold 1658 test lines across 66 tests, gross removal is maybe 400 lines, but each module needs its own Case struct plus runner (the expected messages interpolate per-case paths, so several cases need a closure or an enum rather than a literal), which adds back ~150; -200 to -220 net is the realistic figure and -340 is roughly 55% high. Safety: I checked fix-blame -- config.rs 499-548 and 571-650 and run_only.rs 496-557 are review-fix ranges, i.e. exactly the A3b-1 shadowed-protocol, A3b-3 malformed/unreadable and A7-4 wedged-adb tests. The proposal already says to keep those named and separately set up, which is the right call: unreadable_session_json_warns depends on chmod 000 plus a root check plus permission restoration before any panic, and the_devices_probe_gives_up_on_a_wedged_adb reads back the fake child's pid and asserts it was reaped -- neither survives being normalized into a generic fixture builder. Nothing here touches golden bytes, the frozen phase4 fixture, contract slugs or the shell fingerprint; it is test-only inside owned modules.

#### `H4-5` — Reduce crate maps and infrastructure headers to ownership boundaries

T1 · comment-cut · **-203 lines** · from H4 — the comment mass

Exact logical replacements:

`sabrage-core/src/lib.rs:1`: `//! UI-independent native pipeline paired with demo.sh through contract/pipeline.toml, two byte-shared templates, and PARITY.md. Mutations use Executor; checks remain read-only.`
`contract.rs:1`: `//! Compile-time parity contract; runtime checkout hashing is separate so meta.contract-sync can detect checkout/generated/binary skew. See tests::{contract_parses_and_include_path_resolves, compiled_contract_sha256_differs_from_another_checkouts_contract}.`
`process.rs:1`: `//! Process primitives. spawn_streamed is for cancellable tools and kill-on-drop; wine/dashboard must use Executor::spawn_detached. Output splits on CR, LF, or CRLF; reaps match executable identity. See process cancellation tests and PARITY.md Stop.`
`sabrage-contract-gen/src/lib.rs:1`: `//! Generate the committed zsh projection of pipeline.toml without depending on sabrage-core. Header hash catches stale generation; check() catches hand edits. See parity::tests::generate_matches_the_committed_contract_gen_sh.`
`store/mod.rs:1`: `//! Sabrage-only persistent state. Missing files default, corrupt files fail, and all writes use Executor; settings, library, and Goldberg modules own their individual invariants.`
`events.rs:1`: `//! One stage-event wire format for CLI and Tauri. Shell text remains unlabelled in payloads; renderers add presentation. Serde tags, step ids, and slugs are compatibility surface.`
`executor.rs:1`: `//! Mutation boundary shared by real and dry-run execution; probes still run so plans can distinguish changed from unchanged. External curl/tar/cp preserve shell parity; boxed futures keep Arc<dyn Executor> object-safe.`

*Files.* `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/lib.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/contract.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/process.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-contract-gen/src/lib.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/store/mod.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/events.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/executor.rs`

*Evidence.* The crate/infrastructure maps occupy `sabrage-core/src/lib.rs:1-64`, `contract.rs:1-29`, `process.rs:1-29`, `sabrage-contract-gen/src/lib.rs:1-28`, `store/mod.rs:1-28`, `events.rs:1-27`, and `executor.rs:1-27`. Ownership and invariants are executable at `contract.rs:303`, `contract.rs:385`, `process.rs:1154`, `sabrage-parity/src/lib.rs:43`, `events.rs:694`, and `executor.rs:1564`.

*What breaks.* Only navigational rustdoc detail. No module declarations, public exports, serde definitions, subprocess policy, or store behavior changes.

*Blast radius.* Seven Rust files, comments only. Contract generation, parity assets, event JSON, and executor implementations are untouched.

*Precaution.* Keep the 'check message/remedy strings track doctor.sh verbatim because docs/troubleshooting.md quotes them' sentence in lib.rs (or in checks/mod.rs, but not neither, if H4-2 also lands), since no test enforces it; and treat lib.rs's module map as a separate decision from the other six headers.

*How you would know it worked.* Review a comment-only diff, build rustdoc, run contract-generator/parity tests, and remeasure 232 lines to approximately 29.

*Verifier.* I counted the seven headers: lib.rs 64, contract.rs 29, process.rs 29, sabrage-contract-gen/src/lib.rs 28, store/mod.rs 28, events.rs 27, executor.rs 27 = 232, matching the claim, against ~29 replacement lines, so -203 is exact. All three cited tests resolve. This is the lowest-risk set by fix-blame -- only 8 of 232 lines are review-fix-authored (all in store/mod.rs) -- and I checked each header's landmines for a second home. process.rs's 'Scope warning -- build tools only' (spawn_streamed's kill_on_drop(true) would SIGKILL the game) is restated by the replacement itself, by executor.rs:357's spawn_detached doc, and by PARITY.md:87's wine-process-group row. executor.rs's 'cp -R preserves symlinks in CrossOver's lib/dxmt tree, a naive walk would dereference them' survives at Executor::dir_copy's own doc, executor.rs:320, which this proposal does not touch. contract.rs's compile-time-vs-runtime-hash split -- the actual mechanism behind the A1-1 skew guard -- is preserved in the replacement and pinned by the two named tests; the include-path-depth paragraph is navigational and contract_parses_and_include_path_resolves covers it. sabrage-contract-gen's 'deliberately not depending on sabrage-core' and both tripwires are preserved. events.rs's wire-format stability rule is preserved as 'serde tags, step ids and slugs are compatibility surface', and its zsh-to-event mapping table is a shell mirror the launch-text goldens enforce. No doctests (all fences ```zsh/```text), no deny(missing_docs) or broken_intra_doc_links anywhere in the workspace, no golden bytes, nothing under contract/, scripts/, docs/ or ext/. The one uncovered loss is lib.rs:17-19: 'Console text is not a shared contract -- but check message and remedy strings still track scripts/demo/doctor.sh verbatim, because docs/troubleshooting.md quotes them.' The parity harness explicitly joins on slug plus status and never compares prose, so literally nothing tests this; the comment is the enforcement. Its only duplicate is checks/mod.rs:8-11, which H4-2 also deletes -- so if both land the reason disappears from the crate entirely, leaving only the weaker field-level notes at checks/mod.rs:101 and :153. Separately, lib.rs's 64-line module map is the rustdoc front door for a 40-file crate, and collapsing it to two sentences is a navigability trade the owner may not want at this ratio.

#### `V3-2` — Extract SessionMonitor’s policy into a pure reducer

T2 · extract · **-200 lines** (reviewer claimed -500) · from V3 — session lifecycle + logs

Make `snapshot()` a thin observation adapter: collect the live handle/publication, persisted-state classification, parsed runtime status, external-game PID, and newly tailed encoder lines, then pass a `SnapshotInputs` value plus the monitor’s run-scoped history to a pure `reduce_snapshot`. Keep every named A8/A9 regression test, but feed synthetic observations directly instead of constructing directories, global slots, JSON files, live processes, and logs for every policy row. Retain focused integration tests for the adapters that actually need OS evidence: state-file loading, the real process-table fallback, and Tailer ingestion. This is lines removed at equal assertion coverage; no phase, identity, freshness, encoder-attribution, or finding assertion is dropped.

*Files.* `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/session/watcher.rs`

*Evidence.* `snapshot` currently interleaves global reads, filesystem reads, process enumeration, tail polling, and policy in one method at watcher.rs:372-656: live/persisted selection begins at :381, runtime-file I/O at :438, process-table fallback at :483, tail polling at :495, stall reduction at :519, phase precedence at :543, and encoder-history reduction at :608. The corresponding fixture layer occupies watcher.rs:895-978, while the scenario tests continue through watcher.rs:2145; for example the precedence table at :1525 repeatedly creates paths/files/globals around an already table-driven policy assertion.

*What breaks.* A mistaken observation boundary could change precedence, carry freshness or encoder history across runs, or stop exercising a real adapter. The real-file, process-table, and Tailer smoke tests must remain; confirmed A8-5, A9-5, A9-6, A9-7, A10-8, and A13a-2 test names and assertions must survive.

*Blast radius.* Primarily watcher.rs production and tests. SessionStatus’s wire shape, parity, and golden bytes remain unchanged, but nearly every watcher regression test is rewritten to use the reducer.

*Precaution.* Keep at least one file/global/process-backed integration test per confirmed finding (A8-5, A9-5, A9-6, A10-8, A13a-2) alongside the reducer tests, and diff the SessionStatus values the precedence table produces before and after to prove the boundary moved no policy.

*How you would know it worked.* Run the watcher tests by name, verify the existing phase-precedence table produces identical SessionStatus values, retain integration coverage for runtime_status.json, running_game_pid, and Tailer, then run tier-1 parity tests.

*Verifier.* I read snapshot() at watcher.rs:372-656 and confirmed the description: it interleaves LIVE_SESSION/RUN_PHASE global reads, state::load, a runtime_status.json read, a process-table walk (running_game_pid), Tailer::poll, and then five distinct policy reductions (freshness scoping, stall rule, published-phase precedence, encoder edge-clear, encoder attribution). All monitor mutation is confined to fresh_run_id/ever_fresh/last_fresh_unix_ms/runtime_status/encoder/encoder_run_id/last_phase/preload_pending, so a reduce_snapshot(&mut History, SnapshotInputs) -> SessionStatus is structurally achievable; SessionStatus's camelCase wire shape and the phase words are asserted in session/mod.rs and are untouched by the change. The size claim does not hold up. The scenario tests run watcher.rs:987-2145 and fix-blame.tsv marks 980-1520 as entirely review-fix-authored; I read the largest of them, snapshot_phase_precedence_table at :1525-1638, and it is already table-driven — only ~25 of its 135 lines are the fixture setup a reducer would remove, the rest being rustfmt's vertical tuple rows that survive verbatim. Across the ten scenario tests the recoverable scaffolding is roughly 15-25 lines each, and the production side grows by a SnapshotInputs struct, a History struct and an adapter, so -200 is my estimate and -500 is inflated by more than 40%. The real hazard is coverage, not compilation: A9-6's fix is the preload window (created_at_unix_ms vs the line's own spdlog timestamp, set at monitor construction), A10-8/A9-5 hinge on classify_identity being applied to a real live handle, and A13a-2 is the running_game_pid process-table walk — if all three become synthetic reducer inputs, the tests stop proving that the monitor computes them. I could not compile-check the refactor (build commands are off-limits here), so this is accepted on reading only.

#### `V8-5` — Delete unreachable UI states and narrow IPC declarations to consumed projections

T1 · delete · **-196 lines** (reviewer claimed -205) · from V8 — the Svelte UI

Project responses to what this UI reads instead of reproducing unused nested wire trees. Reduce `ReconcileReport` to `rows`; delete unused `isActivePhase`, `PHASE_TONE`, `settingsStore.save`, and the raw bottles-state getter; omit unconsumed diagnostic fields; and remove the impossible `createdFromTemplate` display. Also delete destructive-fix confirmation state: the only destructive action is withheld before either Doctor or preflight can send it. Make GUI `applyFix` calls send `confirmed: false`, so any future destructive action fails closed until a real confirmation UI is deliberately restored. About 90 lines of this proposal are subsumed by V8-2 and must not be added twice.

*Files.* `sabrage/ui/src/ipc.ts`, `sabrage/ui/src/stores/session.svelte.ts`, `sabrage/ui/src/stores/settings.svelte.ts`, `sabrage/ui/src/stores/bottles.svelte.ts`, `sabrage/ui/src/screens/Settings.svelte`, `sabrage/ui/src/screens/Doctor.svelte`, `sabrage/ui/src/components/GateModal.svelte`, `sabrage/ui/src/screens/Session.svelte`, `sabrage/ui/src/components/Sidebar.svelte`

*Evidence.* `stores/session.svelte.ts:180-189` explicitly retains only `report.rows`, making the nested reconcile declarations at `ipc.ts:620-686` unused by the UI. `isActivePhase` and `PHASE_TONE` are defined at `ipc.ts:471-507`, while Session and Sidebar independently consume `canStop` and their own phase mappings at `Session.svelte:314-363` and `Sidebar.svelte:25-56`. `settingsStore.save` occupies `stores/settings.svelte.ts:69-82`, but the writer uses queued `update` at `Settings.svelte:106-125` and `Settings.svelte:291-298`. A missing runtime file renders only the setup branch at `Settings.svelte:432-445`, so `createdFromTemplate` at `Settings.svelte:643-645` cannot be displayed after a UI-initiated save. Rust withholds both deferred IDs at `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/fixes/mod.rs:68-79`, and its tests establish that `DeleteSessionJson` is the sole destructive action at `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/fixes/mod.rs:826-831`; Doctor and preflight project fixes through that filter at `sabrage/src-tauri/src/commands.rs:93-106` and `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/run/preflight.rs:403-415`. Consequently the confirm branches at `Doctor.svelte:189-193`, `Doctor.svelte:393-409`, `GateModal.svelte:279-283`, and `GateModal.svelte:552-562` are unreachable today.

*What breaks.* No current UI behavior if the registry remains as cited. The TypeScript facade stops documenting fields no consumer reads. A future offered destructive fix will be refused by the backend instead of receiving a GUI confirmation until that feature is explicitly implemented; future consumers of omitted fields must add their projection back.

*Blast radius.* Nine assigned files plus read-only Rust evidence. Runtime payloads and Rust serialization do not change—extra response fields are simply ignored. No contract, parity, or golden bytes are involved. Roughly 90 lines overlap V8-2’s larger removal.

*Precaution.* Land after V8-2 (or subtract its ~90 overlapping confirm-state lines); keep FIX_META.consequence, DEFERRED_FIX_IDS, and RepoInfo's two contract sha256 fields; run `npm run check` before merging since narrowing ReconcileReport and deleting exported helpers changes the exported type surface (I did not compile, per instructions).

*How you would know it worked.* Run `npm run check`; search for removed getters, phase metadata, and confirm state; launch once to exercise reconcile/status; save settings and runtime config; run every currently offered non-destructive fix and confirm it is accepted with `confirmed: false`; verify the withheld destructive action still has no button and is rejected server-side.

*Verifier.* I verified every claim by grep. (a) Nothing outside ipc.ts references `Reconciled`, `SessionState`, `ProcInfo`, `GuardFlags` or `WiredForward`; session.svelte.ts:186-190 stores only `report.rows` and Session.svelte:222-223 is the only reader, so narrowing ReconcileReport to `{ rows: string[] }` drops ipc.ts:622-682 (~58 lines) with no consumer. (b) `isActivePhase` (ipc.ts:474) and `PHASE_TONE` (497) have exactly one hit each — their own definitions; both were added by the review-r1 fix commit 73b790d but nothing consumes them, and PHASE_TONE's `satisfies Record<SessionPhase, ...>` exhaustiveness guard is duplicated by Sidebar's `PHASE_DOT: Record<SessionStatus['phase'], string>` and Session's PHASE_LABEL/PHASE_CLASS (314-335), so no compile-time guard is lost (~22 lines). (c) `settingsStore.save` is never called — every writer goes through `update` (Settings.svelte:110, 294, 357, 376) — and the A13b-4 hardening lives in `enqueue`/`performSave`, which stay (~15 lines). (d) `bottlesStore.state` is never read; StagesPanel:69 uses `load()`'s return value (~3 lines). (f) `createdFromTemplate` at Settings.svelte:643-645 is genuinely unreachable from a UI-initiated save: the `{:else if !configStore.view.exists}` branch at 440-444 renders 'run setup first' instead of the Save form, so the Save button only exists when the file exists (~3 lines). (g) I checked the Rust: `gui_fix_refusal` (commands.rs:1164-1172) refuses only `destructive && !confirmed`, so hard-coding `confirmed: false` is a no-op for every currently offered fix and fails closed for a future destructive one — a strictly stronger door on top of DEFERRED_CONTRACT_FIX_IDS (fixes/mod.rs:79) and run_doctor's projection. It does delete the confirm dialogs that r1 A12-1's fix installed, but r2 A12-1's fix already made those branches unreachable by withholding the button, so this removes dead disclosure rather than re-exposing a known-bad remedy. Sum: 58+22+15+3+3 = 101 of dead code, plus ~5 for the unconsumed contract digests and ~90 for the confirm state = ~196 against a claimed 205. Two cautions beyond the double-count. First, `compiledContractSha256`/`checkoutContractSha256` are the only unconsumed RepoInfo fields (Settings renders the booleans only); they exist because of CONFIRMED A1-1 'a binary can validate checkout Y while executing checkout X's contract', and they cost 2 declaration lines — keep them, they are what a 'which contract am I running' bug report needs. Second, ipc.ts's own doc says FIX_META's `consequence` is kept 'for the CLI-initiated path', so do not let the confirm-state deletion take `consequence` or DEFERRED_FIX_IDS with it. No contract, PARITY invariant, golden byte, fixture or shell fingerprint is touched; the Rust wire payloads are unchanged since extra JSON fields are simply ignored.

#### `V6-5` — Turn the privilege security essay into eight enforceable rules

T1 · comment-cut · **-184 lines** (reviewer claimed -180) · from V6 — core primitives + privilege

Replace the selected prose with exactly these rules: `/// Escape for an AppleScript string; see nasty_paths_round_trip_through_both_quoting_layers.` `/// Quote one /bin/sh argv element; see the same round-trip test.` `/// Return the exact elevation argv; see elevation_argv_is_one_osascript_or_install_shs_two_sudo_calls.` `/// Install the byte-golden host manifest: skip current, announce before prompting, then verify destination bytes.` `/// Reject raw JSON control characters before any prompt.` `/// Cancellation is bounded and reaped; the caller defuses staging because a root descendant may outlive us.` `/// Staging is 0600 create_new; Drop cleans unless cancellation defuses it, and old defused files are age-swept.` `/// Upgrade only likely .app permission failures to TccDenied after emitting one Fatal.` Keep the first one-line description on minor helpers and delete the subsequent examples, history, and rejected alternatives.

*Files.* `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/privilege.rs`

*Evidence.* The selected post-header blocks contain 201 comment lines at privilege.rs:257-336, 370-409, 479-512, 595-675, 705-765, and 801-951. Exact quoting/argv behavior is asserted at privilege.rs:1065-1194; staging at privilege.rs:1198-1241 and 1533-1564; skip/dry-run/cancellation/control-character behavior at privilege.rs:1245-1529; TCC classification and messaging at privilege.rs:1601-1858; privileged bytes at privilege.rs:1862-1875.

*What breaks.* No privileged command, bytes, permissions, cancellation path, classification, or test changes. The security rationale becomes less tutorial-like, but each retained rule points to its executable proof.

*Blast radius.* privilege.rs comments only. The root-owned destination, admin prompts, terminal fallback, TCC behavior, parity bytes, and all confirmed-finding tests remain involved but unchanged.

*Precaution.* Retain the sentences at privilege.rs:398-401 (dylib path, never pre-rendered content), :403-409 (the one mutation outside Executor, and why), :907-909 (a TCC grant applies only to a process started after it) and the 'caller propagates TccDenied instead of re-emitting' contract; cut the rest.

*How you would know it worked.* Require a comments-only diff and manually map each retained rule to the cited assertion. Later run privilege tests and the host-manifest parity goldens.

*Verifier.* I measured 206 comment lines across the six ranges (their 201) and, allowing for the eight replacement rules plus the retained first line on each minor helper, about -184 against their -180 — accurate. Both named tests resolve (nasty_paths_round_trip_through_both_quoting_layers privilege.rs:1090, elevation_argv_is_one_osascript_or_install_shs_two_sudo_calls :1155). fix-blame.tsv shows this proposal mostly avoids the review-fix ranges in privilege.rs (49-56, 109-134, 150-172, 415-473, 595-629, 637-712, 734-785, 856-895): it clips 595-675, 705-765 and 856-895, and for those I checked the review record. A6-1 r1 ('Privileged cancellation can outlive the staging file', high) and the control-character work (r2 A1-7) are both mirrored in PARITY.md — row 122 states the bounded cancel and the deliberately kept 0600 staging file, row 114 states the native-only control-character refusal and that json_escape_string stays exactly install.sh's two substitutions — and the proposed rules ('Cancellation is bounded and reaped; the caller defuses staging because a root descendant may outlive us', 'Staging is 0600 create_new; Drop cleans unless cancellation defuses it, and old defused files are age-swept', 'Reject raw JSON control characters before any prompt') keep the constraints. So Rule C is satisfied for the bulk. Three items are not, and none of them is in PARITY.md. First, privilege.rs:403-409 records that the elevated child is the single mutation in the codebase that does NOT go through Executor, with the reason (osascript needs its stderr captured to tell declined from failed; sudo needs the process's own tty) — it is the documented exception to this crate's central rule, and the exception invites 'fixing' precisely when the reason is gone. Second, privilege.rs:398-401 ('the parameter is the dylib path, never pre-rendered content, so a caller cannot hand this the newline-less comparison form and silently write a file that differs from ./demo.sh install's by one byte') is the byte-parity seam; V6-1 deletes its duplicate in the module header, so if both land it disappears entirely — the parity golden pins the bytes host_manifest_bytes produces, not the signature that prevents the wrong bytes being passed in. Third, privilege.rs:907-909 explains that macOS only re-evaluates a TCC grant for a process started after it, which is why app_management_remedy says 'relaunch Sabrage'; the single offered rule for the whole 801-951 span ('Upgrade only likely .app permission failures to TccDenied after emitting one Fatal') covers none of it, and that span also carries the 'the caller propagates TccDenied rather than emitting a second Fatal' contract that error.rs's already_reported depends on.

#### `H3-3` — Extract local scenario builders while keeping every regression test named

T2 · extract · **-180 lines** (reviewer claimed -500) · from H3 — the test mass

Several large modules repeatedly assemble the same session record, fake command tree, context, and expected event projection around different outcomes. Introduce small per-file builders—not a cross-module scenario DSL—and retain every existing test function and assertion. Incremental to H3-1, the measured targets are run/mod -150, reconcile -95, preflight -65, actions -75, stop -60, goldberg -40, and CLI -15: 500 lines removed at equal assertion coverage.

*Files.* `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/run/mod.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/session/reconcile.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/run/preflight.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/run/actions.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/stop.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/store/goldberg.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-cli/src/main.rs`

*Evidence.* run/mod repeats live-owner launch setup at run/mod.rs:1771, run/mod.rs:1823, and run/mod.rs:1882, then teardown setup at run/mod.rs:2036-2188. Reconcile repeats pending-guard records at reconcile.rs:2456-2556 and detach races at reconcile.rs:2878-3036. Stop repeats wedged-probe and foreign-helper arrangements at stop.rs:907-965 and stop.rs:1442-1528. Actions rebuilds wired rollback fixtures at actions.rs:1085-1497 and Goldberg provenance fixtures at actions.rs:1587-1905. Preflight's protocol/fix scenarios occupy preflight.rs:1577-1916, while Goldberg restore/refusal cases repeatedly build the same payload and paths at goldberg.rs:312-703.

*What breaks.* No test or assertion should be removed. The danger is hiding a meaningful precondition behind a builder default; each test must explicitly set every field material to its finding, and builders must not normalize malformed records or filesystem shapes that the test is meant to preserve.

*Blast radius.* Seven test modules only. Many cases pin review findings, so their names, comments, exact expected events, persisted bytes, and cancellation outcomes remain. watcher.rs is deliberately excluded because its process-global sequencing is load-bearing.

*Precaution.* Land H3-1 first and re-measure; scope the work to run/mod.rs, reconcile.rs and stop.rs only; have every builder return the `lock_session_globals()` guard as a named binding the test holds, and never let a builder create a path a test asserts is absent.

*How you would know it worked.* The affected test-name set and assertion inventory must remain unchanged. Review each builder call against its old setup, then run the seven module suites plus the full core/CLI suites. For stateful cases, compare final record bytes and ordered StageEvent projections before and after.

*Verifier.* Every cited line range exists. But the premise fails on three of the seven files: preflight.rs:1577-1916 already routes through a `fixture(tag, ...)` + `make_everything_pass(&f)` pair (verified at 1577 and 1916), actions.rs:1587-1905 already routes through `goldberg_fixture(root, api_bytes, gbe_bytes)` (verified at 1587), and store/goldberg.rs:312-703 already routes through `test_paths`/`paths_with_payload`/`plugin_dir` (verified at 312 and 695). The proposal's own per-file targets for those three (-65, -75, -40 plus part of actions' -75) are therefore mostly already banked. What is genuinely repeated is run/mod.rs's three live-refusal setups at 1771/1823/1882 — each opens with the same six lines (`lock_session_globals`, `publish_run_phase(None)`, `scratch`, the same `dry_ctx` StageOptions block, `ctx.bottle = ...`) and closes with the same three-assertion 'nothing permanent happened' block — plus reconcile.rs's pending-guard and detach-race families and stop.rs's wedged-probe/foreign-helper arrangements. dupes-fine.md's >=12-line runs across these files total roughly 150 lines, and allowing for sub-threshold repetition I measure about -180. Review history: run/mod.rs 1506-2034, reconcile.rs 1747-2201 and 2914-3037, stop.rs 1263-1536 and actions.rs 1243-1582 are all review-fix ranges, so almost every test this touches is a finding regression (A7-1, A8-1, A9-1/A9-8 are named in the doc comments I read). Nothing is reverted as long as the tests and their doc comments survive, but the blast radius is squarely inside fix-authored code. The specific hazard I found: these tests open with `let _g = session::lock_session_globals();`, whose guard must live for the whole test body; a builder that returns it inside a tuple the caller binds to `_` (or that drops it internally) makes the process-global LIVE_SESSION tests race with no compile error and no immediate red. Second hazard: the same tests deliberately assert on the *absence* of files (`!ctx.paths.session_state_path().exists()` is 'the whole point' per its own comment) — a builder that helpfully creates a store directory would silently void that. No invariant, golden byte, contract, PARITY.md row or fixture is touched. Substantial overlap with H3-1 (the `dry_ctx`, `scratch` and `rows` helpers it would build on), so its delta must not be added to H3-1's blindly.

*Depends on.* `H3-1`

#### `H5-2` — Replace the raw signal subsystem with `signal-hook`

T2 · replace-with-dep · **-175 lines** (reviewer claimed -185) · from H5 — dependency substitution

Use one shared `Arc<AtomicBool>` and register `signal_hook::flag::register_conditional_default` before `flag::register` for both SIGINT and SIGTERM. That primitive implements “first signal arms cancellation; a subsequent signal takes that delivered signal’s default action,” including different-kind and same-poll bursts, without hard-coded signal numbers, FFI, counters, re-raising, or a custom second-action state machine; see the [official signal-hook flag documentation](https://docs.rs/signal-hook/latest/signal_hook/flag/index.html). Keep the finding-pinned behavioral tests, rewritten as child-process signal tests, so this estimate takes no test-coverage credit. Replacement comment: `// First SIGINT/SIGTERM cancels; the next of either kind takes that delivered signal's default action. Pinned by the three second-signal tests below.`

*Files.* `sabrage/crates/sabrage-cli/Cargo.toml`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-cli/src/main.rs`, `sabrage/Cargo.lock`

*Evidence.* The signal rationale occupies sabrage/crates/sabrage-cli/src/main.rs:1167-1245 and the raw implementation occupies :1247-1387. Finding #7, finding #6, burst, and cross-kind assertions are at :2480-2638. Tokio already brings `signal-hook-registry` and libc at sabrage/Cargo.lock:3897-3909; the registry package is at :3217-3224, so the new runtime weight is principally the high-level `signal-hook` crate itself.

*What breaks.* Registration order is load-bearing: registering the conditional default after the flag handler can terminate on the first signal. The replacement must preserve first-signal cancellation’s exit 130, immediate default termination on the second signal, the second signal’s own identity (SIGINT versus SIGTERM), and registration-error handling.

*Blast radius.* One CLI module, one manifest, and its four signal tests. Signal/exit parity is involved, including confirmed-finding pins, but contract files, shell code, stage output, and golden bytes are untouched.

*Precaution.* Register the conditional default BEFORE flag::register for each of SIGINT and SIGTERM (signal-hook-registry runs actions in registration order; the reverse order kills the process on the first signal), share ONE Arc<AtomicBool> across both signals so a cross-kind second delivery is fatal, handle the SigId Result rather than discarding it, and rebuild the three second-signal tests as child-process tests before deleting watch_flag_with_second_action — the practical shape is a #[test] gated on an env var that re-execs std::env::current_exe() with --exact so no new CLI subcommand is added (the demo.sh vocabulary is frozen and main.rs:1430-1492 asserts the usage/exit-2 surface byte-for-byte). Keep, in the shrunken comment, the two pieces of landmine rationale that are NOT in PARITY.md: the registration-order requirement, and the fact that an impatient double-tap during run leaves the detached wine child, the BlackHole routing and session-state.json for the next run/stop to reconcile. Viability of the exact ts of signal-hook's flatten-free API turns on a compile the verifier is not permitted to run.

*How you would know it worked.* Retain child-process tests for: first signal invokes cancellation once; two same-kind signals are fatal even without a polling gap; SIGINT then SIGTERM is fatal as SIGTERM; and the reverse is fatal as SIGINT. Also confirm ordinary cancelled stages still report exit 130 and no raw `extern "C"`, `signal`, `raise`, or hard-coded signal constants remain.

*Verifier.* The regions exist as described: main.rs:1167-1245 is the 79-line signal rationale block, and 1247-1387 is the 141-line raw implementation (SIGNAL_COUNT/LAST_SIGNAL statics, on_sigint/on_sigterm, the extern "C" { signal, raise } block, terminate_via_default_disposition_of_last_signal, watch_termination_signals, watch_flag, watch_flag_with_second_action). Removing 220 and adding ~45 (shared Arc<AtomicBool>, four registrations, the retained polling thread, a trimmed comment) gives -175, close to their -185, so no inflation on their stated basis (prod code only, no test-coverage credit). Review-history gate: git blame -L 1247,1390 shows 67 lines from 2f49c18 (Phase 2), 73 from f4d4315 (Phase 3) and 4 from 3c49c14 (Phase 1) — ZERO lines from any of the five review-fix commits, and fix-blame.tsv's main.rs ranges skip 1167-1387 entirely. The 'Finding #6/#7' the comments cite are pre-review, in-phase iterations, not Codex findings. The one Codex finding in this area, r1 A14-2 ('Second-signal termination bypasses all child and guard cleanup', citing PARITY.md/main.rs/privilege.rs), is REFUTED — the behaviour is a declared divergence, so preserving it (which the proposal does) is correct. Invariants: PARITY.md:75 declares 'SIGTERM is trapped like SIGINT; a cancelled run exits 130 whichever signal started it' and PARITY.md:87 declares the own-process-group wine child; the proposal preserves both. No golden bytes, contract slug, or shell fingerprint moves. Dependency check: signal-hook itself is NOT in Cargo.lock — only signal-hook-registry 1.4.8 at :3217, pulled by tokio's process feature — so this is a genuinely new (small) tree in the one crate whose manifest carries an explicit no-dependency NOTE (that NOTE is about argument parsing, so it does not forbid this). Also worth telling the owner: the module comment's stated reason for the raw trap ('this crate may not edit Cargo manifests (Frame-owned)') is an obsolete task-time constraint, so the manifest edit is now legitimate. The real cost is testability: the current design deliberately factors the fatal action into watch_flag_with_second_action precisely so a test can substitute a fake instead of raise()ing into the test binary; signal-hook moves that action inside its own handler, so all three second-signal tests (main.rs:2543-2638) lose their seam and must become child-process tests, which will add roughly as many lines as the 159 they replace. That is why the honest net is much smaller than -185 and why this is ACCEPT_WITH_CARE, not ACCEPT.

#### `V1-5` — Use one fault-injecting executor in run-stage tests

T2 · test-cut · **-165 lines** (reviewer claimed -145) · from V1 — stages/run — launch state machine

Define one `#[cfg(test)]` delegating executor with modes such as `DenyWrite(path)`, `CancelChildren(calls)`, and `FailWhenArg(device)`. Three local wrappers currently repeat the entire Executor forwarding surface; one implementation preserves every injected fault and assertion while deleting the repeated boilerplate.

*Files.* `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/run/mod.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/run/guards.rs`

*Evidence.* `DenyWriteTo` and its full Executor implementation occupy `mod.rs:1328-1429`; `CancelChildren` repeats it at `guards.rs:909-998`; `FailSwitchTo` repeats it again at `guards.rs:1105-1210`. In particular, the forwarding bodies at `guards.rs:923-983` and `guards.rs:1130-1189` are structurally identical; only `write_atomic` or `run_child` differs.

*What breaks.* No production behavior or assertion should change. A faulty shared mode could weaken call recording, path-specific denial, or the synthesized nonzero child status, so each constructor must retain its current focused regression test.

*Blast radius.* Only test support and constructor call sites in `mod.rs` and `guards.rs`; confirmed-finding tests remain present. No parity, golden bytes, or contract data are involved.

*Precaution.* Host the shared executor in a `#[cfg(test)] pub(crate)` module visible to both test modules (run::tests items are unreachable from run::guards::tests), and preserve each mode exactly: FailSwitchTo delegates to inner before overriding the exit status so the dry-run plan still records every attempt in order; CancelChildren records spec.program and returns Cancelled without delegating; DenyWriteTo denies only the exact path. Re-run a_normal_exit_survives_a_failed_state_save, a_cancelled_switch_leaves_the_guard_armed_for_the_teardown, both vanished-device tests and a_detach_that_cannot_write_its_record_keeps_the_guards_armed and diff their event/plan assertions.

*How you would know it worked.* Run `a_normal_exit_survives_a_failed_state_save`, `a_cancelled_switch_leaves_the_guard_armed_for_the_teardown`, both vanished-device restoration tests, and the detach save-failure test; their event, state, plan, and error assertions must remain byte-for-byte unchanged.

*Verifier.* Verified all three by reading them: DenyWriteTo is mod.rs:1328-1429 (102 lines), CancelChildren is guards.rs:909-998 (90), FailSwitchTo is guards.rs:1105-1210 (106) — 298 lines, of which the fourteen-method forwarding body is byte-for-byte the same in all three; only write_atomic (path-specific PermissionDenied), run_child (record program then Err(Cancelled)) and run_child (delegate, then synthesize ExitStatus::from_raw(1<<8) when the device is in argv) differ. One delegating executor with a mode enum costs roughly 110-130 lines, so ~-165 net; the -145 claim is conservative, not inflated. No production code, no golden bytes, no contract data, no parity surface is touched, and every fault mode and every call site (mod.rs:1442/1609/1676, guards.rs:1012/1247/1286) keeps its own regression test. Two things to get right. First, visibility: guards.rs's own comment already says 'Same shape as super's DenyWriteTo' precisely because it could not reuse it — `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` inside run/mod.rs is private to run::tests, and run::guards::tests is not a descendant of run::tests, so the shared type has to be hoisted into something both can name (e.g. a `#[cfg(test)] pub(crate) mod` under stages/run/ or next to crate::executor). Second, semantics: FailSwitchTo must keep delegating to the inner executor before overriding the status, or the DryRunExecutor stops recording the attempts that a_recorded_device_that_vanished_falls_back_to_the_built_in_output and an_unrestorable_device_prints_the_remedy_and_leaves_the_guard_pending assert on in order; CancelChildren must keep pushing spec.program into its calls vector and must NOT delegate; DenyWriteTo must deny only the exact path. guards.rs:909-1055 is a review-fix range (the r1 A8-3 / r2 A8-2 cancellation work), so treat the tests around it as untouchable while moving the helper. Note also a fourth copy of FailSwitchTo at session/reconcile.rs:1408 — same shape, another area's file; if the shared helper is hosted crate-wide it can absorb that later, but do not touch reconcile.rs in this change. Viability finally turns on a compile I am not permitted to run (cfg(test) cross-module visibility, Arc<dyn Executor> object safety with the new mode field), which is the other reason for ACCEPT_WITH_CARE.

#### `V3-3` — Use one RAII session fixture and table-drive non-golden cases

T2 · test-cut · **-160 lines** (reviewer claimed -245) · from V3 — session lifecycle + logs

Add a `#[cfg(test)]` fixture in session/mod.rs that owns and cleans a unique scratch directory and provides fixture-scoped Paths, SessionState/LiveSessionHandle builders, observed/dead identities, and the foreign-process guard. Reuse it from the owned modules; leave specialized Executor behavior local. Then table-drive literal-only cases such as reconcile row verbs, audio fallback selection, and basic source resolution. This removes repeated setup/cleanup and literal scaffolding at equal assertion coverage. Every byte/wire assertion and every named confirmed-finding test remains; no assertion is intentionally lost.

*Files.* `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/session/reconcile.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/session/watcher.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/logs.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/session/mod.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/session/state.rs`

*Evidence.* Parallel fixture implementations appear at reconcile.rs:1233-1363 plus its duplicate ForeignProcess at :1747; watcher.rs:906-978; logs.rs:677-683 and :725-733; session/mod.rs:739-753 and :816-824; and state.rs:526-570 plus ForeignProcess at :656. The same explicit cleanup statement appears throughout—e.g. reconcile.rs:1605, watcher.rs:1056, logs.rs:1187, and state.rs:591. Literal-only assertion groups include reconcile.rs:2593-2646 and session/mod.rs:1173-1256.

*What breaks.* Only tests should change, but a shared fixture can introduce cross-test interference if it manipulates LIVE_SESSION/RUN_PHASE without the existing serialization guard, or leak a child if its Drop implementation is incomplete. The specialized executor and global-lock tests must not be generalized away.

*Blast radius.* All five owned test modules; production is unaffected under cfg(test). No parity/golden contract changes, and confirmed-finding tests remain independently named.

*Precaution.* Drop-based cleanup must be best-effort (.ok(), never .unwrap()) and must not fire before a failing assertion has printed its path; keep lock_session_globals() around every test that touches LIVE_SESSION/RUN_PHASE and keep clear_live_session run-id-guarded; land V3-2 first if it lands at all, since both rewrite the same watcher tests.

*How you would know it worked.* Inventory named tests before and after, confirm every existing assertion maps to a fixture method or table row, run under both default scheduling and multiple test threads, and check that no fixture path resolves outside its scratch root.

*Verifier.* I opened every cited fixture. reconcile.rs:1749-1770 and state.rs:658-679 define ForeignProcess character-for-character identically (24 lines each, /bin/sleep 30, kill+wait on Drop) — a clean 24-line dedup. scratch()/state_dir() exists five times (reconcile.rs:1233, watcher.rs:906, logs.rs:677, state.rs:526, session/mod.rs:816) at 8-10 lines each and fixture_paths() three times at ~6 lines each, with the only meaningful difference being that reconcile's and mod.rs's variants add a Uuid to the directory name while the other three rely on a pre-clean remove_dir_all; unifying on the unique-per-call form is strictly safer. I counted remove_dir_all across the five files: 40+20+8+2+8 = 78 occurrences, of which five are the pre-clean inside scratch(), leaving ~73 trailing cleanup lines an RAII Drop absorbs. Against that, the shared module costs ~60-80 lines, so I land at about -160 rather than -245; the table-driving half is the weakest part of the claim. I read both cited literal groups: session/mod.rs:1173-1256 (fallback_output_device) does table-drive cleanly for maybe -15, but reconcile.rs:2593-2646 is nine assertions across four different function signatures (audio_row, audio_fallback_row, dashboard_row, forward_row) plus five constant pins, and forcing that into one table needs a closure column that costs more lines than it saves. Those strings are golden row bytes — the same ones PARITY.md:96 defers to — so they must survive one-for-one whatever shape they end up in. Nothing here is production code and no finding is touched. Note the collision with V3-2: both rewrite the watcher test module, so sequence them.

*Depends on.* `V3-2`

RestoreMode is internal to reconcile.rs:268-330, yet its unused wire spelling is asserted at reconcile.rs:2656-2659.

*What breaks.* Out-of-workspace Rust consumers could depend on the public StopPlan or stamped naming helper, although the workspace has no production references. The retained `wine_log_candidate` output and Reconciled/SessionStatus IPC shapes must not change.

*Blast radius.* Four owned files and their unit tests. Log-name parity is involved, so attempt zero and suffix cases remain pinned; no contract or shell file changes.

*Precaution.* Decide StopPlan explicitly: either wire it into src-tauri/src/commands.rs and ui/src/lib/stores/session.svelte.ts as the 2026-08-30-simplify item intended, or delete it and record in that review file that the consolidation was dropped; and when inlining wine_log_candidate_stamped, move its attempt-0 / -{n+1} byte rule onto wine_log_candidate's doc rather than losing it.

*How you would know it worked.* Use a workspace-wide reference search before deletion, compile every workspace crate, retain the existing parity assertion for `wine_log_candidate`, and confirm the runtime-status fixture still parses with application_name present as an ignored field.

*Verifier.* I grep-verified each of the four items across the whole sabrage tree (excluding target/ and node_modules). (1) StopPlan/stop_plan (session/mod.rs:565-602, 38 lines) appears nowhere but its own definition and stop_plan_decides_from_the_status_alone (mod.rs:1010-1068, ~59 lines) — but docs/reviews/2026-08-30-simplify.md:62 shows why it exists: it is the proposed single answer to 'ui/stores/session.svelte.ts:109, src-tauri/src/commands.rs:783 and reconcile.rs each decide Stop separately', and the core half landed while the two consumers never migrated. Deleting it is legitimate dead-code removal, but it discards the landing point of a documented consolidation. (2) wine_log_candidate_stamped (logs.rs:71-78 plus 13 lines of doc) is called only by wine_log_candidate and by two tests at :767-796; sabrage-parity's own pin (lib.rs:1820) calls wine_log_candidate, and sabrage-parity/Cargo.toml:20 already carries chrono, so the helper's stated raison d'etre — sparing other crates a date/time dependency — is already moot. Inlining is safe as long as attempt-0 byte identity and the -{n+1} suffix stay pinned, which the retained tests at :743-765 and the parity test do. Note the helper predates the review rounds (git log -S puts it in f4d4315, Phase 3) and is not in logs.rs's fix-blame ranges. (3) RuntimeStatus.application_name (watcher.rs:50-51) is read nowhere; RuntimeStatus does not cross IPC at all (no hit in src-tauri or ui/src), and the phase3 fixture keeps the key, so unknown-field tolerance stays proven. (4) RestoreMode's Serialize/Deserialize + rename_all and the one assertion at reconcile.rs:2656-2659 have no TS mirror either. Summing: -98 + -47 + -6 + -6 = -157, essentially the claimed -155. No confirmed finding is touched — the logs.rs findings (A8-3/A8-4/A8-6/A8-7) are all Tailer, and reconcile's RestoreMode *semantics* (Full vs SafeOnly, PARITY.md:103) are untouched by dropping its unused serde derives.

Both of those risks are ultimately compiler- or test-enforced, which is why this is a care rather than a kill, but the one-line replacements under-serve them.

#### `C1-3` — Finish the single session authority instead of deleting its unused StopPlan

T2 · collapse · **-150 lines** (reviewer claimed -750) · from C1 — completeness critic

Session policy is still split among four interpretations: session_block_at decides whether mutation is allowed, SessionMonitor derives the displayed phase, commands.rs chooses stop/detach/quit behavior, and the TypeScript store independently chooses cancelStage versus stopSession. Core already contains StopPlan as the intended common decision, but no consumer calls it; V3-4 proposes deleting that half-landed consolidation. Go the other way: collect observations once, feed a pure reducer, and return SessionStatus plus MutationBlock, StopPlan, and quit/detach eligibility. This subsumes V3-2 and replaces V3-4’s dead-code deletion with completion of the cross-boundary design.

*Files.* `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/session/mod.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/session/watcher.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/session/reconcile.rs`, `sabrage/src-tauri/src/commands.rs`, `sabrage/ui/src/stores/session.svelte.ts`, `sabrage/ui/src/ipc.ts`

*Evidence.* session/mod.rs:305-333 enumerates seven liveness signals, while watcher.rs:323-339 separately defines a seven-level display precedence. session/mod.rs:565-602 defines StopPlan/stop_plan, but symbols.tsv identifies both as having zero references outside their own file. The frontend repeats its key branch at stores/session.svelte.ts:123-155. Tauri independently implements stop targeting and execution at commands.rs:919-995 and quit decisions at :1022-1152.

*What breaks.* A precedence or identity error could enable mutation during an external session, stop the wrong bottle, deadlock against the launch lock, lose an exit code, or mishandle quit/detach. The reducer must distinguish observations from effects; forcing filesystem/process I/O into it would make it less testable, not more.

*Blast radius.* Core session/reconciliation, Tauri lifecycle commands, UI session store, quit dialog, and session wire types. PARITY live-session and detach rows are involved; artifact byte goldens are not.

*How you would know it worked.* Run one exhaustive observation table through all derived outputs, asserting that every state which blocks a backend mutation is also displayed as non-idle and that every StopPlan selects the existing correct effect. Preserve the external/demo.sh, PID-reuse, queued-launch, teardown-timeout, and unanswered-quit regressions.
