Use these exact landmine lines: Output: `/// Child bytes plus their original terminator; see output_events_carry_their_chunk_terminator.` Text: `/// Verbatim print -r -- line, including empty and indented values; see sabrage-parity run-text tests.` Check: `/// Final preflight outcome after any autofix.` Launched: `/// Session identity is pid plus start_time; log_path is the child-owned log.` Replace the step ledger with `/// Stable step ids in shell execution order; order and cardinality are pinned by step_ids_are_unique_and_prefixed_by_their_stage and sabrage-parity.` Delete the per-constant shell-command transcriptions; the constant names and stage arrays remain.

*Files.* `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/events.rs`

*Evidence.* StageEvent documentation occupies 81 comment lines at events.rs:175-415, including a 24-line literal transcription of run.sh at events.rs:260-283. The step-id prose occupies 60 comment lines at events.rs:419-553. Wire shape, empty/indented Text, run ids, and step cardinality are asserted at events.rs:587-755; Progress has a real emitter at sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/build.rs:398, and the CLI exhaustively consumes the variants at sabrage/crates/sabrage-cli/src/main.rs:722-778.

*What breaks.* No enum, serde tag, field, constant, array, or test changes. Generated API docs become terser; wire and ordering details remain executable.

*Blast radius.* events.rs comments only. IPC, JSONL, CLI rendering, UI handling, parity, and golden bytes are explicitly untouched.

*Precaution.* Compress only the StageEvent variant docs; leave the per-constant step-id annotations in events.rs's `step` module intact, and keep the sentence explaining why Output's `end` field is `#[serde(default)]`.

*How you would know it worked.* Require a comment-only diff; compare the retained variant sentences with the serialization tests; later run the events tests and parity suite.

*Verifier.* The two regions are real and measured exactly as claimed: 81 comment lines in 175-415 and 60 in 419-553 (their numbers, and mine). All three named tests exist (events_serialize_internally_tagged_with_camel_case_fields :609, the_run_events_keep_their_wire_shape :635, step_ids_are_unique_and_prefixed_by_their_stage :588), plus output_events_carry_their_chunk_terminator at process.rs:1078. events.rs is the least review-touched file in the set — fix-blame.tsv assigns it only 9 lines (208-213, 219-220, 694), all of them the ChunkEnd `end` field from A14-3. The half of this proposal I would take without hesitation is the StageEvent variant prose: the 24-line ```zsh transcription of run.sh's print statements at 260-283 is a copy of bytes that live in scripts/demo/run.sh and are pinned by sabrage-parity's run-text tests (lib.rs:1881-2258, which read run.sh from disk), so the replacement's pointer is real, and 'one sentence per variant' loses nothing the serialization tests do not already hold. Two caveats. The `#[serde(default)]` rationale at 211-213 ('so a JSONL log or an IPC message from before this field existed still deserializes') is A14-3's compatibility landmine — the proposed Output replacement mentions the terminator but not why the attribute must stay, and no test loads an old JSONL record. More importantly, the step ledger at 428-505 is mischaracterised as 'prose transcription': each constant's one-line doc is the map from a step id to the numbered shell block or `# launch-action:` tag it mirrors (RUN_ADB_FORWARDS -> `launch-action: adb-forward-hygiene`, RUN_WINESERVER -> `wineserver-reset` and its fatal timeout, INSTALL_HOST_MANIFEST -> layer 4, the only privileged write). CLAUDE.md's both-sides-same-commit rule is navigated through exactly that correspondence, and step_ids_are_unique_and_prefixed_by_their_stage checks uniqueness and ordering, not which shell block each id stands for. Cutting the 81 variant lines and leaving the 60 step-id lines alone still lands roughly -70 to -80.

#### `V1-6` — Fold duplicate phase assertions into the existing teardown-arm tests

T2 · test-cut · **-118 lines** (reviewer claimed -135) · from V1 — stages/run — launch state machine

Remove lines at equal assertion coverage: move exit-code/phase assertions into the existing normal test, the Stopping/Idle matrix into the existing cancelled/failed/detached/dry-run arm tests, and the RUN_SUPERVISE attribution into the existing detach test. Keep the focused RunPhaseScope ownership test.

*Files.* `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/run/mod.rs`

*Evidence.* The basic arm tests already execute normal/cancelled/detached/dry-run/failed teardown at `mod.rs:1232-1267` and `mod.rs:2035-2188`. The same paths are executed again solely for phase or step assertions at `mod.rs:2512-2618` and `mod.rs:2620-2652`; nonzero status is also a second normal teardown at `mod.rs:2035-2059`. The distinct scope ownership/finalization behavior remains covered at `mod.rs:2406-2452`.

*What breaks.* No assertion is lost: wine status propagation, `[Stopping, Exited]`, surviving exit code/identity, non-normal Idle outcomes, and detach's RUN_SUPERVISE step are relocated. The tradeoff is slightly less one-test-per-concern failure localization.

*Blast radius.* Only `mod.rs` tests. Production, parity, contract data, golden bytes, and review-pinned teardown semantics are unchanged.

*Precaution.* Re-host the normal-exit test on observing_ctx and assert the (row, phase) pairs in one vector so the emit-time ordering is not silently dropped; keep a nonzero rc case (loop over rc 0 and 3, or reuse rc=7) so `exit $rc` parity stays covered; do not disturb mod.rs:1265's live-session assertion; and give every merged arm test a session::publish_run_phase(None) on entry plus an inner block so the post-Drop `is_none()` check is still meaningful.

*How you would know it worked.* Run the remaining normal, cancelled, failed, detached, dry-run, and RunPhaseScope tests and compare their assertion set with the deleted tests before removal.

*Verifier.* The regions exist exactly as described. a_nonzero_wine_status_is_propagated_verbatim is mod.rs:2035-2059 (+blank = 26 lines), a_normal_teardown_reports_stopping_then_a_surviving_exited_code is 2512-2556 (46), cancelled_failed_detached_and_dry_run_teardowns_end_at_idle is 2558-2618 (62), the_detach_row_belongs_to_the_supervise_step is 2620-2652 (34) — 168 deletable lines, against roughly 50 lines that have to be added back into the five surviving arm tests, so my net is about -118 versus the claimed -135 (14% optimistic, well inside tolerance). Review history is clean here: `git blame -L 2495,2693` returns 199/199 lines from f4d4315 (Phase 3), not from any of the five review-fix commits, and fix-blame.tsv's mod.rs fix ranges (1506-2034, 2207, 2241, 2305, 2355) do not cover 2035-2059 or 2512-2652; no finding in findings-index.tsv is anchored to these phase tests. Nothing here is parity, contract, golden-byte or fixture surface — tests only. Assertion-preservation (Rule T) is the real question and it is satisfiable but not for free. Three of the four folds are clean: the RUN_SUPERVISE step assertion drops straight into detaching_marks_the_state_leaves_the_guards_and_keeps_the_file (same dry_ctx, same `seen`); the rc=3 propagation is already redundant with the rc=7 assertion in the phase test; and the matrix's in-scope `session::run_phase()` reads need no sink change because every arm test already builds its own RunPhaseScope. The fourth is not mechanical: `phases(&log) == [Stopping, Exited]` is an emit-time pairing (the blank line belongs to Stopping, the status line to Exited) that dry_ctx's sink cannot observe, so a_normal_exit_prints_the_blank_line_then_the_status_and_clears_the_state has to be re-hosted on observing_ctx and assert the (text, phase) pairs together — which is why I rate this T2 rather than the proposed T1. Two further traps: mod.rs:1265 (`assert!(!session::live_session_is(ctx.run_id))`) inside the merge target IS a review-fix line and must survive the rewrite; and the arm tests currently never call session::publish_run_phase(None), so the relocated `run_phase().is_none()` / `== None` assertions will read a leftover Exited from an earlier test under the same global lock unless each merged test resets the slot on entry and clears it on exit, exactly as the deleted matrix test did. observing_ctx/phases() stay alive either way (run_publishes_preflight_and_clears_it_when_the_preflight_fails still uses both), and the kept the_scope_publishes_identity_and_drop_clears_only_its_own_run already pins '#7 the code is carried' at unit level, which is what makes the teardown-level duplicate genuinely redundant.

#### `V5-6` — Extract runtime-config patch/write test harnesses

T2 · test-cut · **-110 lines** (reviewer claimed -125) · from V5 — config editor + app store

Add `PatchCase` helpers for input/patch/expected bytes/changed keys and a cleanup-owning `WriteFixture` containing config path, backup path, and executor. Keep the frozen deployed fixture and every byte-golden/finding-specific named test; replace repeated scratch creation, template writes, reads, backup listing, and cleanup with helper calls. Convert repeated `effective_string`, `effective_accepted`, enum, and insertion literals into labeled case tables. This is lines removed at equal assertion coverage; assertions lost: none.

*Files.* `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/config/runtime_toml.rs`

*Evidence.* Pure patch cases repeat the same apply/expected-bytes/changed-key shape throughout `runtime_toml.rs:1778-2183`; runtime-reader literal matrices occupy `runtime_toml.rs:2353-2397` and `runtime_toml.rs:2592-2694`. Disk-write tests repeatedly create the same dir/path/backups/executor and clean it up at `runtime_toml.rs:2729-3219`, while fix tests repeat context/file setup at `runtime_toml.rs:3423-3528`.

*What breaks.* No intended assertion or behavior loss. Over-generalized expected-output helpers could hide a wrong expected value, so golden expected bytes must remain explicit literals/fixture comparisons and confirmed-finding test names must not be collapsed away.

*Blast radius.* Test module only in `runtime_toml.rs`; production editor code, the frozen deployed fixture, parity, and golden assertions remain.

*Precaution.* Land as a standalone commit with an explicit before/after assertion ledger. Keep the scratch layout flat (config `<dir>/oxrsys-runtime.toml`, `<dir>/backups`, `session-state.json` and `runtime_status.json` beside them) and provide a second `nested()` constructor for the two `OXRSys/...` create tests rather than normalising all of them. Keep Drop cleanup as `let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(...)` so the 0o555 and directory-backup fixtures cannot make teardown panic. Preserve every finding-named test function name and every per-case rationale message as a labeled table row, keep expected bytes as explicit literals or fixture comparisons, and keep the `effective_string` contrast assertion inside the effective_accepted coverage. Do not touch the `fix_ctx` scratch injection or its doc comment. Verify with `cargo test -p sabrage-core config::runtime_toml` and confirm the test count is unchanged.

*How you would know it worked.* Produce an assertion-coverage ledger mapping every old assertion to a helper assertion or labeled case. Run the complete runtime_toml tests and confirm all original golden fixtures and finding-specific test names remain discoverable.

*Verifier.* I measured the file directly (3584 lines; test module starts at 1705). The boilerplate the proposal targets is real and countable: below line 2190 there are 31 `let dir = scratch("...")` sites, 22 `let path = dir.join(...)`, 14 `let backups = dir.join("backups")`, 13 `let ex = real()/dry()` and 32 `let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir)` lines - 112 lines of pure setup/teardown. A `WriteFixture` owning dir/path/backups with a Drop cleanup removes the path, backups and cleanup lines (68) at a cost of ~16 lines of struct+ctor+Drop, netting about -52; it cannot absorb `real()`/`dry()` cleanly because `a_dry_run_plans_the_write_and_touches_nothing` and `the_replacement_refuses_when_the_file_changed_underneath` need the executor back afterwards for `ex.planned()`. Separately there are 18 single-field `RuntimeConfigPatch { .. }` literals in tests (4 lines each) beyond the existing `patch_bitrate` helper; adding `patch_protocol`/`patch_scale`/`patch_refresh` in the same shape nets about -36. The `effective_string`/`effective_accepted` blocks at 2592-2694 are ~13 five-line assert_eq forms that table-drive to roughly -20 net. The four `edit_protocol` fix tests at 3441-3528 already share `fix_ctx`, so a scratch+ctx+mkdir fixture there is only about -8. Total ~-116, and I am reporting -110 to stay conservative; the claim is not inflated. One cited region is mis-described: 2353-2397 (`the_line_reader_agrees_with_parse_config_toml`) is ALREADY a labeled case table, so there is nothing to convert there - it is the pattern, not the target. Review history: fix-blame.tsv shows runtime_toml.rs carries 1681 review-fix lines, and the test ranges 2292-2724, 2752-2859, 3023-3119 and 3223-3420 are all inside them, i.e. nearly everything this proposal rewrites is regression coverage for CONFIRMED findings A10-1 (create_new/TOCTOU, backup reservation race, cross-process lock, CAS), A10-2 (prune only after commit), A10-4/A10-8 (live-session guard), A10-5/A10-6 (BOM/CRLF/EOF preservation), A10-7 (float precision) and A3b-1/A7-1 (last-valid-wins in effective_accepted). None of those fixes is reverted or weakened by a harness extraction, so `kills` is empty and the Rule T bar (collapse forms, never drop a case) is satisfiable - but only if executed literally, which is why this is ACCEPT_WITH_CARE rather than ACCEPT. The concrete hazards I found by reading: (1) the scratch directory layout is semantic, not incidental - `write_refuses_while_a_session_is_live_and_touches_nothing` comments that the session record lands beside `backups`, and `write_refuses_while_only_the_runtime_reports_a_live_session` puts `runtime_status.json` next to the config, so a fixture that nests the config under `OXRSys/` for tidiness changes what those A10-4/A10-8 tests exercise; (2) `write_takes_the_cross_process_lock_at_the_documented_path` derives `.oxrsys-runtime.toml.lock` from the config filename and asserts a >=1800ms wait, so the filename must stay `oxrsys-runtime.toml` while `write_creates_from_the_template_byte_identically_then_patches` and `a_dry_run_plans_the_create_from_template` deliberately use the nested `OXRSys/oxrsys-runtime.toml` shape - the fixture needs two constructors, not one; (3) `a_failed_write_prunes_nothing_and_leaves_no_reservation` chmods its scratch dir 0o555 and has a root early-return with its own cleanup, and `an_unprunable_stale_backup_still_reports_a_committed_save` turns a backup into a directory, so Drop cleanup must stay `let _ = remove_dir_all` and must not assume files; (4) moving cleanup into Drop means a panicking test no longer leaves its tree behind for postmortem - a small debugging regression worth accepting knowingly; (5) `effective_accepted_keeps_the_last_value_the_runtime_would_accept` deliberately interleaves one `effective_string` contrast assertion (the A3b-1/A7-1 'which is exactly where the raw reader differs' pair), so a single-function table would drop it unless the row carries a function selector or that pair stays outside the table; (6) the `fix_ctx` doc comment ('a Catch2 suite once clobbered the user's live config') and the scratch-injection shape are the pattern round-1 finding A4-10 told other modules to copy - the fixture must keep Paths pointed at scratch so no real Application Support path is ever reachable. Invariants: nothing here touches PARITY.md (the config-reader divergence at PARITY.md:119 is about production readers, not tests), the host XR JSON, the toml template, the cxbottle line, steam_appid.txt, the .sha256 marker, the launch banner/argv, win_path, registry order, contract slug cardinality or the shell fingerprint; nothing under contract/, scripts/, demo.sh, docs/ or ext/ is required; the frozen fixture tests/fixtures/phase4/oxrsys-runtime.deployed.toml is read, not changed. Note also that V5-3 proposes comment cuts in the same file including test-region comments at 2754-2859, 2938-3119 and 3223-3426, which overlap this proposal's regions - the two will conflict textually even though they do not conflict semantically. Finally, per the task's hard rules I did not build or run the suite, so the assertion-preserving claim is verified by reading only; the refactor's safety ultimately turns on `cargo test -p sabrage-core` staying green with the same assertion count.

Parity is pinned by contract::tests::templates_are_the_bytes_the_shell_writes and checks::tests::registry_binds_in_contract_order_and_covers_every_slug; deliberate divergences are in sabrage/PARITY.md." These replacements retain the landmine pointers and remove phase chronology, module inventories, and transcribed behavior already enforced by tests.

*Verifier.* Measured: lib.rs 1-64 → ~2 lines = -62; store/mod.rs 1-28 → ~3 = -25; config/mod.rs 1-15 → ~2 = -13; total ~-100 against a claimed -104, honest. All six named tests exist exactly as cited: contract.rs:412 templates_are_the_bytes_the_shell_writes, checks/mod.rs:616 registry_binds_in_contract_order_and_covers_every_slug, settings.rs:263 a_corrupt_file_is_an_error_never_a_silent_reset, settings.rs:351 a_newer_version_is_refused_and_its_bytes_left_alone, library.rs:680 a_newer_schema_version_is_refused_not_silently_rewritten, library.rs:745/settings.rs:483 a_dry_run_executor_plans_the_write_instead_of_performing_it, runtime_toml.rs:1788 the_shared_template_round_trips_unchanged. The bulk of what goes is genuinely inert: lib.rs 21-64 is phase chronology plus a module inventory that duplicates the `pub mod` list ten lines below it, and config/mod.rs 7-12 restates runtime_toml.rs's own header (which it explicitly defers to, and whose lines 16-64 are themselves review-fix territory and stay). Where Rule C bites is the parts the replacement text silently drops. lib.rs:11-15 carries 'install.sh does literal string equality on the manifest, so a single differing byte makes the two front-ends thrash each other with sudo prompts' — that is precisely the rationale that stops the next editor reformatting the host XR JSON; templates_are_the_bytes_the_shell_writes enforces the bytes but does not explain the consequence. lib.rs:17-19 carries 'check message and remedy strings still track scripts/demo/doctor.sh verbatim, because docs/troubleshooting.md quotes them' — the reason nobody may improve a doctor string — and neither named test is about message text. store/mod.rs:25-28 is a review-fix range (fix-blame gives 18-22, 25, 27-28) and states the Goldberg divergence: it 'refuses rather than claim to have restored an original it cannot authenticate' — the rationale record for A13a-1, which round 2 still lists as [unfixed] at library.rs:515-521, and the proposed store/mod.rs replacement does not mention goldberg at all. store/mod.rs:18-22 is also fix-authored (the S-A13a merge-home wording) and disappears here regardless of whether H2-2 lands. Behaviourally nothing changes; the risk is purely the loss of the constraints, which is fixable by amending the replacement rather than dropping the proposal.

#### `V5-5` — Share store test fixtures while retaining every finding assertion

T2 · test-cut · **-90 lines** (reviewer claimed -200) · from V5 — config editor + app store

Introduce area-local test helpers in `store/mod.rs`: a unique scratch directory with cleanup, `HealthyGameFixture` for bottle/exe/version/DLL setup, and `GoldbergFixture` for live DLL/backup/payload/marker states. Keep separate named tests for every confirmed finding, but express their setup as fixture mutations; merge the standalone success-message check into the ordinary successful-restore test. Table-drive the small settings deserialization/default cases. This is lines removed at equal assertion coverage; assertions lost: none.

*Files.* `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/store/mod.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/store/settings.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/store/library.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/store/goldberg.rs`

*Evidence.* Library validation tests repeatedly build the same bottle/config/exe/version tree at `store/library.rs:1144-1255` and `store/library.rs:1348-1377`. Goldberg tests repeat plugin directory, live DLL, backup, payload, executor, and cleanup setup from `store/goldberg.rs:319-734`; `the_success_message_never_claims_the_original_was_restored` at `store/goldberg.rs:593-617` duplicates the successful restore fixture at `store/goldberg.rs:341-374`. Settings repeats scratch/write/load/cleanup cases at `store/settings.rs:272-449`.

*What breaks.* Production behavior and assertions should not change. A flawed shared fixture could correlate failures or accidentally consult real application-support paths, so the existing scratch-path guarantees and finding-specific test names must remain explicit.

*Blast radius.* Only `#[cfg(test)]` code in the four store files. Confirmed-finding coverage remains individually named; no parity or golden-byte tests are removed.

*Precaution.* Keep `the_success_message_never_claims_the_original_was_restored` as its own named test rather than merging it; keep per-test-unique scratch paths and test_paths()'s sabrage_appsup+oxr_appsup redirection inside any shared fixture; and inventory assertions one-to-one before and after. Sequence it after (or instead of) V5-1/V5-2, which delete the same tests.

*How you would know it worked.* Inventory assertions before and after and require a one-to-one mapping, including all A13 finding tests. Run store/core tests concurrently to prove unique path isolation and confirm no real application-support path is accessed.

*Verifier.* I read the cited test regions. The repetition is genuine: library.rs's validate tests rebuild fake_bottle + a five-line cxbottle.conf write + Beat Saber.exe + BeatSaberVersion.txt + steam_api64.dll in six tests (25 fixture writes, 7 fake_bottle calls, 10 create_dir_all, 21 remove_dir_all across the module), and goldberg.rs repeats plugin_dir + create_dir_all + live-dll write + backup write in thirteen tests (18 create_dir_all, 13 remove_dir_all). But goldberg.rs already has most of the helpers the proposal wants — scratch(), real(), plugin_dir(), test_paths(), paths_with_payload(), revert(), UNMATCHABLE_PIN, NO_GAME_NEEDLE — so what a GoldbergFixture actually absorbs is 2-3 setup lines plus one cleanup line per test (~48 lines) against ~35 lines of new fixture code, net ~-13 there. library.rs's HealthyGameFixture is the best case at roughly -40 net; settings.rs is already lean (2-3 boilerplate lines per test) and table-driving the_current_version_still_loads / a_file_without_a_version_reads_as_the_current_one saves ~3, so ~-5 net. Summed that is about -90, not -200 — an inflation of well over 40%, which the owner should know before budgeting it. Scope-wise this is #[cfg(test)] only in four files; no production code, no golden bytes, no parity artifact, no contract slug, and the frozen phase4 fixture is in a different crate path entirely, so nothing in the invariant list is touched. Rule T: the fixture-mutation rewrite preserves assertions, and the proposal correctly commits to keeping every confirmed-finding test separately named — except for one step that contradicts that commitment. `the_success_message_never_claims_the_original_was_restored` (goldberg.rs:593-617) is not a duplicate of `a_present_backup_is_restored_and_left_in_place`; it is the only place asserting `!report.message.contains("original")`, which is the A13b-7 / A13a-1 invariant that Sabrage never claims to have restored something it cannot authenticate. Folding it into the ordinary restore test keeps the assertion but destroys the name that documents why it exists, and a future editor rewording the success message would then have to reason about it from a bare contains() inside an unrelated test. Two more precautions: the shared scratch helper must keep per-test-name uniqueness (today's scratch(name) keys on name + process id, so a single shared name would make concurrent tests in one binary collide), and it must keep test_paths()'s redirection of both sabrage_appsup and oxr_appsup into the scratch tree or the liveness predicate starts consulting the developer's real ~/Library/Application Support session records. Finally, note this proposal is mostly moot if V5-1 or V5-2 lands, since those delete the very tests it refactors.

#### `H6-3` — Return reconciliation rows instead of serializing the recovery graph to the UI

T2 · collapse · **-86 lines** (reviewer claimed -80) · from H6 — type and wire-surface sprawl

Change the Tauri reconcile command to return Vec<String>, and let the session store keep those rows directly. Delete the UI-only ReconcileReport, Reconciled, SessionState, ProcInfo, GuardFlags, and WiredForward declarations plus the Rust ReconcileReport wrapper. Keep every core recovery type, serializer, fixture, and reconcile test: the simplification is at the GUI boundary, not inside crash recovery.

*Files.* `sabrage/src-tauri/src/commands.rs`, `sabrage/ui/src/ipc.ts`, `sabrage/ui/src/stores/session.svelte.ts`

*Evidence.* commands.rs:1360 defines ReconcileReport as only kind plus rows, and commands.rs:1393 constructs it. ipc.ts:622 starts the six-type recovery graph and ipc.ts:681 wraps it for the command. session.svelte.ts:180 explicitly says report.kind is intentionally not retained because no consumer has read it, while session.svelte.ts:189 stores only rows. Session.svelte:105 awaits reconciliation without inspecting the return value, and Session.svelte:222 renders reconcileRows. The removable regions are approximately 65 TS lines, 14 Rust wrapper/construction lines, and a few store adapter lines.

*What breaks.* Any out-of-tree IPC client that consumes the reconciliation classification or nested process/session state loses those fields. The in-tree UI loses nothing because it already discards them. Core recovery decisions, persisted session state, and all A9-era recovery coverage remain intact.

*Blast radius.* One Tauri command response and its UI wrapper/store. Core session/reconcile.rs, the persisted schema, frozen fixtures, review-pinned tests, and shell parity are untouched. The TS portion overlaps H6-1's standalone code-generation estimate.

*Precaution.* Keep core's Reconciled/SessionState graph and reconcile.rs:2651's camelCase serialization test untouched, and keep the command taking acquire_operation_lock, awaiting reconcile(&ctx) and propagating its error — only the response shape changes, never the side effects.

*How you would know it worked.* Keep the existing core serialization and reconciliation tests unchanged, including the_reconcile_types_serialize_camel_case. Add no replacement taxonomy test; instead verify the command returns the same ordered rows and the Session screen renders them. Run Cargo and UI checks during implementation.

*Verifier.* I verified every consumer. commands.rs:1360-1373 is ReconcileReport (doc 1360-1367, struct 1370-1373; the proposal's :1393 is the fn signature, not the constructor, which is at :1414 — a line-number slip, not a missing region). ipc.ts:622-687 is the six-type graph plus its wrapper, and `git grep` shows WiredForward, GuardFlags, ProcInfo, SessionState, Reconciled and ReconcileReport appear in the UI only in ipc.ts itself plus one `type ReconcileReport` import in stores/session.svelte.ts:24 — no screen, component or store reads a single field of them. session.svelte.ts:180-181 says so in the code: "`report.kind` is intentionally not kept — no consumer has ever read it; only `rows` (the banner text) is", and those exact lines are attributed to a review-fix commit in fix-blame (session.svelte.ts ranges 174, 181-182), so the store half of this decision is already landed policy rather than an accident. Session.svelte:105 awaits sessionStore.reconcile and discards the value; Session.svelte:220-227 builds its banner from reconcileRows plus status.phase/status.ownedByThisProcess only. Counting the lines myself: ipc.ts 622-687 inclusive is 66 lines, commands.rs 1360-1374 is 15 plus the now-unused `use sabrage_core::session::reconcile::Reconciled;` at commands.rs:70, plus roughly 3 lines of store/import adaptation — about -86, so if anything the reviewer under-claimed. Against the review history: the twelve CONFIRMED findings citing reconcile.rs (A9-1, A9-8 and friends) are all about the CORE run path refusing Reconciled::Busy before mutating, and ipc.ts:669-677 (the review-fix lines that added `pending` and `busy` to the TS mirror) exist to keep the hand-mirror in sync, not because a consumer appeared — deleting the mirror removes the sync obligation rather than weakening the refusal, which lives in stages/run/mod.rs. No golden byte, contract slug, fixture, or PARITY.md entry is involved. Two things must not be collateral damage, hence the care rating: the core Reconciled/SessionState/ProcInfo graph and reconcile.rs:2651's the_reconcile_types_serialize_camel_case must stay (SessionState's Serialize is load-bearing for session-state.json regardless), and the command body must keep `acquire_operation_lock`, keep awaiting `reconcile(&ctx)`, and keep `.map_err(...)?` — the doc comment at commands.rs:1376-1392 explains that the lock is what stops a Session-screen remount from tearing down a launch in flight. Also note Session.svelte:216 points at a "NEEDS FROM note" that exists nowhere in the tree (I grepped the whole repo); that dangling pointer is the only hint anyone ever wanted a richer signal, and it should be resolved or deleted in the same change.

#### `V7-3` — Consolidate Tauri boundary policy tests and delete derive tests

T1 · test-cut · **-80 lines** (reviewer claimed -125) · from V7 — CLI + Tauri command layer

Table-drive settings precedence, launch-flag precedence, teardown outcomes, quit decisions, registry operations, and repo-root-source classification while retaining every assertion that pins a confirmed finding. Delete two tautological tests: `app_state_carries_the_new_default_bottle_and_bs_dir_fields` merely reads fields just assigned to a struct, and `repo_root_source_variants_are_pairwise_distinct` merely retests derived `PartialEq`. Lines removed by table-driving retain equal assertion coverage; the only assertions intentionally lost are that assigned AppState fields can be read back and that four enum variants compare unequal under a derived equality implementation. Leave the Last Session tests to V7-4 so the estimates do not overlap.

*Files.* `sabrage/src-tauri/src/commands.rs`

*Evidence.* Settings and environment precedence occupy commands.rs:2323-2436; teardown, deferred-fix, quit, tail, broadcast, dry-run, and registry policies occupy commands.rs:2438-2775 with repeated setup. Repo-source classification spells out six independent calls at commands.rs:2812-2842. The tautological AppState and enum tests are commands.rs:2799-2810 and commands.rs:2844-2857. Confirmed-finding tests such as the teardown deadline at commands.rs:2453-2475 and deferred-fix doors at commands.rs:2506-2551 remain as named table cases.

*What breaks.* Only the two named derive/field-read assertions disappear; all behavioral and review-finding assertions remain. As with V7-2, consolidated failures depend on clear per-case labels.

*Blast radius.* The commands.rs test module only. No contract/parity golden, frozen fixture, or production command changes; tests around confirmed A11/A12 findings remain represented.

*Precaution.* Scope it to the two deletions plus classify_repo_root_source and quit_intercept_decision; keep every finding-id comment (A11-1, A4-2/A12-1, A11-4, A11-5, E-A11) and do not rename the six tests that doc comments point at — wait_for_slot_clear_reports_the_deadline_it_hit, the_stop_and_quit_refusal_claims_only_what_happened, a_withheld_fix_reaches_no_doctor_row_and_no_fix_call, a_tail_unregisters_itself_when_its_task_ends, stop_all_stops_every_tracked_tail, a_dry_run_emits_the_shared_plan_rows_and_a_real_run_emits_none.

*How you would know it worked.* Build a before/after assertion inventory, run `cargo test -p sabrage-app`, and confirm each finding-pinned test condition remains present. Review the table failure messages to ensure they identify the exact policy case.

*Verifier.* The two deletions check out and are clean: `app_state_carries_the_new_default_bottle_and_bs_dir_fields` (2799-2810, 12 lines) only reads back fields it just assigned, and `get_app_state` already pins AppState's shape at compile time; `repo_root_source_variants_are_pairwise_distinct` (2844-2857, 14 lines) retests `#[derive(PartialEq)]` — I confirmed the derive at commands.rs:1885. Neither line range appears in fix-blame.tsv, so neither is review-fix-authored. That is -28 with blanks. The table-driving claim is where the estimate inflates. `classify_repo_root_source` (2812-2842) is six independent calls and tables to about -15; `quit_intercept_decision` (2554-2584) is five cases and tables to about -11. But the block the proposal leans on for its bulk, 2438-2775, is twelve tests over eleven distinct functions — stop_targets_live_session, wait_for_slot_clear, quit_stop_refusal, offered_fix_id/gui_fix_refusal, quit_intercept_decision, PendingQuit, TailRegistry::register/stop/stop_all, SessionMonitorState::remember_broadcast, should_intercept_quit, emit_dry_run_plan, contract slug groups, RunRegistry::cancel/forget. Different signatures cannot share a case table, and each test's cases are already 2-5 lines. Realistic yield there is about -30 of micro-tabling, giving ≈ -80 measured against -125 claimed (~56% over, worth flagging). The settings/launch precedence block (2323-2436) is also less tractable than described: two of its three tests do unsafe process-global env var save/set/restore under WINEVR_BOTTLE_MUTEX, and that scaffolding is per-test, not per-case. On Rule T no assertion has to be lost, and the proposal is right that the confirmed-finding tests stay. My concern is the anchors: fix-blame marks 2453-2671 as review-fix territory, each of those tests opens with the finding id it pins (A11-1, A11-1 round 2, A4-2/A12-1, A11-4, A11-5, /simplify E-A11), and six of them are named as pointers by V7-1's replacement comments. Renaming or merging them silently breaks that chain. No golden byte, contract slug, frozen fixture or PARITY invariant is touched, and this is test-module-only.

#### `H2-3` — Delete the genuinely dead and test-only API tail

T1 · delete · **-77 lines** (reviewer claimed -90) · from H2 — YAGNI and unused surface

Delete Contract::checks_by_slug and its sole BTreeMap import; process::terminate_and_wait; unused isActivePhase and PHASE_TONE exports; AppState.repoRoot and its resolution work; LaunchAction.what; CheckReport.committed; and the Branch enum returned only to tests by rewrite_graphics_backend. Make that rewrite return String while retaining every exact-output, malformed-line, and newline assertion. This removes lines at equal behavioral assertion coverage; the only assertion lost is the internal Branch::{Rewrote,InsertedAfterEnvSection,AppendedSection} tag identity already implied by each expected byte string.

*Files.* `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/contract.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/process.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/fixes/backend.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-contract-gen/src/lib.rs`, `sabrage/src-tauri/src/commands.rs`, `sabrage/ui/src/ipc.ts`

*Evidence.* sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/contract.rs:230 defines checks_by_slug with zero references and sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/contract.rs:31 imports BTreeMap solely for it; sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/process.rs:919 defines terminate_and_wait with zero references; sabrage/ui/src/ipc.ts:474 and sabrage/ui/src/ipc.ts:497 define exports with no consumers; sabrage/src-tauri/src/commands.rs:156 serializes repo_root although no frontend consumer reads AppState.repoRoot; sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/contract.rs:191 declares LaunchAction.what with no reads; sabrage/crates/sabrage-contract-gen/src/lib.rs:329 stores committed although callers use only in_sync/generated; sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/fixes/backend.rs:45 defines Branch, while production discards it at sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/fixes/backend.rs:312 and tests already assert exact output beginning at sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/fixes/backend.rs:367.

*What breaks.* Repository-external Rust callers, if any exist, would lose several public members, and AppState's serialized shape would lose repoRoot. Within Sabrage, only internal branch-tag assertions disappear; byte-level rewrite behavior and all live consumers remain.

*Blast radius.* Six files across contract parsing, process helpers, one fix, contract generation, Tauri DTOs, and TypeScript IPC. Existing parity/golden assertions remain byte-for-byte; no contract or shell file changes.

*Precaution.* Land it as one change and confirm it compiles: `rewrite_graphics_backend` goes from `(String, Branch)` to `String`, so its three return sites and eight test call sites all change together; keep every `assert_eq!(out, …)` and `matches_doctor_anchor` assertion verbatim, and delete an `assert_eq!(branch, …)` only where the same test's byte assertion is present.

*How you would know it worked.* Require zero references to the deleted symbols, run cargo check for the affected crates and frontend type checking, then run the existing backend rewrite tests and contract-generation golden tests without weakening their byte assertions.

*Verifier.* I checked each item by grep over `git ls-files sabrage contract`. (1) Contract::checks_by_slug (contract.rs:230-233) has zero callers and BTreeMap is imported at :31 for it alone — both go, ~6 lines. (2) process::terminate_and_wait (process.rs:917-932) has zero callers anywhere including sabrage-cli and src-tauri, ~18 lines; process.rs fix ranges jump 861-889 → 967-1018, so it is not fix-derived. (3) ipc.ts isActivePhase (:471-476) and PHASE_TONE (:494-508) have zero consumers, ~21 lines. (4) AppState.repo_root (commands.rs:156, written at :273-275, mirrored at ipc.ts:39) is never read by any component — Settings.svelte reads settings.repoRoot and repoInfo.repoRoot, different types — ~6 lines plus a one-line edit to the app_state_carries_the_new_default_bottle_and_bs_dir_fields test, which asserts nothing about it. (5) LaunchAction.what (contract.rs:190-191): only `.id` is ever read (actions.rs:943, parity 1010/1399), there is no deny_unknown_fields anywhere in the workspace (the simplify report states this explicitly at :228), and contract-gen parses pipeline.toml through its own structs, so the generated contract.gen.sh is unaffected — 2 lines. (6) CheckReport.committed (contract-gen:328-330): `check()` still needs the local to compute in_sync; only the field, its doc and its initialiser go — 3 lines. (7) fixes::backend::Branch (45-57) is asserted in 8 tests; I read all of 361-470 and in every case the accompanying byte-exact `assert_eq!(out, …)` uniquely determines which branch ran (including the malformed-key case, where any other branch would have produced different bytes), so the branch identity really is implied — ~21 lines counting the 8 asserts. Production already discards it (`let (rewritten, _branch)` at :312), and the postcondition guard that a review fix added at 313-332 is untouched. Total ~-77 vs a claimed -90: inflated ~17%, inside tolerance. Review-history exposure: only the ipc.ts pair sits in a fix range (458-507), and `git log -S` pins both to 73b790d (review-r1 waves 2-3), i.e. the simplify finding about five hand-written phase sets and two duplicate phase→severity tables. PHASE_TONE was the intended single home for that severity token and was never wired up — Sidebar.svelte:25's PHASE_DOT and Session.svelte:325's PHASE_CLASS are both still there. Deleting PHASE_TONE therefore abandons an intended consolidation, but loses no tripwire: both surviving tables are exhaustive `Record<SessionStatus["phase"], string>`, so a ninth phase is still a type error. Invariants: no golden bytes move (the cxbottle-line output assertions all stay), no contract slug cardinality change, nothing under contract/, scripts/, demo.sh or ext/.

#### `V2-6` — Reuse the install test executor for setup's create-new race

T2 · extract · **-75 lines** (reviewer claimed -95) · from V2 — stages setup/build/install/stop + fixes

Move install's rich delegating TestExecutor to a pub(super), cfg(test) helper within install.rs and add one optional create_new hook. Setup's A5-3 test can configure that hook instead of implementing the entire Executor trait again. Preserve all install-specific knobs and the setup test's deterministic competing-writer behavior.

*Files.* `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/install.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/setup.rs`

*Evidence.* install.rs:676-905 defines a 230-line pass-through executor with operation hooks. setup.rs:666-778 defines another 113-line executor whose only special behavior is create_new at setup.rs:704-713; every other method forwards to inner. The protected race assertion itself remains at setup.rs:780-823.

*What breaks.* No production behavior and no assertion should change. Risk is test-only sibling visibility and preserving with_step propagation of the new hook; a missed propagation would make the A5-3 race test stop exercising the intended branch.

*Blast radius.* Two cfg(test) sections. The write-once config race, manifest bytes, TCC classification, interrupted-backup cleanup, cancellation, and dry-run assertions all remain.

*Precaution.* Hoist TestExecutor out of `mod tests` into install.rs's module body as `#[cfg(test)] pub(super) struct` (a pub(super) inside `mod tests` is not reachable from setup); add an `around(inner: Arc<dyn Executor>)` constructor and have `dry_run` delegate to it; add the create_new hook to the field list in `with`, `with_step` AND both constructors; rewrite the struct's 'behaves as it does under a plain dry run' doc, which stops being true once it wraps a real executor; then run `cargo test -p sabrage-core stages::setup stages::install` and confirm a_config_created_by_another_writer_is_reported_not_replaced still ends with the competing writer's exact bytes.

*How you would know it worked.* Run the install and setup test modules and confirm the setup race still leaves the competing writer's exact bytes while every install executor knob continues to work through with_step.

*Verifier.* Both regions exist exactly as described. install.rs's TestExecutor spans 676-905 (struct 687, impl Executor 752, run_child ends 905) and is a faithful delegating pass-through with five knobs; setup.rs's LosesTheCreateRace spans 666-778 (113 lines) and its only non-forwarding method is create_new at 704-713. The protected assertion is the A5-3 test at 780-823, which the proposal preserves intact. Measured: deleting 666-778 (-113), adding a hook field+doc (4), copies in `with` and `with_step` (2), an `around(inner)` constructor (~12, less if `dry_run` is refactored to call it), a create_new method (~14), and ~8 lines of call-site wiring in setup gives roughly -75, i.e. the claim is inflated by ~25% — under the reject threshold but worth correcting. Three things the proposal gets right or nearly right. (1) Visibility: `pub(super)` on a #[cfg(test)] item hoisted to install.rs's module body IS visible from stages::setup (parent `stages` plus descendants), so the plan compiles in principle — but only if the struct leaves `mod tests`; a `pub(super)` inside install's private `mod tests` would NOT be reachable from setup, and the proposal's wording is ambiguous there. (2) The named risk (missed with_step propagation silently disarming the race branch) is actually a LOUD failure, not a silent one: Executor::create_new has a default body (executor.rs:265-273) that checks path.exists() then write_atomic, so an unpropagated hook writes the template and the assert_eq! against `hand_edited` fails outright. (3) That same default body is the real trap to watch: the shared TestExecutor does not currently override create_new at all, so with a real inner it would bypass the real O_EXCL create_new_real — harmless while the hook is always Some in the only new caller, but the struct's doc ('Everything else delegates, so run() behaves as it does under a plain dry run') becomes false once it can wrap a real executor and must be updated. Review history: setup.rs 666-823 is 100% review-fix mass (fix-blame.tsv row for setup.rs lists 666-823), pinning A5-3 (write-once config race — the only file Sabrage creates for the user); install.rs 691-706/715-718/722-724/727-739/758-761/766/808-816/836-859/900-902 are review-fix lines inside TestExecutor's knobs. The proposal moves rather than deletes all of it and keeps every assertion, so it does not revert A5-3 or any install finding. Invariants: no production code, no golden bytes, no contract slug, no PARITY.md row is touched (PARITY.md cites no .rs paths at all); the frozen phase4 fixture is untouched. The one thing I cannot do under the no-build rule is confirm the cross-module cfg(test) visibility and Arc<dyn Executor> lifetimes actually compile, which is why this is ACCEPT_WITH_CARE rather than ACCEPT.

#### `V4-5` — Generate only the trivial evaluator wrappers and share pure probe helpers

T2 · collapse · **-75 lines** · from V4 — checks + contract + generator

Do not build a runtime check DSL. Add one compile-time `simple_checks!` macro that emits a wrapper function plus its `(slug, Evaluator)` binding from a single declaration, and use it only for the 18 literal wrappers already delegated to `built_output`, `overlay_check`, `submodule_check`, or `tool_check`. Also hoist the identical `first_connected_serial`, `is_executable`, and `basename` helpers to `checks/mod.rs`. Exceptional evaluators—config, hashes, version semantics, registry parsing, adb failures, and run-only deadlines—stay ordinary Rust functions.

*Files.* `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/checks/mod.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/checks/build.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/checks/overlay.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/checks/sources.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/checks/toolchain.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/checks/headset.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/checks/run_only.rs`

*Evidence.* Six build wrappers merely pass a slug and path to one shape at `build.rs:44`; four overlay wrappers do the same at `overlay.rs:66`; three source wrappers at `sources.rs:50`; and five tool wrappers at `toolchain.rs:38`. The exact-shingle evidence confirms the 14-line serial parser is duplicated at `headset.rs:45` and `run_only.rs:199`; executable checks are independently repeated at `build.rs:74` and `run_only.rs:53`, and basename helpers at `overlay.rs:37` and `sources.rs:30`.

*What breaks.* Macro or descriptor mistakes could bind a correct slug to the wrong path or binary. Preserve the path-wiring tests such as `all_six_build_outputs_are_wired_to_their_own_paths` and the tool evaluator table; strict registry coverage alone cannot detect a wrong path selector.

*Blast radius.* Seven owned check modules and their unit tests. Function-pointer registry shape, contract slugs, ordering, tap bytes, and external APIs remain unchanged.

*Precaution.* Hoist only first_connected_serial, is_executable and basename; leave both adb_devices_output implementations where they are (run_only's bound is the A7-4 fix), and re-run the build/tool/source/overlay path-wiring tests plus the strict registry test after the macro lands.

*How you would know it worked.* Run every affected module test plus strict registry and parity tests. Confirm each generated wrapper still has the same function-pointer type and that build/tool/source/overlay path-wiring cases cover every declaration.

*Verifier.* I read every cited site. build.rs:44-66 is six 3-line wrappers over built_output plus six defs entries; toolchain.rs:38-56 is five wrappers over tool_check plus five defs entries; sources.rs:50-60 is three over submodule_check; overlay.rs:66-86 is four over overlay_check. Adding the three helper hoists (first_connected_serial 12 lines duplicated verbatim between headset.rs:45-56 and run_only.rs:199-210; is_executable 6 lines between build.rs:74-79 and run_only.rs:53-58; basename 5 lines between overlay.rs:37-41 and sources.rs:30-34) my arithmetic lands at about -76 net after macro invocation blocks, so -75 is one of the few unpadded estimates in this batch. Two caveats the proposal is right about and one it does not raise. Right: the overlay wrappers are NOT uniform (dxmt_* derive src from dxmt_art.join and dst from cx_dxmt, woxr_* take a Paths field plus cx_wine_lib), so the macro needs expression arms there and the overlay win shrinks to a handful of lines; and build.rs:220 all_six_build_outputs_are_wired_to_their_own_paths is the only thing that catches a wrong path selector, since strict registry coverage only checks slugs. Not raised: run_only.rs:142-195's adb_devices_output is the bounded, child-reaping A7-4 version and headset.rs:34-39's is a plain Command::output() -- they share the name and sit next to the helper being hoisted, so a hoist that 'deduplicates' them too would silently revert the A7-4 fix for run.wired-adb. The duplication comments themselves ('duplicated rather than shared because that one is private to a module this one does not own') are resolved rather than violated by moving the helpers to checks/mod.rs. Nothing changes the Evaluator fn-pointer type, contract slugs, ordering, tap bytes or any external API, and PARITY.md's 'is_executable tests mode bits 0o111, not effective access' row survives unchanged. I did not build, per the harness rule, so macro hygiene and the fn-pointer coercion of macro-generated items are unverified.

| `src-tauri/src/lib.rs` | 1 | 20 → 4 | -16 |
| **Total cut set** | **84** | **3,139 → 364** | **-2,775** |

The two retained blocks add 52 unchanged lines, so all 86 large blocks move from 3,191 to approximately 416 lines.

*Files.* `sabrage/crates/sabrage-parity/src/lib.rs`

*Evidence.* The three comments are at `sabrage-parity/src/lib.rs:253`, `sabrage-parity/src/lib.rs:1132`, and `sabrage-parity/src/lib.rs:1842`. Their assertions remain at `sabrage-parity/src/lib.rs:501`, `sabrage-parity/src/lib.rs:1154`, `sabrage-parity/src/lib.rs:1915`, `sabrage-parity/src/lib.rs:1960`, `sabrage-parity/src/lib.rs:2053`, `sabrage-parity/src/lib.rs:2113`, and `sabrage-parity/src/lib.rs:2172`; the core end-to-end host-manifest assertion is `stages/install.rs:1301`.

*What breaks.* No assertions or golden bytes are removed. Test-source documentation becomes terse, so a future maintainer must inspect helper code or git history for the scanner's full derivation.

*Blast radius.* One parity-test file, comment text only. Tier-1 selection, native public seams, shell fingerprints, byte goldens, and test bodies remain unchanged.

*Precaution.* Keep lib.rs:1873-1880's enumeration of the still-substring-only renderers (wineserver_reset, goldberg_stage, adb_reverse_cleanup, adb_forward_hygiene, fixes::adb::remove_adb_forwards_at, preflight::emit_encoder_notice, the game.version warn row) — either verbatim in the shortened block or relocated to sabrage/PARITY.md with a pointer — because r2 A1-3 is CONFIRMED and unfixed and that text is both its evidence and the checklist its fix sketch consumes. While rewriting, correct the tier-1 statement: the local gate (parity.sh TIER1_PKGS) does include -p sabrage-core; only CI (.github/workflows/parity.yml) is limited to sabrage-parity + sabrage-contract-gen. Verify afterwards that only `///` lines changed (`git diff --stat` should show one file, no `#[test]`/`assert` hunks) and that the doc still names the tests it points at.

*How you would know it worked.* Confirm only `///` lines changed, run the parity crate and `scripts/dev/parity.sh`, and reproduce the table with the supplied comment-block metrics. Overall verification for H4 is 3,139 cut-set lines becoming about 364, with all 84 replacement entries present.

*Verifier.* All three regions exist exactly as described and the arithmetic is honest. Block 1 = lib.rs:253-278 (26 lines, the `mod slug_coverage` counting-rule doc), block 2 = lib.rs:1132-1152 (21 lines, the host-manifest file-form golden doc), block 3 = lib.rs:1842-1880 (39 lines, the `mod run_sh_text_parity` doc): 86 original lines, matching the proposal's '86' exactly. Re-wrapping the three replacement strings at the file's real comment width (existing `///` lines run to 87 cols) yields ~16 lines, so the true delta is about -70 against a claimed -71 — no inflation. Every pointer the replacement text offers is real: all nine cited test fns exist in lib.rs (verified by grep) and the core-side end-to-end test exists at sabrage-core/src/stages/install.rs:1301, so the 'points at the test that enforces it' escape clause of Rule C is genuinely satisfied for blocks 1 and 2. Block 1 is safe: git blame puts 253-278 in the Phase-1 commit 3c49c14 (not in fix-blame.tsv's review-fix ranges for this file, which jump 57-137 → 306-329), the enforced rule itself survives in the assertion message at lib.rs:526-531 ('slug(s) emitted from more than one doctor.sh section'), and the A1-7 fix's own machinery (`strip_comment` at 306-328, `section_header` at 330-336, `loop_body_emits`, the ordered contract comparison) each carries its own local doc comment, so nothing about the fix becomes unexplained. Block 2 is the safest: blame is 2f49c18 (Phase 2, not a review-fix commit), the replacement keeps the load-bearing invariant (the write path stages the file form — trailing `}\n}\n` — not the newline-less comparison form, which is the host XR JSON golden on the invariant list) and names both enforcing tests, and the one paragraph it drops that carries design weight — 'the signature takes a path so the mistake is unexpressible' — is fully restated 30 lines below on the compile-time tripwire `write_path_takes_a_dylib_path_not_content` (lib.rs:1183-1194), which is not touched. Block 3 is where the care is needed. fix-blame.tsv lists 1845, 1847-1855 and 1857-1880 for this file, and blame confirms 6d98864 (review-r1 wave 1) and 5cf052a (review-r1 wave 4) wrote 34 of its 39 lines; more importantly, findings-index.tsv row 112 is r2 A1-3 'CI still skips native unit tests that the parity suite says are required', severity medium, verdict CONFIRMED, marked **[unfixed]**, and its Evidence line in 2026-08-30-codex-round2.md quotes precisely the paragraph this proposal deletes ('states that wineserver_reset, goldberg_stage, ADB cleanup, encoder notices, and game-version wording remain "substring-only" … "still copied from source below"'). The r2 verifier's re-rating down to low is expressly conditioned on that documentation ('the divergence is deliberate and documented (parity.sh:72-74, lib.rs:1673-1676)'), and the finding's own fix sketch part 2 needs that enumeration to know which renderers still have to be made `pub`. PARITY.md does not carry the divergence (grepped: no tier-1/package row), so lib.rs:1873-1880 is the only in-repo inventory. The proposal's replacement text keeps the top-level constraint ('Tier 1 runs parity/contract-gen rather than sabrage-core's platform-shaped suite, so this test pins … callable native renderers') but drops the inventory entirely — that weakens an open finding's only mitigation, which is why this is not a plain ACCEPT. Separately, both the current text and the proposed replacement are inaccurate on one point I verified: `scripts/dev/parity.sh:74` sets `TIER1_PKGS=(-p sabrage-parity -p sabrage-contract-gen -p sabrage-core)` (sabrage-core added in the same commit 6d98864, `git log -S'-p sabrage-core'`), while `.github/workflows/parity.yml:27-28` runs only the two hermetic crates — so 'tier 1 selects sabrage-parity + sabrage-contract-gen only (scripts/dev/parity.sh, .github/workflows/parity.yml)' is true of CI and false of the local gate; the rewrite should fix that rather than carry it forward. No invariant is otherwise touched: comment text only, zero assertions removed (Rule T is not engaged), no golden bytes, no shell fingerprint input, no contract slug cardinality change, nothing under contract/, scripts/, demo.sh, docs/ or ext/, and the phase4 deployed-toml fixture is untouched. Removing the two rustdoc intra-doc links at 1871 and 1874 cannot break anything (links are only being deleted), and the crate has no `missing_docs` deny that a shorter doc comment would trip.

#### `H6-5` — Delete the unused ErrorPayload DTO and its self-test

T1 · delete · **-55 lines** (reviewer claimed -53) · from H6 — type and wire-surface sprawl

Delete SabrageError::payload, ErrorPayload, its public re-export, and the test that exercises only that deleted conversion. Keep SabrageError, kind(), already_reported(), remedy(), display text, and exit-code behavior. This removes an alternative error wire format that no production command uses.

*Files.* `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/error.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/lib.rs`

*Evidence.* error.rs:159 defines payload() and error.rs:196 defines its one consumer-facing DTO. The only exercised conversion is the payload-specific test beginning at error.rs:259; lib.rs:84 re-exports the DTO. Production code still uses kind(), so the retained taxonomy and methods at error.rs:127 must stay. These exact regions total 52 lines plus the one-line re-export.

*What breaks.* An out-of-tree Rust caller importing ErrorPayload or invoking payload() will no longer compile. The removed test loses only assertions about the deleted DTO's JSON field projection; it does not cover live command serialization, display text, error classification, remedies, already-reported behavior, or exit codes.

*Blast radius.* Two core files and one test function. No Tauri command currently exposes ErrorPayload, and no golden, parity, contract, or confirmed-finding test is removed.

*Precaution.* Record that this closes the unlanded second half of accepted /simplify item S-A2-already-reported (Tauri commands keep flattening errors to String); keep kind(), remedy(), already_reported(), exit_code() and tail(), and drop only ErrorPayload from the lib.rs re-export line rather than the whole line.

*How you would know it worked.* Run a repository-wide reference scan after deletion, then the core test suite. Confirm all remaining error kind, display, remedy, already_reported, and exit-code tests still run unchanged.

*Verifier.* I confirmed the deadness with git grep across the whole tree: ErrorPayload appears only at error.rs:160-161 (inside payload()), error.rs:206 (the struct), lib.rs:84 (the re-export) and in two prose lines of sabrage/docs/design/{critique,design-core}.md; `payload(` has no caller outside error.rs's own test at :262 and :269 (the other hits are goldberg.rs's unrelated paths_with_payload test helper). There is also no --json path in sabrage-cli — I grepped main.rs for --json / serde_json::to_string / to_value and got nothing — so the CLI does not serialize errors either. Line-count: 159-168 is 10 lines (doc + fn + trailing blank), 196-217 plus one adjacent blank is 23, the payload_carries_kind_message_remedy_and_the_reported_flag test at 259-279 plus its leading blank is 22 — 55 total, with lib.rs:84 losing only a token, so -55 against their -53. Everything the proposal promises to keep is genuinely still used: kind() has a caller in install.rs, already_reported() is read by sabrage-cli/src/main.rs:1003-1004 through error_already_reported_as_fatal, and remedy()/exit_code()/tail() are untouched. Now the history, which is why this is not a clean ACCEPT. fix-blame puts error.rs 127-168 and 195-280 inside a review-fix range, and `git show 6d98864` confirms the whole block — already_reported(), payload(), ErrorPayload and both tests — was added in one hunk by review-r1 wave 1. The motivating item is the accepted /simplify entry S-A2-already-reported (sabrage/docs/reviews/2026-08-30-simplify.md:15 and :71, altitude finding #9), whose prescription was two-part: "promote error_already_reported_as_fatal to SabrageError::already_reported() in core, AND return a typed { kind, message, remedy, alreadyReported } from the commands so both front-ends read one bit." Only the first half landed — commands.rs still does `.map_err(|e| e.to_string())` at :600, :910, :913, :916, :1221 and everywhere else, so the GUI still cannot see remedy, tail or exit_code and still decides double-reporting by scanning the event stream in GateModal. No Codex finding cites error.rs and no behavior changes if ErrorPayload goes, so this is not a fix revert — but it is the scaffolding for an open item, and deleting it should be a conscious "we are not doing S-A2's second half", not a silent tidy-up. If the owner still intends to type the command errors, keeping ~22 lines is cheaper than re-deriving them later.

#### `H6-4` — Flatten CheckOutcome into DoctorEvent and derive the summary from streamed rows

T2 · collapse · **-50 lines** (reviewer claimed -60) · from H6 — type and wire-surface sprawl

Represent DoctorEvent in Rust as a flattened CheckOutcome plus group and fix, preserving the current flat JSON. Express the TS side as CheckOutcome intersected with those two additions. Remove DoctorSummary and the second fail/warn/total counter in the Tauri command; after a successful stream, derive the displayed counts from the rows the store already collected. This leaves registry evaluation and event ordering in the core.

*Files.* `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/checks/mod.rs`, `sabrage/src-tauri/src/commands.rs`, `sabrage/ui/src/ipc.ts`, `sabrage/ui/src/stores/doctor.svelte.ts`, `sabrage/ui/src/screens/Doctor.svelte`

*Evidence.* CheckOutcome already owns slug, status, message, remedy, detail, and quiet at checks/mod.rs:99. DoctorEvent repeats the first five fields at commands.rs:89, then DoctorSummary adds three counters at commands.rs:136. commands.rs:228 reconstructs each event and independently increments summary counts through commands.rs:260 even though the core doctor walk already returns its report at checks/mod.rs:575. The store appends every streamed event at doctor.svelte.ts:62, while Doctor.svelte:273 uses the returned summary only for fail, warn, and total display. The duplicate Rust/TS fields, constructor, counters, summary DTO, and adapter amount to about 60 lines.

*What breaks.* The command's return value changes from a summary object to unit, so an out-of-tree caller relying on that return object breaks. The UI must calculate counts only after successful completion; deriving a success summary from a rejected partial stream would change current error behavior. The streamed event JSON remains flat and byte-equivalent.

*Blast radius.* Doctor command and UI store/view only. Check registry order, CheckOutcome semantics, fixes, live doctor parity, and the stale-row rejection behavior remain. The TS declaration portion overlaps H6-1.

*Precaution.* Keep DoctorSummary on the wire and delete the duplicate counters by consuming the DoctorReport that core_run_doctor already returns; if counts are ever derived in TS instead, they must count only rows with phase === "done" and must not present a summary for a rejected stream.

*How you would know it worked.* For the same streamed rows, compare old returned fail/warn/total values with the new derived values, including zero-check and warning-only cases. Run doctor core tests, UI checks, and both parity doctor tiers; confirm a rejected run does not present a completed summary.

*Verifier.* Both halves measure out roughly as claimed (my count: ~-3 on the struct, ~-4 on the constructor, ~-10 for DoctorSummary, ~-15 for the counters, ~-14 on the TS side, plus a few lines back for a TS counting helper — about -50 against their -60, inside tolerance). The flatten half is sound in principle: CheckOutcome (checks/mod.rs:108-121) carries slug/status/message/remedy/detail plus `quiet` under `#[serde(skip)]`, and CheckOutcome has no rename_all of its own, so flattening it into DoctorEvent keeps every JSON key spelled identically. Two facts make it need care rather than a clean accept. First, `#[serde(flatten)]` switches the serializer from serialize_struct to serialize_map, which reorders the emitted keys (slug,status,message,remedy,detail,group,fix instead of today's slug,group,status,…) — harmless for a JS object, but I confirmed there is NO test anywhere that would notice if it were not: sabrage-app carries no serde_json dependency at all (commands.rs:2777-2787 says so explicitly and its Phase-4 tests deliberately assert Rust-level construction instead of serialized JSON), and sabrage/ui has no test infrastructure whatsoever. So the flatten lands with zero automated coverage on either side. Second, and this is the part I would change: deleting DoctorSummary moves doctor's FAILCOUNT semantics into TypeScript. CheckStatus::counts_as_fail is documented at checks/mod.rs:87-90 as "lib.sh's fail() is the sole site that bumps the counter", and the same counter is the CLI's doctor exit code (DoctorReport::exit_code, capped at 255 per design-core §10 divergence 13) — re-deriving it in an untested TS store creates a second implementation of a shell-parity number in the one place nothing checks it. The proposal also underestimates the derivation trap: doctor.svelte.ts:69 carries the PREVIOUS run's rows forward as a dimmed skeleton and only prunes stale rows in the catch branch (line 89), so on a successful run whose row set shrank, `rows.length` over-counts `total` unless you filter `phase === "done"`. Meanwhile the far cheaper cut is sitting right there and the proposal walks past it: `core_run_doctor` already returns a DoctorReport with fail_count and warn_count (checks/mod.rs:583-600), and commands.rs:230-259 throws it away and recounts by hand — consuming the returned report deletes the three counters and seven counting lines with no wire change, no TS policy mirror, and no error-path question. The `fix` projection through offered_fix_id at commands.rs:238 (a review-fix line, the A4-2/A12-1 defense) is preserved either way, which I checked.

#### `H6-7` — Prune response fields that the app never reads

T2 · scope-cut · **-37 lines** (reviewer claimed -36) · from H6 — type and wire-surface sprawl

Remove AppState.repoRoot, RuntimeConfigView.modifiedUnixMs, WriteReport.path, and RepoInfo.compiledContractSha256/checkoutContractSha256 from the GUI responses. Preserve RepoInfo.binaryContractMatches and the hash comparison that computes it. Delete the mtime stat helper if it becomes unused. The test reduction is explicit: remove only the assertions that an existing runtime TOML has Some(modified_unix_ms) and a missing file has None; no parser, shadowing, backup, or write-result assertion is lost.

*Files.* `sabrage/src-tauri/src/commands.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/runtime_toml.rs`, `sabrage/ui/src/ipc.ts`, `sabrage/ui/src/stores/config.svelte.ts`, `sabrage/ui/src/screens/Settings.svelte`

RuntimeConfigView declares modified_unix_ms at runtime_toml.rs:243, obtains it through the standalone metadata helper at runtime_toml.rs:406, and tests only its presence/absence at runtime_toml.rs:2212 and runtime_toml.rs:2224; config.svelte.ts:35 re-fetches the view but never branches on the timestamp. RepoInfo exposes both raw hashes and their derived boolean at commands.rs:1900 and commands.rs:2039, but Settings.svelte:853 branches only on binaryContractMatches. The two named mtime assertions are genuinely lost because the feature is removed. The app retains the contract-mismatch decision, changed-key report, backup path, template provenance, and shadowing diagnostics it actually displays.

*Blast radius.* Four response shapes and two metadata-only test assertions. The runtime TOML parsers, frozen fixture, byte-preserving writes, contract hash comparison, A1-1 mismatch warning, and settings unknown-key behavior remain. TS field lines overlap H6-1.

*Precaution.* Keep binaryContractMatches, the compiled-vs-checkout comparison at commands.rs:2031-2037 and the Settings.svelte:866-871 mismatch warning byte-for-byte — those are the A1-1 fix; only the two raw-digest fields go. Delete runtime_toml.rs's modified_unix_ms doc comment ('for "the file changed under us" checks in the UI') along with the field so it does not survive as a claim about a mechanism that no longer exists, and leave the flock/still_safe_to_replace A10-1 guards alone. Remove only the two named mtime assertions at runtime_toml.rs:2212 and :2224.

*How you would know it worked.* After removal, use a reference scan to prove no remaining reads. Run runtime_toml tests after deleting only the two named mtime assertions, command tests for RepoInfo/AppState, and UI checks. Exercise Settings displays for contract mismatch and write reports to confirm their retained fields are unchanged.

*Verifier.* I checked each of the five fields against every consumer in the tree. (1) AppState.repoRoot (commands.rs:156, built at :273-275, ipc.ts:39): the only two getAppState() consumers are doctor.svelte.ts:51 (reads bottles, defaultBottle) and bottles.svelte.ts:25, whose public getters expose bottles/defaultBottle/defaultBsDir/alvrVersion only; a workspace-wide grep for repoRoot finds reads solely of settingsStore.settings?.repoRoot (Settings.svelte:348-376) and repoInfo.repoRoot (Settings.svelte:809), both different objects. Dead. (2) RuntimeConfigView.modifiedUnixMs (runtime_toml.rs:258, :312, helper :406-413, ipc.ts:888): no branch anywhere reads it — config.svelte.ts:38 only NAMES it in a comment explaining why the store re-fetches the view, and Logs.svelte:322's modifiedUnixMs is logs.rs:627's unrelated field. Crucially, this is the one field with review history: round1 A10-1 [CONFIRMED, re-rated medium] on the unguarded config read-modify-write, whose verifier wrote 'the plumbing that would allow it (modified_unix_ms) is unused'. I confirmed A10-1 has since been FIXED by a different mechanism — runtime_toml.rs:1249-1260 takes a cross-process flock (lock_toml / Paths::toml_lock_path, comment tagged 'A10-1'), :1266-1290 uses Executor::create_new (O_EXCL) for the template create, and the final replacement is guarded by still_safe_to_replace, a content compare-and-swap, not an mtime one. So the mtime field is superseded plumbing, and deleting it reverts nothing. (3) WriteReport.path (runtime_toml.rs:287, built :1301, ipc.ts:909): Settings.svelte's lastWriteReport block at :634-647 reads changedKeys, backupPath, createdFromTemplate and shadowed and never .path; the path shown to the user at Settings.svelte:437 and :791 is RuntimeConfigView.path, a different struct that stays. No Rust caller reads report.path either (grep of WriteReport shows only the CLI-side created_from_template/changed_keys uses at :1653 and :1676). Dead. (4) RepoInfo.compiledContractSha256 / checkoutContractSha256 (commands.rs:1907-1913, assigned :2046-2047, ipc.ts:1016-1021): Settings.svelte:853-871 branches only on binaryContractMatches; nothing renders the digests. These lines ARE inside fix-blame's ranges for commands.rs (1907-1919, 2027-2038, 2046-2048) and ipc.ts (1016-1027) — i.e. the A1-1 [CONFIRMED, high] fix wrote them. I read A1-1: its fix sketch asks to 'Surface it in commands.rs get_repo_info as a third RepoInfo flag so the GUI's repo-root panel shows it' — the flag is binaryContractMatches, which the proposal explicitly preserves along with the comparison, so this is not a revert. And the raw digests are not lost to support either: checks/meta.rs:98 attaches with_detail(format!("checkout={want} binary={compiled}")) to the meta.contract-sync failure row, which CheckRow.svelte:39-49 renders (a round-2 fix) and the CLI prints under --verbose. Invariants: PARITY.md mentions none of these; the frozen fixture tests/fixtures/phase4/oxrsys-runtime.deployed.toml is untouched; no golden byte (host XR JSON, toml template, cxbottle line, steam_appid.txt, .sha256 marker, launch banner/env/argv, win_path table, registry order, slug cardinality, shell fingerprint) involves any of these response fields; nothing under contract/, scripts/, demo.sh, docs/ or ext/ is required. UNIX_EPOCH stays imported (still used at runtime_toml.rs:1521 and :1748), so no import churn. Rule T: exactly two assertions die (runtime_toml.rs:2212 view.modified_unix_ms.is_some() and :2224 .is_none()); the proposal names both, they cover only the deleted field, and neither touches the parser, shadowing, backup or write-result assertions in the same tests. No test constructs a full WriteReport or RepoInfo literal, so no fixture needs editing. My count: repoRoot -5, modifiedUnixMs -15, WriteReport.path -3, hash pair -14 = -37 against a claim of -36. Land order relative to H6-1 does not matter; if H6-1 lands first the ipc.ts half becomes automatic.

#### `H6-6` — Fold the non-production NotImplemented check status into Skipped

T2 · collapse · **-28 lines** (reviewer claimed -25) · from H6 — type and wire-surface sprawl

For a non-strict registry entry without an evaluator, emit Skipped with the existing “no evaluator bound” reason. Delete NotImplemented, its helper, and the identical consumer match arms. Update the existing non-strict-registry test to expect Skipped; retain its assertion coverage, the strict-registry rejection test, and all ordinary skipped-status tests.

*Files.* `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/checks/mod.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/checks/tap.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/run/preflight.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-cli/src/main.rs`, `sabrage/ui/src/ipc.ts`, `sabrage/ui/src/components/CheckRow.svelte`, `sabrage/ui/src/components/GateModal.svelte`

*Evidence.* checks/mod.rs:68 describes NotImplemented as a phase scaffold with no shell counterpart. It is created only for an unbound evaluator at checks/mod.rs:343, while the production strict registry rejects missing evaluators at checks/mod.rs:526. CLI rendering handles Skipped and NotImplemented together at main.rs:436; TAP maps both to skipped at tap.rs:24; preflight combines them at run/preflight.rs:455; CheckRow.svelte:27 and GateModal.svelte:473 give them the same UI treatment. Removing the variant/helper and collapsing these arms removes about 25 lines without deleting a scenario test.

*What breaks.* A non-strict/debug registry consumer sees status “skipped” instead of “not_implemented.” Production doctor construction cannot emit the removed value because it uses the strict registry. Future scaffold work would need to use the skipped reason rather than introduce a distinct wire status.

*Blast radius.* One enum literal across Rust, CLI, TAP, preflight, and UI. Registry order, slug cardinality, evaluators, remedies, and contract checks do not change. The one TS literal overlaps H6-1.

*Precaution.* Handle both Skipped|NotImplemented arms in stages/run/preflight.rs (:477 in the ordinary gate and :807 in gate_after_fix); the proposal cites only one and gives the pre-move path src/run/preflight.rs. Replace the evaluate() None arm with CheckOutcome::skipped(self.slug(), SkipReason::new("no evaluator bound yet")) so the message survives. Before deleting the assert_ne! in doctor_walks_only_doctor_visible_checks, confirm unknown_and_duplicate_registrations_are_errors_even_leniently still asserts registry().unbound().is_empty() — that is what carries the guarantee afterwards.

*How you would know it worked.* Run the retained strict and non-strict registry tests, CLI renderer tests, TAP tests, preflight tests, and UI checks. Confirm every former NotImplemented consumer now follows the already-tested Skipped path and no assertion count is reduced.

*Verifier.* Verified every site by reading. CheckStatus::NotImplemented is declared at checks/mod.rs:83, produced at exactly one place (checks/mod.rs:361, BoundCheck::evaluate's None arm via CheckOutcome::not_implemented at :177), and consumed identically to Skipped everywhere: tap.rs:39 maps both to the word 'skipped'; sabrage-cli/main.rs:458 returns None (prints nothing) for both; stages/run/preflight.rs:477 and :807 both route them into cannot_verify; CheckRow.svelte:29 and GateModal.svelte:473 give them the same dim/empty treatment. Production cannot emit it: registry() is build_registry(true).expect(...) (checks/mod.rs:552), and the only build_registry(false) callers in the whole workspace are checks/mod.rs:627 and the parity crate's build_registry(true) at sabrage-parity/src/lib.rs:1344. So the collapse is behaviour-preserving in every reachable path, and the tap word is unchanged, which is what parity actually observes. Review history: fix-blame.tsv reports 0 review-fix lines in both checks/mod.rs and tap.rs; preflight.rs:477/:807 and cli main.rs:458 fall outside the fix ranges (468;520-543 and 762-803;823-828; and 453;459-465 respectively); CheckRow.svelte:26-34 IS a fix range, but grepping round1/round2 shows those lines came from the round-2 'detail' rendering fix and the fix.delete-session-json no-button fix (round2 ~L311-357, ~L997-1007) — neither has anything to do with the not_implemented literal on line 29. Grepping both codex review files for NotImplemented/CheckStatus/tap_word returns nothing, so no CONFIRMED finding is touched. Invariants: PARITY.md never mentions the status vocabulary; contract/pipeline.toml carries no status field; sabrage-parity has no test enumerating CheckStatus variants; slug cardinality, registry order and every golden byte are untouched. Independently, sabrage/docs/reviews/2026-08-30-simplify.md items 11 and its deeper-mechanism note already propose this exact cut (and a larger version that also drops eval: Option, the strict flag and unbound) — H6-6 is the conservative subset of it. Line count: doc block at :72-75 (-3), the variant (-1), the not_implemented helper (-8), the doctor-walk assert_ne block at :655-661 (-6), tap.rs doc :26-31 + arm + test assert (-8), cli main.rs's duplicate `ni` half of skipped_and_not_implemented_print_nothing (-2) = -28, i.e. the claim is if anything understated. Rule T check: three assertions disappear (tap_word(NotImplemented)=='skipped', format_outcome(ni)==None, assert_ne! in doctor_walks_only_doctor_visible_checks). The first two assert about a value that will no longer exist, so nothing is lost. The third is the only one worth naming — it cannot be re-expressed once the variant is gone (a real skip and an unbound slug become the same status) — but its guarantee is already enforced twice: registry() panics on any unbound slug before the test body runs, and unknown_and_duplicate_registrations_are_errors_even_leniently asserts registry().unbound().is_empty() (the proposal keeps unbound() and the strict flag). Rule C check: the tap.rs and checks/mod.rs comments being cut are Phase-1 chronology ('while Phase 1 is in flight', 'when every evaluator is bound this arm becomes unreachable'), not byte-parity landmines; the one landmine worth keeping — that the tap vocabulary is the zsh one and closed to ok/warn/fail/info/skipped — lives in tap.rs's module header at :15-17, which the proposal does not touch. Not verified by compiling, per the no-build rule, but every consumer is an exhaustive match the compiler will point at.

### Scope decisions — the owner's call

*28 items, -27,445 lines as measured in isolation Technically sound; each removes a capability. Priced in the menu above; full reasoning here.*

| id | tier | lines | title | the one thing to know |
|---|---|---:|---|---|
