| `H1-2` | T3 | -4,700 | Make setup, build, and install shell-only maintenance workflows | Ends native setup/build/install (4,604 lines of stage bodies plus glue) and with them the native side of four PARITY.md 'must NOT change' byte invariants and 20 contract-declared Doctor fix buttons — technically expressible, but it is a decision to stop being two implementations of one pipeline for three of five stages. |
| `H7-2` | T3 | -3,150 | Replace the multi-game library with one configured Beat Saber target | Measurement is accurate and the coupling really is severable (nothing in sabrage-core's stages touches `library::`, and `game_id` is already `Option`), but it drops multi-game entries, per-game overrides and last-session history, so the owner decides — and whoever implements it must re-home `library::effective_options`' settings-over-override precedence rule onto `Settings::effective_stage_options`. |
| `C1-4` | T3 | -2,400 | Delete log browsing and derived stream-health telemetry as one feature bundle | Owner's call: a clean, honestly-sized (~2.4k) cut that drops in-app log browsing and detached-session reattachment, the Stalled warning, and the encoder chip — the GUI's only signal of the documented (H.264, in-process) downgrade — while the log files themselves stay on disk and demo.sh never had any of it. |
| `H7-5` | T3 | -2,050 | Turn Doctor fixes into remedy text, retaining only launch autofixes | Sound in principle and it closes five still-unfixed findings including a critical, but 'no contract change is required' is true only in the letter — `DEFERRED_CONTRACT_FIX_IDS` must grow from 2 to 8 so contract/pipeline.toml keeps declaring six fix ids nothing implements — and the cut must not touch stages/mod.rs:770-795 (A11-3's recheck) or runtime_toml.rs's shared rolling-backup helper, so the owner decides on both the capability and the contract rot. |
| `H3-5` | T3 | -2,000 | Cut dry-run as a cross-cutting launcher feature | Drops CLI+GUI write/spawn/admin preview (PARITY.md declares it Sabrage-only, so demo.sh loses nothing) and deletes the fixes for four CONFIRMED findings (A2-3, A5-1, A5-4, A6-2, plus round-2's still-unfixed A5-1) — but it cannot reach -2,200 net because DryRunExecutor is also the hermeticity mechanism for 24 core test sites and sabrage-parity's steam_appid planning golden, which would need an equivalent public non-mutating executor built back. |
| `H1-1` | T3 | -1,758 | Keep the parity doctor; delete the rest of the native CLI | Drops `sabrage setup\|build\|install\|run\|stop\|all` from the terminal (demo.sh still covers all six and tier-2 parity only drives `sabrage doctor`), but it also deletes the CLI half of six PARITY.md ledger rows and hollows out A4-3's GUI-vs-CLI cross-process lock — the owner's call, not a mechanical cleanup. |
| `V8-1` | T3 | -1,557 | Make Sabrage a single-profile launcher; remove Library and EditGame | Real and accurately sized (~1,557 lines), but it deletes the saved-games capability outright and — contrary to the proposal's own blast-radius note — the PARITY-declared Goldberg `.orig-steam` revert has no CLI counterpart, so it becomes unreachable from every front-end; owner's call. |
| `H7-1` | T3 | -1,376 | Keep a doctor-only CLI shim; delete the second pipeline CLI | Real and correctly scoped (tier-2 parity only ever calls `sabrage doctor --bottle X --tap F`), but it retires the native pipeline's terminal front-end and orphans four review fixes whose sole consumer is the stage renderer, so the owner must decide whether `sabrage` stays a two-front-end pipeline before this lands. |
| `H7-3` | T3 | -1,080 | Delete the Goldberg revert action and its provenance journal | The strongest cut in this set — it closes four still-unfixed high findings (A7-3, A13a-1, A13a-2, A13b-3 round 2) by removing the mutation door itself — but it drops the one-click restore capability, so the owner decides; keep actions.rs:473-505 (PARITY.md:115's non-regular `.orig-steam` refusal) and the `already_goldberg` skip at :534-546 untouched. |
| `H7-8` | T3 | -1,070 | Remove the standalone multi-source Logs browser | Technically clean and accurately sized (-1,070 measured vs -1,000 claimed) with the Tailer and every parity-pinned log-naming test untouched — but it drops in-app tailing/filtering of the wine, oxrsys and ALVR logs and past-run browsing, which demo.sh does not replace, so the owner decides whether 'here is the path, use Console.app' is enough. |
| `V5-1` | T3 | -853 | Reduce the game library to a name/path/bottle registry | Real and accurately sized (~-853 in library.rs alone, plus uncounted deletions in commands.rs/ipc.ts/EditGame.svelte/Library.svelte), but it drops per-game launch overrides, last-session history, and the whole Ready/NeedsSetup/Goldberg health snapshot; owner must decide, and if it lands it must bump LIBRARY_VERSION so existing launchOverrides/lastSession are not silently eaten on the next save. |
| `V5-2` | T3 | -739 | Delete the Sabrage-only Goldberg restore action | Sizing is accurate (-739) and the guards it deletes only exist to make the deleted action safe, but it removes a user-facing capability and its blast radius is understated: PARITY.md's 'Planned for later phases' row declaring the revert as landed must be reverted, and the A7-3 provenance-marker machinery in run/actions.rs becomes dead. |
| `V1-2` | T4 | -720 | Retire native wired-forward creation while retaining stale-forward cleanup | Owner's call: drops `sabrage run --wired` forward creation (~720 lines, demo.sh --wired still covers it) but needs a contract row + CLI/PARITY edit, and the deletion would take the r2 A7-4 blocking-probe fix and orphan the wired_forwards readers in session/reconcile.rs and stop.rs. |
| `V8-2` | T3 | -659 | Remove one-click Doctor and fatal-row fixes; keep diagnostics and automatic preflight fixes | Accurately sized (~659 lines) and it removes rather than weakens the review-hardened fix surface, but 'invoke the existing CLI fix command' is false — `sabrage` has no `fix` subcommand — so one-click set-backend / restage-helper / remove-adb-forwards / edit-protocol vanish from every front-end; owner's call. |
| `V8-3` | T3 | -529 | Remove the built-in multi-source Logs browser | Accurately sized (~529 lines) and cleanly isolated — no consumer of the log IPC exists outside Logs.svelte — but it removes in-app log viewing plus Session's 'Open in Logs' and 'Re-attach (follow log)' affordances entirely; owner's call. |
| `V2-3` | T3 | -437 | Retire the executable session.json deletion backend and keep only its deferred tombstone | Drops the whole delete-session-json implementation (-437) that PARITY.md:120 still describes as merely 'withheld' rather than absent, and the owner must also repoint two fixes/mod.rs lock tests (apply_waits_for_the_operation_lock_then_proceeds, apply_holding_lock_is_not_gated_by_a_live_session) that rely on it being a cheap Ok no-op, plus the GUI refusal string that tells users to 'run `sabrage fix …` explicitly'. |
| `H6-1` | T2 | -385 | Generate the TypeScript wire bindings from the serde types | The duplicated mass is real (~538 declaration lines in ipc.ts, and 50 of its 51 exported types have a 1:1 Rust struct/enum), but adopting ts-rs/Specta reverses the design statement written at ipc.ts:1 ("No codegen — update both by hand"), adds an external dependency to a crate whose Cargo.toml documents deliberate minimalism, and makes `npm run check` depend on a Rust build — the owner must decide that trade, and it must be prototyped before it can be accepted. |
| `V1-3` | T4 | -380 | Make run preflight check-only; require Setup or Build for repairs | Owner's call: drops launch-time self-heal for the two conditions that recur in practice (a CrossOver update reverting the bottle's graphics backend, a build-x64 reconfigure sweeping the staged arm64 helper) in exchange for ~380 lines; demo.sh run still repairs both, so a Sabrage launch would fail where the shell succeeds. |
| `C1-5` | T3 | -300 | Remove public dry-run while retaining DryRunExecutor as test infrastructure | Owner's call: removing only the public --dry-run/Plan surface is safe and keeps every DryRunExecutor assertion, but it is ~300 lines rather than 500, it retires two declared PARITY.md CLI/GUI rows the proposal never mentions, and it leaves the A6-2/A5-1 dry-run-honesty rows reachable only from tests. |
| `H2-1` | T3 | -221 | Delete the static About microsite | The 221-line cut is real and touches nothing review-pinned, but it deletes the app's only upstream credits/license panel (LGPL-2.1/MPL-2.0/MIT attribution for Wine, DXMT, oxrsys, wineopenxr, ALVR, Goldberg) — the owner decides whether that attribution has to live somewhere before About goes. |
| `H7-4` | T3 | -220 | Remove the full-screen About experience | Measurement is exact and the screen is genuinely inert code (zero findings, zero review-fix lines), but it is the app's only third-party attribution surface — six upstream licenses including LGPL-2.1/LGPL-3.0/MPL-2.0 — and Tauri's native About shows bundle metadata only, so the owner must name where those notices live before this is cut. |
| `H2-2` | T3 | -196 | Remove the per-game launch-override tier | Sound and worth ~196 lines, but it removes a real per-entry capability and deletes `launch_options_for` — which the review-r1 fix wave added as the single home for the merge — so the owner should decide whether per-game flags stay before this lands. |
| `V7-4` | T3 | -158 | Stop persisting per-game Last Session metadata | Line count is honest (-158) but it drops the Library's Last Session column to a permanently empty '—' and strands the rationale for A13b-5's fix in library.rs, so the owner decides — and if taken it must also sweep sabrage-core/store/library.rs, ui/src/ipc.ts and Library.svelte, which are outside the proposal's stated files. |
| `V3-5` | T3 | -139 | Cut auxiliary and historical log browsing | Drops the OXRSys-runtime tab, the ALVR-session tab and the past-run picker from the Logs screen — demo.sh covers none of them, since the files are only reachable by hand — for about -139 lines in logs.rs plus a coordinated removal in commands.rs, lib.rs and Logs.svelte; the owner decides whether that browsing surface earns its keep. |
| `V2-5` | T3 | -138 | Drop derived Ninja progress events and keep raw build output | Removes the only producer of StageEvent::Progress in the whole app, so GateModal.svelte's determinate progress bar goes dead for the multi-minute oxrsys build — and failing ninja builds also lose their ChildFailed tail, because run_child_ok reports an empty one. |
| `V2-4` | T3 | -122 | Remove setup's duplicate Beat Saber diagnostic step | Sound but it makes native setup silently stop printing setup.sh's four Beat Saber rows and stop dying on a bogus --bottle, so it needs a new PARITY.md Setup row; doctor's game.present and run's preflight do carry the same text and depot remedy. |
| `V4-6` | T3 | -60 | Reduce the generator binary to the one workflow scripts use | Sound and safe — nothing in scripts/CI/hooks uses --check/--repo-root and tier-1's check_reports_in_sync_against_the_live_checkout already runs the identical library check() — but it drops a documented dev affordance for ~60 lines, so the owner decides. |
| `H2-5` | T3 | -48 | Drop unused library timestamps and the per-entry App ID copy | All three fields really are write-only (steam_appid.txt comes from contract().game.appid, never from the entry), but the cut removes the Library/Edit App ID display and permanently drops those keys from existing library.json on the next save, and it thins A13b-5's regression test to last_session alone — a product call, not a mechanical one. |

#### `H1-2` — Make setup, build, and install shell-only maintenance workflows

T3 · scope-cut · **-4,700 lines** (reviewer claimed -4,500) · from H1 — cross-crate duplication and the two front-ends

A personal launcher needs native launch, stop, doctor, configuration, and library behavior; it does not inherently need a second implementation of repository setup and compilation. Keep `scripts/demo/{setup,build,install}.sh` as the only maintenance implementation. The existing panel already computes and copies the corresponding shell commands, so retain that small command card and remove its Run/Dry-run path. Before deleting the Rust stage files, move every required artifact golden—host manifest bytes, runtime TOML creation, markers, overlays, and frozen fixtures—into the core utility or parity module that owns the artifact. This is incremental after H1-1; if H1-1 is rejected, the corresponding native CLI commands must also be removed or converted to instructions.

*Files.* `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/setup.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/build.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/install.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/mod.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/events.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/fixes/mod.rs`, `sabrage/src-tauri/src/commands.rs`, `sabrage/src-tauri/src/lib.rs`, `sabrage/ui/src/ipc.ts`, `sabrage/ui/src/components/StagesPanel.svelte`, `sabrage/ui/src/components/GateModal.svelte`, `sabrage/ui/src/stores/stage.svelte.ts`, `sabrage/ui/src/screens/Doctor.svelte`

*Evidence.* The three implementations occupy `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/setup.rs:1-1124`, `build.rs:1-1395`, and `install.rs:1-2085`, totaling 4,604 current lines before glue. Dispatch is only three match arms at `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/mod.rs:947-955`. The UI already renders copyable shell commands at `sabrage/ui/src/components/StagesPanel.svelte:74-100`, while native execution is the separate `openGate` call at `:102-113`. Whole-stage fixes are three enum variants at `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/fixes/mod.rs:111-117`, map back to stages at `:196-203`, and delegate at `:465-490`. Tauri exposes the generic stage command at `sabrage/src-tauri/src/lib.rs:176-183`. Required artifact tests can continue calling core helpers, as parity already does at `sabrage/crates/sabrage-parity/src/lib.rs:1083-1097`.

*What breaks.* Sabrage no longer executes, dry-runs, streams, cancels, or auto-fixes setup/build/install. The panel and Doctor remedies instead show/copy `./demo.sh` commands. Native-vs-shell parity for those three stage bodies intentionally ends. Review-pinned tests about deleted stage mechanics disappear only with that scope; all byte-level artifact assertions and frozen fixtures must be retained after relocation. PARITY.md and design documentation become stale, but no contract or shell implementation change is required.

*Blast radius.* Large cross-cut across three core stage modules, Stage dispatch/fix metadata, one Tauri command, and the non-run half of GateModal/stageStore. Launch, stop, doctor, session recovery, settings, library, and the shell pipeline stay. Golden bytes and confirmed-finding assertions require explicit relocation review before deletion.

*How you would know it worked.* Downstream: prove every mandatory golden test name still exists, run the hermetic parity/core suites and UI type-check, and inspect the panel/Doctor remedies to ensure all three exact shell commands remain visible and copyable. `scripts/dev/parity.sh --live=off` must still pass without modifying the script. No verification commands were run here.

*Verifier.* The three files are exactly the sizes claimed (setup.rs 1124, build.rs 1395, install.rs 2085 = 4604), dispatch is four match arms at stages/mod.rs:949-959, the whole-stage fixes are three FixAction variants with as_stage() delegation at fixes/mod.rs:111-117/196-203/465-490, and StagesPanel.svelte really does already build a copyable `./demo.sh <stage> --bottle <name>` string (demoCommand/copyCommand at :74-100) separately from the openGate run path — so the mechanics of the proposal are real and the -4500 estimate is if anything conservative (Executor::download/tar_xzf/create_new and privilege::write_host_manifest_privileged all become dead too). Two things it does not fully handle. First, the byte invariants: PARITY.md's closing 'Invariants that must NOT change' list names write-once oxrsys-runtime.toml creation, host-manifest bytes + skip-when-current, the manifest's `}\n}\n` trailing newline and the `.sha256` marker (util::contract_marker_bytes, written at setup.rs:252-253). The pure renderers live in util/privilege and survive, but the two tests that prove the *write path* obeys them — install.rs's `host_manifest_skip_decision_matches_cat_semantics` and `layer_four_stages_the_host_manifest_file_form_byte_for_byte` — need install::run under a recording dry-run executor, and sabrage-parity's own doc comment at lib.rs:1148-1152 explicitly names the latter as 'that half' of a two-part golden. There is no relocation target for it once install.rs is gone; that assertion is lost, not moved. Second, the fix buttons: contract/pipeline.toml declares fix.run-setup on 6 checks, fix.run-build on 7 and fix.run-install on 8. None of them sit on an `autofix` gate (I checked every gate in the 100-400 range), so `every_autofix_gate_has_a_fix_action` still passes and the three ids can be added to DEFERRED_CONTRACT_FIX_IDS with no contract edit — but that is 21 Doctor rows that lose their one-click repair, which is most of what a first-run GUI user gets. Also unhandled: install.rs is 966/2085 review-fix lines pinning CONFIRMED findings (TCC-denied copies, interrupted/partial stock-backup sweep, the system.reg flush re-probe, the control-character manifest refusal) and those regression tests go with the stage. All of that is inherent to a scope cut rather than a defect in the proposal — which is why it is the owner's call, and why it should be decided together with H1-1 (the proposal itself says so).

*Depends on.* `H1-1`

#### `H7-2` — Replace the multi-game library with one configured Beat Saber target

T3 · scope-cut · **-3,150 lines** (reviewer claimed -3,200) · from H7 — feature surface — what a personal launcher does not need

A personal Beat Saber launcher already has the necessary one-target model: Session owns bottle, install directory, and launch toggles, populated from Settings. Delete `library.json`, CRUD transactions, general-game validation, add/edit screens, per-game overrides, and last-session history. The four dedicated files alone cost 2,724 lines (`library.rs` 1,378; Library 577; EditGame 680; store 89); commands, IPC types, routes, and tests bring the inspected standalone price to about 3,200 lines.

*Files.* `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/store/library.rs`, `sabrage/ui/src/screens/Library.svelte`, `sabrage/ui/src/screens/EditGame.svelte`, `sabrage/ui/src/stores/library.svelte.ts`, `sabrage/ui/src/screens/Session.svelte`, `sabrage/ui/src/ipc.ts`, `sabrage/ui/src/App.svelte`, `sabrage/ui/src/components/Sidebar.svelte`, `sabrage/src-tauri/src/commands.rs`

*Evidence.* The existing single-target form is already complete at sabrage/ui/src/screens/Session.svelte:32-42 and loads Settings defaults at sabrage/ui/src/screens/Session.svelte:68-93. In contrast, the Library explicitly supports adding other Windows games, multiple rows, per-game status/history, edit, run, and remove at sabrage/ui/src/screens/Library.svelte:210-380; its backend CRUD surface begins at sabrage/src-tauri/src/commands.rs:2052-2194. The dedicated core implementation spans sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/store/library.rs:1-1378.

*What breaks.* Multiple saved games, non-Beat-Saber entries, per-game launch overrides, add/edit/remove, validity badges, and per-game last-session history disappear. The owner configures one bottle and Beat Saber directory in Settings and launches it from Session; demo.sh already models exactly one `--bs-dir` per invocation. Library transaction/schema/validation assertions are deliberately lost with the feature.

*Blast radius.* Core store, two large screens, one UI store, roughly 150 IPC lines, roughly 220 Tauri command lines, and routing. No pipeline contract or byte-golden artifact changes. This overlaps H7-3's revert UI; combined savings are lower than the sum.

*How you would know it worked.* Exercise initial configuration and launch entirely through Settings and Session; verify no `library.json` commands or wire types remain; run core parity/golden suites unchanged.

*Verifier.* Counts check out: library.rs 1378, Library.svelte 577, EditGame.svelte 680, library.svelte.ts 89, the Tauri library section commands.rs:2052-2250 plus the `game_id`/`last_session_to_record` plumbing (~260), ~150 lines of ipc.ts wire types, ~15 App.svelte routing lines and the Sidebar entry — ~3,156 against a claim of 3,200, i.e. accurate. I verified the severability claim rather than taking it: `grep 'library::' across sabrage-core` returns only doc comments in store/mod.rs and settings.rs, `goldberg.rs:57` (`orig_steam_path` for the `origSteamPresent` probe), and nothing in `stages/`. commands.rs:331-345 documents `game_id` as `Option<String>` with 'the Session screen's ad hoc launch leaves it None', so the single-target path already runs today. store/mod.rs:1-12 states outright that everything under `~/Library/Application Support/Sabrage/` is 'GUI-only state ... never a parity artifact, never read by the shell pipeline', so no contract, golden-byte, fixture or shell-fingerprint invariant is touched. Review history is the interesting part and it argues *for* the cut rather than against: eight confirmed findings cite library.rs/Library.svelte/EditGame.svelte, and four of them are still open residue in round 2 — A13a-1 r2 (non-current Goldberg builds presented as the Steam original), A13b-3 r2 (Library bypasses the failed-load quarantine), A13b-3/A13b-7 (revert authenticates an unverified backup). Deleting the feature closes those doors rather than reverting anything. The one fix with a foot outside the deletion is A13a-6 (older builds destroying newer store schema fields), whose settings.rs half survives untouched. Two real precautions the proposal understates: `library::effective_options` is documented in store/mod.rs:20-22 as 'the single home of that precedence rule' for settings-over-per-game-override, so its logic must land on `Settings::effective_stage_options` rather than evaporate; and the `game_id`/`record_last_session` excision at commands.rs:695-725 cuts into the run command's heavily fix-blamed region (commands.rs 746-826 is review-fix code), so that slice needs care. Because this removes user-visible capability — multiple saved games, non-Beat-Saber entries, per-game launch overrides, validity badges, per-game history — it is the owner's decision, not a mechanical cleanup.

#### `C1-4` — Delete log browsing and derived stream-health telemetry as one feature bundle

T3 · scope-cut · **-2,400 lines** (reviewer claimed -2,700) · from C1 — completeness critic

H7-8/V8-3 remove the Logs screen while deliberately retaining Tailer; V3 defends Tailer because SessionMonitor consumes it. Held together, these are one optional product feature: Tailer exists for the browser and for parsing an encoder chip, while the encoder history and Stalled phase exist to enrich the Session screen. If the launcher only needs to launch, stop, and show its current operation output, remove Logs, TailRegistry, Tailer, past-run browsing, encoder-log parsing, and Stalled history together. Retain wine-log creation/naming, supervised run output, runtime_status_live, persisted-session/process liveness, and external-session blocking. This subsumes H7-8, V8-3, V3-5, and the telemetry portion of V3-2.

*Files.* `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/logs.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/session/watcher.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/session/mod.rs`, `sabrage/src-tauri/src/commands.rs`, `sabrage/src-tauri/src/lib.rs`, `sabrage/ui/src/screens/Logs.svelte`, `sabrage/ui/src/screens/Session.svelte`, `sabrage/ui/src/ipc.ts`

*Evidence.* metrics/per-file.tsv:29 gives logs.rs 1,466 lines and :35 gives watcher.rs 2,147, including 1,478 watcher test lines; Logs.svelte is another 448 lines at :25. watcher.rs:227-282 stores Tailer, encoder attribution, freshness history, and previous phase; :498-535 parses encoder rows and derives Stalled. Outside the Logs IPC, the only production Tailer consumer is watcher.rs:28,233,319. commands.rs:1431-1595 owns TailRegistry and four log commands, while Session.svelte:351-358 and :488-512 expose the derived encoder/health result.

*What breaks.* Users lose in-app current/runtime/ALVR/historical log browsing, external/detached log reattachment, the encoder-mode chip, and the Stalled warning. Diagnosis moves to the files themselves. Removing runtime_status_live or process-table fallback would be unsafe and is explicitly not part of this cut.

*Blast radius.* Logs and session UI, Tailer and most of its regression suite, Tauri tail task ownership, IPC log types, and SessionStatus fields. Log-name parity and creation are involved and must stay; contract and artifact bytes need not change.

*How you would know it worked.* Keep and run wine-log naming/golden tests; confirm supervised pipeline/run rows remain visible; prove an external demo.sh session and a fresh live runtime still render non-idle and block mutation; and verify no Tailer, LogSource, TailRegistry, EncoderInfo, or Stalled consumer remains after the cut.

#### `H7-5` — Turn Doctor fixes into remedy text, retaining only launch autofixes

T3 · scope-cut · **-2,050 lines** (reviewer claimed -2,200) · from H7 — feature surface — what a personal launcher does not need

Doctor already renders each check's remedy, so remove its Fix buttons and the general-purpose manual mutation framework: eight-action metadata, the hand-mirrored TypeScript table, Tauri fix command, confirmation/cancellation UI, whole-stage fix aliases, the withheld known-bad session deletion, and manual-only wrappers/tests. Keep `set_graphics_backend_for_launch`, helper restaging used by preflight, and ADB cleanup used by run as ordinary stage helpers. The inspected price is roughly 2,200 lines, led by the 436-line known-bad action, manual portions of the 1,039-line registry, and UI/IPC/command state machines.

*Files.* `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/fixes/mod.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/fixes/session_json.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/fixes/backend.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/fixes/adb.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/fixes/helper.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/config/runtime_toml.rs`, `sabrage/src-tauri/src/commands.rs`, `sabrage/ui/src/ipc.ts`, `sabrage/ui/src/components/CheckRow.svelte`, `sabrage/ui/src/components/GateModal.svelte`, `sabrage/ui/src/screens/Doctor.svelte`

*Evidence.* CheckRow already prints remedy text independently of the Fix button at sabrage/ui/src/components/CheckRow.svelte:37-60. The manual registry and eight actions occupy sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/fixes/mod.rs:60-125 and sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/fixes/mod.rs:235-493; the known-bad, GUI-withheld deletion is an entire module at sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/fixes/session_json.rs:1-436. The launch path's load-bearing autofix dispatch is distinct at sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/run/preflight.rs:825-852.

*What breaks.* One-click graphics-backend, helper, ADB, protocol, and whole-stage remedies disappear. Doctor continues to explain the remedy; Setup/Build/Install remain in StagesPanel, protocol remains editable in Settings, and demo.sh remains available. Tests for manual confirmation, queuing, cancellation, and the removed destructive door are deliberately lost; tests for launch autofixes and stage helpers remain.

*Blast radius.* Broad but subtractive across fixes, Doctor, GateModal, IPC, and one Tauri command. Contract fix IDs may remain descriptive; no contract change is required. Review-finding tests for surviving autofix paths remain load-bearing.

*How you would know it worked.* Verify every Doctor failure still shows message, detail, and remedy with no Fix control; run launch-preflight tests proving graphics/helper autofixes still work; verify StagesPanel and Settings cover the retained manual workflows.

*Verifier.* Measured ~-2,050 against a claim of -2,200: fixes/mod.rs ~700 of 1039, session_json.rs 436 (whole), backend.rs ~200 of 793, adb.rs ~150 of 768, helper.rs ~40, commands.rs ~200, ipc.ts ~60, CheckRow.svelte ~20 of 107, Doctor.svelte ~180 of 542 (110 `fix`-matching lines), GateModal ~80. Not inflated. I verified the retention story holds: only three contract rows are `autofix`-gated (pipeline.toml:109-110, 255-256, 262-263), mapping to just SetGraphicsBackend and RestageHelper, so preflight.rs:838-852's dispatch survives on two variants; and run's ADB cleanup calls `fixes::adb::remove_adb_forwards_at` directly at stages/run/actions.rs:192, not through FixAction, so it survives too. CheckRow.svelte:41-42 does print `remedy` independently of the Fix button at :52-61, exactly as claimed. On review history the cut is net-positive rather than a revert: 22 confirmed findings cite fixes/, and the Fix door owns a critical (A12-1 r1, an undisclosed known-bad destructive repair) plus five round-2 residues still marked unfixed — A4-2/A12-1 r2 (the frontend hand-mirror bypasses the deferred-fix registry at ipc.ts:305-313), A4-4 r2 (mandatory recheck turns an ADB query failure green), A11-2 r2 (fix canceller has no reachable run id), A4-1 r2. Removing the door closes all of them. But three things temper it. First, the contract gate: fixes/mod.rs:780-793's `every_contract_fix_id_is_modelled_or_explicitly_deferred` asserts the unmodelled set equals `DEFERRED_CONTRACT_FIX_IDS` *exactly*, so deleting six variants forces that constant from 2 entries to 8 — the test stays green and contract/ is untouched, but pipeline.toml then declares six fix ids the binary models as permanently withheld, which is contract rot the owner may well want resolved in contract/ (that resolution is T4). Second, two fixes sit on the boundary and must not be swept: A11-3 r2 (high, 'Queued forbidden operations are not rechecked after a session becomes live') lives in stages/mod.rs:770-795 and is shared with the stage path, and A4-9's rolling-backup naming fix is shared between session_json.rs and config/runtime_toml.rs — the proposal lists runtime_toml.rs (3,584 lines, 1,681 of them review-fix code) among its files without saying what it would do there, which is the vaguest part of the write-up and matches its own 0.72 confidence. Third, it removes real one-click capability. Sound direction, owner's call.

#### `H3-5` — Cut dry-run as a cross-cutting launcher feature

T3 · scope-cut · **-2,000 lines** (reviewer claimed -2,200) · from H3 — the test mass

Dry-run is explicitly Sabrage-only, yet it threads through the Executor implementation, every stage, CLI parsing/rendering, Tauri commands, UI state, parity prose, and roughly 1,250 test lines. Remove the user-facing capability and its plan model while retaining all non-dry-run parity and byte goldens; rewrite the steam_appid planning golden around a test-only recorder so that required assertion remains. Current-tree target: executor -560; setup -170; install -160; stop -150; run modules -330; stages/mod plus build -130; config/fixes/store/session/privilege -300; CLI -210; Tauri -120; UI/TS -60; PARITY.md -10—about 2,200 total lines. This overlaps approximately 140 test lines with H3-1 through H3-4, so do not add all headline deltas blindly.

*Files.* `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/executor.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/mod.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/setup.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/build.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/install.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/stop.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/run/mod.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/run/actions.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/run/guards.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/run/preflight.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/privilege.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/config/runtime_toml.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/fixes/adb.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/fixes/backend.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/fixes/helper.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/fixes/session_json.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/store/library.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/store/settings.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/session/state.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-cli/src/main.rs`, `sabrage/src-tauri/src/commands.rs`, `sabrage/ui/src/ipc.ts`, `sabrage/ui/src/stores/stage.svelte.ts`, `sabrage/ui/src/components/StagesPanel.svelte`, `sabrage/ui/src/screens/Session.svelte`, `sabrage/PARITY.md`

*Evidence.* PARITY.md:69-72 states four times that dry-run has no demo.sh counterpart and is an additive Sabrage capability. Its core plan model occupies executor.rs:87-215 and the DryRunExecutor implementation occupies executor.rs:991-1261. CLI surface and plan rendering are main.rs:116, main.rs:634-679, and main.rs:929-1021; Tauri carries the option and emits the same plan at commands.rs:308-344 and commands.rs:618-641; the UI explains and exposes it at StagesPanel.svelte:102-126 and StagesPanel.svelte:159-166. Large dedicated test regions include executor.rs:1563-1741, setup.rs:917-1123, install.rs:1000-1224, stop.rs:1154-1261, commands.rs:2684-2726, and main.rs:2333-2373.

*What breaks.* Users lose previewing writes, removals, commands, administrator prompts, and ordered plan text from both CLI and GUI. Tests for dry-run non-mutation, plan kind/order/text, would-verbs, and no-auth-prompt behaviour are retired; several pin review findings, so this proposal is valid only as an owner-approved capability deletion. The shell remains unchanged because it has no dry-run mode.

*Blast radius.* Cross-cuts core, CLI, Tauri, UI, and PARITY.md, but requires no contract/, scripts/, demo.sh, docs/, or external-machine change. Retained pipeline parity, launch env/argv/banner, win_path, registry, template, marker, shell fingerprint, and exact steam_appid bytes stay gated.

*How you would know it worked.* After owner approval, require zero product references to `dry_run`, `dryRun`, `DryRunExecutor`, `PlannedAction`, or `DRY_RUN_PLAN_TITLE`; test-only recording names may remain. Run all Rust/UI tests and both parity tiers. Exercise every real stage from CLI and GUI to confirm ordinary execution, cancellation, event ordering, and golden outputs are unchanged.

*Verifier.* The scale is real: 702 `dry_run`/`dryRun`/`DryRun`/`PlannedAction` references across 32 files. Every cited region exists — the plan model at executor.rs:87-215 (PlannedAction/PlannedKind/DRY_RUN_PLAN_TITLE/dry_run_plan_body), DryRunExecutor at executor.rs:991-1261, its test block at 1563-1741, and the CLI/Tauri/UI surfaces. PARITY.md's CLI/GUI table (lines 66-72) does state four times that `--dry-run`, the 'would …' verbs, the trailing `-- plan (dry run)` section and the 'would prompt for administrator authorization' row are additive Sabrage capabilities with no demo.sh counterpart, so removing them costs no shell parity and no golden bytes — this is exactly the shape of change PARITY.md exists to make decidable, and it is the owner's call, not mine. Two things the proposal understates and the owner needs before deciding. First, the arithmetic: `DryRunExecutor::new` appears at 24 sites and `dry_run: true` at 30, and the test helpers I read in guards.rs, actions.rs and run/mod.rs use it precisely as the machine-safety mechanism ('nothing here can touch the real machine'); sabrage-parity additionally drives the real `actions::goldberg_stage` through it to pin steam_appid.txt's byte length (lib.rs:1461-1518), and that crate has no async runtime, relying on DryRunExecutor's futures resolving on the first poll. A test-only recorder cannot serve sabrage-parity unless it is public (or feature-gated) in sabrage-core, at which point most of DryRunExecutor is rebuilt under a new name — so net is closer to -2,000 than -2,200, and part of what is 'deleted' is really renamed. Second, the review history: findings-index.tsv shows A2-3, A5-1, A5-4 and A6-2 are all CONFIRMED findings about dry-run honesty (fabricated postconditions, unperformed writes reported as completed, install mutations claimed successful), and round 2 records A5-1 as still unfixed. Deleting the feature deletes those fixes and their regression tests. That is a legitimate way to close a bug class, but it is a capability decision with a live known defect attached, not a cleanup. Scope is otherwise clean: nothing under contract/, scripts/, demo.sh, docs/ or ext/ is required, the shell has no dry-run mode to diverge from, and the launch banner/env/argv, win_path table, registry order, cxbottle line, toml template, .sha256 marker, shell fingerprint and the frozen phase4 fixture are all untouched.

#### `H1-1` — Keep the parity doctor; delete the rest of the native CLI

T3 · scope-cut · **-1,758 lines** (reviewer claimed -1,750) · from H1 — cross-crate duplication and the two front-ends

The shell should be the sole terminal pipeline frontend. Preserve only `sabrage doctor`, because tier 2 explicitly compares it with `demo.sh doctor`; remove native setup/build/install/run/stop/all, their StageEvent console renderer, dry-run renderer, signal machinery, and command-only tests. Production lines `main.rs:629-1387` and tests principally at `main.rs:1611-1777,1809-1777` through `:2639` become unnecessary, while the doctor parser, formatter, and goldens remain. This leaves one terminal owner—the permanent shell—and one graphical owner—the Tauri app.

*Files.* `sabrage/crates/sabrage-cli/src/main.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-cli/Cargo.toml`

*Evidence.* `sabrage/crates/sabrage-cli/src/main.rs:1-14` says the file only parses arguments and renders core events; `:141-147` dispatches six stages plus `all`; the stage-only implementation begins at `:629-650` and runs through the signal watcher at `:1249-1387`; stage-only tests occupy `:1611-1777`, `:1809-1777` through `:2477`, and signal tests `:2478-2639`. `scripts/dev/parity.sh:7-10` identifies `sabrage doctor`—not the other CLI commands—as the tier-2 oracle. `sabrage/crates/sabrage-cli/Cargo.toml:10-15` confirms the handwritten grammar and Tokio entry point exist specifically for CLI parity/stages.

*What breaks.* The native `sabrage setup|build|install|run|stop|all` interface, its `--dry-run`/`--quiet` behavior, console StageEvent rendering, and Ctrl-C/SIGTERM contract disappear. Assertions tied solely to those deleted commands disappear with the interface; no doctor assertion may be removed. `sabrage doctor`, both parity tiers, the Tauri frontend, core stages, demo.sh, and all retained byte-golden tests remain. PARITY.md's description of the broader CLI becomes stale but is out of scope.

*Blast radius.* One binary source file and its manifest. No contract or shell change is required because `scripts/dev/parity.sh` only consumes the doctor subcommand. Review-regression tests for deleted CLI-only behavior are involved; core/parity goldens are not.

*How you would know it worked.* Downstream: run the retained `sabrage-cli` doctor tests, all workspace tests, and `scripts/dev/parity.sh` with tier 2 enabled. Confirm the live doctor diff is byte-identical and that the CLI usage exposes only doctor/help/version. No commands or tests were run during this scan.

*Verifier.* The region exists exactly as described. main.rs is 2639 lines; production stage code runs 629 (`── sabrage setup|build|install|stop ──`) through 1387 (end of the signal machinery) = 759 lines, and the stage-only test blocks are 1611-1778 (stage arg parsing + merge_stage_options, stopping before the two merge_doctor_options tests at 1779-1808 that must stay), 1809-2477 (stage rendering, stage_event_lines, A14-5 colour gating, dry-run plan, run/all wiring) and 2478-2639 (signal watcher tests) = 999 lines. 759+999 = 1758, so the -1750 claim is honest (slightly conservative — `format_line_event`/`fatal_line`/`fatal_lines`/`closing_line` at 474-560 also become stage-only). The parity oracle claim checks out: scripts/dev/parity.sh's do_tier2 builds `-p sabrage-cli` and runs exactly `$bin doctor --bottle <b> --tap <t2>`; nothing else in the harness invokes the binary. sabrage-parity depends on sabrage-core and sabrage-contract-gen only (Cargo.toml dev-deps), never on sabrage-cli, so tier-1 is untouched. No CONFIRMED finding is reverted: A14-1/A14-3/A14-5 live inside the deleted renderer and vanish with the feature rather than being weakened, A14-4's `unknown_command_outcome` is on the retained dispatch path, and A2-8/A6-5/A4-3's fixes all live in sabrage-core. What makes this the owner's decision rather than mine: fix-blame says 649 of main.rs's 2639 lines came from the review-fix commits and most of them are inside the deleted regions; PARITY.md's 'CLI / GUI', 'Run (launch)' and 'Declared by the 2026-08-30 adversarial review' sections carry six rows that only describe the CLI stages (--dry-run/--quiet, the trailing `-- plan (dry run)` section, the FATAL remedy continuation, `error:` suppression, SIGTERM-exits-130, `sabrage all` chaining in-process) and would have to be deleted from the ledger; and A4-3's cross-process flock plus PARITY.md's 'External sessions' row both name `a sabrage run from a terminal` as the hazard they exist for — with the CLI gone the flock's only remaining counterparty is a second GUI instance, which tauri-plugin-single-instance already prevents. Nothing breaks, but a declared capability and a chunk of the divergence ledger go with it.

#### `V8-1` — Make Sabrage a single-profile launcher; remove Library and EditGame

T3 · scope-cut · **-1,557 lines** (reviewer claimed -1,560) · from V8 — the Svelte UI

Delete the Library and EditGame screens, `libraryStore`, their IPC projection, and route glue; retarget About’s CTA to Session. Session already launches an arbitrary bottle and Beat Saber directory with the same four flags, so the library is a second configuration and launch surface rather than a prerequisite. The estimate is measured from 1,346 complete screen/store lines plus about 214 lines of IPC, routing, navigation, and library-only session bookkeeping.

*Files.* `sabrage/ui/src/screens/Library.svelte`, `sabrage/ui/src/screens/EditGame.svelte`, `sabrage/ui/src/stores/library.svelte.ts`, `sabrage/ui/src/ipc.ts`, `sabrage/ui/src/App.svelte`, `sabrage/ui/src/components/Sidebar.svelte`, `sabrage/ui/src/stores/session.svelte.ts`, `sabrage/ui/src/screens/About.svelte`, `sabrage/ui/src/types.ts`

*Evidence.* `Library.svelte:26-207` owns refresh, launch, per-entry status, and removal; `Library.svelte:210-380` renders the complete table/detail UI. `EditGame.svelte:32-251` independently owns load, validation, per-game overrides, Goldberg restore, and save, followed by its 247-line template/style surface at `EditGame.svelte:254-680`. The dedicated store is `stores/library.svelte.ts:18-85`, and its wire API occupies `ipc.ts:1034-1181`. The non-library launch path already constructs the complete `LaunchOpts` at `Session.svelte:164-184`, with persistent defaults edited at `Settings.svelte:86-168`.

*What breaks.* The app no longer keeps multiple named game/install entries, per-game launch overrides, last-session metadata, live validity rows, or the Goldberg restore UI. Users launch the one configured install from Session. Rust library commands and persistence remain unused until a separately owned backend cut removes them.

*Blast radius.* Nine assigned UI files plus the screen union. No contract, shell, parity bytes, or golden tests change. The Sabrage-only Goldberg revert remains implemented in Rust/CLI and documented at `sabrage/PARITY.md:124-127`, but loses its GUI entry point.

*Precaution.* If the owner takes it: update sabrage/PARITY.md:126 (the revert row) and sabrage/docs/design/design-app.md in the same commit, and decide explicitly whether `revert_original_steam_dll`/the library Tauri commands stay as dead Rust or get a CLI entry point.

*How you would know it worked.* Run `npm run check`; verify no library/edit imports or routes remain; configure bottle/path in Settings; launch, stop, and inspect status through Session. Confirm existing `library.json` is neither read nor modified by the UI.

*Verifier.* I counted the regions myself: Library.svelte 577 + EditGame.svelte 680 + stores/library.svelte.ts 89 = 1346 whole-file lines; ipc.ts's library section is exactly lines 1034-1182 (149 lines, bounded by the `// -- library (Phase 4)` and `// -- native dialogs` banners); App.svelte contributes ~26 (two imports, `editGameId` + its 5-line doc, `editGame`/`addGame`/`doneEditing` with their comments, and the two route branches at 89-92); Sidebar.svelte's Library NavItem block is 12; types.ts's `edit` union member plus its doc is ~2; and session.svelte.ts's `launchedGameId` bookkeeping is ~22 (the 66-75 doc, the 77-85 applyStatus guard, line 98, the 256-258 getter). Total 1557 against a claimed 1560 — the estimate is honest. Feasibility checks out: I grepped every library-only IPC symbol (`getLibrary`, `newGameTemplate`, `saveGame`, `removeGame`, `validateGame`, `revertOriginalSteamDll`, `libraryStore`) and none is referenced outside Library.svelte/EditGame.svelte/library.svelte.ts; `suggestBsDir` and `pickFolder` ARE used by Settings.svelte (lines 64/144/145/348) and must stay, and the proposal's cited ipc range correctly stops at 1182, before them. There are no UI tests at all to break (sabrage/ui/package.json has only dev/build/check — no vitest; the `*.test.ts` paths in findings-per-file.tsv are fix-sketch proposals, not files). Review history: Library/EditGame are the anchor for nine CONFIRMED findings (A13a-1, A13a-2, A13b-1/2/5/6/7/10, plus r2 A13a-1/A13b-1/A13b-3), and session.svelte.ts:66-87/98/256-258 is exactly the A13b-1 fix range. Deleting the screens removes the surface those fixes hardened rather than reverting the fixes — the Rust guards in store/library.rs and store/goldberg.rs stay intact — so this is not a fix revert, it is a capability cut. The capability trade is bigger than stated: I read sabrage-cli's USAGE (main.rs:83-120) and grepped main.rs — there is no `revert`, no `library`, no `game` subcommand, so 'the Goldberg revert remains implemented in Rust/CLI' is wrong; after this cut `store::goldberg::revert_original_steam_dll` has no caller in any front-end, and sabrage/PARITY.md:126 still declares it as a landed Sabrage-only capability. Also lost with no replacement: per-game launch overrides, last-session metadata, live validity rows and multiple named installs. Nothing under contract/, scripts/, demo.sh or ext/ is touched and no golden byte moves, so it is not blocked by scope — it is purely a product decision.

#### `H7-1` — Keep a doctor-only CLI shim; delete the second pipeline CLI

T3 · scope-cut · **-1,376 lines** (reviewer claimed -1,100) · from H7 — feature surface — what a personal launcher does not need

Retain `sabrage doctor`, `--tap`, help/version, and doctor rendering because tier-2 parity invokes them. Delete public `setup`, `build`, `install`, `run`, `stop`, and `all`; their stage argument parser, signal machinery, execution glue, and feature-specific tests duplicate the permanently maintained demo.sh. Preserve the byte/golden stage-event projections in a test-only module rather than counting them as savings. The inspected price is about 600 production lines plus 500 command/parser/signal tests.

*Files.* `sabrage/crates/sabrage-cli/src/main.rs`, `sabrage/PARITY.md`

*Evidence.* The CLI advertises the six stage commands explicitly as demo.sh equivalents at sabrage/crates/sabrage-cli/src/main.rs:83-117 and dispatches them at sabrage/crates/sabrage-cli/src/main.rs:132-147; their implementation occupies sabrage/crates/sabrage-cli/src/main.rs:629-1390. The live parity harness builds this crate solely to invoke `sabrage doctor --tap` at scripts/dev/parity.sh:245-259.

*What breaks.* `sabrage setup|build|install|run|stop|all`, CLI `--dry-run`/`--quiet`, in-process `all`, and its Ctrl-C/SIGTERM behavior disappear. demo.sh covers the corresponding terminal operations. Tests asserting the deleted parser, all-chain, and signal behavior are deliberately lost; doctor/TAP and parity/golden assertions remain.

*Blast radius.* Mostly one 2,639-line file plus PARITY.md ledger cleanup. No contract, shell, artifact-golden, or doctor-registry change. Standalone estimate overlaps H7-6 because CLI dry-run disappears here too.

*How you would know it worked.* Confirm help exposes only doctor, version, and help; run the doctor CLI tests and tier-2 parity; confirm the retained stage-event golden projections still pass and no stage command dispatch remains.

*Verifier.* The region exists exactly as described. `main.rs:83-117` is the six-stage usage block, `:132-147` the dispatch, and stage-only production code runs `637-1388` (752 lines) plus `closing_line` (548-560), `format_line_event`/`fatal_line`/`fatal_lines` (474-525), ~22 usage lines, 6 dispatch lines and a handful of imports — 849 production lines. The test module is 1389-2639 (1251 lines); doctor-only tests occupy roughly 1399-1613 and 1780-1811 (~247 lines), leaving ~1004 stage tests. Raw removal is therefore ~-1853; honouring the proposal's own stated retention of the byte/golden stage-event projections (`stage_event_lines`, 712-798, plus its ~390 lines of tests) nets ~-1376. The claim of -1100 is conservative, not inflated. I confirmed the parity dependency directly: `scripts/dev/parity.sh:245-259` builds sabrage-cli solely to run `doctor --bottle <b> --tap <f>`, and `sabrage-parity/src/lib.rs` contains no reference to `stage_event_lines`, `closing_line` or the CLI at all (its one mention at :1856 points at `stages/run/mod.rs`'s own test). So tier-1 and tier-2 both survive. Review history: nine confirmed findings cite this file. None is *reverted* by the cut — A14-1 (empty option values), A14-3 (CR passthrough), A6-5 (NeedsAdmin method) and A14-5 (per-stream color gating) all live in the stage surface and disappear with their subject rather than being weakened. But two consequences the proposal does not name: (1) `print_bootstrap_error` (main.rs:185-190) uses plain `eprintln!`, and `cmd_doctor` (339-402) reads only `use_colors().stdout`, so `Colors.stderr`, `colors_from`'s per-stream independence and every `fatal_*` helper become dead the moment the stage renderer goes — A14-5's fix and its three regression tests lose their subject entirely. (2) PARITY.md:113 justifies the cross-process flock (the A4-3 CONFIRMED-high fix) as existing 'so the GUI and the `sabrage` CLI serialize against each other'; the proposal's blanket 'PARITY.md ledger cleanup' must edit that rationale without deleting the flock. Invariants: no contract, shell, golden-byte or fixture change; PARITY.md rows 69-74 and 127 are CLI-stage rows that need ledger edits, which the proposal lists. The retention plan is also self-defeating on its own terms: keeping ~480 lines of stage-rendering code alive purely so byte-shape tests against setup.sh/build.sh/install.sh keep passing preserves dead production code. This is not wrong, it is a product-shape choice — CLAUDE.md names 'the Sabrage GUI + `sabrage` CLI' as the native pipeline — so it is the owner's call, not mine.

#### `H7-3` — Delete the Goldberg revert action and its provenance journal

T3 · scope-cut · **-1,080 lines** (reviewer claimed -1,050) · from H7 — feature surface — what a personal launcher does not need

Keep launch-time Goldberg installation and the write-once `.orig-steam` backup, but remove the optional button that temporarily restores Steam's DLL. The dedicated 744-line module contains only 115 production lines and 496 feature tests; the remaining price is provenance-marker logic, Tauri/IPC plumbing, Library validity, and EditGame UI. The module itself says demo.sh has no counterpart and the next pipeline launch reinstalls Goldberg unconditionally.

*Files.* `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/store/goldberg.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/run/actions.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/store/library.rs`, `sabrage/src-tauri/src/commands.rs`, `sabrage/ui/src/ipc.ts`, `sabrage/ui/src/screens/EditGame.svelte`, `sabrage/ui/src/screens/Library.svelte`, `sabrage/PARITY.md`

*Evidence.* The capability's stated purpose and lack of shell counterpart are documented at sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/store/goldberg.rs:1-16, with its production implementation at sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/store/goldberg.rs:65-247. Revert-only provenance bookkeeping lives at sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/run/actions.rs:380-424. The load-bearing launch backup and byte artifacts are separate at sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/run/actions.rs:473-505.

*What breaks.* One-click temporary restoration for launching through real Steam disappears. A user must copy the backup manually, use Steam verification/reinstall, or maintain a separate install; demo.sh does not provide a revert. The 496 lines of revert refusal/race/provenance assertions disappear because their mutation door no longer exists.

*Blast radius.* One whole core file plus small slices of run actions, validation, command, IPC, and UI. Preserve the `.orig-steam` backup, Goldberg install, `steam_appid.txt`, flag-file, and launch golden tests. Overlaps H7-2.

*How you would know it worked.* Confirm no revert command, wire type, button, or provenance marker remains; verify a normal launch still creates the backup once and installs every Goldberg byte exactly as before.

*Verifier.* I verified the module really is revert-only. goldberg.rs:1-16 says so in its own header, and its entire public surface is `RevertReport`, `revert_original_steam_dll`, `revert_with_pin` and the `pub(crate) orig_steam_path` helper. Consumers outside the file are exactly three: `library::validate`'s `origSteamPresent` probe, a doc reference in store/mod.rs, and the Tauri command at commands.rs:2151-2191. The revert-only provenance journal in actions.rs (`goldberg_backup_marker` :388, `goldberg_backup_is_goldberg` :407, `record_goldberg_backup` :414) has exactly one non-test reader — goldberg.rs:221 — so deleting both halves together is clean and the launch path's behaviour is bit-identical. Measured: goldberg.rs 744 + actions.rs provenance ~46 and its tests ~140 (fix-blame range 1768-1903) + commands.rs revert ~50 + ipc.ts ~15 + EditGame revert UI ~60 + Library validity ~20 + PARITY.md rows ≈ 1,080 against a claim of 1,050 — accurate. On review history this is the reverse of a regression: seven confirmed findings cite goldberg.rs and every one of them is about the revert door lying about what it restores (A7-5, A13a-1, A13a-2, A13b-2, A13b-6, A13b-7), and round 2 re-confirmed four of them as still unfixed (A7-3, A13a-1, A13a-2, A13b-3). The provenance marker IS A7-5's fix, but it exists solely to let revert refuse — remove revert and the vulnerability class goes with it rather than the guard being weakened. Invariants: PARITY.md:115 ('a non-regular `.orig-steam` refuses the launch') and PARITY.md:126 (the revert row in the Sabrage-only list) both need ledger edits, which the proposal lists; the `steam_appid.txt`, flag-file and Goldberg launch goldens pinned at sabrage-parity/src/lib.rs:1071-1254 are in a different code path and must not move. Ordering note: `orig_steam_path` is `pub(crate)` for `library::validate`, so landing H7-3 before H7-2 means re-homing that one-line helper; landing H7-2 first makes it moot. It removes a capability the user can otherwise only get by copying `.orig-steam` by hand, so it is a scope call.

#### `H7-8` — Remove the standalone multi-source Logs browser

T3 · scope-cut · **-1,070 lines** (reviewer claimed -1,000) · from H7 — feature surface — what a personal launcher does not need

Keep log creation, filenames, paths, and the core Tailer used by SessionMonitor. Delete only the standalone Logs route: four tabs, filtering/follow state, past-run listing, source-resolution wire enum, Tauri tail registry/pollers, and associated UI-facing tests. GateModal already shows structured rows and console output, while Session exposes the current log path. The inspected price is about 1,000 lines.

*Files.* `sabrage/ui/src/screens/Logs.svelte`, `sabrage/ui/src/App.svelte`, `sabrage/ui/src/components/Sidebar.svelte`, `sabrage/ui/src/ipc.ts`, `sabrage/src-tauri/src/commands.rs`, `sabrage/src-tauri/src/lib.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/logs.rs`

*Evidence.* The optional browser's source enum and past-run functions are at sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/logs.rs:95-150 and sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/logs.rs:618-668; its Tauri polling registry and commands occupy sabrage/src-tauri/src/commands.rs:1417-1595. GateModal independently renders stage rows and console output at sabrage/ui/src/components/GateModal.svelte:532-585, and Session shows the log path at sabrage/ui/src/screens/Session.svelte:501-504. The Tailer remains required by SessionMonitor at sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/session/watcher.rs:227-233 and sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/session/watcher.rs:318-320.

*What breaks.* In-app filtering/following of Wine, OXRSys, and ALVR logs and browsing prior runs disappear. Log files still exist and the live pipeline output remains visible, but deeper diagnosis moves to the displayed path and ordinary macOS/terminal tools. Source-resolution, past-run ordering, and Tauri tail-registry assertions are lost; Tailer rotation/truncation coverage remains.

*Blast radius.* One 448-line screen, approximately 180 Tauri lines, approximately 55 IPC lines, source/listing slices and tests in logs.rs, plus routing. Core logging and watcher parity are untouched.

*How you would know it worked.* Run setup/build/install/run/stop and confirm GateModal/Session still show their output and paths; run SessionMonitor/Tailer rotation tests; confirm no webview tail tasks or Logs route remain.

*Verifier.* I traced the whole surface. The deletable set is genuinely isolated: LogSource + resolve_source (logs.rs:94-151, ~58 lines), PastRun + list_past_runs (logs.rs:617-668, ~52), their tests (logs.rs:801-991, ~190), the Tauri logs section commands.rs:1417-1600 (~184) plus its two registry tests (~60), Logs.svelte (448), the ipc.ts block (~55) and about 20 lines of routing — 1,070, so the -1,000 estimate is honest, not inflated. Outside that set, logs::resolve_source/list_past_runs/LogSource/PastRun have exactly three callers, all in the deleted Tauri commands, and I confirmed SessionMonitor keeps Tailer (watcher.rs:227-233 field, :319 Tailer::open with RUNTIME_LOG_PRELOAD_LINES), so the rotation/truncation/backlog suite in logs.rs:993-1464 survives untouched, as do the parity-relevant wine_log_candidate naming tests (logs.rs:744-800) that pin the beatsaber-<ts>.log/-2 collision behavior PARITY.md's Run section declares. No golden bytes, no contract slug, no shell fingerprint, no frozen fixture is involved. The review history costs are real but are bug-surface, not fix-reverts: A11-5 (TailGuard/TailRegistry self-unregistration) and A12-6 (Logs.svelte's seq guard against out-of-order tab switches, the review-fix range Logs.svelte:57-62) both vanish with the feature they protect, and A8-6/A8-4's Tailer-side halves stay. Two accuracy gaps in the proposal, both trivial: its file list omits ui/src/screens/Session.svelte, which has two live onNavigate('logs') call sites (:504 'Open in Logs' and :528 'Re-attach (follow log)') that must go with the route or the Screen union in types.ts will not typecheck; and no Logs.test.ts exists, so no UI test is lost. This is a capability removal with no invariant collision — squarely the owner's call.

#### `V5-1` — Reduce the game library to a name/path/bottle registry

T3 · scope-cut · **-853 lines** (reviewer claimed -850) · from V5 — config editor + app store

Keep only `id`, `name`, `bs_dir`, and `bottle`. Remove persisted `LaunchOverrides`, `LastSession`, `appid`, and `added_at_unix_ms`; use global launch defaults, derive the fixed appid from the contract, and rely on launch preflight/Doctor instead of a second install-health dashboard. This also removes `upsert_editable`, session recording, option merging, `GameValidity`, `GameStatus`, `GoldbergState`, and their test matrices.

*Files.* `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/store/library.rs`

*Evidence.* `store/library.rs:36-75` defines 40 lines of override/history-rich entry schema; `store/library.rs:126-175` adds merging, session recording, and option lookup solely for those fields. `store/library.rs:322-324` already derives the appid from the fixed contract, while `store/library.rs:331-614` spends 284 lines on per-game option merging and a health snapshot that restates launch gates. The associated owned tests occupy `store/library.rs:824-1092` and `store/library.rs:1094-1377`.

*What breaks.* Per-game flag overrides, last-session timestamps/log summaries, stored appid/added time, Ready/NeedsSetup badges, inline validation, and Goldberg-status display disappear. Existing JSON fields will be ignored and stripped on the next save. Tauri commands, IPC types, and Library/EditGame UI consumers outside this reviewer’s files require cleanup; confirmed-finding tests tied exclusively to the removed features become inapplicable, while CRUD transaction tests must remain.

*Blast radius.* Owned change is `store/library.rs`; compilation also exposes callers in `src-tauri/src/commands.rs`, `ui/src/ipc.ts`, `Library.svelte`, and `EditGame.svelte`. No contract, shell, parity ledger, or golden artifact bytes change; the run stage continues taking its appid from `contract/pipeline.toml`.

*How you would know it worked.* Load an existing rich `library.json`, save it as the reduced schema, and exercise add/edit/remove/run using global launch defaults. Confirm launch preflight and Doctor still reject every hard-gate failure formerly represented by `GameValidity`, then run the core and Tauri test suites.

*Verifier.* I opened store/library.rs (1378 lines) and counted the actual removal: production side ~392 lines (LaunchOverrides 36-47, LastSession 49-60, four GameEntry fields, upsert_editable 121-146, record_last_session 148-160, launch_options_for 162-172, effective_options 330-346, GoldbergState 350-389, GameStatus 391-403, GameValidity 405-421, bottle_template_value 423-436, validate/validate_with_bottle/validate_pinned 438-637, plus dead imports) and test side ~461 lines (an_edit_racing_a_recorded_session_keeps_both, the upsert_editable/record_last_session/effective_options/launch_options_for tests, and everything from fake_bottle at 1096 to EOF). Total ~-853 against a claim of -850 — not inflated. Every cited anchor exists as described (36-75 is exactly the override/history schema; 322-325 is indeed `appid: contract().game.appid as u32`). Invariants: I verified steam_appid.txt is written from `contract().game.appid` in stages/run/actions.rs:572, never from the entry, so the golden bytes are untouched; PARITY.md declares nothing about the library or per-game overrides (its only Library-adjacent rows are the cross-process operation lock and host.manifest library_path); the frozen phase4 fixture and the shell fingerprint are unaffected. Review history is the real question. library.rs is cited by 8 confirmed findings. A13a-3 (concurrent library transactions) survives — the proposal explicitly keeps LIBRARY_LOCK/transact and its interleaving test. A13a-6 (newer-schema refusal) survives. But A13b-5 (upsert_editable's server-owned-field merge), A13a-5 (Ready must not be shown when steam_api64.dll is absent), A13a-1 r1+r2 and A13b-7 (the two-ways-to-be-Goldberg classification at library.rs:515-549, the r2 anchor) all disappear with the features they hardened. That is deletion, not weakening: with no last_session there is nothing for an edit to erase, with no badge there is nothing to lie, so I did not treat it as a fix-revert — it is a capability removal, which is the owner's call. Two things the proposal understates. (1) It says existing JSON fields 'will be ignored and stripped on the next save' without bumping LIBRARY_VERSION; that is precisely the downgrade data-loss shape A13a-6 exists for, and load()'s own doc says any schema change must bump the version. (2) The hard gate it leans on is real but late: `dep.goldberg` in checks/pinned.rs only verifies the repo's own third_party payload, so the missing-game-dll gate is the run stage's die in goldberg_stage — you learn about it after pressing Run, not from a badge. Also `store::goldberg::orig_steam_path` is `pub(crate)` solely for library::validate, so its visibility and doc need adjusting if V5-1 lands without V5-2.

#### `V5-2` — Delete the Sabrage-only Goldberg restore action

T3 · scope-cut · **-739 lines** (reviewer claimed -737) · from V5 — config editor + app store

Delete `goldberg.rs` and its module export. If V5-1 is not also selected, move only the five-line `.orig-steam` path helper into `library.rs`; classification can remain read-only. The launcher still installs Goldberg on every run, but no longer offers the unusually risky convenience action for swapping a DLL back.

*Files.* `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/store/goldberg.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/store/library.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/store/mod.rs`

*Evidence.* `store/goldberg.rs:3-16` explicitly identifies this as a Sabrage-only divergence with no shell counterpart and says the next run reinstalls Goldberg unconditionally. The complete action is `store/goldberg.rs:107-247`; its test-only support and 12 tests occupy `store/goldberg.rs:249-744`. The only area-local reusable fragment is `orig_steam_path` at `store/goldberg.rs:53-63`, consumed by library validation at `store/library.rs:532-550`.

*What breaks.* Users lose the Restore `.orig-steam` backup button and must use some out-of-band recovery path. The Tauri command, registration, IPC wrapper, and UI button outside this reviewer’s files must be removed. Seven review-hardened refusal/classification tests disappear only because the mutating operation itself becomes unreachable.

*Blast radius.* Deletes nearly all of `store/goldberg.rs` and one `store/mod.rs` declaration; downstream command/UI cleanup is required. `run.sh`, the native Goldberg install stage, `.orig-steam` creation, `steam_appid.txt` golden bytes, and parity remain untouched.

*How you would know it worked.* Confirm no `revert_original_steam_dll` symbol or UI invocation remains, while normal native and shell launches still install Goldberg and preserve `.orig-steam`. Run the run-stage golden/parity suites plus core/Tauri compilation.

*Verifier.* goldberg.rs is 744 lines; the whole file goes except `orig_steam_path` (fn plus doc, ~11 lines) moved into library.rs, plus `pub mod goldberg;` and the five-line bullet in store/mod.rs — net ~-739 against a claim of -737. Every cited anchor checks out: the DIVERGENCE header is at 3-16, revert_original_steam_dll's body runs from ~107 to 247, tests are 249-744, orig_steam_path is at 53-63, and library.rs's validate consumes it at ~532-550. Review history: goldberg.rs carries 565 review-fix lines and 7 confirmed findings (A13a-1 r1 and r2, A13a-2 r1 and r2, A13b-7, A7-5/A7-3). Every one of those fixes is a guard on the mutating action itself — the three-way Goldberg backup test, the operation lock taken first and held through the copy, the argv scan for a running Beat Saber, the message that says '.orig-steam backup' and never 'the original'. Deleting the action makes all of them unreachable rather than weaker, so this is a scope cut, not a fix-revert, and I did not kill it on that basis. Invariants: PARITY.md's 'Invariants that must NOT change' block is untouched (Goldberg hash-tolerance at run, steam_appid.txt bytes, the .sha256 marker, the banner — none involve revert), and run.sh/goldberg_stage/.orig-steam creation stay. But the proposal's claim that 'parity remains untouched' is wrong on one point: PARITY.md line 126 explicitly declares 'Revert-original-steam_api64.dll action — landed in Phase 4', and that ledger row would become a lie. Second understated consequence: `goldberg_backup_is_goldberg` at run/actions.rs:407 has exactly one non-test caller, goldberg.rs:221, so removing the revert strands the whole A7-3 provenance mechanism (goldberg_backup_marker, goldberg_backup_is_goldberg, record_goldberg_backup and their tests, ~50 more lines) as dead pub(crate) code that will warn; it also means Sabrage stops writing a Sabrage-only artifact under ~/Library/Application Support/Sabrage/goldberg-provenance/, which is a bonus, but it must be handled deliberately in the same commit.

#### `V1-2` — Retire native wired-forward creation while retaining stale-forward cleanup

T4 · scope-cut · **-720 lines** (reviewer claimed -740) · from V1 — stages/run — launch state machine

Keep the ordinary non-wired hygiene that removes stale stream forwards, but stop offering Sabrage's `--wired` creation mode; the shell remains the escape hatch. This removes a disproportionate amount of rollback, persistence, cancellation, fake-adb, and preflight-special-case surface for one optional path.

*Files.* `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/run/actions.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/run/preflight.rs`

*Evidence.* The retained non-wired cleanup is only `actions.rs:183-195`. Wired creation and rollback occupy `actions.rs:98-156` and `actions.rs:198-294`, with its fixture/test matrix at `actions.rs:1084-1581`; the only reason for preflight's blocking-thread evaluator is `run.wired-adb` at `preflight.rs:314-358`, tested again at `preflight.rs:1714-1727` and `preflight.rs:1912-1949`. ALVR reverse cleanup is independent at `actions.rs:636-658` and stays.

*What breaks.* `sabrage run --wired` would cease creating `tcp:9943`/`tcp:9944`; users needing that mode must use `demo.sh run`. The deletion intentionally loses assertions covering forward creation, failed-create rollback, indeterminate removal persistence, and cancellation mid-loop. Contract gate data, CLI/help/settings, and PARITY must change or explicitly retain a rejected/no-op compatibility option, so this is blocked as T4.

*Blast radius.* Assigned changes are confined to `actions.rs` and `preflight.rs`, but clean removal affects `StageOptions.wired`, the `run.wired-adb` registry/contract row, parity tests, CLI/Tauri/settings inputs, and reconciliation of old wired records. Existing non-wired cleanup and ALVR reverse cleanup remain covered.

*How you would know it worked.* Keep and run the non-wired cleanup and adb-reverse tests; remove only wired-creation expectations. Then verify native parity records the deliberate feature cut while `demo.sh --wired` and tier-2 shell behavior remain unchanged.

*Verifier.* Measured and the regions are exactly as described: actions.rs:98-156 is stream_forward_specs + rollback_forwards, 198-294 is the --wired creation half of adb_forward_hygiene, 183-195 is the retained non-wired `remove_adb_forwards_at` branch, and every test between 1084 and 1581 is wired (wired_without_adb_dies…, wired_with_no_device…, wired_plans_both_forwards…, a_failed_forward_removes_both_ports…, a_rollback_whose_removal_fails…, a_cancellation_mid_loop…, a_failed_save_between_the_ports…, a_cancel_during_the_device_probe…) plus their fake-adb fixtures; goldberg fixtures start at 1587. preflight.rs:314-358 is CHILD_PROBE_SLUGS + `evaluate`, and CHILD_PROBE_SLUGS is literally `["run.wired-adb"]` with the doc saying 'exactly one today', so the claim checks out. ~-720 after leaving shared fixtures (real_ctx/adb_calls) in place — the -740 estimate is honest. This is a genuine capability cut, not an error, so it is the owner's decision, but three things should be priced in. First, it needs edits outside the review area: the `run.wired-adb` [[check]] row (contract/pipeline.toml:394-398), the `adb-forward-hygiene` [[launch_action]] whose `what` text names wired creation, StageOptions.wired, the CLI flag, and PARITY.md's 'CLI help text includes --wired/--verbose' row — all out of scope, hence T4. Second, preflight.rs:314-358 is the r2 A7-4 fix (high, CONFIRMED: 'Wired preflight can hang forever before reaching the cancellable probe'), and fix-blame.tsv confirms 314-379 are review-fix lines; deleting the generic spawn_blocking evaluator rather than just emptying CHILD_PROBE_SLUGS throws away a mechanism built for a class of problem. Third, actions.rs:206-267 is r1 A7-4's write-before-mutate fix and mod.rs:206-232 is r2 A9-2's retained-forward carry-forward; with nothing left writing sess.wired_forwards, those readers plus session/reconcile.rs:902-941 and stop.rs's restore path become permanently dead code that this proposal does not remove. Also note parity/src/lib.rs:2088-2095 pins 'wired mode: adb forward' and 'a later non-wired run clears these two' with the site attributed to stages::run::actions::adb_forward_hygiene — the fragments survive in run.sh, but the attribution becomes a lie.
