T3 · scope-cut · **-437 lines** (reviewer claimed -430) · from V2 — stages setup/build/install/stop + fixes

Delete session_json.rs, remove its module declaration and machine-global wineserver helper, and make the retained DeleteSessionJson dispatch return a concise `withheld; edit pinned IPs in place` error. Keep FixAction::DeleteSessionJson, its contract spelling, and DEFERRED_CONTRACT_FIX_IDS as a wire-compatible tombstone. This removes an entire implementation whose own header says it is known broken and whose only supported GUI door already refuses it.

*Files.* `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/fixes/session_json.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/fixes/mod.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/fixes/backend.rs`

*Evidence.* fixes/session_json.rs:4-14 says deletion can produce an 800x900 black screen and is withheld from the GUI; its implementation is fixes/session_json.rs:67-166 and its nine-test module is fixes/session_json.rs:168-436. fixes/mod.rs:68-74 describes it as withheld but directly reachable, and fixes/mod.rs:486 still dispatches it. The actual GUI refusal is enforced at sabrage/src-tauri/src/commands.rs:1164-1169, while the CLI command list at sabrage/crates/sabrage-cli/src/main.rs:83-120 contains no fix command. fixes/backend.rs:226-230 exists solely for this machine-global fix.

*What breaks.* A direct external caller of sabrage_core::fixes::apply_holding_lock(DeleteSessionJson) can no longer back up and delete the file. The deliberately lost assertions are absent-file no-op, backup-before-delete, dry-run planning, collision-safe backup naming, executor-mode precedence, and any-wineserver refusal. The enum, deferred registry cardinality, serde spelling, GUI refusal, and contract identity remain.

*Blast radius.* Three owned Rust files; no contract or shell change. Tauri continues to compile against the retained enum. Comments in commands.rs:1192-1198 claiming an explicit CLI escape hatch are already inconsistent with the enumerated CLI commands and should not drive retention of 436 dead/known-bad lines.

*How you would know it worked.* Check that no production reference to session_json remains, all contract/deferred registry tests still pass, Tauri still deserializes the retained action, and direct dispatch returns the new withheld error without filesystem access.

*Verifier.* The premise checks out: session_json.rs is 436 lines, the only production caller is the fixes/mod.rs:486 dispatch arm, backend.rs:229's any_wineserver_alive exists solely for it (its one other reference is a single test line at backend.rs:528), the GUI refuses it at commands.rs:1163-1169 via FixAction::is_deferred, and sabrage-cli has no `fix` subcommand at all (USAGE lists doctor/setup/build/install/run/stop/all) — so the escape hatch the fixes/mod.rs:74 comment and the commands.rs refusal string both advertise does not exist today. Measured: 436 + ~8 (any_wineserver_alive and its doc) + 1 (backend test line) + 1 (`pub mod session_json;`) minus ~4 lines of new withheld-error arm and ~5 lines of test repointing = about -437 against a claimed -430. It touches no contract file (cfg.session-pins keeps `fix = "fix.delete-session-json"`, the enum, the serde spelling and DEFERRED_CONTRACT_FIX_IDS all stay, so the cardinality tests at mod.rs:727/757/790 still pass) and no golden bytes. It does not revert r1 A4-2 — the deferral is the fix and it survives — but it goes past what the review decided ('until the in-place pin editor lands') and past what the ledger records: PARITY.md:120 says 'only the destructive one-click action is withheld', which stops being true, so that row needs rewriting. The one under-described cost is test collateral: fixes/mod.rs deliberately uses DeleteSessionJson as its side-effect-free probe action (the doc at mod.rs:520-524 says so), and two tests expect Ok from the dispatch — apply_waits_for_the_operation_lock_then_proceeds (mod.rs:561, 'an absent session.json is a no-op, not an error') and apply_holding_lock_is_not_gated_by_a_live_session (mod.rs:672). Those pin A4-1/A11-2 lock behaviour and there is no obviously equivalent substitute action that stays off the machine (RemoveAdbForwards may find a real adb; RestageHelper errors; EditProtocol writes config). Removing a capability is the owner's call, so: SCOPE_DECISION.

#### `H6-1` — Generate the TypeScript wire bindings from the serde types

T2 · replace-with-dep · **-385 lines** (reviewer claimed -410) · from H6 — type and wire-surface sprawl

Replace the hand-written response/event declarations in ipc.ts with a serde-aware compile-time binding generator such as ts-rs or Specta. Keep hand-written invoke wrappers, ergonomic request aliases, FIX_META, and the Settings/LaunchDefaults unknown-key index signatures; this proposal replaces type transcription, not UI policy. Generate before dev/build/check and do not add a runtime get-types IPC command. The dependency is build-time only, so it does not violate the core crate's stated rejection of runtime archive/network dependencies for shell-parity reasons.

*Files.* `sabrage/ui/src/ipc.ts`, `sabrage/ui/package.json`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/Cargo.toml`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/events.rs`, `sabrage/src-tauri/src/commands.rs`

*Evidence.* sabrage/ui/src/ipc.ts:1 explicitly describes a hand-mirrored IPC boundary; large declarations begin with StageEvent at ipc.ts:116, CheckOutcome at ipc.ts:183, SessionStatus at ipc.ts:526, log types at ipc.ts:700, runtime-config types at ipc.ts:843, settings at ipc.ts:945, RepoInfo at ipc.ts:1009, and library types at ipc.ts:1039. Their Rust sources already carry serde definitions, including events.rs:180, session/mod.rs:119, runtime_toml.rs:243, and commands.rs:107. package.json:6 currently has no binding-generation step. sabrage-core/Cargo.toml:16 and Cargo.toml:23 explain that the deliberately absent dependencies concern runtime parity and a hand-rolled output splitter, not compile-time type export. The measured declaration regions total about 504 lines; allowing roughly 90–95 lines for generator setup, export annotations, and retained manual aliases yields approximately 410 removed lines.

*What breaks.* A clean UI build breaks if binding generation is not wired into every dev/build/check entry point. The generator must reproduce serde tag, rename, flatten, default, UUID, nullable, and omitted-field semantics exactly; in particular, generated Settings and LaunchDefaults types need manual extension for preserved unknown keys. An external consumer importing declarations directly from ipc.ts may need a new generated-module import. Runtime JSON and behavior should not change.

*Blast radius.* Touches most Rust types that cross Tauri and the UI import surface. Golden bytes, StageEvent parity, registry order, FIX_META behavior, and the contract remain unchanged. Its line estimate is standalone and overlaps the TS portions of H6-3, H6-4, H6-6, and H6-7, so the estimates must not be summed blindly.

*How you would know it worked.* Generate from a clean checkout, run the generator twice and require a byte-identical second result, then run Cargo tests, the UI type/Svelte checks, and parity tier 1. Diff representative generated shapes for StageEvent, CheckOutcome, SessionStatus, Settings, RuntimeConfigView, and library records against their present declarations; verify no new runtime command exists.

*Verifier.* I measured the declaration surface myself with a block scanner over sabrage/ui/src/ipc.ts: 51 exported interfaces/type aliases spanning ~538 lines including their doc comments (the reviewer's ~504 is honest; my number is higher because their scan under-counted the StageEvent union, which actually runs 111-172). I then checked every named TS type for a Rust counterpart — GameRow, GameStatus, GameValidity, LaunchOverrides, LastSession, GameEntry, GoldbergState, BsDirSuggestion, RepoInfo, RevertReport, QuitChoice, QueuedStage, LaunchOpts, StageRunOpts, FixRunOpts, RuntimeConfigValues/View, WriteReport, InvalidValue, Settings, LaunchDefaults, EncoderInfo, LogBatch, PastRun, LogSource all resolve to a Rust struct/enum; only RunDoctorArgs and RuntimeConfigPatch are TS-only. So the premise (this is transcription, not policy) holds and the -410 is not inflated: subtracting the ~53 lines of hand-retained types plus ~55-80 Rust export annotations and ~25 lines of wiring lands around -385. The reviewer's dependency argument also checks out: sabrage-core/Cargo.toml's "Deliberately NOT added" comments (lines 16-23) are about reqwest/flate2/tar and tokio_util::codec for runtime shell-parity reasons, not about compile-time type export, and neither PARITY.md nor CLAUDE.md declares the hand-mirror as an invariant; sabrage/docs/design/design-app.md:27 only labels ipc.ts "hand-mirrored". Codegen is even an established pattern here (sabrage-contract-gen writes scripts/demo/contract.gen.sh). What stops me short of ACCEPT_WITH_CARE is three things I verified. First, sabrage/ui has zero tests — package.json has only dev/build/check/tauri, no vitest, no *.test.ts anywhere under git ls-files — so every serde-fidelity subtlety the generator must reproduce is guarded by nothing but svelte-check, and svelte-check cannot catch a consumer-side mistranslation (StageEvent's `end?: ChunkEnd`, which mirrors `#[serde(default)]`, still typechecks if it is generated as required). Second, the Settings/LaunchDefaults index signatures the proposal promises to keep are the landed fix for CONFIRMED A13b-8 and its still-open round-2 successor A13b-4 ("Nested future launch settings are still stripped on downgrade") — ipc.ts fix-blame ranges 947-955, 961-973, 975-976, 987-989 are exactly those lines — so those two types cannot be generated at all and must stay hand-written and hand-reconciled forever. Third, a large share of what sits inside the declaration blocks is landmine doc comment with no Rust counterpart (DoctorEvent.fix's "resolve through contractFixIdToAction before offering a Fix button" at ipc.ts:18-25, SessionPhase's `external` paragraph at 444-449, CheckOutcome's "distinct from DoctorEvent" note at 178-182); generating the types either loses them or forces them into Rust doc comments, which converts a line removal into a line move. Finally, the hard rules forbid me from running the generator or a build, and this proposal's viability genuinely turns on whether ts-rs/Specta reproduces internally-tagged StageEvent + rename_all_fields, flatten, serde(default), serde(skip), and Uuid exactly — so I could not rate it above ACCEPT_WITH_CARE even on the merits. Nothing here touches golden bytes, the contract, PARITY.md, scripts/ (parity.sh runs cargo only, never npm), or the shell fingerprint.

#### `V1-3` — Make run preflight check-only; require Setup or Build for repairs

T4 · scope-cut · **-380 lines** (reviewer claimed -360) · from V1 — stages/run — launch state machine

A launcher with dedicated setup/build stages does not strictly need launch-time permanent repairs. Treat failed `Autofix` gates as blocking checks with their existing remedies, deleting the fix dispatch, recheck, dry-run simulation, failure translation, and AutoFixed event path.

*Files.* `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/run/preflight.rs`

*Evidence.* The walk's special dispatch is at `preflight.rs:298-304`; all auto-fix machinery occupies `preflight.rs:701-879`. Its dedicated tests occupy `preflight.rs:1729-1813`, `preflight.rs:1979-2082`, and `preflight.rs:2132-2149`. The current contract-visible promise—fix, recheck, then emit one final Check—is stated at `preflight.rs:17-20`.

*What breaks.* A stale graphics-backend line or unstaged helper would stop Launch and require Setup/Build instead of being repaired automatically. This deliberately removes AutoFixed ordering and post-fix failure assertions. Because the contract marks these native gates `autofix` and the shell still repairs them, an honest implementation requires an out-of-scope contract/PARITY policy change; otherwise reject this proposal.

*Blast radius.* Production and tests in `preflight.rs`; externally, contract gate semantics, parity renderers, and GUI AutoFixed presentation are involved. No byte-golden launch argv/env/banner behavior is involved.

*How you would know it worked.* Replace auto-fix tests with blocking-remedy tests, then run contract/parity checks and confirm Setup/Build followed by Run succeeds while a direct stale Run fails before any preparation mutation.

*Verifier.* Regions verified: the dispatch is at preflight.rs:298-304 (`match spec.native_gate { Gate::Autofix => autofix(...) , _ => gate(...) }`), and 709-879 is autofix + fix_failed + gate_after_fix + apply_fix + post_fix_die; the module header's promise ('autofix → apply the slug's fix, re-check, and only then decide … that is the outcome of the re-check, preceded by the AutoFixed event') is at lines 8-20 as claimed. The cited test ranges line up with the autofix tests. ~-380, so the -360 estimate is honest, arguably conservative. It is implementable — the gate vocabulary already supports per-side values (contract/pipeline.toml:109-110, 255-256, 262-263 are the only three `autofix` rows, all `shell_gate = "autofix"` AND `native_gate = "autofix"`), so flipping the native column to `block` is a legal contract edit — but the edit is in contract/, which is out of scope, hence T4. What is actually lost is not scaffolding: `bottle.gfx-dxmt`'s fix uses `set_graphics_backend_for_launch` specifically because run rewrites cxbottle.conf right before the `wineserver-reset` launch action kills that wineserver (documented on apply_fix), and CLAUDE.md records both trigger conditions as live recurrences ('a CrossOver update silently reverts the global overlays'; 'run self-heals by restaging from build-helper-arm64'). Two smaller consequences to price in: post_fix_die is `pub (A1-3)` and is called for real by sabrage-parity/src/lib.rs:2011-2019 (inside the A1-3 fix-blame range), so its run.sh text pin disappears with it; and with the gate flipped to block, `block_die` has no arm for these three slugs, so they fall through to the `_ => (outcome.message, outcome.remedy)` arm and the die text becomes the check's own words rather than run.sh's — a new native-only sentence needing its own PARITY row. r1 A7-1 (CONFIRMED, 'Live-session refusal occurs after permanent autofixes') is not reverted by this — the refusal stays ahead of everything — it simply loses its subject.

#### `C1-5` — Remove public dry-run while retaining DryRunExecutor as test infrastructure

T3 · scope-cut · **-300 lines** (reviewer claimed -500) · from C1 — completeness critic

H7-6 was rejected at the wrong unit: it coupled deletion of the product mode to deletion of the hermetic recorder that tests and parity require. Remove only the CLI flag, GUI Plan buttons, IPC dryRun fields, and user-facing plan rendering. Keep DryRunExecutor, StageCtx::for_fixture, executor plans, privilege argv assertions, and all mutation-safety tests as internal infrastructure. This is narrower than H3-5 and preserves the exact assertion that killed H7-6.

*Files.* `sabrage/crates/sabrage-cli/src/main.rs`, `sabrage/src-tauri/src/commands.rs`, `sabrage/ui/src/ipc.ts`, `sabrage/ui/src/components/StagesPanel.svelte`, `sabrage/ui/src/components/GateModal.svelte`, `sabrage/ui/src/screens/Session.svelte`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/executor.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/mod.rs`

*Evidence.* CLI help exposes the product feature at main.rs:116; parsing lives at :634-679 and rendering at :929-1022. Tauri carries dry_run in DTOs at commands.rs:311-344 and emits plan rows at :625-643; StagesPanel.svelte:102-111 passes the UI choice. The load-bearing internal seam is separate: stages/mod.rs:263-276 constructs a forced-dry fixture context, and executor.rs:1696-1766 asserts that dry-run mutates nothing. The privileged-write dry-run assertion cited by H7-6 consumes that internal recorder, not a human-facing button.

*What breaks.* Users can no longer preview stage writes, spawns, or admin argv from the CLI/GUI. Internal tests still plan operations, but there is no supported product path for inspecting those plans interactively.

*Blast radius.* CLI grammar/help, Tauri request types, GateModal/StagesPanel/Session launch controls, and plan-rendering tests. DryRunExecutor, executor mutation invariants, privilege tests, parity recorders, and golden bytes remain.

*How you would know it worked.* Confirm --dry-run is absent/rejected and no UI control can send dryRun; retain all internal DryRunExecutor and privilege assertions; run the same stage tests against forced fixture contexts; and verify production StageCtx creation always selects RealExecutor.

#### `H2-1` — Delete the static About microsite

T3 · scope-cut · **-221 lines** (reviewer claimed -222) · from H2 — YAGNI and unused surface

A personal launcher does not need a 210-line in-app credits and pipeline-explainer screen, especially one whose Setup button is permanently disabled. Delete About.svelte and its navigation/switch wiring, and start on Library instead.

*Files.* `sabrage/ui/src/screens/About.svelte`, `sabrage/ui/src/App.svelte`, `sabrage/ui/src/components/Sidebar.svelte`, `sabrage/ui/src/types.ts`

*Evidence.* sabrage/ui/src/screens/About.svelte:1 begins a self-contained 210-line screen; sabrage/ui/src/screens/About.svelte:32 defines six static upstream cards; sabrage/ui/src/screens/About.svelte:125 renders a disabled Setup button; sabrage/ui/src/App.svelte:7 and sabrage/ui/src/App.svelte:87 are its only shell wiring; sabrage/ui/src/components/Sidebar.svelte:65 supplies its only navigation entry; sabrage/ui/src/types.ts:6 supplies the screen variant.

*What breaks.* The app would open on Library instead of About and would lose visible pipeline orientation, upstream credits, license labels, and the disabled Setup placeholder. Confirm separately that distribution does not rely on this screen for required attribution.

*Blast radius.* Four frontend files; no Rust, IPC, contract, parity, golden bytes, or review-pinned tests are involved.

*How you would know it worked.* After implementation, require zero About-screen references with rg, run the frontend type check, launch the app, and verify Library is the initial screen and every remaining sidebar route renders.

*Verifier.* Measured: About.svelte is exactly 210 lines and fully self-contained (no imports beyond the Screen type, no store, no IPC). App.svelte loses 3 lines (import :7, the `{#if screen === "about"}` guard at :87 and `<About …>` at :88; :89 becomes the opening `{#if}`, and :20's initial state is a one-word change to "library"). Sidebar.svelte loses the 8-line NavItem block at :65-72. types.ts is a 0-line change (one union member off line 6). Total -221 against a claimed -222 — the estimate is honest. Review history: fix-blame.tsv gives About.svelte and types.ts 0 review-fix lines; App.svelte's fix ranges are 26-39, 69-75, 94, 96 (the launchRequest/doctorRequest menu plumbing) and Sidebar's are 2, 4, 10-13, 34, 53-54, 127 (the status-dot/PHASE_DOT area) — the About wiring lines are outside every one of them. findings-per-file.tsv shows App.svelte cited by 2 confirmed findings and Sidebar by none; grepping both round-1 and round-2 reports for About turns up only round1:1302, which mentions About incidentally as a manual verification step for the menu-id finding, not as a requirement. Invariants: no golden bytes, no contract slug, no parity artifact, no PARITY.md row, no Rust at all. There is no frontend test runner (package.json has dev/build/check/tauri only; round1:1468 and the round-2 A13b-1 block both say so explicitly, and there is no *.test.ts anywhere under sabrage/ui/src), so no assertion is lost. Two residues the owner should price in: sabrage/docs/design/design-app.md:29/:144/:233 documents About as a real Phase-0 screen (docs are out of edit scope here, so they simply go stale), and the screen is the only place the six upstream projects and their licenses are named in the shipped app. That is a capability removal, not a mechanical cleanup, which is why this is the owner's call rather than an ACCEPT.

#### `H7-4` — Remove the full-screen About experience

T3 · scope-cut · **-220 lines** (reviewer claimed -225) · from H7 — feature surface — what a personal launcher does not need

Delete the 210-line static pipeline diagram and upstream-card screen, its route, and sidebar entry; start on Session instead. Tauri already creates the native macOS About item. This has essentially no operational launcher value, although the owner must confirm where required third-party notices remain before accepting the cut.

*Files.* `sabrage/ui/src/screens/About.svelte`, `sabrage/ui/src/App.svelte`, `sabrage/ui/src/components/Sidebar.svelte`, `sabrage/ui/src/types.ts`

*Evidence.* The screen is static project descriptions, license labels, and CSS at sabrage/ui/src/screens/About.svelte:1-210; it is the initial route at sabrage/ui/src/App.svelte:20 and rendered at sabrage/ui/src/App.svelte:87-89. A native About menu item already exists at sabrage/src-tauri/src/lib.rs:17-19.

*What breaks.* The in-app architecture diagram, upstream credits, license labels, and disabled Setup teaser disappear. Native About remains, but this recommendation does not establish that it satisfies every redistribution-notice obligation; that must be checked before deletion.

*Blast radius.* One screen and approximately fifteen routing/navigation lines. No tests, parity, golden bytes, or runtime pipeline behavior.

*How you would know it worked.* Confirm the app opens on Session and all navigation still resolves; separately audit packaged license/notice placement before removing the credits.

*Verifier.* Everything the proposal describes is exactly where it says. About.svelte is 210 lines; App.svelte:7 imports it, :20 makes it the initial route, :87-89 renders it; Sidebar.svelte:65-71 is the nav entry (7 lines); types.ts:6 carries the `"about"` variant. Total ~220 against a claim of 225 — the most accurate estimate in this batch. It is also the cleanest on review history: About.svelte has 0 confirmed findings in findings-per-file.tsv and 0 lines in fix-blame.tsv, the only file in this set with both. The App.svelte lines it touches (87-89) are outside that file's fix-blamed ranges (26-39, 69-75, 94, 96), and flipping the initial route from 'about' to 'session' does not intersect A12-5 r2 (Run Doctor as a navigation command, App.svelte:59-66). No contract, parity, golden-byte or fixture invariant is anywhere near it. What stops this being a plain ACCEPT is the thing the proposal itself flags but does not resolve. I read the content: About.svelte:20-79 is a structured upstream credits table naming Wine/CrossOver (LGPL-2.1), DXMT (MIT), oxrsys (MPL-2.0), wineopenxr (LGPL-2.1), ALVR (MIT) and Goldberg (LGPL-3.0), with author attribution and source URLs. This is the only place in the app any of that appears — src-tauri/src/lib.rs:18 is `PredefinedMenuItem::about`, which renders the bundle's own name/version/copyright and nothing else, so it is not a substitute. Given `setup` fetches sha256-pinned DXMT binaries and the Goldberg dll onto the user's machine, whether those notices are obligated and where they should live is a product/licensing judgement, which by the rubric belongs to the owner. If the answer is 'notices move to a packaged NOTICE file or the README', the cut is trivially safe and mechanical.

#### `H2-2` — Remove the per-game launch-override tier

T3 · scope-cut · **-196 lines** (reviewer claimed -204) · from H2 — YAGNI and unused surface

Keep the four global launch defaults and delete the second, per-library-entry global/on/off layer: LaunchOverrides, its persisted field, four EditGame selectors, the client merge, and the now-test-only Rust merge API/tests. This is stronger than the prior effective-options-home proposal: it removes the entire precedence dimension instead of moving its implementation.

*Files.* `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/store/library.rs`, `sabrage/ui/src/screens/EditGame.svelte`, `sabrage/ui/src/screens/Library.svelte`, `sabrage/ui/src/ipc.ts`

*Evidence.* sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/store/library.rs:38 defines the four-field optional LaunchOverrides type; sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/store/library.rs:164 exposes the otherwise test-only lookup; sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/store/library.rs:331 implements the merge; sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/store/library.rs:1034 devotes 59 test lines to that precedence; sabrage/ui/src/screens/EditGame.svelte:149 contains 38 lines of three-way selector state and sabrage/ui/src/screens/EditGame.svelte:455 contains the 38-line control card; sabrage/ui/src/screens/Library.svelte:109 performs the live client-side merge; sabrage/ui/src/ipc.ts:1039 mirrors the type over IPC.

*What breaks.* Users could no longer make audio, dashboard, wired, or verbose flags differ by library entry; all entries would use Settings. This is not equal assertion coverage: the removed assertions are that Some overrides beat global values, None falls through, and lookup of an unknown game ID returns None. The A13b-5 last-session race assertion must remain unchanged.

*Blast radius.* Library JSON loses launchOverrides on the next save, while older files remain readable because unknown fields are ignored. Four implementation files and the library unit tests change; pipeline flags, parity behavior, golden bytes, and global Settings remain.

*Precaution.* If it lands: rewrite store/mod.rs:20-22, which carries a real intra-doc link to [`library::effective_options`]/[`library::Library::launch_options_for`] (and settings.rs:44's backticked mention), or rustdoc breaks; and accept that saved per-game overrides are dropped on the next write of an existing library.json.

*How you would know it worked.* Load a pre-cut library.json containing launchOverrides, save it successfully without those keys, then launch from Library with each global flag both true and false. Run the core library tests and frontend checks, retaining the last-session race tests.

*Verifier.* Measured by region: library.rs loses the LaunchOverrides struct + doc (38-48, 12 lines with its blank), the field at :73, launch_options_for's doc+body (163-175, 13), the template initialiser at :326, effective_options' doc+body (330-345, ~16), the two merge tests at 1036-1092 (57) and ~5 scattered test-setup/assert lines (:646, :724, :737, :935, :943, :991) = ~105. EditGame.svelte loses the three-way selector block at 149-186 (38) and the Launch-overrides card at 455-491 (37) = 75. ipc.ts loses the LaunchOverrides interface + doc at 1039-1047 and the field at :1067 = ~11. Library.svelte is roughly net-zero (the four `entry.launchOverrides.X ??` operands collapse to `global?.X ?? false`), minus the 4-line mirror comment at 77-80. Total ~-196 vs a claimed -204: honest. Review history is where this gets interesting. fix-blame.tsv puts library.rs:163-175 and 1066-1093 squarely inside review-fix ranges, and `git log -S'launch_options_for' master..HEAD` pins them to 6d98864 (review-r1 wave 1) — i.e. the S-A13a-effective-options-home item in sabrage/docs/reviews/2026-08-30-simplify.md, whose recorded resolution was 'core owns the merge; the TS copy is what gets deleted'. That resolution is only half-landed: nothing outside tests calls launch_options_for (grep over `git ls-files sabrage`), commands.rs uses LaunchOpts.gameId only for record_last_session (:720-725, :2216-2264), and Library.svelte:77-80 still declares the client the source of truth. So H2-2 does not revert a bug fix — it deletes the duplication that S-A13a was about, which is a legitimate alternative resolution — but it does discard a deliberate review-fix seam, and that is the owner's call to make knowingly. No CONFIRMED Codex finding is touched: A13b-5 (upsert_editable) keeps its last_session preservation; the round-2 A13b-1 quarantine fix (Library.svelte fix range 82-100, the `!settingsStore.loadOk` guard) is untouched and still needed, since the `?? false` fallbacks survive. Invariants: no golden bytes, no contract slug, no parity fixture, nothing in PARITY.md, no library.json fixture anywhere (tests/fixtures/phase4 holds only oxrsys-runtime TOMLs) and sabrage-parity never mentions GameEntry. One data note the proposal states correctly but understates: GameEntry is a closed serde struct with no deny_unknown_fields, so old files still load, but the first save after the cut silently eats every stored launchOverrides value — and library.rs's own module doc (`load`'s comment) makes exactly that silent-eat the reason LIBRARY_VERSION exists.

*Evidence.* launch allocates a tapped Arc<Mutex<Option<LaunchedInfo>>> and observes Launched solely for persistence at commands.rs:694-725. The dedicated decision/writer occupies commands.rs:2196-2269, with its regression fixture at commands.rs:2916-2952. The visible consequence is the Library's Last Session timestamp and exit/log detail, consumed at ui/src/screens/Library.svelte:157-173 and rendered at ui/src/screens/Library.svelte:302 and ui/src/screens/Library.svelte:336-337.

*What breaks.* New GUI launches no longer update `GameEntry.last_session`; the Library's Last Session column remains `—` for new entries or shows stale historical data for existing entries, and exit code/log-path history is no longer recorded. Launching, streaming events, validation, and library CRUD continue.

*Blast radius.* commands.rs launch plumbing and its Last Session tests. Existing library.json schema remains compatible; no contract, shell, parity, or artifact-golden bytes are involved. Core LastSession code remains because it is outside this proposal's owned files.

*How you would know it worked.* Run `cargo test -p sabrage-app`, invoke a library-originated launch in a disposable profile, verify the normal StageEvent stream and outcome are unchanged, and verify library.json is no longer rewritten when the session settles.

*Verifier.* The measurement is the most accurate of the four. Removing the tee in `launch` (694-712 plus the record block 716-726) is -30, `channel_sink_tee` and its doc (401-415) is -16, the last-session section (2196-2269: LaunchedInfo, last_session_to_record, record_last_session) is -74, and its test (2916-2952) is -38, totalling about -158 against a claimed -160. Nothing in the golden set is involved: no host XR JSON, toml template, cxbottle line, steam_appid.txt, .sha256 marker, launch banner/env/argv, win_path table, registry order, contract slug or shell fingerprint, and library.json stays schema-compatible. It does not directly revert a CONFIRMED finding — A13b-5's fix (`upsert_editable` preserving server-owned fields) and A13a-3's fix (`library::transact`) both live in sabrage-core/store/library.rs and would survive untouched. But it removes a real user-visible capability and leaves three loose ends in files the proposal does not own. library.rs:130-137 justifies `upsert_editable` entirely with 'the launch path writes last_session behind its back (Library::record_last_session), so a plain whole-entry upsert would delete a session that ended while the form was open' — with no writer left, that rationale reads as obsolete and the next editor has a standing invitation to delete A13b-5's fix. Library.svelte:128-138 refreshes the library on launch settlement specifically because 'the backend has already written this run's lastSession into library.json (commands.rs awaits record_last_session before returning)' — that refresh becomes a pointless round trip. And lastSessionCell/lastSessionDetail at Library.svelte:157-173, rendered at :302 and :336-337, would show '—' and 'No sessions yet' forever for new entries while showing stale data for existing ones. `Library::record_last_session` plus its two tests in library.rs also become dead code. The trade: -158 lines in commands.rs, buying a dead column in the GUI and a stranded invariant next to a confirmed fix; demo.sh never had this feature at all, so no parity obligation is broken either way. That is a product call, not mine.

#### `V3-5` — Cut auxiliary and historical log browsing

T3 · scope-cut · **-139 lines** (reviewer claimed -205) · from V3 — session lifecycle + logs

A personal launcher can keep the live wine console and pipeline output without also being a three-source log browser. Remove OxrsysRuntime, AlvrSession, arbitrary File selection, PastRun metadata, and list_past_runs; replace the WineConsole fallback with a private newest-wine-log path lookup. The runtime monitor may continue using Tailer internally, so encoder/session safety behavior is untouched. The owner must decide whether direct OXRSys/ALVR inspection and the past-run picker justify this surface.

*Files.* `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/logs.rs`

*Evidence.* The optional source model occupies logs.rs:96-150, including three non-live-wine variants at :111-118. Historical-run metadata and enumeration occupy logs.rs:618-668. Their wire, resolution, and listing tests occupy logs.rs:798-988—about 190 lines before retaining the smaller live-wine priority tests.

*What breaks.* The Logs screen loses the OXRSys runtime tab, ALVR session tab, past-run list, and opening arbitrary historical files. Existing non-owned Tauri commands and TypeScript/Svelte consumers would require coordinated removal before this compiles; this is an intentional product cut, not equal coverage.

*Blast radius.* Owned core change is logs.rs, but the decision propagates to the log IPC commands and Logs UI. Wine-log naming parity and Tailer’s A8 regression behavior remain; no contract or shell changes are required.

*How you would know it worked.* After owner-approved consumer changes, verify the live wine console resolves the live handle, persisted session, then newest wine log in that order; confirm stage output remains visible and all watcher/encoder Tailer tests still pass.

*Verifier.* The proposal describes the code accurately. LogSource lives at logs.rs:96-118 with three non-live-wine variants, resolve_source at :120-150, and PastRun/list_past_runs at :618-668. Consumers are real and outside the owned file: src-tauri/src/commands.rs:1526 (start_log_tail, whose from_end flag is keyed off LogSource::AlvrSession because that file is unbounded), :1581 list_past_runs, :1591 get_log_source_path, registered at src-tauri/src/lib.rs:189, and ui/src/screens/Logs.svelte:14-20 where the four tabs and the past-run list live. So this cannot land in logs.rs alone; it is a product decision with a cross-layer edit behind it, which is exactly why SCOPE_DECISION rather than ACCEPT. On the safety side I found nothing that blocks it: all four review findings that cite logs.rs (A8-3, A8-4, A8-6, A8-7) are Tailer semantics, which the proposal keeps and the runtime monitor keeps using; the wine-log naming parity rule (PARITY.md:86) and its tests are untouched; nothing under contract/, scripts/ or demo.sh is involved. My line count is lower than the claim: the removable production is ~-62 (the enum and resolve_source collapsing into one wine_console_path of ~25 lines, and PastRun/list_past_runs giving way to an ~18-line newest-log scan), and of the ~190 test lines cited only ~77 actually go, because the five resolve_source WineConsole priority tests at logs.rs:853-968 are retained in adapted form — so -139, roughly 50 percent below the -205 claimed. The trade to weigh: the two removed tabs are the only in-app view of ~/Library/Application Support/OXRSys/oxrsys-runtime.log and alvr/session_log.txt, neither of which demo.sh surfaces either, so the fallback is opening them in a terminal.

#### `V2-5` — Drop derived Ninja progress events and keep raw build output

T3 · scope-cut · **-138 lines** (reviewer claimed -115) · from V2 — stages setup/build/install/stop + fixes

Replace both run_ninja_build_ok calls with run_child_ok and delete the parser, second sink, and progress-specific tests. The wrapper currently forwards every raw event unchanged and adds only a derived StageEvent::Progress, so build diagnostics remain available in the ordinary output stream.

*Files.* `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/build.rs`

*Evidence.* build.rs:309-312 explicitly calls the parser a decoration that is never load-bearing. The dedicated parser is build.rs:313-320, the duplicate execution path is build.rs:378-412, and only two calls use it at build.rs:525-536 and build.rs:567-578. Progress-specific coverage occupies build.rs:697-727, build.rs:1303-1310, and build.rs:1348-1394; build.rs:408 shows raw events are forwarded unchanged.

*What breaks.* The UI loses derived Ninja current/total progress and its progress-bar updates. The intentionally lost assertions are parser acceptance/rejection cases and the emitted Progress tuples; raw compiler/Ninja output and failure handling remain.

*Blast radius.* build.rs only; StageEvent::Progress remains available for other producers. No build argv, dry-run plan, golden output, contract, or shell behavior changes.

*How you would know it worked.* Confirm both Ninja builds use run_child_ok, real child output still reaches the sink, dry-run still records Spawn plans, and no build test expects Ninja Progress events.

*Verifier.* Everything cited exists: parse_ninja_progress at build.rs:307-321, run_ninja_build_ok at 377-412, the two call sites at 525 and 567, and the three test blocks at 696-726, 1302-1310, 1347-1393; adding the now-stale '# Ninja progress' module-doc section at 61-75 the proposal does not mention, I measure about -138, so if anything the claim understates. Two things the proposal's what_breaks understates. First, I grepped for Progress producers and consumers across the workspace: build.rs is the ONLY producer, the CLI renders it as vec![] (main.rs:760), and the sole consumer is GateModal.svelte:88/251/514-526, which draws a determinate bar from label/current/total. So this does not just 'lose derived Ninja progress' — it makes the Progress variant, the CLI arm and the GUI bar dead code, and the GUI loses its only quantitative feedback during the longest operation in the pipeline. Second, it is not purely behaviour-preserving on the error path: run_ninja_build_ok calls process::run_ok, which attaches the last CHILD_TAIL_LINES chunks to ChildFailed (process.rs:311-330), while run_child_ok goes through Executor::run_child -> spawn_streamed (no tail) and constructs ChildFailed with tail: Vec::new(). A failing oxrsys or helper build would stop carrying its tail. The module doc argues that tail buys nothing because the lines already streamed, and build.rs's own test 'run_child_ok_maps_a_real_failure_to_child_failed_with_no_tail' shows empty-tail is the file's chosen shape — so this is defensible, just not silent. Dry-run behaviour genuinely is unchanged (the wrapper already delegates to run_child_ok there and the planned-Spawn bookkeeping is identical), and no contract, argv, golden byte or shell behaviour moves. Removing a shipped GUI capability is the owner's call.

#### `V2-4` — Remove setup's duplicate Beat Saber diagnostic step

T3 · scope-cut · **-122 lines** (reviewer claimed -124) · from V2 — stages setup/build/install/stop + fixes

Delete setup_game and its four tests, and stop calling it from setup::run. Setup should fetch/build prerequisites and create configuration; game presence, version, and DepotDownloader remedy already belong to Doctor, while Run performs the stronger steam_api64 completeness gate immediately before mutation/launch.

*Files.* `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/setup.rs`

*Evidence.* setup.rs:100 invokes the diagnostic; setup.rs:366-395 only checks Beat Saber.exe and emits advice; setup.rs:506-598 spends 93 lines testing its four output branches. The step itself admits Doctor owns verification at setup.rs:370-373. Doctor's dedicated game checks include presence and the same depot remedy at sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/checks/game.rs:40-63, and Run checks the more operationally relevant steam_api64 prerequisite at sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/run/actions.rs:492-495.

*What breaks.* Setup no longer prints found/missing/skipped game rows, the DepotDownloader command, or an error for a nonexistent --bottle supplied only for this check. That deliberately diverges from setup.sh and leaves step::SETUP_GAME un-emitted; the four exact-output assertions are intentionally lost. Doctor and Run retain the actual guards.

*Blast radius.* setup.rs only. No setup mutation changes. Shell-output parity and the PARITY.md ledger are involved, so the owner must accept this as a deliberate front-end scope difference; no contract edit is required.

*How you would know it worked.* Run setup tests after removing the four diagnostic cases, then confirm Doctor still reports game.present/game.version and Run still refuses an incomplete install.

*Verifier.* Measured: setup_game is setup.rs:368-395 (28 lines with its section banner), the call at setup.rs:100, the module-doc bullet at 26-27, and the four tests at 506-598 (93 lines) = about -122 against a claimed -124, so the estimate is honest and the region exists as described. The claim that the guards survive elsewhere is true: checks/game.rs:40-63 emits the same 'Beat Saber 1.29.4 not found at <dir>' text with contract().depot_command as its remedy, and run/preflight.rs:649 uses depot_command too, so depot_command does not become dead. Nothing in sabrage-parity pins setup's rows (the crate's only setup coverage is the pinned-asset and BS_DIR resolution comparison at lib.rs:92-118), and leaving step::SETUP_GAME declared keeps events.rs's 28-step-id test green, with no UI consumer of the step list. But scripts/demo/setup.sh:57-70 really does emit these four rows, and setup.sh line 59's `require_bottle` really does die on a bogus bottle name — so this is a visible output divergence from the shell in the one stage where the two are otherwise row-for-row, and PARITY.md's Setup table currently declares exactly one divergence. That makes it a deliberate front-end scope decision (and a PARITY.md edit), not a mechanical cleanup. No mutation, no byte contract and no golden fixture is touched.

#### `V4-6` — Reduce the generator binary to the one workflow scripts use

T3 · scope-cut · **-60 lines** (reviewer claimed -55) · from V4 — checks + contract + generator

Keep the generator library, `check()`, all golden/security tests, and the `--write` behavior used by `scripts/dev/parity.sh --regen`. Reduce `main.rs` to accept only `--write` against `compiled_repo_root`, removing the hand-written multi-mode parser, `--check`, `--repo-root`, help text, and demo.sh-style usage exit codes. Tier-1 already calls the library's byte comparison directly, so the binary need not be a second check UI.

*Files.* `sabrage/crates/sabrage-contract-gen/src/main.rs`

*Evidence.* The 94-line shim spends `main.rs:16` through `main.rs:54` defining usage, modes, and argument parsing, then duplicates check/write presentation branches at `main.rs:56`. The load-bearing comparison remains in the library at `sabrage/crates/sabrage-contract-gen/src/lib.rs:334`, and the committed byte-golden remains at `lib.rs:377`. The repository automation invokes only `--write` at `scripts/dev/parity.sh:132`.

*What breaks.* Manual `cargo run -p sabrage-contract-gen -- --check`, custom `--repo-root`, and `--help` stop working; existing documentation describing those options becomes historical until separately updated. `scripts/dev/parity.sh --regen` must continue accepting the retained `--write` spelling.

*Blast radius.* Only the generator executable CLI. Generator output, library API, contract files, shell scripts, parity tests, golden bytes, and hostile-value quoting are unchanged.

*Precaution.* If the owner takes it: keep the literal `--write` spelling parity.sh:133 passes; reword the four stale --check mentions in the same crate (Cargo.toml:8, lib.rs:10, :307, :322) in the same commit; and leave sabrage-contract-gen::check() public — sabrage-parity/src/lib.rs:140 is its only remaining caller and it is the tier-1 staleness gate.

*How you would know it worked.* Run the retained `scripts/dev/parity.sh --regen` workflow in a disposable clean checkout and confirm unchanged output, then run the generator/parity tests that call library `check()` and `generate()` directly.

*Verifier.* MEASUREMENT: sabrage/crates/sabrage-contract-gen/src/main.rs is exactly 94 lines and the region is as described — USAGE at :16-23, enum Mode at :25-28, the hand-rolled arg loop at :34-49, the Check/Write presentation branches at :56-82, fail() at :85-88, usage_error() at :90-94. A minimal --write-only replacement (module doc + two `use` lines + a `main` that calls gen::write(&gen::compiled_repo_root()) and prints wrote/unchanged/error) lands at ~34 lines, so the real delta is about -60; if the usage/exit-2 guard is dropped entirely it is ~-66. The claim of -55 is conservative, not inflated. REVIEW HISTORY: fix-blame.tsv line 72 records `sabrage/crates/sabrage-contract-gen/src/main.rs\t94\t0\t` — zero lines from any of the five review-fix commits, and the empty range column confirms it. `git log --oneline master..HEAD -- .../main.rs` returns only 3c49c14 (Phase 1, the original scaffold); none of 6d98864/73b790d/5cf052a/1e0540f/16fd7a3 touched it. findings-per-file.tsv and findings-index.tsv have no entry for this path, and grepping both round-1 and round-2 review documents finds contract-gen findings only against src/lib.rs (A1-2 at lib.rs:62-101, A1-8 at lib.rs:176-226) — never main.rs. Nothing here reverts or weakens a CONFIRMED fix. INVARIANTS: the generator's *output* is untouched, so the committed scripts/demo/contract.gen.sh, the `# contract-sha256:` header recipe, the zsh_scalar/zsh_word hostile-value encoders (A1-8), the byte-golden `generate_reproduces_the_committed_file_byte_for_byte` (lib.rs:377) and the per-field mutation test (lib.rs:423) all keep working unchanged. sabrage/parity/shell.fingerprint covers demo.sh + scripts/demo/*.sh only — a Rust file cannot dirty it. sabrage/PARITY.md has no row for the generator CLI; its only contract-gen-adjacent row (:111, meta.contract-sync / COMPILED_CONTRACT_SHA256) is about sabrage-core, not this binary. No contract/, scripts/, demo.sh or ext/ change is required: scripts/dev/parity.sh:133 invokes `cargo run -q -p sabrage-contract-gen -- --write` and that spelling is retained; .github/workflows/parity.yml:27-28 and scripts/dev/hooks/pre-push run `cargo test`/`parity.sh --live=off`, never the binary. I grepped every .sh/.yml/.toml/.md/.rs in the repo for `-- --check` and found no invocation anywhere. REDUNDANCY CLAIM VERIFIED: sabrage/crates/sabrage-parity/src/lib.rs:138 `check_reports_in_sync_against_the_live_checkout` calls `sabrage_contract_gen::check(&repo_root())` against the working checkout and asserts `report.in_sync` — byte-for-byte the same work `--check` did, run on every push and in CI. So the CLI flag is genuinely a second UI over covered logic, exactly as the proposal argues. WHAT IS ACTUALLY LOST: `--check` as a standalone one-shot (documented as such in sabrage/docs/reviews/2026-08-30-partition.md:162 and sabrage/docs/design/design-parity.md:53), `--repo-root` for operating on a foreign checkout (used by nothing in-repo; generate_from_repo reads contract/ from disk so it was never a compiled-vs-checkout hazard), `--help`, and the exit-2-on-usage-error convention that main.rs:90 notes as demo.sh parity. All are real capabilities, none is load-bearing. Because a capability disappears rather than being preserved, the rubric puts this at SCOPE_DECISION rather than ACCEPT; the owner should weigh ~60 lines against a dev affordance that is cheap to keep. INCOMPLETE FILE LIST: the proposal lists only main.rs, but four in-crate prose sites would go stale and should land in the same edit — Cargo.toml:8 ('The bin is a thin --write/--check shim over the lib'), lib.rs:10 ('hand edits are caught by --check'), lib.rs:307 ('This is what --write/--check use'), lib.rs:322 ('Outcome of a --check run'). All four are inside the owned crate; the intra-doc link `[`check`]` at lib.rs:18 refers to the function and stays valid.

#### `H2-5` — Drop unused library timestamps and the per-entry App ID copy

T3 · scope-cut · **-48 lines** (reviewer claimed -47) · from H2 — YAGNI and unused surface

GameEntry does not need addedAtUnixMs or its own appid: the former is never read, while every launch writes the contract App ID directly. LastSession likewise does not need endedAtUnixMs because the UI uses only start time, exit code, and log path. Delete those three persisted fields, their server-owned merge plumbing, disabled/display-only App ID UI, and associated test setup.

*Files.* `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/store/library.rs`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/run/actions.rs`, `sabrage/src-tauri/src/commands.rs`, `sabrage/ui/src/ipc.ts`, `sabrage/ui/src/screens/Library.svelte`, `sabrage/ui/src/screens/EditGame.svelte`

*Evidence.* sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/store/library.rs:55 declares ended_at_unix_ms and sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/store/library.rs:71 declares appid/added_at_unix_ms; sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/store/library.rs:135 treats the latter two as server-owned solely to preserve them; sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/run/actions.rs:572 obtains the actual launch App ID from contract().game.appid; sabrage/ui/src/screens/Library.svelte:157 reads only startedAtUnixMs, exitCode, and logPath from session history; sabrage/ui/src/screens/Library.svelte:296 merely displays the copied App ID; sabrage/ui/src/screens/EditGame.svelte:309 renders it as a disabled field; sabrage/src-tauri/src/commands.rs:2949 is the only behavioral assertion about ended_at.

*What breaks.* The Library would stop displaying App ID, saved JSON would no longer retain creation/end timestamps, and future duration or added-order features could not use old records. The specifically lost test assertions are App ID/server-owned metadata preservation and endedAt >= startedAt; last-session race, start time, exit code, and log-path coverage remain.

*Blast radius.* Library JSON schema, two small UI fragments, IPC types, and test fixtures change. Old JSON remains loadable through serde's unknown-field behavior; launch steam_appid.txt golden behavior and contract parity remain untouched.

*How you would know it worked.* Load an old library containing all three fields, save it, and verify the fields disappear without losing games or last-session data. Run library/Tauri tests and confirm steam_appid.txt still comes solely from the contract golden path.

*Verifier.* Verified each claim. `ended_at_unix_ms` (library.rs:57) is written at commands.rs:2227 and read by exactly one assertion (commands.rs:2949, endedAt >= startedAt); Library.svelte reads only startedAtUnixMs (:157), exitCode and logPath (:167-171). `added_at_unix_ms` (:72) is set once in new_entry_template (:325) and otherwise only preserved (:145) — no reader at all. Per-entry `appid` (:71) is display-only: Library.svelte:296 and EditGame.svelte:311 (a disabled input whose own hint already says the value is fixed and that launch writes the contract's App ID), while the actual steam_appid.txt write is `contract().game.appid.to_string()` at stages/run/actions.rs:572, golden-pinned in sabrage-parity at :1255/:1497/:1701 — so the launch byte path is genuinely independent of the field. Measured: ~22 lines in library.rs (3 field decls, the merge lines 145-146, template 324-325, and ~13 test setup/assert lines at 644-645, 727, 798, 839, 919-920, 923, 937-938, 945-946, 967, 989), ~4 in commands.rs (:2104 doc, :2227, :2949), 3 in ipc.ts (1053, 1065-1066), 1 in Library.svelte, ~14 in EditGame.svelte (the 309-316 field block plus .appid-field/.appid-input CSS) = ~-48 against a claimed -47: accurate. Review-history exposure is the one thing to weigh. library.rs:126-150 and 915-955 are both review-fix ranges implementing and pinning A13b-5 (round1:1520, CONFIRMED, 'Saving an edit can erase a newly recorded last session'), whose recommendation was explicitly to preserve 'id, appid, addedAtUnixMs, and lastSession' server-side. This does not revert that fix — upsert_editable keeps preserving last_session, which is the data the finding was about — but it does delete the two defensive assertions (`saved.added_at_unix_ms == 1` and `saved.appid == 620980`, both tagged 'server-owned') and requires rewriting the doc at :135 that names the server-owned set. Invariants are clean: no parity fixture, no golden bytes, no library.json fixture, nothing in PARITY.md, and library.rs's own load() rules stay intact (old files still parse; the fields are simply dropped on the next save, which is user data loss for endedAt/addedAt that no feature currently uses but a future duration/added-order feature could not recover).

---

## Rejected — and what killed each one

This is the more useful half of the report. It is the record of what in Sabrage looks like bloat
and is not — usually because a confirmed finding from the August review rounds put it there.

| id | title | killed by | why |
|---|---|---|---|
| `C1-1` | Replace the Tauri/Svelte application boundary with a direct Rust UI | PARITY.md Cmd-Q/AppKit-terminate row; A11-4 (r1, CONFIRMED) | Reject: swapping Tauri/Svelte for a native Rust UI deletes ~11.9k lines but adds an unwritten UI of unknown size, and it dissolves the app.exit interception that PARITY.md's Cmd-Q row and A11-4's fix both stand on. |
| `H1-5` | Replace cross-boundary narration with invariant-and-test pointers | Rule C — landmine rationale with no adequate replacement: events.rs:1-27's zsh-construct→StageEvent mapping table and its wire-Stability warning, parity/lib.rs:253-278's section-disjointness counting rule, parity/lib.rs:1842-1880's 'sabrage-core's frozen-text tests are NOT in the tier-1/CI gate' note, and parity/lib.rs:1132-1152's host-manifest `}\n}\n` byte rule | REJECT as written — 12 of the 21 cited blocks get no replacement text at all ('just delete it'), and three of the nine supplied replacements drop the constraint they were meant to keep, including the host-manifest trailing-newline rule whose replacement just points the test's doc comment at its own name. |
| `H5-3` | Generate wire types with `ts-rs`, but keep the IPC facade handwritten | A13b-8 / A13b-4 (flattened-extras index signature + 'spread, don't rebuild' guidance); measured net ~-120 vs claimed -320 | Reject: once the committed bindings.ts, ~50 cfg_attr(TS) derive annotations, the re-export shims and the freshness test are counted the net is roughly -120 rather than -320, and the flattened-Settings/LaunchDefaults index signatures plus their 'spread the loaded object, don't rebuild it' guidance ARE the fix text for confirmed A13b-8/A13b-4 — shapes ts-rs has no first-class way to emit. |
| `H6-2` | Replace wire-history essays with landmine-sized comments | guts the ipc.ts:306-324 rationale guarding A4-2 / A12-1 (both CONFIRMED in round 1, A4-2 and A12-1 re-found as [unfixed] in round 2), and drops events.rs's launch-banner byte-parity landmine and the serde(default) back-compat note behind A14-3 | Reject: sabrage/ui has zero tests, so the ipc.ts comments this deletes are the only enforcement of policies that already regressed twice, and the replacement strings drop exactly the reasons (delete-session-json's black screen, why `bare in FIX_META` is not the test, the five-line launch banner that must be byte-identical) — the measurement is honest but the content is not deletable. |
| `H7-6` | Remove dry-run as a product mode | loses privilege.rs::a_dry_run_plans_the_staging_write_and_the_elevated_argv (the only assertion that the host-manifest JSON never reaches an elevated command line); deletes the code fixing r1 A5-1, r1 A5-4, r1 A6-2 and r2 A5-1 | Dry-run is not a leaf feature: DryRunExecutor is the never-touch-the-machine base of StageCtx::for_fixture and install.rs's TestExecutor, and the only way the elevated-write argv is ever asserted, so deleting it destroys assertions rather than duplication — if the owner wants the mode gone, drop only the CLI/GUI --dry-run surface and keep DryRunExecutor as the hermetic test executor. |
| `H7-7` | Adopt a Stop-or-Cancel quit policy; remove detach and faux re-attach | reverts A11-4 (r1, CONFIRMED) — the unanswered-quit fallback the fix added resolves to the very detach path this deletes | Deleting the quit-timeout state machine together with detach re-opens A11-4 (a webview that never answers app://quit-requested makes the app permanently unquittable), because QuitIntercept::GiveUp's whole definition is 'stop asking and take the keep-running detach answer' — a Stop-or-Cancel policy would first have to define and implement a new give-up answer. |
| `H7-9` | Replace four stage dissertations with executable references | erases the landmine rationale for r2 A5-3 (critical, the O_EXCL write-once config create) and r1 A5-7 / r2 A5-2 (the foreign-checkout helper scan) with no replacement text and no pointer to their tests | The arithmetic is exact (-390) and every named test really exists, but the replacement drops two CONFIRMED fixes' rationale without naming their enforcing tests — re-propose with a setup.rs line for the create_new/O_EXCL write-once rule citing a_config_created_by_another_writer_is_reported_not_replaced and a stop.rs line for the foreign-checkout helper scan citing a_foreign_helper_is_reported_even_when_the_local_reap_matched, and it becomes acceptable. |
| `V1-1` | Stop owning audio and dashboard lifecycle in Sabrage | contract [[launch_action]] audio-route + dashboard (no per-side field); reverts A1-3's parity text pins | Rejected: `audio-route` and `dashboard` are contract launch-action rows with no per-side gate field and a parity test asserting LAUNCH_ACTION_IDS == contract order, so this cannot land without editing contract/ and run.sh, and it deletes the eight pub renderers sabrage-parity pins run.sh's audio/dashboard text against. |
| `V1-4` | Remove keep-running detach from the launch state machine | PARITY.md's Cmd-Q / Dock-Quit detach row (AppKit terminate: is uninterceptable, live-found 2026-08-29) | Rejected: the proposal's premise ('quitting must either stop Wine or be refused') is unreachable on the exact path detach exists for — a Dock/logout/AppleScript quit sends AppKit `terminate:`, which tao cannot intercept — so removing detach does not remove the leave-it-running state, it removes the honest record of it and leaves the Mac on BlackHole with the dashboard open and nothing on disk saying so. |
| `V1-7` | Replace long launch narratives with invariant and test pointers | reverts r2 A7-1 / r1 A7-2 (preflight.rs:125-156), r1 A7-6 (preflight.rs:762-777), r2 A7-5 (actions.rs:480-482); drops the PARITY.md-declared unquoted-value divergence and the dry-run Drop landmine | Rejected as written: the prescribed replacements delete the in-code records of three CONFIRMED review fixes (r2 A7-1/r1 A7-2 last-valid TOML semantics, r1 A7-6 autofix Check/Fatal, r2 A7-5 non-regular-file .orig-steam refusal) plus the PARITY-declared unquoted-value divergence and the '`Drop` must not fire under --dry-run' landmine, so only a narrower header-only compression that carries those four constraints forward should be re-proposed. |
| `V2-1` | Replace eight module essays with compact source-of-truth headers | Rule C landmine rationale; deletes the module-doc text written by the A5-1, A5-3/A5-4, A4-1, A4-2 and A2-3/r2-A5-5 fixes plus the adb/helper PARITY 'must NOT change' invariant lists | The line count is honest (545 header lines -> ~24), but the eight one-line replacements do not restate the constraints they delete — byte contracts ('spacing is a byte contract', section banners verbatim, 'arm64e alone must NOT satisfy', 'never --remove-all', 'Warn, never Fail'), the review-fix rules (dry-run may not claim a postcondition it did not perform; the live-session refusal runs twice; the staged helper is validated at its destination) and the TCC 'this stage must not re-emit that prose' rule survive nowhere else. |
| `V3-1` | Replace lifecycle narration with invariant-and-test pointers | PARITY.md:96 (reconcile row-text delegation); A9-3/A9-8 rationale at state.rs:42-71; #200/#201 + A9-5 rationale at watcher.rs:326-339 | The line count is honest (-627 measured vs -625 claimed) but the replacement text drops landmine rationale the reviews wrote — most fatally the reconcile row-text table that sabrage/PARITY.md:96 explicitly delegates to that doc comment — so this cannot land as written. |
| `V4-1` | Replace repeated module catalogs with source-of-truth pointers | A3b-3 (config.rs:18-36 is the only record of that divergence; PARITY.md has no row) + checks/mod.rs:12-14 read-only-probe and :7-11 verbatim-doctor.sh-prose invariants | Reject: the 19 module headers are not a transcription of contract/pipeline.toml (CheckSpec has no prose field and contract.rs:161 states check prose is deliberately impl-owned), and the prescribed replacements drop checks/mod.rs's read-only-probe and verbatim-prose rules, run_only's 'prose comes from run.sh not doctor.sh' exception, contract.rs's include-path-depth landmine, and config.rs's record of a still-undeclared A3b-3 divergence. |
| `V5-4` | Replace store histories with terse contracts and regression pointers | Rule C — guts the landmine rationale behind A13a-1 (r1+r2), A13a-3, A13a-5, A13a-6 and A13b-7 | Reject as written: the one-sentence replacements for library.rs and goldberg.rs delete the constraints they are supposed to preserve (the mandatory LIBRARY_VERSION bump, why LIBRARY_LOCK is not OPERATION_LOCK, the two-ways-to-be-Goldberg pin-plus-payload-plus-provenance test, the op-lock-first ordering, why the argv scan is deliberately not scoped to bs_dir, and the never-say-'the original' rule) and name no tests; only the config/mod.rs header (15 lines, no review-fix blame) is safe to compress as specified. |
| `V6-1` | Replace eleven module essays with compact contracts | misstates winpath's load-bearing invariant (invents a nonexistent 'UNC passthrough'); orphans three live 'see this module's header' cross-references; drops A6-5's tty-not-stdout rationale and the untested spawn_streamed/wine-launch landmine | Reject as written: the winpath replacement asserts a 'UNC passthrough' invariant that does not exist in win_path while deleting the two real ones, and process.rs:648, process.rs:676 and executor.rs:366 all point at headers this empties — the purely narrative headers (error.rs, contract-gen, sabrage-parity, tap.rs, core lib.rs; 136 lines) can still be cut on their own. |
| `V6-4` | Replace Executor and process postmortems with invariant pointers | guts the fix rationale of CONFIRMED A2-2, A2-7 (r2 A2-3) and r2 A2-4, and deletes A2-3's 'mutations must never come through here' guard while r2 A2-1 records that hole as still open | Reject: 14 comment blocks — 11 of them written by review-fix commits — are replaced by 9 generic lines, deleting why copy_atomic must not be std::fs::copy (A2-2), why write_atomic fsyncs before the audio switch (A2-7), why create_new publishes via link(2) because an empty oxrsys-runtime.toml is valid TOML (r2 A2-4), and the 'mutations must never come through capture()' dry-run guard (A2-3, still partly open as r2 A2-1). |
| `V6-7` | Compress error and utility narration to test-named contracts | reverts A1-8 (round-2 A1-7 adjudication) + PARITY.md 'Control characters in the checkout path' | Rejected: the replacement drops the round-2-adjudicated 'json_escape_string stays exactly install.sh's two substitutions, widening must land on both sides' rule that PARITY.md declares, loses bs_version's untested empty-marker-file quirk, and its own replacement text is factually wrong (says UnityPlayer.dll where the code scans Beat Saber_Data/globalgamemanagers). |
| `V7-1` | Compress review-era narration into one-line landmines | A11-1 (r1+r2), A11-3/A11-2 (r1+r2), A4-2/A12-1 (r1+r2), A10-4, A11-4, A11-5, A14-3, A14-5 | Reject: the cited blocks are 510 comment lines that sit almost entirely inside review-fix ranges for eight CONFIRMED findings (three of which round 2 caught re-broken), and the estimate is 2.3x inflated (-494 measured, not -1120) — the only safely cuttable part is the two module headers, ~117 lines. |
| `V8-4` | Replace review-era narration with compact invariant comments | weakens A12-7 and A13b-8 (Rule C: landmine rationale deleted with no replacement and no test to point at) | Reject: the nine supplied replacement lines cover only a fraction of the ~520 cut, and both they and the uncovered blocks drop parity landmines — demo.sh's flag order and bare-switch rule (the A12-7 anchor), the settings `extra`-spread rule (A13b-8), and onStageQueued's cancellable-lock-wait note (A11-3) — with no UI test to point at, because sabrage/ui has no test runner at all. |

## Cleared as load-bearing by the reviewers themselves

Every reviewer was asked to name what looks like bloat in its area but must stay, so nobody
re-proposes it in six months.

**C1 — completeness critic**

- *Make Sabrage shell out to demo.sh and delete the native pipeline* — This violates the explicit product contract, not merely an implementation preference: CLAUDE.md:104-120 defines two independent implementations, and :137-138 forbids either core pipeline from invoking the other. The independent native evaluators, stages, artifact writers, and live parity witness are irreducible.
- *Delete or absorb sabrage-parity* — sabrage-parity/src/lib.rs:1-16 has no runtime surface and deliberately reads the working checkout so it can catch compiled/checkout drift; :42-54 independently byte-compares generated shell output. Its 2,335 lines are test product, not a fourth application implementation.
- *Collapse runtime TOML reading to toml_edit or ordinary serialization* — runtime_toml.rs:13-30 records the actual consumer’s table-blind, last-valid, line parser; :46-64 explains why toml_edit answers only whether rewriting is safe. Removing either view changes effective values or destroys hand-maintained bytes. Its implementation may be scenario-tested more compactly, but the dual semantics stay.
- *Delete Executor, reconciliation, or detach as defensive overengineering* — Executor is the mutation/dry-run boundary at executor.rs:220-381; session/mod.rs:305-333 enumerates seven real liveness sources; and src-tauri/src/lib.rs:32-41 plus :279 handles an AppKit termination path ordinary close interception cannot catch. Consolidate their policy and tests, but the underlying mutation, crash recovery, external-session, and terminate-time obligations remain.

**C2 — contradiction critic**

- *Delete or merge sabrage-parity and its artifact goldens* — It is a dev-only two-sided witness tying core renderers to checkout shell artifacts. per-file.tsv:6 shows 1,120 test lines; merged.md:1299 and later not_worth_it entries identify registry order, shell fingerprints, launch env/argv, win_path, and exact steam_appid bytes as distinct product assertions.
- *Unify the runtime TOML reader and format-preserving editor* — They intentionally model different semantics: Config.cpp-compatible table-blind last-accepted reads versus safe byte-preserving edits. runtime_toml.rs has 1,881 test lines and the frozen fixture/CRLF/BOM/shadowing cases prove the distinction (per-file.tsv:3; merged.md:1305, :1390-1391).
- *Delete production Executor, reconciliation states, or Tailer semantics* — Executor is the production dry-run/mutation boundary; reconciliation distinguishes live, dead, recycled, and unverifiable identities; Tailer owns bounded rotation/truncation/backlog behavior. Their forwarding and fixture boilerplate can shrink, but the production boundaries and finding-pinned tests are load-bearing.
- *Remove the short landmine comments left after compression* — Host-manifest file-vs-comparison bytes, logical repo-root spelling, failed-probe divergences, privilege staging, global-test serialization, and BSD-sed behavior are not inferable from ordinary tests alone. The long histories can go; the one-line constraint and executable pointer must remain.

**H1 — cross-crate duplication and the two front-ends**

- *Deleting or merging sabrage-parity* — It has no runtime surface and every dependency is dev-only (`sabrage/crates/sabrage-parity/Cargo.toml:6-15`). It deliberately reads checkout files rather than compiled copies (`src/lib.rs:12-16`), calls the core renderer for artifact comparisons (`:1083-1097`), and reads native preflight/action lists directly from core (`:1363-1413`). That is a two-sided witness, not a fourth renderer; its parity and golden tests are the product.
- *Generating or fetching FIX_META over a new Tauri command* — Rust owns enforcement metadata at `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/fixes/mod.rs:235-331`, but the TypeScript table also carries UI labels, confirmation consequences, stage navigation, and withheld-button policy (`sabrage/ui/src/ipc.ts:216-340`). A `get_fix_defs` command would add transport, initialization, and failure surface while leaving UI policy handwritten. H1-3 should generate only the FixAction type, not this value table.
- *Unifying the CLI and Svelte StageEvent renderers* — The core intentionally supplies one structured event domain (`sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/events.rs:1-27`). The CLI projects it into shell-shaped stdout/stderr and terminal repaint chunks (`sabrage/crates/sabrage-cli/src/main.rs:712-815`), while GateModal projects it into typed visual rows (`sabrage/ui/src/components/GateModal.svelte:46-71`). These are different presentations, not duplicated decisions. Delete the optional CLI presentation via H1-1; do not force terminal concerns into Svelte or UI rows into core.
- *Making sabrage-contract-gen depend on sabrage-core to share its small TOML structs* — The generator intentionally declares only the shell-consumed subset so additions to the core schema do not silently become shell inputs (`sabrage/crates/sabrage-contract-gen/src/lib.rs:21-28`). The duplicate model is only `:62-102`; extracting a shared crate or importing the full core dependency graph would save roughly forty lines while weakening the independent-consumer check.
- *Removing UI session-phase predicates because the backend rechecks them* — The UI predicates are already centralized at `sabrage/ui/src/ipc.ts:461-491` and explicitly exist to disable controls with an explanation, not to enforce correctness (`:478-484`). The backend remains authoritative at `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/mod.rs:689-717`. Removing the UI copy saves little and either degrades feedback or causes screens to rediscover phase sets independently.
- *Replacing the retained doctor parser with clap* — The binary manifest records the concrete incompatibility: the doctor/live-parity interface requires demo.sh's error text and exit-2 behavior, while a general parser supplies its own diagnostics (`sabrage/crates/sabrage-cli/Cargo.toml:10-13`). The custom common-flag grammar is only `sabrage/crates/sabrage-cli/src/main.rs:230-302` and its byte-sensitive cases are pinned at `:1403-1495`. Once H1-1 removes stage parsing, a new dependency would not produce a material net reduction.

**H2 — YAGNI and unused surface**

- *Delete sabrage-cli or sabrage-contract-gen because their public APIs look locally unreferenced* — They are parity infrastructure, not spare product APIs: scripts/dev/parity.sh:75 includes both core/generator tests, scripts/dev/parity.sh:132 invokes sabrage-contract-gen --write, and scripts/dev/parity.sh:249 builds sabrage-cli for the live tier. Removing either requires an out-of-scope script/contract change.
- *Unify the two runtime TOML readers* — They intentionally answer different questions. sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/config/runtime_toml.rs:50 distinguishes toml_edit's typed view from the runtime's physical-line interpretation, and sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/config/runtime_toml.rs:725 documents the table-blind, last-wins runtime view. This was already considered and rejected for correctness.
- *Collapse Executor and its seven implementations* — The apparent generality has live callers: sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/mod.rs:216 selects RealExecutor versus DryRunExecutor in production, while sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/mod.rs:235 and sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/mod.rs:275 inject safe fixture executors. It is the mutation/dry-run test boundary, not preparation for hypothetical callers.
- *Treat low-reference Tauri DTOs as dead types* — Serde and invoke boundaries are invisible to ordinary symbol counts. FixRunOpts at sabrage/src-tauri/src/commands.rs:321, QueuedStage at sabrage/src-tauri/src/commands.rs:521, and RepoInfo at sabrage/src-tauri/src/commands.rs:1900 are constructed/deserialized through commands and mirrored in sabrage/ui/src/ipc.ts; deleting their types wholesale would delete live IPC contracts.
- *Delete the game library wholesale* — Keeping a small library is an explicit product function, and its large-looking transaction/validation surface is review-pinned. In particular, sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/store/library.rs:130 preserves last-session data across editor races and sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/store/library.rs:825 tests confirmed finding A13b-5. Only optional metadata and override tiers are credible cuts.
- *Remove session reconciliation, log tailing, or Doctor fix machinery as personal-app overkill* — Those areas touch real processes and persistent recovery state and carry dense confirmed-finding coverage. Their volume is deliberate safety and parity work, not unused surface; cutting them would violate the instruction to retain tests that pin review findings.
- *Generate FIX_META through another Tauri command* — The TypeScript table at sabrage/ui/src/ipc.ts:229 has live Doctor and GateModal consumers, and replacing it introduces a command, DTO, and startup failure surface. The prior simplification pass correctly found that the added IPC surface costs more than the hand mirror saves.

**H3 — the test mass**

- *The 1,120-line sabrage-parity test crate* — It is the executable product boundary: generated shell bytes are pinned at sabrage-parity/src/lib.rs:42-60, the shell fingerprint at sabrage-parity/src/lib.rs:152-238, win_path and steam_appid content at sabrage-parity/src/lib.rs:1206-1250, strict registry order at sabrage-parity/src/lib.rs:1343-1358, launch env/argv at sabrage-parity/src/lib.rs:1719-1820, and verbatim banner text at sabrage-parity/src/lib.rs:2172-2212.
- *Most of runtime_toml.rs's remaining test mass and the frozen Phase-4 fixture* — The two parsers intentionally model different semantics: the editable TOML view and the runtime's table-blind last-wins reader are compared at runtime_toml.rs:2197-2278 and runtime_toml.rs:2593-2694. Identity, CRLF, BOM, shadowing, backups, and live-write guards are separately pinned from runtime_toml.rs:1779 through runtime_toml.rs:3416, and the deployed fixture is loaded at runtime_toml.rs:1726-1732. Tables can shrink wrappers, not this semantic matrix.
- *watcher.rs's large state-transition tests* — They already consolidate scenarios deliberately. watcher.rs:1808-1819 explains that snapshot reads process-global LIVE_SESSION and separate tokio tests race; the sequential matrix beginning at watcher.rs:1818 is therefore synchronization, not verbose scaffolding. Only small parser literal families belong in H3-2.
- *The parity SyncFsExecutor and multiple steam_appid.txt checks* — The planned write records only byte length, so it cannot distinguish exact digits from a same-length impostor; sabrage-parity/src/lib.rs:1521-1537 states why a synchronous real writer is required, and the exact on-disk assertion is at sabrage-parity/src/lib.rs:1662-1704. Core separately checks placement and bytes at actions.rs:1723-1742. These layers assert different facts and must not be merged away.
- *process.rs cancellation, process-group, and pipe tests* — These are not tests of Tokio or Unix trivia. They pin the launcher's teardown contract: group cancellation at process.rs:1153-1170, a descendant outliving its leader at process.rs:1172-1201, a descendant releasing pipes while ignoring SIGTERM at process.rs:1203-1252, and the uncancelled background-child case at process.rs:1257-1284.
- *Core plus parity launch env, argv, and banner tests* — The core tests at actions.rs:1927-2025 validate the native constructors, while parity independently ties them to run.sh at sabrage-parity/src/lib.rs:1712-1820 and sabrage-parity/src/lib.rs:2172-2212. That apparent three-layer repetition is precisely the golden boundary required by Rule T.

**H4 — the comment mass**

- *The full BSD-sed divergence note on `rewrite_graphics_backend` (`sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/fixes/backend.rs:59-90`).* — This is a measured shell-compatibility exception, including the no-final-newline cell where BSD sed mangles the section header. `branch_insert_preserves_absence_of_a_trailing_newline` pins the Rust choice, but no hermetic test runs real BSD sed against every documented cell; deleting the rationale invites a well-meaning parity rewrite.
- *The test-global serialization warning (`sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/session/mod.rs:691-710`).* — A future test that touches `RUN_PHASE` without the guard creates a nondeterministic cross-test race. The suite cannot deterministically test that every future test acquires the guard, and the comment also explains why resetting `LIVE_SESSION` would race unrelated modules.
- *The short tripwires retained for host-manifest file/comparison bytes, runtime-TOML semantics, `win_path`, and logical repo-root spelling (`util/mod.rs:202`, `config/runtime_toml.rs:1`, `util/winpath.rs:1`, `paths.rs:135`).* — These look repetitive because goldens exist, but they identify which superficially cleaner implementation is forbidden before an editor changes it. The paragraphs can become test pointers; the one-line constraints cannot disappear.
- *The short warnings retained for failed-probe divergences (`checks/config.rs:18-36`, `checks/network.rs:14-29`).* — Malformed JSON and failed adb queries produce deliberately different native verdicts from the shell in rare machine states. Tests pin native behavior, but an editor also needs the PARITY pointer before trying to make the doctors superficially uniform. The obsolete `preserve_order` caveat at `checks/config.rs:38-47` should not stay.
- *The security-boundary warning in `privilege.rs:1` after compression.* — TCC denial, root authorization, private staging, shell quoting, and file-form bytes are distinct constraints. Tests catch current mechanics, but a one-line boundary warning prevents the obvious and dangerous simplification of treating every protected write as sudo.

**H5 — dependency substitution**

- *`clap` for the CLI parser* — The manifest deliberately requires byte-identical shell errors and exit 2 at sabrage/crates/sabrage-cli/Cargo.toml:10-13. The removable parser is only `parse_common_flag` plus two thin loops at sabrage/crates/sabrage-cli/src/main.rs:230-303 and :650-682; the custom usage text at :83-121 and parity assertions at :1430-1492 must remain. `clap` would therefore need custom error/help formatting around a derive stack to save well under 100 net lines.
- *`tokio_util::codec` for child output* — The entire custom state machine is only sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/process.rs:212-281. It preserves bare CR, CRLF-as-one, blank chunks, read-boundary splits, EOF-without-delimiter, and the original terminator, all pinned at :952-1018 and :1070-1109. Enabling `codec` and writing a custom `Decoder` would reproduce that state machine plus add framing/error adaptation; `LinesCodec` cannot express the contract.
