The manifest records this choice at sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/Cargo.toml:22-25.
- *A filesystem-watcher or log-tailer crate for `Tailer`* — The mass is not notification plumbing. `Tailer` owns cursor, signature, partial-line and old-incarnation backlog state at sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/logs.rs:218-258; `poll` implements same-inode truncate/regrow detection, bounded reads, rotation partitioning, and deferred markers at :365-580. Finding-pinned cases at :1215-1464 prove those semantics. A watcher could announce a change but would not replace this read/cursor/backlog contract.
- *More—or less—`sysinfo` for process scanning* — `sysinfo` already supplies targeted PID observation at sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/process.rs:590-612, one shared exe+argv table scan at :684-772, and the separate environment-aware wineserver scan at sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/fixes/backend.rs:150-183. The manifest explains why exe and argv refreshes both exist at sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/Cargo.toml:28-34. Removing it recreates OS process inspection; broadening the shared snapshot to always fetch environments would complicate the common path to save only a small specialized scan.
- *`reqwest`, `flate2`, or `tar` for setup* — They are deliberately absent because setup executes the same `curl -fL --retry 3` and `tar -xzf` commands as the maintained shell implementation, preserving tool choice, flags, and failure text while avoiding three dependency trees; this is explicit at sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/Cargo.toml:17-20. Native libraries would replace parity-visible subprocess behavior, not merely hand-rolled transport code.
- *Full `tauri-specta` command generation* — There are 27 registered commands at sabrage/src-tauri/src/lib.rs:176-204, but the frontend facade constructs six Channels and supplies semantic normalization—for example sabrage/ui/src/ipc.ts:60-70 and :348-434—alongside listeners, dialogs, and policy helpers. Full command generation therefore removes mostly short `invoke` expressions while leaving the large, load-bearing facade. H5-3 captures the larger type-mirroring win with a smaller dependency and integration surface.
- *`mockall` or `delegate` for the test `Executor` wrappers* — These tests need partial delegation to a real `DryRunExecutor`, preservation across `with_step`, planned-child recording, and only one injected fault per wrapper, as shown at sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/run/guards.rs:918-998. A shared concrete fault injector removes the same repeated forwarding without adding a proc-macro dependency or translating finding-pinned tests into expectation DSLs.
- *Removing low-use `thiserror`, `hex`, or `nix`* — Low reference counts do not make them line-negative. `thiserror` derives structured Display/Error/source behavior for the central error enum at sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/error.rs:18-55 and registry errors at sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/checks/mod.rs:437-449. `hex` replaces encoding loops in sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/util/hash.rs:9-30 and the contract generator at sabrage/crates/sabrage-contract-gen/src/lib.rs:145-155. `nix` keeps process-group and PID signalling typed and safe at sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/process.rs:38-39 and :897-914. Hand replacements add source while removing only small dependencies.
- *Unifying `toml` and `toml_edit`* — `toml_edit` exists to preserve comments, unknown keys, and ordering in the user-edited runtime file, explicitly documented at sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/Cargo.toml:48-51. The runtime’s strict editable view and naïve last-wins interpretation are deliberately different contracts; forcing one parser through the other risks changing behavior rather than removing redundant parsing.

**H6 — type and wire-surface sprawl**

- *Collapsing the main StageEvent variants* — The variants encode behavior rather than decorative labels: GateModal branches on event classes at sabrage/ui/src/components/GateModal.svelte:51 and updates distinct progress/output/preflight/session state at GateModal.svelte:242, while the CLI gives fatal, finish, line, text, and output different rendering/lifecycle treatment at sabrage/crates/sabrage-cli/src/main.rs:718. Producers also emit real progress events at sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/build.rs:389. Merging Line, Text, Output, Fatal, StageFinished, or Preflight would disturb parity and golden wire semantics for little reduction.
- *Removing CheckOutcome fields or collapsing the remaining CheckStatus values* — CheckOutcome.quiet looks wire-unused because serde skips it, but the CLI consumes it when deciding output at sabrage/crates/sabrage-cli/src/main.rs:449; its definition documents that split at sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/checks/mod.rs:111. Pass, Warn, Fail, and Skipped have different doctor/preflight meaning and rendering at sabrage/ui/src/components/CheckRow.svelte:27 and sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/checks/tap.rs:24. Only NotImplemented is uniformly redundant.
- *Deleting the core SessionState and Reconciled taxonomies* — They are persisted recovery policy, not GUI presentation types. SessionState records process identity and ownership inputs at sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/session/state.rs:117, while Reconciled variants encode materially different restore/cleanup decisions at sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/session/reconcile.rs:192. H6-3 should stop exporting this graph to the UI, not weaken it internally.
- *Removing flattened unknown settings and launch-default fields* — The TS boundary deliberately permits unknown keys at sabrage/ui/src/ipc.ts:945 and sabrage/ui/src/ipc.ts:966, and the update wrappers preserve loaded objects at ipc.ts:992. Those fields prevent an older UI from dropping newer TOML keys during round trips. Binding generation must retain this open-object behavior even if the Rust struct appears to offer a closed schema.
- *Unifying the two runtime TOML interpretations* — RuntimeConfigView exposes parse, shadowing, and effective-value diagnostics at sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/runtime_toml.rs:243, and Settings renders invalid/shadowed states distinctly at sabrage/ui/src/screens/Settings.svelte:432. The strict editable view and the runtime-compatible last-wins interpretation are deliberately allowed to disagree; merging them would change behavior and invalidate review-pinned fixtures rather than merely reduce wire surface.
- *Generating or fetching FIX_META through a new IPC command* — FIX_META contains UI labels and confirmation policy at sabrage/ui/src/ipc.ts:216, while contractFixIdToAction separately validates the backend action mapping at ipc.ts:330. Turning this small behavior table into another command would add a runtime protocol, loading/error state, and Rust response type; H6-1 should generate structural types while leaving this UI policy manual.
- *Merging StageOutcome with StageFinished* — StageFinished closes the event stream and carries shell-equivalent termination information consumed by the CLI at sabrage/crates/sabrage-cli/src/main.rs:784. The UI launch promise has a separate session lifetime and resolves with StageOutcome at sabrage/ui/src/ipc.ts:575, which GateModal consumes after event processing at sabrage/ui/src/components/GateModal.svelte:604. Combining them would blur event completion versus command completion.
- *Deleting QueuedStage because it is a one-field wrapper* — The backend emits the queued identity before lock acquisition at sabrage/src-tauri/src/commands.rs:516, and the UI records that run ID for cancellation before stageStarted at sabrage/ui/src/ipc.ts:787. Its small wrapper closes a real cancellation race; deleting it would trade a few lines for weaker lifecycle semantics.
- *Cutting the game-library type family as wire bloat* — The library is stated product scope, and the UI distinguishes validity, path, version, and editable launch data at sabrage/ui/src/screens/Library.svelte:299 and sabrage/ui/src/components/EditGame.svelte:325. A wholesale library cut could remove substantial code, but it is a product decision broader than simplifying redundant wire taxonomies and would discard review-pinned behavior.
- *Collapsing the SabrageError enum after deleting ErrorPayload* — The enum variants carry different paths, child-process tails, shell-verbatim messages, and exit semantics at sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/error.rs:19, with behavior-specific methods at error.rs:127. The dead alternate DTO can go, but the core error taxonomy remains the control-flow and diagnostic contract.

**H7 — feature surface — what a personal launcher does not need**

- *Cutting fresh-machine Doctor checks individually* — The registry is contract-ordered and shared with launch preflight at sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/checks/mod.rs:387-399; Doctor walks that exact visible subset at sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/checks/mod.rs:575-608, and tier-2 diffs every emitted slug at scripts/dev/parity.sh:256-259. Removing checks requires contract and shell changes, making it a blocked T4 rather than an H7 cut.
- *Deleting crash reconciliation together with detach* — Dead, recycled-PID, and unverifiable classifications prevent signalling unrelated processes or restoring guards under a possibly live stream at sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/session/reconcile.rs:12-32. PARITY.md:94-103 explains that this restores audio/dashboard/ADB state after failures that shell traps cannot survive. H7-7 may remove intentional detach, not crash recovery.
- *Deleting the Executor trait after dry-run* — It remains the single mutation seam used by hermetic tests: the invariant is stated at sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/executor.rs:220-245. Replacing its `curl`, `tar`, or `cp -R` operations with dependencies would also discard deliberate shell-equivalent flags, failure modes, and symlink behavior documented at sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/executor.rs:12-20.
- *Deleting Tailer with the Logs screen* — SessionMonitor embeds Tailer at sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/session/watcher.rs:227-233 and opens it for runtime health at sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/session/watcher.rs:318-320. Rotation/truncation logic therefore remains production session-state machinery even if the human-facing log browser goes.
- *Deleting the launch-time `.orig-steam` backup or Goldberg goldens* — The backup is created once and is the only retained Steam DLL, while a non-regular backup path must block overwrite at sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/run/actions.rs:473-505. The exact `steam_appid.txt` and related artifact bytes are pinned in sabrage/crates/sabrage-parity/src/lib.rs:1071-1254. H7-3 removes only the optional restore door and its provenance journal.
- *Deleting launch autofixes with the Doctor Fix UI* — Run preflight maps contract-gated autofixes and applies the graphics-backend specialization at sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/stages/run/preflight.rs:825-852. Those operations reproduce run.sh behavior and are not the same capability as an arbitrary Doctor-row Fix button.
- *A `--json` feature cut* — There is no such CLI option to remove: the complete usage surface at sabrage/crates/sabrage-cli/src/main.rs:83-120 omits it. Only two stale comment phrases remain at sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/error.rs:111 and sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/error.rs:196-203, worth incidental comment correction rather than a material proposal.
- *Parity, contract, artifact-golden, and frozen-fixture tests* — The parity crate has no runtime surface and exists solely as hermetic cross-front-end enforcement at sabrage/crates/sabrage-parity/src/lib.rs:1-16; its contract-generation byte check begins at sabrage/crates/sabrage-parity/src/lib.rs:35-54 and artifact goldens at sabrage/crates/sabrage-parity/src/lib.rs:1071. These tests are the product boundary, not test-volume bloat.

**V1 — stages/run — launch state machine**

- *Collapsing explicit async release, Drop fallback, and persisted recovery into one guard mechanism* — They cover different failure domains: persistence precedes mutation at `guards.rs:341-356`, async release restores and records completion at `guards.rs:427-465`, and Drop can only perform synchronous best-effort recovery at `guards.rs:512-541`. Detach additionally requires save-before-disarm at `mod.rs:708-729`. If automatic audio/dashboard ownership stays, all three layers stay.
- *Merging preflight.rs and actions.rs into one linear script-shaped file* — Preflight is a contract-ordered evaluator/gate walk at `preflight.rs:262-305`; actions are permanent mutations invoked sequentially at `mod.rs:206-237`, including write-before-mutate rollback state at `actions.rs:219-267`. Combining files removes almost no logic and obscures the important check-versus-mutation boundary.
- *Unifying the launch TOML view with the doctor/parser view* — Launch deliberately reads runtime last-valid values and raw fallbacks at `preflight.rs:125-184`, then overrides the two protocol gates from that single fact at `preflight.rs:510-599`. Those semantics are regression-tested at `preflight.rs:1007-1064` and `preflight.rs:1815-1910`; using the doctor's raw/awk view would change accepted launch configurations.
- *Replacing detached Wine spawn, exclusive log creation, or the pure argv/env/banner renderers* — The child must survive Sabrage and write directly to its log; the exclusive-open retry is implemented at `actions.rs:789-863`. Env, argv, and visible banner bytes are isolated at `actions.rs:680-787` and golden-tested at `actions.rs:1926-2030`. These are parity/product surfaces, not scaffolding.
- *Reducing Goldberg staging or its artifact tests while the feature remains* — The stage creates a one-time original DLL backup, installs Goldberg, writes newline-free `steam_appid.txt`, and creates three empty flags at `actions.rs:488-594`. Exact on-disk bytes and backup non-overwrite behavior are pinned at `actions.rs:1628-1765`; those are explicitly protected golden assertions.
- *Simplifying wired rollback by clearing all recorded forwards after any removal attempt* — Rollback retains only successfully removed ports at `actions.rs:131-156`, while creation records intent before mutation at `actions.rs:219-267`. The failed-removal, cancellation, and failed-save regressions at `actions.rs:1312-1520` prove distinct recovery states. Remove the wired feature or keep this machinery.
- *Removing live-session refusal, RunPhaseScope, or fresh-token teardown* — Launch must refuse external/foreign sessions before permanent actions at `mod.rs:132-203`; transient phase publication is run-id guarded at `mod.rs:400-445`; cancelled teardown needs an executor not bound to the fired token at `mod.rs:679-705` and `mod.rs:856-876`. These mechanisms pin confirmed concurrency and cleanup findings rather than merely decorating process launch.

**V2 — stages setup/build/install/stop + fixes**

- *A generic data-driven shell-stage engine* — The entrypoints are already flat, but their work is not homogeneous: setup sequences fetch/extract/write-once operations at setup.rs:96-101 and setup.rs:115-318; build constructs toolchain-specific children and validates helper modes at build.rs:493-671; install coordinates atomic backup, registry flush, TCC and privilege boundaries at install.rs:105-415; stop combines identity-checked signalling, process scans and session reconciliation at stop.rs:217-275. A common engine would still require custom callbacks for nearly every step and would trade a small line saving for a new execution abstraction.
- *Install's partial DXMT backup protocol* — The UUID partial-copy plus same-directory rename prevents a truncated cp -R from becoming the trusted rollback at install.rs:144-247, while cleanup and sweeping live at install.rs:581-624. The interrupted/leftover cases are pinned at install.rs:1534-1871. This is review-fix mass, but it protects the only stock rollback and should not be collapsed to a naive directory copy.
- *The strict registry-flush poll and its tests* — registry_current and wait_for_registry_flush deliberately wait for the same three-token state launch accepts at install.rs:496-571. Cancellation, late flush, stale-value and timeout behavior are pinned at install.rs:1683-1764 and install.rs:1888-2028. Removing the poll or reverting to the shell's loose ActiveRuntime grep would restore a confirmed false-green path.
- *Host-manifest byte and TCC paths* — The privileged manifest layer separates comparison bytes from privileged write bytes at install.rs:361-415; exact file-form and error-classification coverage is at install.rs:910-968 and install.rs:1301-1455. These are parity/golden and permission-boundary behavior, not expendable ceremony.
- *Stop's verified reap and cross-checkout helper scan* — reap rechecks (pid,start_time), waits for actual exit, and distinguishes dry-run at stop.rs:488-605; the foreign-checkout scan is report-only at stop.rs:607-664. Real termination, recycled PID and foreign-helper cases are pinned at stop.rs:1357-1535. Simplifying this back to pkill would discard confirmed review guarantees.
- *Stop's port and audio probes as an easy scope cut* — They duplicate Doctor conceptually, but they are exact stop.sh-visible behavior and now have bounded/cancellable probes at stop.rs:397-484 and stop.rs:668-766. The confirmed hung-probe fixes are pinned at stop.rs:847-983. Removing them is a larger parity-policy decision than the setup diagnostic cut and would delete tests the owner explicitly classified as review-finding pins.
- *Setup's write-once config and dry-run postcondition tests* — create_new prevents an editor or demo.sh race from overwriting the sole user TOML at setup.rs:281-312, with the deterministic competing-writer test at setup.rs:666-823. Fresh/current dry-run distinctions are exercised at setup.rs:918-1124. This is the safety boundary around the one file Sabrage edits.
- *Replacing backend's three-branch editor with a TOML library* — rewrite_graphics_backend intentionally preserves run.sh's replace/insert/append and trailing-newline behavior at fixes/backend.rs:59-137; its byte matrix is fixes/backend.rs:363-472. A semantic parser would rewrite unrelated bytes and lose the shell-visible contract.
- *Flattening ADB query/removal failures into unchanged* — list_forwards distinguishes an empty table from spawn, timeout, and nonzero failures at fixes/adb.rs:78-112, while removal targets only contract ports per serial at fixes/adb.rs:134-217. The failure and multi-serial behavior is pinned at fixes/adb.rs:540-768.
- *Adding reqwest, flate2, or tar crates for setup* — The dependency inventory explicitly records that these were omitted so setup uses the same curl and tar tools, flags, and failure shape as the shell while avoiding three dependency trees (metrics/deps.md:32-39). setup.rs:192-276 delegates the pinned download and extraction through the executor, so adding these crates would increase dependency surface rather than materially shrink this owned code.

**V3 — session lifecycle + logs**

- *Collapsing Live/Dead/IdentityMismatch/Unverifiable* — The classifier is already the small pure function the size suggests: reconcile.rs:302-320. IdentityMismatch selects SafeOnly and forbids PID signalling at reconcile.rs:323-330, while Unverifiable deliberately permits no restore; the safety boundaries are pinned at reconcile.rs:1544-1593 and :1712-1744. Removing a state recreates confirmed A9-5 or risks signalling a recycled PID.
- *Replacing per-guard persistence with one final cleaned flag—or field presence alone* — GuardFlags records three independently completed effects at state.rs:98-113. Reconcile saves audio, dashboard, and each successful forward immediately at reconcile.rs:802-942; the mid-loop disk snapshot is explicitly verified at reconcile.rs:2135-2199. Existing records may contain a guard field plus a true completion bit, so interpreting presence alone would replay already-finished cleanup. This is the crash-resume mechanism, not bookkeeping bloat.
- *Removing foreign-owner, schema, run-id, or record-lock checks* — The owner identity check includes PID reuse and wine liveness at state.rs:262-305. Save, mark-detached, and clear enforce ownership/schema/run identity under the record lock at state.rs:308-353 and :405-515. These are direct fixes for confirmed A9-3, A9-8, and the stop/detach race; the regression tests at state.rs:729-847 are load-bearing.
- *Swapping Tailer for a generic file-follow dependency without a contract proof* — Tailer’s required behavior is more than following rename rotation: it bounds bytes, lines, and unterminated data; detects same-inode truncate/regrow before and after reads; preserves queued old-incarnation lines; and defers the rotation marker to the first new batch at logs.rs:380-595. Confirmed A8-6/A8-7/A8-4 tests occupy logs.rs:1151-1464. Cargo.toml:16-25 deliberately excludes download/archive crates and an unused codec feature, but gives no blanket objection to a tail crate; nevertheless a replacement is only smaller if it demonstrably satisfies every one of these semantics.
- *Removing external-session and Beat Saber process detection* — The mutation door combines live handle, phase, persisted identity/owner, unreadable state, fresh runtime plus live PID, and the process-table fallback at session/mod.rs:307-489. The monitor mirrors external evidence at watcher.rs:434-489. These paths enforce demo.sh coexistence and pin confirmed A8-5, A10-8, and A13a-2; reducing them would make the GUI advertise Idle while mutation commands refuse—or allow mutation under a live shell session.
- *Removing the detach token or its stop-race hedges as a refactor* — Cancel and detach intentionally have opposite semantics at session/mod.rs:19-34. Detach refuses to supersede Stop, waits for the matching live slot, rechecks cancellation, and marks only the matching on-disk run at reconcile.rs:1138-1205; the three race/timeout regressions are at reconcile.rs:2914-3037. Deleting detach would be a cross-UI T3 product decision, not a lifecycle simplification.
- *Deleting the log-name collision suffix or attempt-zero parity test* — Attempt zero remains byte-identical to demo.sh while later attempts avoid truncating a same-second run at logs.rs:71-92; PARITY.md:86 records this deliberate divergence. V3-4 can remove the unused stamped helper, but not the naming behavior or its retained table.

**V4 — checks + contract + generator**

- *Replace all checks with a declarative runtime DSL* — Only about 18 wrappers are truly data-shaped. Protocol last-wins parsing (`checks/config.rs:67`), compiled identity (`checks/meta.rs:63`), bounded adb (`checks/run_only.rs:159`), failed-probe semantics (`checks/network.rs:106`), registry-line ordering (`checks/bridge.rs:30`), and host-manifest diagnosis (`checks/host.rs:35`) would require escape hatches or an interpreter. The macro-sized proposal captures the useful repetition without creating a second contract language.
- *Delete or flatten the strict registry join* — `build_registry` detects duplicate, unknown, and missing evaluator bindings at `checks/mod.rs:490`, then restores contract order at `checks/mod.rs:518`. Slug/order coverage is a parity product invariant, not dispatch ornament.
- *Remove `NotImplemented`/lenient registry scaffolding within this area alone* — It looks obsolete because all slugs are now bound, but `BoundCheck.eval` and `NotImplemented` are consumed by parity, CLI rendering, tap mapping, and run preflight outside the owned files. Removing it is a cross-area API change, not a local simplification.
- *Merge the generator's contract subset with `sabrage-core::Contract`* — The independent subset at `sabrage-contract-gen/src/lib.rs:62` deliberately proves which fields reach the shell. The field-mutation test at `lib.rs:423` pins the A1-2 failure mode; depending on the full core model would hide omitted shell emissions and pull the full runtime crate into a tiny development tool.
- *Cut generator golden or hostile-shell tests* — The committed-file byte equality at `sabrage-contract-gen/src/lib.rs:377`, per-field body mutation at `lib.rs:423`, structural hostile-value assertions at `lib.rs:578`, and real-zsh source test at `lib.rs:618` are the generator's product contract and pin confirmed A1-2/A1-8 findings.
- *Cut config, meta, network, or run-only regression tests touched by review fixes* — A3b-1/A3b-3 are pinned at `checks/config.rs:499` and `config.rs:571`; compiled-contract identity at `checks/meta.rs:210`; failed adb queries at `checks/network.rs:251`; and the bounded preflight child at `checks/run_only.rs:496`. Their fixture boilerplate can shrink, but their assertions and named cases must remain.
- *Remove compiled contract identity/hash duplication* — The on-disk header check and compiled identity answer different questions. `contract.rs:269` exposes the binary's baked hash, while `checks/meta.rs:63` first checks checkout/header consistency and then binary/checkout equality; this pins confirmed A1-1.
- *Cut individual doctor features such as audio, headset, or network checks* — Their slugs and gates live in `contract/pipeline.toml` and the maintained shell doctor emits the matching rows. Removing or silently skipping them requires out-of-scope contract/shell changes and would break tap parity.

**V5 — config editor + app store**

- *Collapse the runtime reader into `toml_edit` or remove one of the two parsers* — They answer different questions by design: values come from the Config.cpp-compatible reader while `toml_edit` only determines safe edit placement (`runtime_toml.rs:46-64`). Literal quotes, last-valid semantics, multiline strings, quoted keys, and BOMs demonstrably disagree in tests at `runtime_toml.rs:1844-1928`, `runtime_toml.rs:2305-2509`, and `runtime_toml.rs:2620-2694`.
- *Replace the format-preserving editor with line replacement or normal TOML serialization* — The editor must find the final runtime-visible bare assignment across tables while ignoring dotted/quoted keys (`runtime_toml.rs:811-901`) and restore BOM/CRLF/EOF shape after `toml_edit` rendering (`runtime_toml.rs:971-1046`). The frozen fixture and byte assertions at `runtime_toml.rs:1778-2069` and `runtime_toml.rs:2696-2725` are product contracts.
- *Share config backup/CAS machinery with the JSON stores* — The JSON stores are app-owned and already delegate one atomic replacement to `Executor::write_atomic` in eight lines (`store/settings.rs:211-220`, `store/library.rs:269-280`). The TOML is human/runtime/shared state requiring live-session refusal, cross-process locking, compare-and-swap, backup reservation, rollback, and post-commit pruning (`runtime_toml.rs:1217-1381`, `runtime_toml.rs:1453-1604`); abstracting them together would erase materially different contracts.
- *Add a generic JSON repository for all three store files* — There are only two JSON repositories: settings load/save at `store/settings.rs:184-220` and library load/save at `store/library.rs:200-280`. `store/goldberg.rs:107-247` is a DLL restore action with no JSON state. A generic helper would save only the two short serde/read/write sequences while complicating their different version and transaction policies.
- *Delete settings/library version and unknown-field protections while retaining autosave* — Settings deliberately preserves unknown top-level and nested launch keys (`store/settings.rs:53-65`, `store/settings.rs:113-125`) and refuses newer versions before autosave (`store/settings.rs:184-208`); tests at `store/settings.rs:288-375` pin downgrade survival. Library likewise refuses a newer schema before mutation (`store/library.rs:185-224`). These are confirmed-review data-loss guards, not decorative schema machinery.
- *Remove `LIBRARY_LOCK`/`transact` because writes are atomic* — Atomic rename does not make load-mutate-save atomic; the lock explicitly spans the transaction at `store/library.rs:227-267`. The interleaving test at `store/library.rs:780-822` proves why a removed entry must not be resurrected. Even a reduced CRUD-only library can receive concurrent save/remove commands.
- *Simplify Goldberg provenance or liveness checks while keeping Restore* — If the feature remains, its risk is the feature: it copies a DLL on the machine. The three-way Goldberg test and operation/session/process refusals at `store/goldberg.rs:158-235` exist because neither a pin nor current payload alone proves backup provenance; the protected cases are exercised at `store/goldberg.rs:396-591` and `store/goldberg.rs:619-734`. The honest simplification is V5-2’s scope cut, not weakening these guards.

**V6 — core primitives + privilege**

- *Delete `Executor` and test only against real temporary directories* — Deleting the trait does not delete the operations; their signatures move onto RealExecutor. A meaningful deletion requires cutting dry-run itself: roughly 430 assigned production lines in the plan model (`executor.rs:87-216`), DryRunExecutor (`executor.rs:991-1261`), and privilege's dry branch/helper (`privilege.rs:425-430`, `privilege.rs:479-503`), plus its invariant/render tests (`executor.rs:1563-1879`). Real temp directories can cover filesystem calls but cannot safely stand in for run_child, detached Wine launches, or sudo/osascript; parity also uses recorded actions for launch env/argv goldens (`sabrage-parity/src/lib.rs:1418-1838`). The trait earns its cost unless the owner cuts dry-run and replaces the parity recorder with another abstraction.
- *Reduce privilege to a direct `osascript` invocation or replace staging with `tempfile`* — The call is only the center of the boundary. Two-layer injection defense is at privilege.rs:257-366, skip/announce/verify behavior at privilege.rs:382-477, bounded elevated-child cancellation at privilege.rs:632-700, and create-new 0600 staging with cancellation defuse plus stale sweeping at privilege.rs:703-787. `tempfile` could hide a few creation/drop lines but cannot remove the unusual keep-on-cancel and age-sweep policy, so it adds a dependency without removing the hard part.
- *Collapse StageEvent or the step-id vocabulary* — The vocabulary is consumed, not speculative: StageEvent accessors cover every variant at events.rs:334-415; Progress is emitted at stages/build.rs:398; the CLI exhaustively renders or deliberately suppresses variants at sabrage-cli/src/main.rs:722-778. Step arrays are validated for uniqueness/order/cardinality at events.rs:587-605 and Stage::steps is used to size live run progress at stages/run/actions.rs:960. Only the prose ledger is excess.
- *Replace ChunkSplitter and process-group handling with standard line APIs* — Standard line APIs erase the LF/CR/CRLF/EOF distinction represented at process.rs:187-281 and carried into output events at process.rs:537-568. Cancellation also depends on process-group liveness and bounded pipe draining at process.rs:389-514. Those exact seams are pinned at process.rs:977-1017, 1077-1108, and 1153-1284; replacing them would reopen confirmed A14-3/A2-5 behavior.
- *Remove the parity-only block_on or SyncFsExecutor as test scaffolding* — The dependency-free poller is at sabrage-parity/src/lib.rs:1424-1447 and SyncFsExecutor at lib.rs:1523-1660. Dry-run records only byte counts, so the synchronous real writer is what lets the steam_appid.txt golden assert the actual on-disk bytes at lib.rs:1662-1711 without adding tokio. This is awkward-looking but directly supports a mandatory golden.
- *Replace the version-stamp scanner with a regex dependency* — The scanner at util/mod.rs:93-198 deliberately implements the shell's leftmost digit-dot extraction across binary noise, and its edge cases are table-pinned at util/mod.rs:537-558. A regex would save only about 60 production lines, add another core dependency, and make exact grep-equivalent byte behavior less obvious; it is not a material lever.
- *Cut the Tauri quit/exit commentary* — Unlike the broad module essays, these comments guard GUI lifecycle behavior with no local test harness: menu quit routes through request_quit at src-tauri/src/lib.rs:32-41, page-load tail requests intentionally discard only transport errors at lib.rs:211-217, and RunEvent::ExitRequested must be prevented while cleanup is delegated at lib.rs:255-294. These are compact landmine rationales around previously reviewed quit/teardown behavior, so Rule C says keep them.

**V7 — CLI + Tauri command layer**

- *Generate or collapse the roughly 27 Tauri commands into one generic dispatcher* — The registration list is long but mechanical at lib.rs:176-204; many leaf commands are already 3-12 lines, such as read_runtime_config at commands.rs:1759-1764, get_settings at commands.rs:1829-1836, and validate_game at commands.rs:2142-2149. The mass lies in heterogeneous lifecycle and policy work—stage cancellation at commands.rs:571-615, stop/quit at commands.rs:739-1153, tail ownership at commands.rs:1427-1575, and guarded config writes at commands.rs:1766-1825. A generic enum command would require a UI IPC rewrite and discard typed arguments for little net deletion.
- *Replace the frozen CLI parser with clap* — The actual parser is small: shared flag consumption is main.rs:230-268 and the two outer loops are main.rs:274-303 and main.rs:650-682. It must preserve the unusual flags-before-command diagnostic at main.rs:159-175, literal usage at main.rs:83-121, first-bad-token behavior, exact errors, and exit 2. The manifest explicitly records that clap's generated text/codes violate those constraints at sabrage/crates/sabrage-cli/Cargo.toml:10-18; disabling clap's help/error rendering and restoring custom strings would keep nearly all current surface plus a dependency.
- *Delete the native CLI wholesale* — It is the only true multi-thousand-line feature cut, but it violates the stated always-keep live parity product: tier 2 builds sabrage-cli and runs its doctor at scripts/dev/parity.sh:245-251. The binary also owns the frozen seven-command dispatch at main.rs:132-155. Removing it requires out-of-scope script/PARITY policy changes, so it is blocked under this review's rules rather than a valid V7 cut.
- *Share the CLI renderer with the GUI renderer* — They do different jobs. The CLI converts events into shell-compatible stdout/stderr bytes, TTY color, and CR repaint behavior at main.rs:407-627 and main.rs:712-815. Tauri forwards the structured StageEvent unchanged at commands.rs:391-415 and commands.rs:571-615; the Svelte UI chooses visual rows. The only textual GUI projection, reconcile_row_text at commands.rs:1324-1358, intentionally emits uncolored request/response banner text and is only about 28 code lines.
- *Replace the raw signal counter with tokio::signal solely for size* — The counter is deliberately a count rather than a flag so two deliveries inside one 50 ms interval remain two events at main.rs:1259-1268, and the tests pin same-kind bursts and mixed SIGINT/SIGTERM delivery at main.rs:2544-2637. Signal-stream APIs commonly coalesce pending notifications; unless a replacement proves those semantics, it trades a small production block for a regression in the impatient-second-signal escape hatch. V7-1 and V7-2 remove the narration/test scaffolding without touching the mechanism.
- *Remove the quit, teardown, tail-registry, or settings-path state as generic defensive bloat* — These are review-finding seams, not speculative abstractions: timeout/detach distinctions drive stop and quit at commands.rs:739-986; custom Quit and AppKit termination handling live at lib.rs:32-41 and lib.rs:262-291; TailGuard and page-load cleanup cover task leakage at commands.rs:1427-1567 and lib.rs:211-225. SettingsPathsCache at commands.rs:1651-1728 was itself an already-applied simplification and is invalidated by save_settings; re-proposing its removal would undo that pass.

**V8 — the Svelte UI**

- *Three generic Card/Form/Row components* — The apparent repetition has different semantics and scoped styling. Settings uses positive controls inverted into negative wire flags (`Settings.svelte:69-74`, `Settings.svelte:154-168`), Session edits negative flags directly (`Session.svelte:37-41`), and EditGame uses nullable three-way overrides (`EditGame.svelte:149-186`). Card spacing and titles also differ at `Settings.svelte:940-962`, `Session.svelte:604-644`, and `EditGame.svelte:565-674`; generic components would replace visible lines with prop/slot machinery for little net reduction.
- *Merge the six rune stores* — They are small but have genuinely different lifecycles: serialized, failure-reconverging writes at `stores/settings.svelte.ts:20-123`; an hours-long launch plus standing subscriptions at `stores/session.svelte.ts:89-222`; streamed ordered checks at `stores/doctor.svelte.ts:62-94`; and modal ownership at `stores/stage.svelte.ts:40-89`. A generic store would move those lines behind callbacks rather than remove them.
- *Unify GateModal’s run and non-run execution paths* — A stage promise settles with the operation (`ipc.ts:342-364`), while `launch()` intentionally remains pending until a potentially hours-long session ends (`ipc.ts:575-605`). GateModal therefore owns short stage state at `GateModal.svelte:212-264` but observes app-lifetime launch state at `GateModal.svelte:334-364`; collapsing them risks reintroducing hidden-gate cancellation and ownership failures.
- *Generate all of ipc.ts inside V8* — The types do not originate from one Rust schema: custom Tauri projections such as DoctorEvent live at `sabrage/src-tauri/src/commands.rs:89-116`, while StageEvent lives at `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/events.rs:186` and other shapes live in session/config/store modules. Proper generation requires Rust derives, generator infrastructure, and a dependency outside this reviewer’s files; checked-in generated declarations would also preserve much of the line count. Narrowing and comment removal are the in-scope wins.
- *Replace FIX_META with a get_fix_defs command while keeping Fix buttons* — The static table is concentrated at `ipc.ts:216-304` and already feeds both Doctor and GateModal. Adding a command creates another Rust/IPC shape and failure path merely to move a small table. Either retain it with the fix feature or take the larger V8-2 scope cut.
- *Delete the Settings repository/contract diagnostics* — This is also the checkout selector and rollback path (`Settings.svelte:343-385`), and it visibly distinguishes shell-contract sync from binary-contract mismatch (`Settings.svelte:801-872`, `ipc.ts:1005-1027`). Removing it leaves an installed app with no safe way to select or diagnose the checkout it will mutate.
- *Delete QuitDialog or detach handling as modal bloat* — The dialog is the responder for intercepted live-session quits (`QuitDialog.svelte:1-70`, `ipc.ts:759-775`), while Session exposes the same detach policy at `Session.svelte:293-309`. Removing only the UI would leave a backend quit protocol without a responder; choosing an unconditional stop/keep policy requires an out-of-scope backend and parity decision.
- *Refactor or trim styles.css* — The fixed design system already centralizes blueprint frames at `styles.css:108-130`, forms at `styles.css:199-238`, cards at `styles.css:240-257`, and dialogs at `styles.css:296-321`. Changing it is outside the visual-preservation constraint, and moving scoped screen rules into it changes ownership without eliminating the distinct rules.
- *Mechanically delete About for 210 lines* — About is the only in-app upstream attribution and license surface, rendered at `About.svelte:101-115`. Removing it is a separate product/licensing T3 decision, not a safe mechanical simplification, and it is smaller than the proposed library, fix, and logs cuts.

---

## Reviewer headlines

Each reviewer's own summary of where its lines went. Read together they are the most honest
answer to "why is this 67k lines".

**C1 — completeness critic** (5 proposals)

> The 3 ACCEPT plus 41 ACCEPT_WITH_CARE proposals claim -9,819 lines raw, but their duplicated executor, scratch-fixture, wire-type, and test cuts reduce the credible net to about 8,000, leaving Sabrage at roughly 59,000 lines. It would still contain about 25,000 test lines and 8,000 lines of comments/docs—surprising residual mass after a “simplification” pass. A requirements-known rewrite is plausibly 36,000–40,000 total lines: roughly 17,000 production, 15,000 tests, and 4,000–8,000 comments/blanks; the biggest structural lever is eliminating the webview/IPC application boundary while retaining the independent Rust pipeline.

**C2 — contradiction critic** (12 proposals)

> The corrected headline is an estimated 7,348 lines saved, not 9,819: the accepted set overstates savings by 2,471 lines. The biggest lever is selecting H3-1 as the sole shared test-support refactor, then treating the executor, RAII, fixture, and table proposals as incremental work.

**H1 — cross-crate duplication and the two front-ends** (5 proposals)

> The parity crate is not a fourth implementation: it is test-only, reads the checkout, and calls core renderers (`sabrage/crates/sabrage-parity/src/lib.rs:1-16`, `:1083-1097`). The largest honest lever is product scope: retain `sabrage doctor` for the live parity harness while deleting the other native CLI commands, then optionally make setup/build/install shell-only—about 6,250 incremental lines. If both scope cuts are rejected, the credible behavior-preserving reduction is roughly 1,160 lines from generated IPC types, shared test executors, and comment compression.

**H2 — YAGNI and unused surface** (5 proposals)

> The credible H2 cut is 667 lines: two optional GUI concepts, 90 lines of genuinely dead/test-only surface, 104 redundant comment lines, and 47 unused persisted metadata lines. The single biggest lever is deleting the static About microsite (-222); deleting per-game launch overrides is nearly as large (-204) and removes an entire precedence axis rather than merely relocating its merge. Most low-reference Rust items are nevertheless serde/Tauri/parity seams, so visibility narrowing alone would not materially shrink the app.

**H3 — the test mass** (5 proposals)

> At equal assertion coverage, the credible target is 2,058 fewer test lines: runtime_toml 1,881→1,741, reconcile 1,851→1,656, run/mod 1,624→1,389, watcher 1,478→1,438, actions 1,323→1,213, CLI 1,258→1,088, preflight 1,255→1,160, stop 1,046→978, guards 738→613, setup 702→627, and the remaining files 14,919→14,114—28,075→26,017 overall. The biggest behaviour-preserving lever is one cfg(test) harness for executor faults, scratch roots, and event capture; it removes about 840 lines without deleting a test or assertion. The larger owner-choice lever is deleting dry-run: about 2,200 current-tree lines, including 1,250 test lines; after discounting roughly 140 lines that overlap the refactors, the full portfolio is about 4,118 lines and leaves roughly 24,907 test lines.

**H4 — the comment mass** (6 proposals)

> The current artifact contains 86 comment blocks of at least 20 lines: 84 can be compressed from 3,139 physical lines to about 364, while two blocks totaling 52 lines should remain intact. That removes an estimated 2,775 lines—21.8% of all comments and 4.1% of the 67,088-line app—without changing tests, contracts, golden bytes, or behavior; the biggest lever is replacing prose proofs and shell transcriptions with one invariant plus a test/PARITY pointer. The maintenance cost is already visible: `checks/config.rs:40-47` says `serde_json/preserve_order` is absent, while `sabrage-core/Cargo.toml:8-10` enables it and `PARITY.md:18` relies on it.

**H5 — dependency substitution** (4 proposals)

> Four material substitutions can remove about 1,215 lines net: test-directory RAII, signal handling, generated Rust-to-TypeScript wire types, and shared test executor fault injection. Only the first three justify dependencies; none of the existing low-use dependencies is worth removing. No severe bug was encountered.

**H6 — type and wire-surface sprawl** (7 proposals)

> Most of the removable mass is not the Rust taxonomy itself; it is the second, hand-maintained TypeScript spelling of that taxonomy and hundreds of lines narrating the wire contract. The biggest standalone lever is compile-time generation of the TS bindings, worth about 410 lines while preserving the current JSON exactly. Beyond that, the honest semantic cuts are narrow: one UI-unused reconciliation graph, one duplicated doctor DTO, one dead error DTO, one non-production status, and several unread response fields.

**H7 — feature surface — what a personal launcher does not need** (9 proposals)

> The best near-zero-value cut is the non-doctor CLI: demo.sh already owns that terminal vocabulary, while parity.sh needs only `doctor --tap`. The largest absolute lever is collapsing the multi-game library into the existing single-target Session + Settings flow; dry-run is similarly large but buys meaningful safety and therefore ranks lower. Prices are standalone and overlap—notably library/revert and CLI/dry-run—so they must not be summed mechanically.

**V1 — stages/run — launch state machine** (7 proposals)

> Within the current feature/parity contract, most launch-state machinery is load-bearing: automatic audio/dashboard ownership, crash recovery, detach, and wired rollback genuinely require distinct states. The biggest lever is a T4 policy decision to make audio and dashboard management manual, removing about 2,050 assigned-area lines. Without scope cuts, comment compression and test-scaffolding consolidation can still remove about 584 lines at equal behavioral coverage.

**V2 — stages setup/build/install/stop + fixes** (7 proposals)

> These files are not large because four shell stages were inflated into a reusable state-machine framework: the entrypoints are already flat, while each stage carries different safety obligations. The largest behavior-preserving lever is roughly 895 lines of shell transcription and review narration, followed by about 245 lines of test-harness and table compaction. Three optional scope cuts remove another roughly 669 lines; no severe bug was encountered.

**V3 — session lifecycle + logs** (5 proposals)

> The four-way classifier is only about 20 lines (`session/reconcile.rs:302`); the bulk is crash-safe side-effect orchestration, telemetry history, review regressions, and narration—not an abstraction tower. The single largest safe cut is replacing the 693 lines in long comment blocks with invariant-and-test pointers, removing about 625 lines. Structurally, extracting `SessionMonitor`’s policy from its filesystem/global fixture harness can remove roughly another 500 test lines without weakening the confirmed-finding coverage; the guard flags and rotation machinery themselves are load-bearing.

**V4 — checks + contract + generator** (6 proposals)

> The registry and `contract.rs` are not the dominant mass: they enforce contract ordering, gates, and binary/checkout identity, while many evaluators have exceptional shell semantics that resist a useful DSL. The removable bulk is narration and proof scaffolding: the six proposals below remove about 1,124 lines while retaining all slugs, gates, tap behavior, golden bytes, and finding-pinned assertions. The biggest lever is therefore comment and test consolidation, not replacing the registry.

**V5 — config editor + app store** (6 proposals)

> The biggest lever is product scope: make `library.json` a small name/path/bottle registry and remove the Sabrage-only Goldberg restore, eliminating roughly 1,587 owned lines if both cuts are accepted. If features stay, the credible behaviour-preserving reduction is about 1,000 lines, principally compressed comments and shared test fixtures—not the two-parser split or persistence guards. Proposal deltas are alternatives and overlap where a T3 deletion subsumes comment/test cuts.

**V6 — core primitives + privilege** (7 proposals)

> These foundations are large chiefly because they carry review-pinned boundaries, not because `Executor` is gratuitous: it is the production dry-run mutation boundary (`sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/executor.rs:220-381`, `sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/executor.rs:991-1261`), and parity launch goldens consume it (`sabrage/crates/sabrage-parity/src/lib.rs:1418-1838`). Privilege’s code is mostly the actual quoting, cancellation-safe staging, elevation, and verification boundary (`sabrage/crates/sabrage-core/src/privilege.rs:257-797`), so reducing it to “one osascript call” is false economy. The biggest honest lever is about 1,251 lines of narrative and shell-transcription comments, retaining executable tests and compact landmine pointers.

**V7 — CLI + Tauri command layer** (4 proposals)

> The 5,889 lines did not go into 27 mechanical wrappers: most wrappers are already tiny, while the real mass is review-era narration and repetitive test construction around parser, renderer, and lifecycle policies. The behavior-preserving cuts below remove about 1,695 lines; the single biggest lever is compressing long histories into one-line landmines and test pointers. The CLI renderer is not materially duplicated by the GUI, which consumes structured events rather than shell-shaped text. No severe bug surfaced.

**V8 — the Svelte UI** (5 proposals)

> The UI’s volume is feature surface plus defensive lifecycle code, not an abstraction tower: Library, EditGame, and their store alone occupy 1,346 lines (`Library.svelte:1`, `EditGame.svelte:1`, `stores/library.svelte.ts:1`), while the IPC facade spans 1,219 lines (`ipc.ts:1`). The biggest lever is a T3 single-profile launcher that removes the library/edit flow for about 1,560 UI lines. If features stay, the credible behaviour-preserving reduction is roughly 725 lines from comment compression and provably dead or unconsumed UI/IPC surface; generic cards or merged stores will not materially move the total.

---

## Method

**Measure first.** A deterministic pass produced eleven tables before any model ran: per-file
code/test/comment split, test-fn inventory, comment-density and every block ≥12 lines with its
full text, a token-shingle duplicate detector, a pub-symbol reference census, a struct/enum
census, a dependency inventory, an index of all 180 round-1/round-2 findings, and — the one that
did the most work — `fix-blame.tsv`, mapping every current line to the review-fix commit that
wrote it. Every reviewer prompt carried these, so proposals are quantified rather than vibed.

**Scan.** Fifteen Codex `gpt-5.6-sol` reviewers at `xhigh` in read-only sandboxes, all fifteen
concurrent: eight vertical (one subsystem each) and seven horizontal (one lens each, whole tree).
The horizontal lenses exist because a purely vertical partition cannot see duplication, and every
vertical reviewer has an incentive to defend its own subsystem's necessity. Two rules were carried
verbatim in every prompt — **Rule C**: a comment earns its place only if deleting it could let a
future editor break something no test would catch, and a proposal to cut one must supply the
replacement text; **Rule T**: the parity and golden tests are the product, and a test cut must be
reported as lines removed *at equal assertion coverage*. All fifteen completed at `xhigh` on the
first attempt; no capacity failures and no reduced-effort runs.

**Verify.** 23 opus verifiers, ~5 proposals each, told to refute by default. Each re-measured the
claim by reading the files, checked it against `fix-blame.tsv` and the 180-finding index, checked
it against `PARITY.md` and the golden-byte list, and rated it. This caught real inflation — one
proposal was 3× over-claimed, another 5× — and it is why the table above prints both the
reviewer's claim and the measured number. Verification was by reading, not building: fifteen
agents sharing one `target/` would have serialized for an hour, so a handful of proposals whose
viability turns on a compile result are rated `ACCEPT_WITH_CARE` and say so.

**Critique and synthesis.** Two more Codex reviewers over the merged set: a completeness critic
("what did fifteen reviewers miss?") and a contradiction critic ("which savings are double
counted?"). An opus pass then reproduced the contradiction critic's arithmetic line by line —
finding two sub-10-line slips and keeping the conservative figures — and attributed every removed
region to exactly one proposal. A final opus pass sequenced the survivors into the five waves.

**Provenance.** All fifteen reviewers plus both critics ran at `xhigh`. 93 proposals, 93 verified,
0 verifier agents lost. Prior art this run was told not to re-propose: the 19 items applied by the
2026-08-30 `/simplify` pass and its 6 skipped-with-cause items.

**What the contradiction critic said, for the record.** The corrected headline is an estimated 7,348 lines saved, not 9,819: the accepted set overstates savings by 2,471 lines. The biggest lever is selecting H3-1 as the sole shared test-support refactor, then treating the executor, RAII, fixture, and table proposals as incremental work.

**Scope.** Recommendations target `sabrage/` only. `contract/`, `scripts/`, `demo.sh`, `docs/`
and `ext/` were read-only reference; a simplification reachable only through a contract or shell
change is recorded as blocked, not proposed. Nothing in the repository was modified by this scan.
